# Continuity State

Chapters 1–20 of Volume 01 exist as finished prose (`chapters/volume-01/chapter-0001.md` … `chapter-0020.md`). The batch summaries are in `state/batch-summaries.md`, the per-chapter summaries are in `state/chapter-summaries.md`, and immediate character state is in `state/character-state.md`. The bootstrap canon below remains authoritative for the first volume and later batches unless a future outline explicitly records a change in `state/canon-changes.md`.

## World and timeline

- Alderquay is a river-delta city built on reclaimed marsh and governed through the Continuance Charter.
- The Long Rain struck the Alder Fault forty-one years before the opening. During the emergency response, crews built the First Atlas, the original design of the Common Atlas.
- The First Atlas was **plural by design**: several named routes, custody distributed among district crews, and deliberate gaps left wherever two accounts of a place could not be reconciled. Its gaps were meant to stay open.
- The crews added an emergency setting, the **Singular Mode**, which forced one conflicting account to become the Index’s version and suspended the others. It was logged as temporary.
- The Quieting occurred because the Singular Mode was still engaged after the flooding began to recede and its forced output was then treated as the record. Nine districts ceased to be reachable, and tens of thousands were displaced or lost.
- The Registry of Continuance was formed afterward, with a founding rule of no single authority over a route and no gap closed without a hearing. It reversed both clauses, rebuilt the Singular Mode as a standard instrument, and began using emergency closures for land, identity, and political control.
- Lark Street was erased eighteen years before the opening. Soren Rook approved a necessary route severing to stop an immediate seamquake, then used the emergency to erase claims and suppress inquiry. Neris Rook died during the evacuation. Her route trace remains distributed among survivors and is not a recoverable person.

## Magic rules

- A **full remnant** forms when a place is forcibly cut off from all three supports of the **triad: name, route, witness**. These three words are canonical; do not substitute body, address, or witness community when describing an erasure.
- **Partial erasure:** one support withdrawn leaves a ruin, a disputed address, or a rumor. Two supports withdrawn leave a **thin remnant** — unstable, offering at most one service, unable to maintain its own witness, and the usual site of a small seamquake. All three withdrawn leaves a full remnant with a cue, a service, a demand, and a failure. A half-completed erasure, with one support left carrying pressure it was never built for, can produce a full remnant more coherent and more dangerous than a cleanly erased one.
- Unless a passage says “thin remnant,” *remnant* means the full kind.
- Remnants occupy seams and follow a dominant memory grammar: Cadence, Keepsake, Kinship, Mercy, Ledger, Weather, or Threshold. There are no other categories.
- The seven grammars are a vocabulary, not a solution. Every street combines a dominant grammar, a leaking secondary grammar, its own disguised cue, its own demand, and its own failure condition, so two streets of the same grammar can require opposite handling.
- Habit memory is repeated human use, not a perfect recording of the past. A rumor without a lived route or shared practice is not magical.
- A viable map uses a threshold, a meaningful turn, a witness, and a return route. Durable restoration also needs a blank for current life.
- A map can trace, guide, stabilize, restore, or hold a route. It cannot read minds, resurrect the dead, force consent, or prove one history is the only truth.
- Fixed restoration without a blank creates a claimed place and binds the custodian to inherited roles.
- Atlas residue may offer one short Field Book prompt. It is incomplete and never solves the moral or political choice.

## Power costs and their fixed onset

`bible/power-system.md` is authoritative. A cost may not appear before the stage that introduces it.

| Cost | Onset | Permanence |
|---|---|---|
| Orientation blank | Volume 1, Stage 1, first crossing | Accumulates; some routes never return in the long run. Every Volume 1 loss stays recoverable when marked |
| Sensory detail failing | Volumes 2–3, Stage 2 | Unpredictable; some details do not come back |
| First personal blank | Volumes 4–5, Stage 3 | Permanent; requires another person’s testimony |
| Visible seam-scar and dependence on being maintained | Volumes 5–6, Stage 4 | Permanent physical cost; solitary major fieldwork unsafe |
| Record blank | Volumes 7–8, Stage 5 | Permanent within official records; relationships become infrastructure |
| Authorial blank | Volumes 9–10, Stage 6 | Permanent civic cost; includes the lost sound of Neris’s voice |

## People and relationships

- Milo Rook is thirty, a Civic Atlas Office field surveyor, with a controlling-authority flaw and a private Lark Street goal. He carries the brass route-token stamped Lark Street and keeps his own Field Book, which is not office property. **His working life is fixed so it reconciles:** apprenticed into the office at nineteen, in the post six years, in his second year five years ago at twenty-five, which is the sluice injury Iona Sen remembers and the only way anyone states his age on the page. Note that the batch uses *six years* for two things — his tenure and the interval since Orrin's order — and they coincide.
- Mara Quill is thirty-one, a Registry Continuity Records advocate, professional foil, and the only planned romantic partner.
- Pell Harrow is a seventeen-year-old courier; his brother Tern is found alive in a moving community in Volume 8 and does not return to the old neighborhood.
- Iona Sen is a seam physician and former Thread Warden. Tarin Kest is a reform-minded Registry inspector. Sel Iri leads the Open-Door Choir.
- Orrin Sable is the local Volume 1 antagonist; Halden Vey is the institutional antagonist; Soren Rook is the final antagonist and Milo’s father.
- Soren’s method must be stated on the page: a complete inventory of excluded routes, a bounded operation with a published shutdown, and a sealed residual layer meant to store the suspended pressure. He is right about the bookkeeping and wrong about holding, and a singular Index is also what keeps him alive.
- Volume 01 introduced cast not in the series bible: Ada Fenn, Lena Marr, Owen Fitch, Tomas Rusk, Jo Lask, and the dead maintenance worker Jonas Venn. Their fixed names, roles, and wants are registered in `bible/terminology.md` under “Volume 01 local register.” No new final enemy, grammar, or faction may be added here or later.
- Ada Fenn is the woman in the laundry work coat holding a door in the Chapters 3–4 overlap. The reader and Milo recognize her at the same moment in Chapter 5, when she says the third strike still comes through her hand; the crossing in Chapter 4 gives him no name and no reason to expect it. She is the standing third support of Bellwether’s triad, which is why the seam can be negotiated with and why she is dangerous to the order.
- **Volume 01 supporting cast introduced in Chapters 1–10**, now fixed: **Dilley Farr**, route-room supervisor at the Civic Atlas Office, who refuses to sign a deletion and walks Milo to the north window without making it visible; **Cass Ardery**, Survey Enforcement officer carrying the instruction *a seal*; **Danner**, the contractor's foreman at Floodgate Four, who winds the gate to eighteen inches by hand and writes *no window* on the form; **Rue** and **Idar**, Streetkeepers in Pell Harrow's round. Registered in `bible/terminology.md`. No new faction, grammar, or antagonist has been introduced.
- **Mara Quill's countersignature is on the page.** In Chapter 6 she finds her own block capitals on the six-year-old intake routing slip for the Bellwether file, in the box that meant *I have seen that an address is affected and passed it on*. It is the concrete form of her fear of complicity and must be carried forward, not resolved inside Volume 1.
- **The annotating hand is not Soren and is not named as anybody.** In Chapters 1–10 the recurring hand appears as a bracket-and-dot survey mark: on the Chapter 1 timetable margin, where the ink carries a damp halo and bridges the printer's fold — the ink arrived wet and was pressed onto the fold this week, so the press is not the clue — the ink is older than the paper, because the mark was not drawn for this stock and came off something else; and on the Chapter 6 works-perimeter annex, compared by Mara in the records alcove on the 5th and written down as *consistent with*, *facsimile, not original, cannot be sworn to*. Chapter 9 **ends** on Mara stopping at *there's writing on this* and nothing more; the two unsigned margin questions on the carbon attachment are **read aloud in the alcove on the 13th**, the day before the hearing, and it is **Chapter 10** that carries the full reading, the two different leads, and Milo's *consistent with, not sworn to, possible third instance, no more*. **Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–10.** His only presence is the method: a polite young man from Crown Heights asked Ada Fenn what the third bell was for nine years ago, wrote her answer down, and eleven months later a sentence from her own kitchen turned up in a document she never showed him. Later volumes may not confirm the parentage, the authorship, or the Lark order without evidence on the page.
- **Attachment 9 — the copied maintenance route.** A carbon copy of a pump-house and embankment maintenance schedule, copied from an older survey book, whose diversion line names a destination that can be dated out of use and cannot be placed on any current map. It is not an invented name: it has current and correct neighbouring streets and a bus route that stopped serving it in a datable year, so it went out of use by people getting older rather than by a decision. In Chapter 9 Milo refuses it as the demonstration's route. It is a clue, not a solution, and it opens the Volume 2 question.

## Ending constraints

- Soren initiates the One Survey, which is the Singular Mode run deliberately and citywide. It briefly suppresses seamquakes by forcing singular agreement, then draws excluded pressure through the Common Index and causes the terminal Greywake.
- Milo refuses both sole authorship and a fixed, claimed restoration of Lark Street.
- The coalition maintains a distributed Atlas. Selected places return as living, revisable districts; unstable places remain marked hazards or memorials.
- Soren releases the Index and dies. Neris is not resurrected. Milo loses a fixed autobiographical coordinate, reliable official location, and the remembered sound of Neris’s voice.
- The larger extent of the Margin remains intentionally open, but no new cosmic antagonist may be introduced.
- The final image leaves a walkable blank, not a perfect map.

## Volume 01 plan in force

- `outline/volume-01.md` is written and authoritative for Chapters 1–60: central pressure, Chapter 30 midpoint reversal, Chapters 54–57 climax, Chapters 58–60 concrete resolution, and the next-volume question. Volume 1 ends at Stage 1 with an orientation blank that is still recoverable; persistence and Mara’s return route belong to Volume 2.
- **Bellwether’s triad accounting is fixed (CR-010):** name withdrawn and route closed, with the witness support broken rather than removed and one routine — Ada’s evening third strike and door-check — left standing and overloaded. Two withdrawn, one standing: a near-completion full remnant. Never write it as all three withdrawn, and never as a thin remnant that later grew. The Chapter 30 reversal is where the volume states this on the page.
- **The western culvert runs on one state track (CR-011):** throttled to a maintenance slit in Chapter 7 because a full seal would flood the works zone; the Chapter 9 crew arrives with the seal kit and does **not** finish the job, because the public passage occupies the site for the shift; and the climax’s floodgate test drives that same throttled gate back out to full flow, which is what lets the pinned seam widen. It is throttled and never sealed from Chapter 7 onward, and ends the volume gated, silted, and re-aligned.
- **The orientation blank has two stages in Volume 1 (CR-012):** Chapter 4 costs the remembered route while the Field Book stays intact; Chapters 51–53 cost the written line. The batch must not book the written-line loss early, and the two may not be collapsed.
- `outline/batches/volume-01-batch-0001.md` is written and authoritative for Chapters 1–10. Chapter 3 gives the first Atlas residue as the incomplete prompt “A return mark is missing,” not the witness rule; Chapter 4 earns the first half of the working rule and Chapter 5 the second, and the bells’ original purpose stays unresolved until the Chapter 30 reversal.
- The opening batch ends with a supervised one-shift passage, a temporary stay, an incomplete report, and a copied maintenance route pointing to a place with no official address. It does not defeat Orrin’s order or restore Bellwether.
- **What Chapters 1–10 actually put on the page**, for Chapters 11–20 to inherit: a one-shift provisional plan of a handrail, eleven inches by nine, with a threshold, a turn, four consented names with revocable terms, Lena Marr’s tenancy named in full, *one household has declined to be entered* with no name or address, and a deliberately empty right-hand third headed *for people who are not here yet*. That plan is a revocable one-shift document, not the neighborhood-scale provisional map of Chapters 41–50, and it must not be inflated into one.
- **Chapter 7’s culvert action is fixed on the page:** Danner winds the gate down to eighteen inches of sill at nine minutes past seven on the 6th of Sazur, by hand, in front of witnesses, because a full seal would back water into the works zone and the low streets. The Chapter 9 passage occupies the site for the whole shift, so the seal crew has *no window* and Danner writes that on the form twice. The gate is therefore throttled to eighteen inches from Chapter 7 onward, is never sealed in Volume 1, and is driven out to full flow by the 22nd of Sazur floodgate test. This is CR-011 on the page and must be carried exactly.
- **Atlas residue usage in the batch:** two appearances, both in Chapters 3 and 4 — *A return mark is missing* and *The lane counts*. Both are incomplete and Milo limits each in his own notes before acting. Chapter 9 has none, and he notices and distrusts the silence. Later batches should stay at this frequency.
- Cedar Court must keep three incompatible readings of the third turn alive through the opening batch, plus the fourth theory the closure notice supplies, so the midpoint can overturn all of them. On the page these are Ada’s clear-the-lane, **Jo Lask’s handover** (on a run the count is passed door-to-front at the third call and the tram does not move until it has been handed, so the third turn belongs to whoever is at the door), and Lena Marr’s count, with Tomas Rusk’s works shift interval and the notice’s count of the dead alongside them.
- **Milo is across twice in Chapters 1–10** (Chapter 4’s lane, at half past twelve, and Chapter 8’s loop, at ten past eight on the 6th) and does not cross in Chapter 9. Iona Sen corrects him on the point in Chapter 8 and he records the number himself, with the two dates, in the signed Chapter 9 report. Later batches may add crossings; they may not retroactively inflate the batch. The **crossing point is fixed on the page** as the west face of the works hoarding, about sixty metres from the disused platform end (the Chapter 3 Field Book note and the Chapter 6 sworn disclosure agree), and Pell’s observation that Milo came out “through the middle… there’s no middle” is why the return point is a different point on the same alignment. The **disused platform stands two hundred metres north of the works gate**, which is the figure Chapters 2, 4 and 9 all use. The **yard on the 9th holds roughly eighty people**, which is the figure Chapters 8 and 9 both use for the same crowd.
- **The Chapter 5 dawn proceeding is a Directions Bench variation, not the hearing.** The 5th grants a one-shift inspection and no stay; the 9th is that inspection; the 14th is the hearing. Chapter 6 is built on Orrin leaning on the 14th and Mara forcing him to separate the two facts in front of the clerk, and Chapter 7 refers back to *the variation on the fifth*. Later batches must not call the 5th a hearing, and must not treat it as the 14th.
- **The interval is called by hand, and the hands change.** In Chapter 7 Milo calls it himself from the arch foot for nine minutes, from seven until nine minutes past seven, until his voice fails on Danner’s come-along; he then hands it to **Pell on the tram side**, because Pell is the only one who can count, and refuses to call it a reward. **Pell keeps it up in Chapter 8 from seven until the moment Jo comes out at ten past eight — the hour he promised in Chapter 7, spent and paid, and he takes no credit for it and goes back to his round.** Jo Lask is the caller in Chapter 9, from his tram, and the marshal keeps the tally. Nobody inside a seam may call the count from outside it.
- **Owen Fitch is a hole in the plan and an addition to the record at once (CR-017).** Mara put his initials on the Registry instrument of the 5th-of-Sazur one-shift inspection, because the protective finding required *persons in temporary accommodation* and there was no other wording that got him into that yard without initials. She told him so in the Chapter 9 yard, in front of eighty people, with the instrument in her hand, and cannot take him off it. He required the refusal line to carry those initials. Milo concedes on the Chapter 10 landing that the plan is **not clean**: it holds nothing, and the Registry holds initials, a chainage, a date and a finding. **Later batches may not treat his entry as an absence from the record or let him be quietly scrubbed.** His standing refusal — one household declined, no name, no address — is the volume's counterweight to Lena Marr's, and Chapter 30 has to hold both.
- **The second source of witnessing is on the page and unwritten.** On the 10th, on the fourth-floor landing, Milo Rook asks Owen Fitch to take the third-floor stairwell check off Ada Fenn, who is ill and will not ask. Owen agrees in his own words: once a night, going up, not the ground floor, stopping whenever he likes, **not written down anywhere**, and Milo is to tell Ada herself and not the yard, because a rota that goes round the yard would stop her doing it altogether. **Chapters 11–20 must build a third source — a rota, a written schedule, a door-check another resident can perform — and must not turn the second into a dependency.**
- **The loop is three hundred and forty feet and the embankment span is a hundred and forty metres**, throughout. Chapter 7's fold reads in feet and the longer span in metres, so the two no longer describe the same thing.
- **Orrin’s argument against Milo’s map is on the page and unanswered.** In the Chapter 10 corridor he says the map did not make the seam coherent, it made it legible; that a believed route is a used route; and that on the 22nd it will be standing in the way of a river. He demands Milo say so to the yard *before* the test and not after. Milo writes the argument down in Orrin’s own words without editing it. This is open, and the next batch must not resolve it by reassurance.
- **A pinned seam** is the Volume 01 climax condition: a seam held open by a live order rather than sealed. It is registered in `bible/terminology.md` and is a condition only, not a new remnant type, grammar, or failure state. Later volumes may use it but may not treat it as a new category.
- **The clue chain is a standing thread:** one annotating hand is confirmed on two documents — the Chapter 1 timetable’s old-ink margin correction, which was **pressed wet onto a folded, damped sheet** — the halo says the ink arrived already wet, the fold says the sheet was shut when it landed, and the fold itself is this office’s own tray handling and dates nothing. **The press is this week’s; the ink is not.** The sound conclusion is only that the mark was not drawn for this stock and came off something older, so the ink predates the paper. Do not let anyone read the chronology as *fold before mark before paper*; a fold cannot predate the sheet it creases, and a mark transferred this week is not older than the sheet it landed on. The Chapter 10 carbon copy carries two unsigned marks in *different* inks, so at least one of them is a different author and the volume does not settle which. The chain is not resolved in Volume 1 and may not be named as Soren without evidence. Tracked in `outline/volume-01.md` under “Tracked clue chain.”

## What Chapters 11–20 fixed on the page, in the same form as CR-013

Each item below is now prose rather than plan, and each is load-bearing in a way a later batch could break without intending to. None of them changes a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder, or the planned ending, and none needs a numbered canon-change entry; they are recorded here and in `bible/terminology.md`.

1. **The hearing of the fourteenth of Sazur, and its result.** It sits from eleven and finishes at twenty to two. The continuity order is **upheld in full**, the culvert finding is upheld, and the twenty-second's test proceeds. Three things come out of it and none of them is a victory: the temporary stay **survives to the timetable review on the twenty-eighth** and is minuted clause by clause so its size is exact; the Registry is **directed to produce the complete file by the twenty-first** with the annexes in filed order and any withdrawn page, on Orrin's own offer invoked against him; and the **inspection sheet's refusal line is read into the record** — *One household has declined to be entered. Initials already held on the instrument of the fifth. Said no with knowledge of them* — after a clerk asks how to index a household with no name and Mara Quill refuses in open court to have the initials struck. **Later batches may not describe the fourteenth as a win, and may not say the order was lifted.**
2. **The flood levy.** The contractor's bond lapsed on the eleventh, so the works moved onto the district flood levy before the hearing, and the hearing **amends it: eleven per cent on the rateable, plus a per-head additional schedule on temporary accommodation, calculated on two hundred and thirty-one people in unlicensed rooms in the low streets who are not rateable.** The Bench cannot hear a rates schedule; Mara says she will take it to a rates authority before the week is out. The two hundred and thirty-one figure is the district's, and is used in Chapters 11, 14, 17 and 19.
3. **The three procedural dates are now distinguished on the page by a chair of the Bench**, in open court: *the variation of the fifth of Sazur sat at the Directions Bench at dawn and produced one shift of inspection; the inspection was on the ninth and it is a document; this is the hearing.* Later batches may not blur them.
4. **The address/money trap, which is the batch's engine and is not solved.** The relocation payment is assessed on **displacement from a named place**, so the name must remain on the compensation schedule and off the street index. A current address for Cedar Court — which the clinic, the school and the post all require — ends the payment. Lena Marr finds this herself, at the works-office counter, before anyone explains it. **Chapters 58–60 must resolve this by a route that costs somebody money, not by an administrative convenience.**
5. **Who may speak for a household.** A household is a named consenting adult, a dwelling, and a signature. Cedar Court's block tenancy was severed by the closure, and the last registered head of household for the block is **old Mabb, asleep on the third floor since the night of the closure six years ago, awake on the sixth of Sazur only to ask Owen for a light — a Saturday, and the only time in six years.** Every clerk who has come to the counter since has been entirely correct and has gone away again. **No character in Chapters 11–20 says the name Mabb out loud; Ada Fenn is the only one who has been up there in six years.**
6. **Cedar Court has five storeys.** Ground, second, third, fourth, fifth. Ada Fenn on three, Mabb on three, Owen Fitch on four, Ilse Oller on two, and **six rooms on the fifth that nobody in the district has been inside.** The fourth and fifth floors were kept out of the Chapter 21 drill on the twentieth, at Ada's request for the fifth and at Owen's insistence for both.
7. **The third source of witnessing is the accountancy-paper rota in Rue's satchel**, and it belongs to the courier round, not to Cedar Court's wall and not to Milo. A sheet folded in three, a column of floors, a column of nights and names, and at the foot the rule **anyone can cross their own name off, nobody else crosses it for them — not a rule, a promise.** Its **third-floor line** is Owen Fitch's name in his own capitals with, beside it in his own hand, **three words: *not asked to.*** Milo supplied the paper and was not in the room when it was written. **By the twenty-first it has a name on three of its five lines, one of the three struck through by the woman it belongs to, and the fourth and fifth floors with nothing against them. It did not run on the twenty-first or the twenty-second because the works footprint was shut, and the round is two of four.** It is not a replacement for Ada and must not become one.
8. **Owen's arrangement is unchanged and is not a rota, a term, or a job.** Once a night, going up, the third floor, stopping when he likes, not the ground floor, not the second, unwritten, and not to be thanked. The *not asked to* on the rota is his own sentence on other people's paper, and the terms are deliberately absent from it, because a rota that recorded the terms would be a job and a job is something a landlord can ask about. **The record still reads the same way it did on the ninth: one household declined, initials already held, said no with knowledge of them.**
9. **Milo is across four times in Volume 1: the fourth, the sixth, the eighteenth and the twenty-first of Sazur.** The crossing point is fixed as before (the west face of the works hoarding, about sixty metres from the disused platform end). The third and fourth crossings are from **the ground-floor door of Cedar Court, out at the coal hatch side**, and they are not on the alignment. He states the count in a yard on the twenty-second because Iona Sen makes him, and the count is written into his own record with the four dates and the four places. **Chapters 1–10's two are not inflated and may not be.** The Field Book is sound throughout, the written line home is complete and legible, and **the written-line loss remains a Chapters 51–53 event.**
10. **The orientation blank's lost routes, as listed on the page in Chapter 20:** the route to the office; a corridor of six years' use; his own front door; the four hundred metres between the pie shop and the chandler's; and the way from Iona's consulting room to the dispensary, taught to him on the sixteenth and gone by the morning of the twenty-second. All are remembered routes, all are recoverable when marked, and all of them are marked by somebody else. **Later batches may add to this list and may not remove from it.**
11. **Iona Sen's conditions are signed on the page, in the Field Book, dated the sixteenth of Sazur**, and are binding on Milo's field work for the rest of the volume: a named person outside holding a count, a stated number of minutes, walked in and walked out by someone who is not his chalk and who can stop the work with no argument permitted on the way back, and the clinic the morning after every time. He also asks, in writing, each morning, who is taking him where. **This is the batch's method change, in a form later batches can check.**
12. **The false route and the long sheet exist as documents.** Nineteen inches by eleven, drawn over three nights, anchored on Owen's third-floor labour and Jo Lask's live interval without asking either, with no count in it because Milo could not obtain one without admitting what he was doing. It is filed **as false**, with a page and a half of his own confession attached including *I knew that, and there was no way to put one in that did not require me to admit what I was doing.* **It must not be destroyed, redrawn, or succeeded by a longer version. A later batch may not have him produce a private complete route.**
13. **The disclosure of the Lark token, and the refusal that followed it.** On the eighteenth of Sazur Milo told Mara Quill that the district is his mother's erased neighbourhood, that Lark Street was a residential block with a tram turnaround, that his mother was its conductor and directed the evacuation, that she was not found afterwards, and that the token is a brass route-token stamped LARK STREET. He did it so that she could disqualify him. **She offered to file it as a disclosure and he refused, and she recorded the refusal, signed, dated, in her own hand: *offer made, refused, and recorded — M.Q.*** This is the volume's relationship milestone paid for by disclosure rather than affection, and it is the disclosure discipline later batches must preserve: nothing about the token has reached a Registry file, and Milo's stated reason for refusing is that a Registry file is where his mother's district goes to be lost.
14. **Mara's counterpart disclosure, Chapter 16.** The intake routing slip's annex box carries **two sets of initials** — hers, and one above it in a different pen, undated, ticked before hers, which she has read four times and cannot bring herself to read aloud. She has been working this case off her own hours in a records alcove for six years and has not told the review panel, and she says the reason is that procedure is her armour and she is good at it. **She is a legitimate counterpart to Milo's disclosure, and later batches should not let either of them be the only one who paid.**
15. **Orrin's argument has been proved and is unanswered.** Milo read it out of his own notebook, in Orrin's words, twice — once in the works office on the twentieth, once in a yard of thirty-one people — and Danner arrived at the same mechanics independently from a stop log. The drill then demonstrated it: the water went down the alignment and the service court settled nine inches over forty feet. **What gave was the service door:** it could take a steel plate in four hours and cannot be plated, because a variation takes six days, the test is on the twenty-second, the works need the door open for a gauge at ten, and Orrin's reason for refusing — that the day he plates a door on a surveyor's rod without a variation is the day a man with a lever plates the evidence locker — is good and unanswered on the page. Milo's standing condition, in writing in a yard: no further passage before the twenty-eighth, and none after it until somebody who is not him says in public that they want one.
16. **The twenty-second of Sazur, stated once and briefly.** The whole structure came out to full flow at ten past six. The clay held, the wall held, and the water went down the alignment and came out of the low point, which is what a foreman said in a fortnight it would do. The service court came out of it a foot and a half deep in grey silt, sets standing in ridges, the handrail four feet out of true, and three storage cages emptied into the river, one of them a filing box of a dead woman's letters. Nothing collapsed. Nobody drowned. Six people on the fifth floor woke at four in the morning and Owen Fitch went up unasked. **This is not the volume climax, it is not a seamquake, and it is not a first opening of the culvert.** The culvert was throttled to eighteen inches on the sixth and is driven out to full flow on the twenty-second, per CR-011, and is **never** sealed.
17. **Tomas Rusk gave the book on the twenty-first at seven in the morning**, in his own front room, with Mara writing verbatim. It contains the works bell schedule and the reach (proving the third call was a works shift interval at ten to two, and settling nothing); the **maintenance ledger number cited on the withdrawn replacement page**, which means whoever wrote that page had a mechanic's private book in front of them; and the second-shift roll, on which **Jonas Venn's next-of-kin line is not empty.** He would not say the name out loud on a set of steps. **His condition — why the road shut in the middle of a run — is unpaid, and that is the Chapters 21–30 hinge.**
18. **Jonas Venn's widow, on the page, unnamed.** Fifty-four, mends other people's clothes, a daughter in Wragg Street, four rooms behind a boatyard on the Ferry Road side, alive and living there. **Lena Marr told her on the twenty-first at about half past ten, in four sentences.** She asked three questions: has anyone been to see him, have they been paid, and **where is he buried** — and the third cannot be answered, because the disposal authority names a cemetery whose register for that district was not kept after the closure. **The death record is not reopened in this batch. What exists is a person to ask, a six-year debt, and no record of a grave.**
19. **Verrick Assal is named and has spoken**, on the nineteenth, for the first time in six years: off shift on the relief, came out at ten past one for his coat, the three calls at ten to two, a woman in a blue coat at the door, he said *clear* and she ticked him off, and when he got to the other end the door was shut. **He cannot produce one thing that happened between the door and the frontage, and he does not know what the third bell was for. He confirms no reading, supports no account, and must not be used again to confirm one.**
20. **The three readings are on the page as three families** — an evacuation, a count of the dead, a later reuse of an older route — and **Lena Marr rejects the family tree in front of twenty-two people.** The five Cedar Court accounts do not map one-to-one onto the three families, and Milo says so himself: Ada's check and Jo's handover are both service readings, Lena's list and the notice are both counts, Tomas's interval and Jo's works bell are both reuse readings. **All five accounts and all three families survive Chapter 20 intact, and the Chapter 30 reversal now has to overturn an organising principle rather than five memories.**
21. **The drainage fact and the rod, and it is now the district's problem.** The district drainage record states a 2ft 6in culvert laid in 1904 along Bridge Street and checked sound. It is a lead lateral the size of a wrist. The real culvert under Bridge Street is the works' storm main, which is dead and valved, and the ground west of Bridge Street is packed tip fill about eleven years old. **Milo sounded it on the seventeenth: four feet brick, seven feet brick, eleven feet open with standing grey water.** The low streets' own surface water has been coming up in a dry week for eleven years and a courier round has been saying so. This is the reason the twenty-second mattered, and it is not a magical discovery.
22. **The service door's engineering, fixed and reusable:** a personnel door in the flood-control wall, opened by a key Danner holds, two foot six, on the path from the door under the embankment to the low end, standing **eleven inches above the low point of the old alignment**, with fourteen feet of clay behind it where the drawing says. The works need it open for a gauge at ten on the twenty-second. A steel plate is in the stores and cannot be fitted without a variation, and a variation takes six days.
23. **The copy register discovery, Chapter 20.** The Bench's own direction produced the file with its provenance, and the provenance is the Crown Heights repository **copy register**. The maintenance volume that Attachment 9 was copied from **went out on an ordinary district works office reference and came back eleven days after the date of the original safety report on a Crown Heights repository reference** — a person in the central archive had the book in the week after a pump-house failure and did not write why. **And that register entry carries a second pair of unsigned margin marks in two different leads, the upper a bracket-and-dot and the lower not, and the lower one asks *who gave the order* — as it does on Attachment 9.** So: two documents carry a pair, the upper and lower are different hands on both, and the lower question is the same question. **The documents that carry a bracket-and-dot on its own are the Chapter 1 timetable margin and the Chapter 6 annex; a third, the Chapter 10 carbon, carries one that is *consistent with* them and not sworn to; a fourth, the Registry certification of the twenty-first, is demonstrably drawn rather than pressed; and a fifth, this register entry, carries it again with a second mark under it.** No later batch may call the copy register the third pair. **This is the Volume 2 question's first real purchase, and it is about maintenance records being used, not about the Quieting.**
24. **The certification mark of the twenty-first is the first forensic statement about the hand, and it does not identify anybody.** The Registry's bracket-and-dot certification on the certified copy is **drawn on the sheet** — wet on the arrival of the nib, sitting in the paper, no halo, bridging nothing — where the Chapter 1 timetable margin and the Chapter 6 annex are demonstrably not. Milo writes that this is **a difference in method and not in age**, and nothing more. **No later batch may convert this into an identification.**
25. **Atlas residue used once in Chapters 11–20**, in Chapter 18, at the ninth minute of the ninth of the drill: *By the fourth, a count is not a number. It is a person saying a number, out loud, so that somebody else can hear that a person said it.* The book had been silent since the fourth of Sazur and is silent again after. **Later batches should stay at or below this frequency.**
26. **New supporting cast, registered in `bible/terminology.md`:** Hettie Brann (water clinic counter, former laundry hand), Ola Petrossian (clinic nurse, four months, walks the route card and finds the fence), Ilse Oller (second landing, sixty, the woman the false route takes through a wall, and the person who strikes her own name off the rota), Verrick Assal (the man on Lena's mother's list, now named and now spoken to). No new faction, grammar, antagonist, or category has been introduced.
27. **The Volume 01 supporting cast from Chapters 1–10 is unchanged by this batch:** **Dilley Farr**, route-room supervisor; **Cass Ardery**, Survey Enforcement officer carrying the instruction *a seal*; **Danner**, the contractor's foreman, whose first name is still unused on the page; **Rue** and **Idar**, Streetkeepers in Pell's round. Note for later batches: **Rue has been given a gender and a first-name-only credit and no surname, deliberately. Do not give her the surname Harrow, which is Pell's.**

## What Chapters 21–30 fixed on the page, in the same form as CR-013

Each item below is now prose rather than plan, and each is load-bearing in a way a later batch could break without intending to. None of them changes a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder, or the planned ending. The single exception is item 1, which is the volume's planned midpoint and is recorded as prose in `state/canon-changes.md` CR-020 alongside the rest of the batch.

1. **The midpoint reversal, stated on the page in Chapter 30, and binding on every later chapter.** Bellwether was a **coordinated evacuation that the order interrupted while residents were still being moved.** The three calls are the works' shift calls, sounded from the **pump-house gantry** at ten to two and heard throughout the reach because of the height of the frontage wall. **The first two calls moved people toward the old service court. The third is a check: a person saying that a space has been cleared before the next turn.** A check is not a bell and a count is not handed to a bell. **It is not a massacre echo, not a count of the dead, and not a rite, and it is not a haunting made by a death.** Later batches may not reopen this.
2. **The triad accounting, stated aloud in a yard by Ada Fenn in Chapter 30, and canonical for the volume.** The order **withdrew the public name** and **closed the ordinary routes** — two supports. It **broke the witness support by scattering the routines and the people who kept them**, and it could not suppress one of those routines, because the woman who kept it never left the river core. **Two withdrawn, one left standing and overloaded.** Ada Fenn rejects the words *third support* and *broken* and will not be put on any rota, and the page records her rejection. Never write it as all three withdrawn, and never as a thin remnant that later grew. This satisfies the promise CR-010 makes about Chapter 30.
3. **The corruption is paragraph six of the original safety report, and it is on the page, in full.** *The reach is worked from the pump house. The crew change is sounded from the gantry and is heard throughout the reach owing to the height of the frontage wall. The three calls at the change are not a signal to persons and indicate no entry and no refusal. The works consider that the frontage is at all times a service access and not a public route.* It was filed in the first week, it **survived the replacement page**, and **the sentence it is up against — *no evidence was found of any hazard to persons at the service court* — did not.** An engineering classification, made in the first week, governed the case for six years, and nobody revisited it because a correct statement about a bell cannot be corrected from inside a report. **This is the load-bearing fact of the reversal and it is a document, not a magic failure.**
4. **Nobody is made wrong, and the shape of the answer.** Tomas Rusk is right that the three calls are a works bell and wrong only about the word *change*. Jo Lask is right that a count is passed from a person to a person and wrong only about *only*. Lena Marr is wrong only about *counting*. Ada Fenn never interprets the bell and gave nothing away. **Verrick Assal has been asked three times and has said nothing three times, and has asked in writing that it be recorded that he was asked three times, said nothing three times, and is not a witness** — and has offered to answer for the pump house instead. **The document is wrong, and that is the whole of the correction.**
5. **Jonas Venn is separated from the lane, on the page, for the first time.** A fault in a second-stage main in flood season, a real accident, a real death, and a real concealment. **The sentence that is the closure is a sentence that put *a hazard at the frontage* and *nobody at the frontage* into one document.** The death did not create the grammar and is not in it. **The death record is still closed and reopening it is a Chapters 58–60 job.** The widow is alive behind a boatyard on the Ferry Road side, unnamed, and still cannot be told where he is buried.
6. **Tomas Rusk's condition is paid, in his own words, and is not retracted.** The road shut in the middle of a run because the crew came off the frontage to the pump house and the reach emptied and a frontage with nobody on it is a road that is not being kept. **He was on the reach alone with a lamp and no bell, and he has had six years to decide which of those two things he would rather have been, and has not.** He also said, for the first time out loud, that he has been talking about a bell and not about a lane, and that he never claimed a works bell could not be used for something else.
7. **The return mark that failed, and the failure is a handover, not a lapse.** On the 26th Milo needed a turn, read the accountancy paper on the Wednesday morning, saw that the **second-floor line carries a name with a line through it in a woman's own hand**, and decided that a line crossed off is a line occupied by nobody. He wrote it in at 08.10 and went in at 13.58. **The lane believed his sheet rather than the woman.** He is not to be left alone with a piece of paper about a person; that is Iona Sen's finding and it is not a condition and cannot be signed.
8. **The four signed conditions, all satisfied, and the work failed anyway.** Chapter 24 records that the count, the stated minutes, the walker who could stop the work, and the clinic in the morning were all in place and the route went round. **A condition is a floor and Milo has been treating it as a ceiling.** The count on the 26th was Pell Harrow's, from the path head, with a trade counter, nine minutes stated. **Rue, not Jo Lask, is the person who said out loud — before Milo did — that the four missing seconds were nobody's fault.**
9. **Crossings: five in Volume 1**, dated and placed in his own hand and **said out loud in a yard on the 26th** — the fourth, the sixth, the eighteenth, the twenty-first, and the **twenty-sixth** of Sazur, in at 13.58 and out at 13.58 from the ground-floor door of Cedar Court, out at the coal hatch side. The fifth crossing is the first one with a **named count-holder from outside and a named walker who can stop the work**, and the first one that was broken by a withdrawn labour rather than a taken one.
10. **The orientation blank's lost routes, now seven, listed on the page:** the route to the office; a corridor of six years' use; his own front door; the four hundred metres between the pie shop and the chandler's; the way from Iona's consulting room to the dispensary; **the way from the ferry steps up to the chandler's yard**; and **the two hundred and fourteen metres from the ground-floor door of Cedar Court to the Alder Quay tram steps, which is the route he wrote on a card on the sixteenth of Sazur for Ola Petrossian to walk.** All are remembered routes, all recoverable when marked, all marked by other people. **Later batches may add to this list and may not remove from it.**
11. **The timetable amendment, the 28th, and the death of the stay, all on the page.** A four-paragraph Line 7 service amendment laid on the 23rd for the review on the 28th: the Alder Quay–Bridge Street loop withdrawn; the service court and its diversion corridor closed from the first working day; the disused platform recovered and the nameboards removed; and the withdrawn alignment brought to a schedule level by the district works, **certified by the surveyor of record**. **The stay ended at 10.25 on the 28th in one line and had never done anything else, and it was not extended, renewed, or converted into anything larger.** The **certification box** reads *certified by a person familiar with the route withdrawn*; it is filled **no person available**; the fallback is the surveyor of record from survey; and the amendment is laid on **a description of a bend**, which Barrow says he has never done in nineteen years.
12. **The regrade, its price, and the winter.** **Eleven weeks**, the best case, from the 28th; the alignment on eleven-year-old tip fill; the fill moved again under a plate on the 30th and made a new dip. **With the diversion out of the alignment, the low streets' surface water goes back to the district's own 1904 lead lateral — a pipe the size of a man's wrist — behind the works' dead and valved storm main.** Danner states it in public on the 28th: **the low streets flood in November in a dry month.** The number is in a note at the foot of a form under Mara Quill's name and will not be answered in the ordinary course.
13. **The round, as it now stands.** Nine stops until the 28th, **five on a Sunday**; the round's last run through the works footprint at seven on the morning of the 28th; **seven stops on foot through the works gate on the shift change from the 29th, twenty-two minutes each way, unpaid, for eleven weeks**, taken by Rue in public with the price said out loud and made to be said back. **The letterbox is gone** — a corrugation in the works hoarding, six years old, cleared by Pell since he was fourteen, nobody built it, flat sheet now, no corrugation in flat sheet.
14. **The accountancy-paper rota, as it now stands.** Five lines, in Rue's satchel, belonging to the courier round. **Four names on the sheet when it went out on the 28th and again on the 3rd, and one of the five lines still carrying nothing**: the ground (Rue, with Idar on the Wednesday), the second (Ilse Oller, struck through in her own hand on the 21st and not restored), the third (**Owen Fitch, in his own capitals, with *not asked to* in his own hand**), and the fourth since the 24th. **A fourth-floor line has carried a name since the 24th, with four words beside it — *asked for herself, Tuesday* — written by the person it belongs to, and Milo did not ask and did not write it down. On the third of the month a fifth-floor line, which had nothing against it since the seventeenth, carried a name in a hand nobody in the yard recognised, with three words that are not a note and not a refusal: *Second and Thursday.*** **Ada Fenn has refused to be on it, including by way of signing herself out of it**, and Milo walked away and did not write the fifth-floor name down. **The rota is not a replacement for Ada and must never become one.**
15. **The five accounts and the three families, resolved rather than tidied.** The three families Milo drew on a table leg on the 19th (an evacuation, a count of the dead, a later reuse of an older route) **are not used again in this batch and the table leg is a wet board with nothing legible on it.** What replaced them is not a family and not a shape: it is **a list of what is not in dispute**, said out loud on the 3rd by Milo Rook, corrected twice by Jo Lask and Tomas Rusk, and then the question being changed from what the calls were *for* to what the third one is *asking for*. **Three honest witnesses describing one night three ways is the price of the answer, not an obstacle to it.** **Ruling, CR-022: Chapter 28's tally of *four people and a document* counts the accounts that were sworn, not the accounts that exist.** The four are Jo Lask, Tomas Rusk, Lena Marr, and the closure notice as a document. Verrick Assal is in the room, is asked three times, and confirms nothing, which is his own written request and is not an account. **Ada Fenn's account is on the page in Chapters 22 and 29 and is deliberately not in the room, because she refused it and refused it in a yard of forty.** **Ada Fenn still never interprets the third bell anywhere in Chapters 1–30, and a later batch may not put her in a room and hand her the bell.**
16. **The arithmetic, now on the page, and it is arithmetic and not symbolism.** **The interval between the second and the third call is thirty-one seconds** and has been in two independent readings since the 4th. **The extra Bellwether Line 7 entry carried a platform time of forty-one seconds on a segment with no slack**, so it was a hold, not a stop, and a hold is a car standing at a place waiting for something to be done. **The difference is ten seconds.** Milo refused the two coincidental forties (forty-one seconds and Lena's mother's forty-one names) in front of the yard and Jo Lask said: *you don't want the two forties, you want the ten.*
17. **The closure notice is one sentence with a semicolon in the middle, and the district has been reading half of it for six years.** *One loss recorded; persons to be withdrawn from the interval affected between the second and the third calls.* **Persons** is a plural, they are being **withdrawn from** an interval, and the interval is thirty-one seconds long. Read on the 2nd, in public, by Lena Marr, for the first time in the volume.
18. **Ada Fenn's withdrawal, on the page, and it is legitimate and is not a problem to be solved.** She has said no **thirteen times**: to the certification box, to a question from the Board school, to being put on the rota, and to the words *third support* and *broken*. **She has said, in a yard of about forty, that she is stopping**, that she is sixty-eight with a fever coming and two people on her floor over seventy, and that if she dies on that stairwell the only thing anybody will say is that the closure got her. **Nobody has ever walked up three flights of stairs to ask her whether she would mind stopping, and she said that out loud, and she is not going to be asked again.** She has given exactly one true thing — what a door-check is, and that the practice is the only part of it anybody can hand to somebody else — and her own name for herself is **a woman with a habit, and those are not the same.** She has still **not interpreted the third bell** and refused the one available morning in a room of forty on purpose.
19. **The new work, and it is a change in what the team is doing, not in what they know.** The team is not trying to reopen Bellwether; the order will be upheld in October and the name will not come back in this volume. **It is trying to build a witness that changes hands.** The rota of five lines is the whole of the district's answer to a check that has been going out every evening for six years, and it is one line and a half, and one of those is a man who has written three words next to his own name so that nobody can use him.
20. **Milo's public condition, and what it turned out to be, on the page.** The sentence he said in a yard on the 22nd was not a restraint on his work. **It was an instruction he gave to a foreman**, and a foreman told that the district's surveyor has declared no passage will not send two of his own into a court with a tape and a torch. He broke it to un-break it on the 26th, and on the 3rd he said out loud, in a yard, that **a man who has promised himself a limit ends up owing it to other people**, and that he is not asking that yard for a passage.
21. **The clue chain, one step and no resolution.** The Registry **confirms** the copy-register line is a return, gives the shelfmark and the date, and states that an internal consultation reference is not producible and that the officer holding one is not an officer of the district. **The reference on the file index against *p.9 withdrawn* and the internal consultation reference on the copy register are the same form — four letters and two numbers, a clerk's hand — and Milo writes the resemblance down once and says nothing for four days.** **The withdrawal reference is a Crown Heights repository consultation reference, not a district continuity reference, so page nine was withdrawn on the instruction of a desk that is not in the Registry's organisation and is not in the district's book.** The **twenty-first's Registry certification is still demonstrably drawn where the Chapter 1 timetable margin and the Chapter 6 annex are demonstrably pressed: a difference in method and not in age, identifying nobody.** **Two documents carry a pair of marks, not three.** **Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 21–30**; Milo got two letters of something into a corridor on the 29th and stopped, and Mara Quill left it where it was and said so on the record.
22. **Mara Quill's own disclosure, and what it cost.** To get the answer to item 21 she had to state a personal interest in the file in writing, which is **a disclosure about her own conduct**: that she has worked this file off her own hours for six years in a records alcove and has not told her review panel. **The review is now opened on her own hand.** **The second set of initials in the intake routing slip's annex box is still unresolved and still hers alone.**
23. **The relationship step, paid as disclosure and not as affection, and it is a method change.** On the 29th Milo said that anything of his bearing on the file now goes to her in writing, in his hand, with a date on it, **whether she can file it or not and whether it helps him or not.** She refused the apology that came with it, told him exactly what that refusal costs her every day, and **did not ask him for the Lark disclosure**, because a disclosure gets filed and Lark Street would be in a building in Crown Heights for the rest of her working life, put there by four hundred correct jobs. **Nothing about the token has reached a Registry file and nothing has been said about how either of them feels.**
24. **Milo's method, restated in Iona Sen's words on the 27th, and it is now the volume's standing rule:** a route is only a route if another person can say it in words and walk it without him; **and he is not to be left alone with a piece of paper about a person, not once, not ever.** He has written that second one down nowhere.
25. **The accountancy-paper rota's rule, unchanged:** *anyone can cross their own name off, nobody else crosses it for them — not a rule, a promise.* Ilse Oller's strike-through of the 21st has not been restored, and **Ada Fenn's refusal to be on the sheet at all is a third kind of answer and is recorded as one.**
26. **New supporting cast, registered in `bible/terminology.md`:** **Barrow**, a Line 7 timetable division clerk, nineteen years in the job, who asked for the stay to be minuted clause by clause and laid the amendment on a description of a bend; and **Ostrow**, the man in the fen with the maintenance list. **No new faction, grammar, antagonist, or category has been introduced.** Halden Vey, Sel Iri, and Tarin Kest do not appear in this batch; **Tarin's audit of the order remains reserved for Chapters 31–40.**
27. **Volume 01 supporting cast from Chapters 1–20, unchanged by this batch:** Dilley Farr, Cass Ardery, Danner (first name still unused on the page), Rue and Idar (Rue still has no surname and must not be given one), Hettie Brann, Ola Petrossian, Ilse Oller, Verrick Assal, old Mabb, Ardin Ossick, Wragg Street, and the marshal, the woman in the pie shop, the gate-man, the woman at the school door, the woman at the Recovery counter, the works clerk. **Old Mabb is not named in Chapters 21–30, is not woken, and is not written down.** **Rue has no surname on the page and must not be given one.**
28. **Atlas residue used once in Chapters 21–30**, in Chapter 24, at the ninth minute of the fifth crossing: ***A line crossed off is not a line given.*** It describes what Milo did and not what the road wanted, and he records that limit in his own notes before acting on it. The Field Book is otherwise silent in the batch — on the 30th, standing above an empty service court with the machine on it, the entry is **Nothing. Noted, not read.** **Later batches should stay at this frequency.**

## Structural state

- Scope deviation from the catalog is recorded and unratified: `state/canon-changes.md` CR-001.
- The Volume 01 outline and the Chapters 1–10 batch cards are populated. The outline phase authorized filling them while CR-001 was still open; the deviation is recorded as CR-008 and remains the controller’s to ratify or reject.
- The phase ledger and any completion marker are controller-owned and untouched.

## What Chapters 31–40 fixed on the page, in the same form as CR-013

Each item below is now prose rather than plan, and each is load-bearing in a way a later batch could break without intending to. None of them changes a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder, or the planned ending. The single exception is item 1, which records what the batch **did not** reopen.

1. **The Chapter 30 reversal is not reopened, and is not the thing under dispute.** The batch tested it, quoted it at an audit, and left it. **Tarin Kest records on the page that he has not tested the account of a coordinated evacuation interrupted by the order and that he does not accept that it establishes a hazard of any kind** — and that this is *not* the reason he is excluding the surveyor's record. Nobody in Chapters 31–40 re-argues what the third call was for, nobody hedges it, nobody re-decides it, and **Ada Fenn does not appear in the batch at all.** The bells' answer remains the one fixed in Chapter 30 and is binding on Chapters 41–60.
2. **The triad accounting is used once, correctly, and it is not restated as news.** In Chapter 32 **Orrin Sable** describes the rota — not the triad — and says: the name is off the index, the routes are shut, and no party in the district has authority over a sheet of accountancy paper in a satchel, so the day the last name is crossed off, the sheet reads as empty, and a document that reads as empty is a document that never existed. **He is right about the rota and has never seen it.** The triad itself is not re-explained in this batch and must not be.
3. **A structural rhyme is on the page and it is about forms, not about Bellwether.** **Paragraph six of the original safety report and the fourth finding of Cass Ardery's field report of the 11th are the same shape of sentence:** a true statement about supports, made in the first week or in the first inspection, in a form with no box for the part that is not true, which then governs the case because it cannot be corrected from inside the document. **This is a finding about how this city loses a street. It is not a second reading of the bells and it identifies nobody.**
4. **Tarin Kest, in full.** Twenty-eight, Survey Enforcement field inspector, nine days in the division when he arrived on the fourth of the month, auditing **the maintenance of the order and not the order**. Competent, scrupulous with documents, and wrong about one of them in about ninety seconds. **He is not a friend and never pretends to be one.** He writes his paragraph on the fourteenth and does not change it after the seamquake, and says why: *if I change it after a bridge comes down then it is a reaction and not a decision.*
5. **The paragraph, in the terms it stands in.** The surveyor's record is excluded from October **as to interpretation, construction and cause**; measurements of level, fall, settlement and aperture are **admitted as measurements, unverified, on the surveyor's own signature**; the account of the evacuation is not in issue and is not tested and does not establish a hazard. **Milo's interpretation is out and his measurements are in. This is the shape of the October hearing and later batches should build on it.**
6. **The level has left Milo's file.** On the 21st of the month, at Mara Quill's insistence and with Milo's consent given after the event, **the schedule level certified under paragraph four goes into the works' file under Danner's signature.** Danner said yes on the Thursday in six words — *Plate fixed. Six twenty-five. My plate.* — and said, while standing, that he will not be able to stand behind a level over a trench with four feet in it at a Bench in October, and did not ask for that to be written down. **Later batches may not put the level back into the surveyor's file, and the finding of the twentieth names him as the certifying officer of a level and nothing else.**
7. **The plate variation, the plate, and the dates.** The variation went into a works office **at 07.20 on Monday the sixth** and **Danner's *alternative considered* box is filled in his own hand, on the form and again on the notice, because he will not leave a space on a district form.** The district works office returned it **stamped at nine on Tuesday the seventh** and **the notice went up on the works-office hoarding at 09.10 that morning**; **Milo Rook read it on the Tuesday evening of the seventh**, and Danner added the line at the foot of the notice **on the Sunday morning after, which is the twelfth**. Six days on the clock: **available and fitted on Sunday the twelfth at ten past six in the morning**, in nine minutes, by two of Danner's own, with the door shut and the fixings drawn from inside on a ladder. Danner wrote *plate fixed* on the form at **06.25**. The plate is lawful and it is the contradiction: **the same form says *effect on drainage: the low point of the alignment to remain clear of standing water*, and the plate guarantees that sentence is untrue.** Later batches may not remove the plate, unseal it, or call it anything but what it is.
8. **The mechanics of the consequence, on the page, in a foreman's words.** A door is not a hole in a wall; it is a hole that goes *through* it, and air comes in one side and goes out the other. **Four inches of steel on the through end makes the low point of the alignment a pocket with a roof and a wall and no way out.** The ground under it is a **soft lens** of about eleven-year-old river silt, nine foot down and about twenty foot across, which drains sideways because the diversion was cut into it in August; the low streets are the low ground next to it; and the trench thirty feet from Bridge Street fills with river. **The soft lens will settle about two further feet across the reach before the first of November and then stop.** Nobody drowns and nothing collapses, and the culvert is not moved.
9. **The flooding, the figures, and the count.** Four inches on the crown of Bridge Street by half past one on the twelfth, lying in a band about nine feet wide, in a month with **nine-tenths of an inch of rain**; six inches by the sixteenth; the district flood line is **nine inches**. **The count, done over two days by six named people standing on doorsteps without knocking: two hundred and forty-nine people, fifty-four rooms, eleven streets.** The district's own figure of the **eighth of Sazur** was **two hundred and thirty-one**, so the schedule is **eighteen short**, and there are **thirty-one rooms with somebody in them on no list at all**. The per-head additional schedule is **two and four a head a week** — **five hundred and fifty-four pounds and forty a week**, and **forty-three pounds and two** for the eighteen. **A rate is built on a property and a person in a room is not a property, and the count of people and the count of rooms will never be the same number, and a rate cannot be built on a door.** Later batches may not close the gap administratively; the Rescue Division's finding stands and **Chapters 58–60 still have to make this cost somebody money.**
10. **The rota, exactly as it stands after the 8th of the month. The counting rule is now stated aloud on the page and is binding: five lines, five names, and four people behind four of the five names, with the fifth-floor name counted as having nobody behind it.** **Rue says it in this form, in Chapter 33, and says she will say it the same way every time she is asked, because the day she says it a different way is the day the sheet is a lie.** A line with a stroke through it is counted as having a person behind it, because the person who put the line through it is the person it belongs to and nobody has put it back. The five lines: the ground (Rue, with Idar on the Wednesday); the second (struck through by Ilse Oller on the twenty-first of Sazur); the third (**Owen Fitch**, own capitals, ***not asked to***, three words); the fourth, a name since the twenty-fourth with four words beside it, *asked for herself, Tuesday*; and **the fifth, a name added on the third of this month, in a hand nobody in the yard recognised, with three words beside it that are not a note and not a refusal: *Second and Thursday.*** **Idar went up to the fifth-floor landing on the Wednesday of the eighth, at about two o'clock: a light on, a door, two knocks, no answer, and he came back down.** The post is addressed to that name. **Nobody goes up again. Nobody reads the name. Milo Rook has not written it down and has written down that he has not asked.** The rule at the foot is unchanged. **Ada Fenn is not on it and is not asked again, and she does not appear anywhere in Chapters 31–40.**
11. **The six-day crossing, and who held it.** The **tenth of the month at 14.06**, in and out at 14.06, from the ground-floor door of Cedar Court at the coal hatch side, **eleven seconds inside a stated nine minutes, four of them at the beginning.** The count of nine was called from the path head by **Rue**; the walker was **Verrick Assal**, who said yes because he has been asked three times about a bell he was not in a room for and would like once to be asked about a thing he is, and who did not have to stop the work. **Jo Lask refused the turn first, in writing, in advance, and was right**, and the words he gave for it are *free at four* rather than *available*. The lane asked the same question it asked on the twenty-sixth of Sazur and **there was nobody at a door**, and that is all, and nothing supernatural happened. **Milo read all six crossings out loud in a yard of nineteen with Iona Sen's tally present.** The refusal and the crossing are the same day: **the request was written out in full on the night of the ninth and handed to Jo Lask on the morning of the tenth, and the crossing was at 14.06 the same afternoon, so Chapter 34 sits after Chapter 33, which is the eighth, and may not be placed before it.**
12. **The orientation blank, eighth route, and how it came back.** The lost route is **the two hundred metres from the works gate to the end of the disused platform**, which he has walked at least nine times since the fourth of Sazur. It was given back the next morning in **chalk on a gate post at about four feet, with a date**, by the gate-man, on his own initiative after Rue told him what the surveyor would be like, and who said *it is not for you, it is for the next man* and *I am not doing it twice a week.* **Eight named remembered routes, no more and no fewer, all recoverable, all marked by somebody else, and none removed.** The Field Book is intact and the written line home is complete, correct and legible.
13. **The Field Book, in this batch, is mostly a silence and that is the point. The silences are now dated and paired on the page, and the pairs are six days and nine days.** It wrote **nothing on the fourth and nothing on the sixth** when the alignment came up to the schedule level, **one line on the tenth** (*A blank left by accident closes*, six words, out of a page that was not clean), and **nothing on the nineteenth** during the seamquake; its last line before this batch was the handrail line of the twenty-sixth of Sazur, and it was also silent on the thirtieth of Sazur. **In Chapter 39 Milo pairs the thirtieth of Sazur with the sixth, which are six days apart, and the tenth with the nineteenth, which are nine days apart, and he writes both pairs down and declines to read anything into them**, in his own hand, saying he is not going to decide at two in the morning that silence means anything. **Later batches may use these two pairs and may not add a third without a date.** Atlas residue was used **once in the batch**, in Chapter 34, in **six** words: ***A blank left by accident closes.*** It describes the rota and does not say who fills a blank or whether one should be filled, and he limits it on the facing page before acting and does not take the book near that yard for a week. **The written-line loss remains a Chapters 51–53 event and is not hinted at anywhere.**
14. **The four Thursdays, in the exact terms the minute records.** **One named person**, once a week, in daylight, **on the last Thursday of each of the next four weeks — the twenty-third of this month, the thirtieth, the sixth of next month and the thirteenth** — with the consent of the works given by the site manager, the person may stop at any point and the stopping needs no reason, **nothing is recorded as a household route**, and it **expires at noon on Friday the fourteenth of next month and cannot be extended by anybody including the chair, and there is no form for extending it because there is no form for a thing that is only ever four weeks.** The minute also says in terms that it **does not suspend the order, does not restore the public name, makes no variation of the order, protects nobody and restores nothing.** **The named person is Lena Marr, and Mara Quill did not name her: she asked her.** **Lena's price, named by her and minuted: two of the nine school places are lost in the spring because the mother of two of the children is a named person on a Registry instrument.** This is the same shape as the stay of the tenth of Sazur and **is not extended, renewed or converted, and does not restore the name.**
15. **Tarin Kest's application, and its cost to him.** On the twenty-first of the month, in a corridor, in front of a clerk and about nine other people, he lodged a variation application **in his own name, in his own hand**, on the Filewell precedent. The clerk completed it with **no person available** in the person-familiar box. He said thank you, put it in the inside pocket of his coat, and said: *a form with an entry in it is a document and a document is what a department can be made to do something with.* **He goes back to a desk in Crown Heights on the twenty-second.** He is not a friend and is not to be made one inside this volume.
16. **The precedent, the third reference, and the rules on them.** The **Filewell order** is a district continuity order of the fourth year back on thirty-four dwellings at the low end of Filewell and a yard behind it, made under the standing instruction of the Prefect's office, **varied and allowed to lapse on the fourteenth of the following month** on a completed schedule of works, a restored district drainage, and a certificate signed by the works foreman who fitted the gate. **And on the file index, against the paragraph the order-maker's report sits behind, a repository consultation reference in the same form as the one against *p.9 withdrawn* on the Bellwether file.** **This is a third *reference*, not a third *pair* of margin marks.** The rule is unchanged and binding: **two documents carry a pair, not three** — Attachment 9 and the copy register — and the two confirmed hand-marks are the Chapter 1 timetable margin and the Chapter 6 annex, with the Chapter 10 carbon recorded as *consistent with and not sworn to* and the twenty-first's Registry certification **drawn** where those two are **pressed, a difference in method and not in age**. Kest has **not matched the strings** and says so, and did not open the third box. **Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 31–40.**
17. **Tomas Rusk's withdrawal, in his own words, and why it is not a defeat.** He did not take the account back. **A document is a thing and it is the same in six years; a sworn account is a man saying out loud that he will stand behind it for the rest of his life.** His account says a frontage with nobody on it is a road that is not being kept; a road cannot be put right; and the man who swore it would be a mechanic in a room in Crown Heights swearing that the district had a road it was not keeping, in a file for a hundred years, with nobody knowing which half mattered. **The reason is a wage: four years of a district works pension, thirty years of service and a knee. Nobody argued him out of it and Milo Rook looked for a wrong reason for a week and there is not one.** **The October position is three sworn accounts and one unsworn, which is exactly what Mara Quill predicted in front of forty people on the second of the month.**
18. **What Tomas Rusk said once and will not say again.** That **whoever wrote the replacement page on the ninth of that month six years ago had a mechanic's private book in front of them**, and the maintenance ledger number on that page is the number on the first page of a book that has never been in the district's possession. **He has now said it out loud for the first time and will not say it again.**
19. **The seamquake, in the terms the joint finding uses, and what it is not.** **The nineteenth of the month, at six o'clock, on the third call.** The withdrawn alignment had been brought to **schedule level across ninety feet passing beneath the public deck at the Alder Quay interchange** at half past three that afternoon, with a three-foot spirit level and the bubble in the middle, and a man wrote *level* on the site sheet in his own hand. On the call, **the west side of the interchange went nine inches out of true over about thirty feet of street in one movement.** The deck **ratcheted** about its three ways on solid ground. **The footbridge stair tower — a hundred and one steps, on one masonry pier, built in 1938 — came down across the running rails and came down eleven feet out of place.** **Eleven people on it, nine off it on their own feet, two not off it by themselves, and nobody else.** **Four hurt, and the four through the treatment room are those same four**: a works man (Petts, out of the trench a fortnight before, *nobody told us* on the twelfth), two men off the second gang with wrists, and a tram driver of thirty-one called Whelan. **The overhead went, the junction box sheared off its base, the Alder Quay substation feeder followed, and the evening service was worked by hand from about seven.** **Nobody was in the water, the wall held, and the culvert is where it was on the twenty-second of Sazur and has not moved.**
20. **The joint finding of the twentieth, and who is in it.** Four paragraphs, made by the works and Survey Enforcement together. The second paragraph, in the terms that are now binding: *that the level was certified by the surveyor of record under paragraph four of the service amendment of the twenty-eighth of Sazur, upon a description of the alignment withdrawn certified as a description of a bend, no person familiar with the route being available; and that the alignment so certified included ninety feet passing beneath the public deck at the Alder Quay interchange; and that the public deck rests upon four ways of unequal founding over a common fill; and that the differential movement of nine inches at one way, with none at three others, is consistent with that founding and not with any other cause; and that the cause of the movement is that a surface was made level across a place which had been two surfaces for six years.* **Orrin Sable is named in the finding once, in a list of persons who were present, in the same line as a tram marshal and a man with a straight edge, and the order is not in the finding at all.** **This is the volume's evidence and not its settlement, and it is not a pinned seam, which is not named in this batch and is not due before Chapter 51.**
21. **Jo Lask's eleven seconds came true, and it came true because of them.** The diversion was worked without power at the junction, so a signal stood where a box had been, so the count could not be handed where it is handed, and it was eleven seconds late. **Jo Lask — suspended from driving since the twenty-second of Sazur, four weeks and half pay, so that the four weeks do not run out until the twentieth of this month, and not suspended from the platform — took the count by hand at the Ferry Road turn for four services and got every one right, and nobody stopped him, and a man from the tram authority wrote a note about it that Jo Lask has never asked to see.** He said on the twenty-eighth of this month, in a yard, in the rain, to about twenty people, that nobody had asked him to read it out. It is now on a printed timetable. **The suspension date is the twenty-second of Sazur, from his own letter in Chapter 23, and later batches may not move it.**
