22. **The disused platform is out of service and its nameboards are in the river.** **The ground under it went nine inches on the nineteenth** and the coping came up, so paragraph three of the amendment — recover the platform, remove the nameboards — was done that night by an accident rather than by a programme, and the nameboards went into the water.
23. **The fourth-floor rota line, and the one unnamed cameo.** The fourth-floor line has carried a name since the twenty-fourth of last month and Rue has not asked for the name or the four words and will not, and nobody in this batch has. **In the count of the seventeenth, a woman of sixty from the second landing of Cedar Court came out for the first time in six weeks, would not stop, would not talk, and counted, and Rue did not tell Milo Rook her name and Milo Rook did not ask.**
24. **Ada Fenn's absence, written down as a rule for later batches.** **She does not appear in Chapters 31–40 and her name is not written once in them.** She is referred to **twice, both times as a woman of sixty-eight, and both times by somebody else and never by Milo Rook** — in the yard count of Chapter 33 and inside Lena Marr's written consent read out in Chapter 40. In no case is she asked anything, thanked, replaced, argued back, or put on anything, and nobody climbs three flights of stairs about her. **Two lines carry the whole of what the district does without her:** Orrin Sable's argument in Chapter 32 that a rota with no standing over it reads as empty when the last name is crossed off, and Lena Marr's written consent in Chapter 40 that nobody has stood at a door since a woman of sixty-eight decided to stop. **Later batches may not solve it, thank her, name her, or let a neighbour become the door-check.**
25. **Owen Fitch's standing, unchanged and uncorroborated.** He refused nothing and was asked for nothing this batch. **Verrick Assal walked Milo Rook in and out and Milo asked him, and Jo Lask refused first and was right** — the method change working once, on the page. Owen's third-floor check is unchanged and unwritten, his three words are on the rota, and **nobody in this batch thanks him, quotes him approvingly, or uses his name to win an argument**, except Lena Marr, who describes *the way Owen says no* in a yard and means it.
26. **The address/money trap is untouched and is still a trap.** The name must stay on the compensation schedule and off the street index; a current address for Cedar Court ends the payment. **Nobody applied for one in this batch, nobody solved it, and the *no person available* phrase appeared a third time, on Tarin Kest's own application in the district store.** The register's answer to who speaks for the block is still a man on the third floor who is asleep, **his name is not said out loud anywhere in Chapters 31–40, he is not woken, and nobody in this batch — least of all the inspector — is the person who notices him.**
27. **The clues that are carried and not advanced.** **Pell's card, the Monday of the thirteenth, in Rue's hand on the back of a delivery docket:** *Ostrow asked me who keeps a road that is not on a map. I said the people who live on it. He said that is what the list says, and he has had the list a long time, and that is the part I cannot get past either. Do not tell me what to do with this. I have not written his name on anything and I am not going to.* **Pell has not seen Tern, Ostrow has not been asked a question, and nothing is resolved.**
28. **New supporting cast, registered in `bible/terminology.md`:** **Tarin Kest** (Survey Enforcement field inspector, twenty-eight), **Nell Crale** (sixty-three, Bridge Street, cold store and milk-and-ice round), **Petts** (a works man, the trench of the fourth and one of the four hurt), **Whelan** (a tram driver of thirty-one, one of the four), **Rennick** (the contractor's site manager), **Perrins** (chair of the Directions Bench), and **Calder Row / Filewell** as the precedent site. **No new grammar, faction, antagonist, or category has been introduced.** Halden Vey and Sel Iri are absent; **the pinned-seam condition is not named and is not due before Chapter 51.**
29. **Atlas residue used once in Chapters 31–40**, in Chapter 34, in **six** words: ***A blank left by accident closes.*** It describes a piece of accountancy paper and does not say who fills a blank or whether a blank should be filled, and Milo Rook limits it in his own hand on the facing page before he acts on it and does not take the book near that yard for a week. **The Field Book is otherwise silent in the batch, on the fourth, the sixth, the thirtieth of Sazur and the nineteenth, and the silences are written down in two dated pairs and deliberately not interpreted.** Later batches should stay at or below this frequency.

## What Chapters 41–50 fixed on the page, in the same form as CR-013

Each item below is now prose rather than plan, and each is load-bearing in a way a later batch could break without intending to. None of them changes a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder, or the planned ending. Every figure here is quotable from a chapter in `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0041.md` … `chapter-0050.md` and this list was written from the manuscript and not from memory.

1. **The batch is a working project and it fails in the middle of itself, twice, in ordinary ways, and neither failure is supernatural.** **Nobody took a turn**, so the sheet came off the nail on Friday the 7th at about eleven and nothing went up until the Monday the 10th at about ten; and **a man who lets four rooms behind a boatyard used a works office copying machine on the Monday the 3rd at half past two** and filled six boxes with six names. A knee is bad, a channel crossing is rotten, and a sheet of paper is copied. **A later batch may not make the project's failures mysterious and may not turn the copied sheet into a magical artefact, a conspiracy, or a new faction.**

2. **The four Thursdays, all four of them, on the four dates, in these terms.** **The consent** is given by the site manager — Rennick — on a four-line form reading **SITE / PERSON / OCCASIONS / PERIOD**, on Wednesday the 22nd in **eleven minutes** in a portacabin, with the four dates written out in full, the hours written on the form, and the word **site** and not **route**. Milo Rook is outside the door and is not admitted. Rennick's reason, said once: a person at a door in daylight is a person in a photograph, and he would rather have one person on his site who is a person than a return saying nobody is here, which is what he has been certifying every morning since the sixteenth. **The first, on the 23rd at ten to two**, at the ground-floor door, coal hatch side: the third call asked, Lena Marr said it, nothing whatever happened, and the reading she makes him write and then corrects one word of is *at the third call, on the coal hatch side, at ten to two, in daylight, on Thursday the twenty-third of this month, the space at the foot of the door was clear.* **The second, on the 30th: it did not ask.** She waited past the interval and stopped at about twenty seconds past two, was told the stopping needs no reason, gave one — her legs were cold — and nobody wrote it down. **The third, on the 6th of next month:** the call asked, she said it, and about a second and a half later a voice from off the outside stairs at about the height of the second-floor landing said the same word and the door went; nobody went up, Rue said *stay where you are* to about four people who had started, and Milo Rook did not go and put the word in brackets on the facing page and wrote beside them that he did not ask who it was and did not go and that both are correct. **The fourth, on the 13th: there was no door**, because the works' notice has the coal hatch side behind a fence with a trench in it, and she said *clear* into the rain and the plant did not stop and nobody in the trench put their hands down, and **she wrote a card in her own hand recording the fourth Thursday as not worked, because she was not at the threshold and because a man in that trench has been saying *nobody told us* every working day since the twelfth.** The card went to Hettie Brann, who did not read it, and then to the clerk at the gate. **The instrument expires at noon on Friday the 14th. It is not extended, renewed, minuted again, converted, or turned into a schedule by anybody, and Mara Quill says in a yard that she is not going to look for a fifth Thursday in any form, because a thing which is only ever four weeks is the only instrument she has ever made that could not be turned into a form.**

3. **The turn sheet, exactly as it stands on the fifteenth. This is the second source of witnessing and it is now the district's main artefact.** One page, typed, **on the employer's stationery of the flood-control contractor**, so the head of a contractor is at the top of it, and the head is not coming off. Four columns: **DAY, HOUR, TURN, DONE.** The hour is written as *from about ten to two*, because a mechanic said the person waits until it stops and then says it. **There is no route in the head of it** — the third version had one and the fourth replaced it with one line, *the way the round goes out at eight and in at four*, which is Rue's, because a route written on a wall gets walked by people who were not on it. The rule at the foot, in three parts, verbatim: *Nobody writes anybody else's name. If you wrote a line you may cross it out and write another. Nobody may cross out a line they did not write. / When the week is done the sheet comes off the nail and it is not kept, and whoever took the last turn puts up the next one. / Nothing on this sheet goes into an office. A person may take it off the nail.* **It is spiked behind the dispensary counter in the ground floor of Cedar Court. Hettie Brann holds it and does not read it and does not enter it in anything, and says *I do not read it* to a badge the same as to anybody, because the day she reads it is the day it is in a book and the day it is in a book is the day somebody can be told to produce it.** It is held on **alternate weeks by Hettie Brann and by Mara Quill, and on the week it is on Mara Quill's side of the nail the rule at the foot is in her hand and not his** — the only sentence in any version in Advocate Quill's handwriting, written down by Rue. **Four versions exist, dated, in a bundle tied with a piece of twine, and Rue holds the bundle and Milo Rook hands it over.** **Milo's part is arithmetic, labour, versions and refusal, and he is none of the other three things, and he said so in a porch in front of about thirty people at Lena Marr's request.**

4. **The deliberate blank, and who fills it, and the fact that this is a design decision and not an accident.** There is about an inch and a half of white space at the foot of the page below the rule, and **nothing in it.** Milo Rook wanted to put a word in it on the third, wrote the word in his own notebook, and crossed it out; **the word was *floor*.** Rue refused a day in it, because she has had a hole with a name in it for six weeks and will not go up, and because if a day went in it somebody would work out which building it was for inside a fortnight. **The design is Pell Harrow's, aged seventeen, and the sentence is: *Then leave it and put a line under it that says leave it. Not left because nobody has come. Left because it says so.*** And under the space, written in by Rue on Monday the 10th at about eleven in her own hand, in the same pen as the rule, and read out twice: ***This line is left empty. It is not an oversight. It is for a person who was not here.*** Milo Rook said in public that **he did not make the decision, because the moment it has an author it is a document about a person, and it belongs to whoever writes in it, and it is the only line on that sheet that does not belong to him and the only one that is going to survive.** **The line is filled, and then changed, by a person who was not present at the first crossing on the fourth of Sazur:** the woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court, who has never been named on the page, came down at about four on the Monday the tenth on her own because there was nothing on the nail, wrote **\*Second.\*** in the empty line, and under it, in her own hand, ***I was not here on the fourth of Sazur and I am not here now by anybody's asking. Take it or leave it.*** **On Wednesday the 12th she changed it to \*Third\*.** Nobody in the porch touched it except her; Milo Rook was in the building both times and did not have the sheet in his hand either time and has written that down as a fact about himself and not as a virtue. **The blank was left empty and a copy of the sheet had it filled in, in a good hand, in about eleven seconds, and that is the batch's cost and the reason the blank became deliberate. Later batches may not describe the blank as a loss, a mystery, or a thing to be completed, and nobody may fill it for her.**

5. **Three sources of witnessing now stand, and the volume guardrail is satisfied.** **The rota** is unchanged: five lines, five names, one line struck through by the woman whose name it was and not restored, **four people behind four of the five names and nobody behind the fifth-floor name**, the promise at the foot unchanged, and Ada Fenn not on it and absent from the whole batch. **The schedule** is the turn sheet, above. **The door-check as a written turn** exists and is not Ada Fenn's habit: the TURN column, the one line in the blank, and the woman of sixty from the second landing, who did two turns in pencil in one afternoon and has said out loud that they are not turns because she did not agree to them and that counting them is the first lie anybody has told about her in nine years. **Ada Fenn does not appear in Chapters 41–50, her name is not written once in them, she is not named, thanked, replaced, argued back or put on anything, and nobody climbs three flights of stairs about her.** Later batches may not solve it, thank her, name her, or let a neighbour become her door-check.

6. **The three answers, on the page, in three mouths, and none of them chosen.** **A street** — the woman of sixty from the second landing: a name on the index, a number on the door, a shop that sends to it, a post that comes to it, a doctor that can be found, and a school that can be asked whether a child lives here. **A way to walk** — Doreen Sankey, forty-one, two rooms behind a boatyard: *not the name of it. The way*, eleven minutes from his door to the tram steps, and he would rather be hard to find. **A memorial** — Tomas Rusk, sixty-one, from a kitchen table with his stick against the edge of it: a road with people on it that you walk through, somebody who knows the people, and a place to put flowers, and *I would rather be in a thing that is wrong than outside of it.* They are not synonyms and the people who hold them are not the same people. **Lena Marr refused to pick**, said the choice has to be made in a yard and not in a kitchen with a plastic wallet, and **by the fifteenth of the month she is organising a thing and is not the centre of it.**

7. **Owen Fitch's refusal, and the fact that it is not a wound to be healed.** On the sixth of next month, at about half past five, **outside, at a washing line behind the boatyard, alone, with a stick, deliberately not in the yard**, because *in there I am the man on the third-floor line with three words next to my name, and out here I am nobody, and the yard would make it a thing that happened.* A memorial route needs a turn and he is the only person anybody can think of. *You can have a memorial with nobody at the turn. It will just be a road with flowers on it and no way of finding out whether the people on it are where you think they are, and that is worse than what we have got now.* And: *if you put my name on that sheet in any form I will take it off myself, in my own hand, and you will all watch, and none of you will say a word about it afterwards.* **Nobody thanked him, nobody healed him, and no character in this batch softens him.** And on the fifteenth **his name was put on a piece of paper for him anyway** — item 8 — and that is the new problem, and it is his, and it is not resolved.

8. **The copied sheet, the second route, and the false witness. This is the batch's cost and it is local, small, ordinary and administrative.** **Neville Cobb lets four rooms behind a boatyard on the Ferry Road side.** He came into the works office on Monday the 3rd at half past two asking about a length of lead pipe, used the machine for about four minutes, and put in **a shilling and two coins for a ream**. He had three people looking at rooms and wanted to show them the block was organised, and a contractor's head means a thing a company has agreed to. **He filled in six empty boxes**: four names off his own rent book, and two names the woman at the door of the Board school gave him when he asked, in the way a woman who keeps a list gives a list, by reading it. **One of the two was the woman of sixty from the second landing of Cedar Court**, and she has said in a yard that the fault is hers, in that order, and would not have it softened. **The copy's head carried a second route nobody authorised: boatyard steps, along the towpath, under the embankment, up the tram steps, and back.** A man called Carraway, looking at rooms, walked it at about half past three on Tuesday the 4th and put his foot through **eleven feet of rotten board on the old wash-house channel crossing**, a plank and a joist with a rail on one side and nothing on the other, and went in to the knee in a tide and was got out with a boat hook. **The boatyard owner nailed the boards shut on the Wednesday the 5th with three-inch nails and said the crossing was out for five days**, which put Doreen Sankey on the wrong side of his own gate, with the dispensary counter in Cedar Court and the milk and the only cold store within four streets on the far side. Reopened on the 10th with three new boards, for nothing. **The sheet was not withdrawn**, on Lena Marr's stated reason that withdrawing it concedes that it was the district's and puts a woman of sixty and a man of forty-one on a district list. **The repair is that the two people named wrote their own lines out, in their own hands, with their own reasons, and nobody wrote for them: \*I only read it\*, and a name crossed out with nothing beside it and no reason given.** **Five copies are now in circulation** and later batches may not reduce that number without an on-page reason.

9. **Doreen Sankey, and the district's debt to him.** Forty-one, two rooms behind a boatyard on the Ferry Road side, nine years, **paid nobody anything for any of them**, and on **no rate book, no district index, no rate, no tenancy and no schedule, and never has been.** One of the thirty-one rooms on no list. **The Recovery officer wrote four lines in her own hand, dated them, and kept them — *No record of this person. No rate. No tenancy. No schedule. He is not on any list in this office and never has been. He was on a sheet of paper in a yard and that was not this office* — and would not give them to him, because the moment it is a piece of paper about a person it wants a file number and the moment it has a file number it is a record, and a record is a thing that can be produced.** **This is a positive statement that a person does not exist, made in an advocate's own hand, and it is a thing a later batch may use and may not quietly undo.**

10. **Milo Rook's failure, which is a failure at the thing he is good at, and Iona Sen's finding on paper.** **On the evening of Monday the 3rd at half past five**, the day Cobb used the machine at half past two, he had that copy of the sheet in his hands for **about six minutes** and **read the head, the date, the reduction, the four columns, the rule and the route in the head, and did not read the column of names** — because he has been told not to be left alone with a piece of paper about a person — and Rue picked it up and read the six names out loud. **On the evening of Tuesday the 4th at half past five, two hours after the route on that copy had been walked by a man looking at rooms, he stood in the porch again and read a second copy out loud, and he did not know about the walk for nineteen hours, which puts him at the counter on the Wednesday morning.** Chapter 44 stages the first reading and Chapter 45 the second, and the walk is the Tuesday at about half past three. **Later batches may not collapse the two readings into one, and may not put either of them on a day other than the 3rd and the 4th.** **On Wednesday the 12th Iona Sen made him write the fifth finding down in his own hand, dated the sixteenth of Sazur, signed M.R., took the page for a locked drawer, said she would not give it back, and then said out loud in the rain in a yard that writing it down has made it worse, because there is now a piece of paper about a person and he is the man holding it, and that it is on her, and that it will not help.** The finding is *you are not to be left alone with a piece of paper about a person, not once, not ever, for as long as you are in this district.* **It is now on paper for the first time in the volume, it is unhelped, he is not cured, and later batches may not treat being cured of it as an achievement.**

11. **Mara Quill's decision about the promise, and it is the batch's relationship work.** She asked for the bundle in the porch at ten past twelve on the 12th with about twelve people there; the asking took four minutes and the answer took nine. **The promise of the 29th of Sazur, forty-four days held, stands, is hers, and does not get wider**, because she cannot carry thirty people and because the day she promises a woman of sixty that nothing about her reaches her in writing is the day she has become a second Registry — and she has a review on her own hand on the third of next month. **What is given away instead is the design**, because a document is a document by the method it was made in and he is the method, and there are three copies of his design in the district and one has six names on it that four people did not agree to. **She refused to rescue him twice more: she will not file a fifth Thursday in any form, and she designed the fifth version as a sheet of *turns* and a sheet of *hours somebody will not be there*, which she had to do herself and could not hand to him.** He argued for about nine seconds and accepted it. **Nothing romantic has happened and nothing is going to, and neither of them has said anything about how either of them feels.**

12. **Tomas Rusk's sentence, true and unsigned, and the condition that is not written.** Asked for by Mara Quill, by telephone at nine in the morning and in person in a kitchen at two o'clock, in these words: ***On the night of the closure the road shut in the middle of a run. Not at the end of it. In the middle. The crew came off the frontage to the pump house and the reach emptied, and a frontage with nobody on it is a road that is not being kept.*** He says it in a room, in the street, or in a book, and it will not be sworn. **He attaches one thing to the account itself, has not decided what it is, has said so out loud so that nobody can say he sprang it on them, and will not be asked about it twice; she has promised the same. The condition itself is Chapters 51–53 and is not written anywhere in Chapters 41–50.** And his instrument, given because he cannot get to a door: **the interval inside the pump house is twenty-nine seconds, not thirty-one**, because the shaft is a two-inch flat on a six-inch drum and it takes up before it takes out and there is a lad in it with his hand on a lever; so a person at a door counting to thirty-one is counting a machine, and **the instrument is: do not count, wait until it stops, and then say it.**

13. **Orrin Sable's project, as engineering necessity and as political instrument, in one scene, both true, unresolved.** **The soft lens was booked to take about two further feet across the reach before the first of next month and then stop, and it stops on the first of next month, which is Monday the first, three weeks after Chapter 50.** A level is no use on ground that is still going down, a level has to be certified by a foreman, and **the floodgate test is on the sixth of next month, which is Saturday the sixth and twenty-one days after the last page of the batch**, so **the window in which the last reach can be laid, levelled, proved and certified is the second, the third, the fourth and the fifth of next month — four days — and it has not opened.** The trench for it is four foot deep and about eight foot wide and goes under the only made public path on the reach, and the north side is not a path. **The works' notice went up on the hoarding at 09.10 on Saturday the 8th, on the same hoarding as the plate notice, in the same typeface, and closes the coal hatch side access from the ninth to the nineteenth of this month inclusive, and says that the path to the north is not a public route, and there is no alternative provision, and it is signed for the works by the site manager.** He said he put it up on a Saturday and told Milo Rook to write down both the day and the hour, and that he **decided not to move the date and said so out loud**, and that Milo may write it down and it does him no good whatever. **The political instrument is in the same shape and the same clause: a works notice with no order behind it that says a path is not a public route; a four-day window that costs a building of about nine people its only dry door; and a certificate bought by bringing a foreman back at full rate for eleven days, which resets his review and means a man in a good coat will say he was *reinstated*.** He has not been softened, made wrong, suspended, or made a final antagonist. **The plate is on the only door in the flood-control wall and it is there to stay. The order stands. The public name is not restored and no variation of the order is made.**

14. **Danner's certificate, in his own hand, and it is a document that cannot be corrected from inside itself.** On Monday the 10th he put a variation of the second stage on the trestle table with the hours filled in his own hand and the **\*alternative considered\*** box filled in his own hand because he will not leave a space on a district form: **reach brought to schedule level over the soft lens reach on or before the fifth of next month, and the level certified on ground recorded as settled on the first of next month; no alternative available in the period; certifying officer T. Danner, foreman, district flood stage** — and then, in the same hand: ***I record that the certification is made in the fourteenth day of a standard deviation in the readings at the trench, and I will not be able to say in a room that I stood behind it, and I am certifying it because the level has to be certified by a man and the only man is me.*** **The level is not back in Milo's file and may not be put back.** He is on half rate under a review with no date and has been brought back at full rate for eleven days, which resets the review, and the second paragraph of the joint finding of the twentieth says a surface was made level across a place which had been two surfaces for six years, and that finding went to a Bench on the 21st of October and is about him.

15. **The count, for the third time, and the money, and the letter.** The district's figure of **two hundred and thirty-one on the eighth of Sazur**. **Two hundred and forty-nine in fifty-four rooms in eleven streets on the 16th and 17th**, on doorsteps, without knocking. **Two hundred and fifty-two in fifty-five rooms in eleven streets on the 31st and the 1st, thirty-three rooms on no list at all, twenty-one short of the schedule** — four people up behind the boatyard and on the Terrace, one person out of a first-floor room on Sazur Row, two rooms and one room gone. The per-head additional schedule is **two and four a head a week, five hundred and fifty-four pounds and forty, and forty-three pounds and two for the eighteen short.** **A rates authority has asked the Recovery officer for a schedule of rooms, by street and dwelling, within fourteen days, and failing such a schedule the schedule of two hundred and thirty-one stands and the per-head charge continues on that figure.** She has fourteen days and cannot make one, and is going to write and say so. **Lena Marr refused to put the new number on anything**, because the moment a number reaches a rates authority they stop counting people and start building a list of rooms. **The works clerk asked her, in a corridor, in words, whether the count of the seventeenth could be adopted, and she said no twice and he said *it is only a schedule* twice**; the levy is a schedule and a schedule is a contract and eleven weeks of a regrade is paid out of it. **A rate is built on a property and a person in a room is not a property, the count of people and the count of rooms will never be the same number, and a rate cannot be built on a door. No batch closes this administratively and Chapters 58–60 still have to make it cost somebody money.**

16. **The school bill, unchanged and unpaid.** **Nine children out of the school until the spring intake. Two of the nine are lost for good** because their mother is a named person on a Bench instrument of the 21st. **Seven come back in the spring if the intake opens, and the intake opens on a tenancy, a temporary registration or a Continuity referral, and every one of the three asks for a place.** **No third of the nine has been quietly regained and none may be.** The woman at the door of the Board school keeps the list, the list closes at noon, and she has said that she will not be the door-check and is right, because a woman who stands at a door is a woman who will be asked whether the people behind it are where she thinks they are, and she has thirty-one children in three rooms. **She read two names off her list to a man who lets rooms and one of them was the woman of sixty's, and she has said in a yard that this is the fault, in that order, and would not have it softened.**

17. **The address/money trap is untouched and is still a trap.** The name must stay on the compensation schedule and off the street index, and a current address for Cedar Court ends the payment. **Nobody in this batch applied for one, nobody solved it, and no clerk solved it.** Nobody has attempted to obtain a current address. **The register's answer to who speaks for the block is still a man asleep on the third floor; his name is not said out loud anywhere in Chapters 41–50, he is not woken, and nobody in this batch — least of all the inspector — is the person who notices him.**

18. **Tarin Kest's application is refused, and the refusal is now on a Crown Heights file with the four Thursdays in it.** On the 29th of the month, four paragraphs: the Filewell precedent is accepted, the ground stated is a person familiar with the route withdrawn and there is not one, the entry so made is the correct entry — **and the department notes, among the matters considered, that a Bench minute of the 21st of the current month permits one named person to walk the threshold of the withdrawn alignment on four dates, and that the department is aware that no similar arrangement is proposed after the fourteenth of next month.** **It grants nothing and it is not a solution, and it is dangerous, because a thing in a list of matters considered in Crown Heights is a thing somebody with a form can produce.** **He has not returned to the district, has not changed his paragraph, and is not a friend.** The *no person available* phrase is on a fourth instrument and may not become a solution.

19. **The timetable cycle, and the count that is a signal again.** **The Line 7 sheet turns over on the first of next month.** The diversion is permanent. The minute and a half at the Ferry Road turn is gone off the running time and the running time on the diversion is **eleven seconds shorter** where a car comes out of the turn and goes past the box, and **the box in the diversion panel now reads *COUNT AT FERRY ROAD TURN — AUTOMATIC. NO STAFF TURN*.** The hand came off the count on the 19th, earlier in the same month, because there was a form in the authority's file about a conductor doing it, and that is what a form does. **Jo Lask refused the ask of the 27th and was right, and he is not suspended any more, and the four weeks ran out on the 20th of the month.** His reason, unchanged: *a man who is available at a time is a man who can be asked at a time*, and a document that can lean on him turns him into a habit. **He asked Milo Rook to tell the woman he said the same eleven words he said on the tenth; Milo Rook was not asked, he was told, and has written that down. The interval guarantee is Chapters 51–53 and has not been asked for.**

20. **The first finding of the batch's method, and it is small, and it is the one everybody will build on:** **a sheet of paper that has to be replaced every week, on a nail, in a building, by a person who can be found, is a thing that survives a timetable.** Eleven weeks, eleven sheets, four hundred and forty turns. It is an argument about what a person owes a street and it was arrived at by a man doing sums about how many sheets a district needs in a month, and it is the only finding in the batch anybody is going to write down. **Milo Rook found it and then said in a yard that he is not going to be the person who holds it, and that is the whole of the method change under a project.**

21. **The tandem facts, kept separate, and deliberately not joined.** **Pell Harrow came back on the 27th with no news of Tern** — he has not seen Tern, he asked Ostrow one question he will not write down, and he got a true answer — **and with a second fact he gave once and then stopped: a woman in Wragg Street keeps a nineteen-year-old sheet for three rooms over a chandler's, with a column for the room, a column for the name, a column for what is owed, and a fourth column with a tick in it in every third line; the tick is in a different, copying hand; he has seen about six in four months in four streets; the woman cannot say what it is for and says her husband did it, and her husband has been dead four years. He will not go back and look.** **Milo Rook does not join that to the copied turn sheet.** He writes them in different paragraphs and records why: *joining them would be me deciding that they are the same, and a man who decides two things are the same in a book is a man who has started making the case instead of the record.* **Ostrow's list in a hut and a tick in a fourth column are the same kind of thing, they are not connected on the page, the list is not a finding, Ostrow confirms nothing, and it is the next volume's question planted once and not answered. It does not touch the Quieting, the First Atlas, or the One Survey.**

22. **The clue chain is carried and not advanced, and is not Soren.** **Two documents carry a pair of marks, not three. Three references exist and are references and not pairs: the Crown Heights repository consultation reference against *p.9 withdrawn*, the one on the copy register, and the one on the Filewell index.** The 21st's Registry certification is **drawn** where the two confirmed marks are **pressed, a difference in method and not in age.** Kest has **not matched the strings.** **Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–50 and is not named as the hand here.** Keep the chain separate from the withdrawn page, the certified copy, the maintenance ledger number, and Attachment 9, and do not turn a fold into a chronology. **No new grammar, faction, antagonist, or final enemy has been introduced. Halden Vey and Sel Iri do not appear in person and are not for this batch.**

23. **The triad accounting is not used at all in Chapters 41–50** — not restated, not news, not a thin remnant that later grew — and that is a deliberate silence and not an omission. The thin remnant at the western culvert is not mentioned. **The answer to the bells is used and not re-decided**: the third call asks for *clear* on the 23rd, the 30th, the 6th and the 13th, and is answered three times by a person at a door and once by nobody, and that is a check and a check is a person saying a space is clear before the next turn. Nobody in this batch re-argues what the bells were for, nobody hedges it, and **nobody decides it again.**

24. **The culvert is not sealed, the 22nd of Sazur is not a first opening, the pinned-seam condition is not named and is not due before Chapter 51.** Old Mabb is not woken, is not written down, **and his name is not said out loud.** The **floodgate test date is on the record and has not been announced**: the sixth of next month is on a hoarding, in a yard, in a mouth, and in a form, and **this batch did not run the test, did not alter the Line 7 timetable, and did not stage the climax.** The floodgate climax is Chapters 54–57.

25. **Crossings and the power ledger.** **Stage 1 held. No advance and no new cost. Milo is across six times in Volume 1: the fourth, sixth, eighteenth, twenty-first and twenty-sixth of Sazur, and the tenth of the previous month at 14.06.** **This batch adds no crossing and the reason is on the page: on the tenth of this month he is under an awning with nothing in his hands and the door is shut, and that is the shortest reason he has ever written down.** The orientation blank is **eight named remembered routes and no ninth**, all recoverable, all marked by other people, and none removed; he has been to four of the eight this month without losing any of them and has written down that he does not know what that means. **The Field Book is intact and the written line home is complete, correct and legible; he walked it twice in the week ending the fifteenth. The written-line loss remains a Chapters 51–53 event and is not hinted at, booked, or found by any character anywhere in this batch.**

26. **Atlas residue was used once in Chapters 41–50**, in Chapter 48, in **five** words: ***An empty line is a door.*** It describes a piece of accountancy paper and does not say who ought to stand in the gap or whether anybody should. He limits it on the facing page before he acts on it, dates it, and does not take the book near the porch until the fifteenth. **The Field Book is otherwise silent in the batch, on the 23rd, the 9th, the 10th, the 11th, the 13th, the 14th and the 15th, and it wrote once, on the 12th.** **Five dated pairs are now in the book — six days, nine days, three days, three days, two days — and he writes all five down, joins none of them to any other, and looks at a sixth, of one day, and does not write it.** **Later batches may add to the record with a date and may not collapse the pairs, may not write them as a pattern, and may not use the word *pair* as though he had decided what one is.** The **Lark token was cold again on the 13th and he did not take it out**; it opened nothing, summoned nobody, replaced no witness, and was not used as a key.

27. **The problem this batch hands to Chapters 51–60, stated as it is on the page.** **Somebody in this district is putting the people who keep a street onto paper, and the people on it cannot take themselves off it.** **Five copies** of a document with a flood-control contractor's head on it: in a letting agent's office in Wragg Street, in a house on the Terrace, one not come back, **one in a letter box in the Board school office addressed to *the person keeping the street***, one in a fourth-floor flat in Wragg Street that a man who lets rooms has shown to two prospective tenants. **One of them has the last line of the rule cut off in the copying, and the deliberate space filled in — with \*Owen Fitch\*, and under it \*third floor\*, in the same hand, and no words beside it.** **He came down and said the sentence this batch ends on: to take a name off a document somebody has to stand up in a room and say it was never ours, not that he did not agree and not that there is a rule at the foot — and if it was never theirs then it comes off the nail, and on the fifteenth there is nothing.** Nobody explained it to him. **This is a document problem, it is local, it is administrative, and it is the next volume's question reached from a second direction. It does not touch the Quieting, the First Atlas, or the One Survey, and it must not be answered inside Volume 1.**

28. **Nothing was won that cannot be taken back.** The order stands and is untouched. The public name is not restored and no variation of the order is made. **A four-inch steel plate is on the only door in the flood-control wall and it is there to stay.** The regrade is running, the low streets are under six inches and rising in a month with under an inch of rain, and the fifth pump is on Sazur Row. A foreman is back on full rate certifying a level he has written on a form that he cannot stand behind. The disused platform is out of service and its nameboards are in the river. **Nine children are out of a school until the spring and two of the nine are lost for good because Lena Marr put her name on a Bench instrument.** Three sworn accounts and one unsworn go to the next Bench, with a mechanic's sentence true and unsigned attached to nothing, and a condition he has not stated. **The worker's death record is still closed and the widow still cannot be told where he is buried, and the woman of fifty-four who mends other people's clothes is alive and unnamed on the page.** The four-Thursday instrument expired at noon on the 14th and **the district has nothing for the fifteenth and the sheet it built is on a contractor's letterhead in five houses with a man's name on one of them.**

## What Chapters 51–60 fixed on the page, in the same form as CR-013

Each item below is now prose rather than plan, and each is load-bearing in a way Volume 2 could break without intending to. None of them changes a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder, or the planned ending. **Every figure here is quotable from a chapter in `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0051.md` … `chapter-0060.md`, and this list was written from the manuscript and not from memory.** Chapters 51–60 **close Volume 01**, and Volume 2 inherits this list whole.

1. **The pinned seam, named once, and only here.** The word is in the Field Book on the sixth of December at about 21.20, at the bottom of a page that already has the day's times on it, under a line, **with no date under the line, and it is the only undated thing in two hundred and eleven entries.** It is defined on the facing page, dated, in his own hand, and the definition is canonical: **a seam held open instead of sealed, because a live continuity order keeps redirecting pressure into it; it does not close while the order stands; its pressure travels toward whatever the timetables, gates, and records point it at; it comes loose when the order is suspended and the routes around it are re-cut; and it is quiet, not healed.** It is **a condition, not a remnant type, not a new grammar, and not a new failure state**, and the page says so in those words. It may produce a full seamquake first and then stop producing new ones, and the page says that too, and says why that is worse to live beside. **The word does not appear in Chapters 1–50 in this sense** — the four earlier uses of *pinned* are paper pinned to a board, in `chapter-0016.md`, `chapter-0024.md`, `chapter-0026.md` and `chapter-0028.md` — **and no later batch may retrofit the concept into earlier chapters.**

2. **The altered Line 7 timetable, and the test date announced with it, twenty-one days early, out loud, on purpose.** Sunday the sixteenth of November, about eleven in the morning, in a yard, to about sixty people, standing on a trestle table. The works notice: **the final test on Saturday the sixth of December**; the last reach brought to a schedule level **between the second and the fifth inclusive**; certified on ground recorded as settled on **the first**; access by the north path only; signed for the works by R. Rennick; **entered under the standing instruction of the office of the Prefect, ref. 11/D/6.** The Line 7 amendment of the first of the month, in the same notice: the loop withdrawn and not reinstated; the diversion permanent; **nine seconds taken out at the Alder Quay interchange** while the reach is made level beneath it; **the count automatic at the Ferry Road turn and at the interchange, with no staff turn at either**, so the count has nowhere to be handed at all. **The eleven seconds and the nine seconds are different numbers and the prose does not add them, and a later batch may not add them.**

3. **Barrow's minute of the seventeenth of November, four lines, in his own hand, on the authority's division mark, and he came to the district to write it.** *Noted: the works notice of the sixteenth, final test Saturday the sixth of the month following. Noted: the Line 7 amendment, the diversion permanent, the loop withdrawn and not reinstated, nine seconds withdrawn at the interchange, no staff turn at either box, count automatic at both. Noted: a temporary arrangement is not a reason to vary an order, in advance, in a file, and that having been said in advance it is very difficult to unwrite. Noted: the district states that the count is a person and that a signal is not a person. I record that the authority's position is that a count is a function of the timetable division. I have minuted the district's position as stated, and not adopted it, and there is a difference.* He gave two copies and left with the cover under his arm. **He also minuted the Crown Heights refusal of the twenty-ninth of November by implication. The reason for doing it that way is not in this batch and is not his: *a man who offers you a form is worth four men who will not fill one in* is a Field Book entry of Milo's about Jo Lask in Chapter 42, and no later batch may put the sentence in Barrow's mouth.**

4. **The district's decision, in nine minutes on the seventeenth, and it is a decision not to act.** **No fifth Thursday**, because a fifth Thursday is a form and dies on a date somebody in Crown Heights picks out of a diary. **No going to the Bench before the sixth**, because the classification is in the file, cannot be excluded, and will be read out before the district has finished its second sentence, and *after that the only two sentences anybody will hear are ours and his* — **and after the sixth it is not a claim, it is an event, with witnesses and a time on it.** And **the count.** This is what made the public safety appeal possible on the eighth of December and not on the sixteenth of November.

5. **The four working conditions, each one attached to a named person, and every one of them paid for.** **The residents split** over three days into four positions, and the fourth is Owen Fitch's: **nobody goes in, and the reason gets written down in the district's own book.** **The supervised return** is on Rue's round, out at eight and in at four, first name and floor and nothing else, nineteen names on the twentieth of November and **twenty-six by the twenty-first**; **Lena Marr wrote the notice and handed it over inside four minutes and does not run it.** **Owen Fitch refuses the turn and every other part**, and the refusal includes a disclosure — **that he has not been on that street since the night of the closure and did not go back when the order came** — and he offers one thing that is not a role: the ground-floor door, in daylight, once, on the sixth, **saying nothing, not going in, not a turn, not counted**, about four minutes, gone before the round was in. **The record he asked for exists and is not the record he asked for**: the line that went into the record of the seventh carries no figure at all, and Rue has written down in her own book that he asked for a number, was not given one, and was not going to be given one, and **nobody explained his position to him and nobody thanked him.** **Tomas Rusk's condition is a name: Bridge Reach.** **Jo Lask's guarantee costs him a printed rule book with his name in it and ends his inquiry.**

6. **Tomas Rusk's condition, in his own words and true to his account, and it costs him and is not curable.** *The road is not the lane.* A true sentence about a place that does not exist can be used against a man who did not know it did not exist; the frontage between the pump house and the low end has a name in the district's own drainage book and in the works' own schedule and has been a maintained frontage since before he was at the pump house; **his sentence is about Bridge Reach, and if the record is not in before the sixth, he will not have said a true thing about a place that does not exist.** **What it costs him is his name**: a page in the reopened order file, at his own request, with his name at the foot of it and **no oath on it**, and the clerk's four words at the coal hatch side — *Requested by a mechanic.* **It is not a bargain for his oath and it is not his oath: he still will not swear**, because a wage, four years of a district works pension, thirty years of service and a knee, and **nobody in this batch argued him out of it and nobody tried.** **It cannot be taken back**, and he has said in a yard that it is the last thing he will ever put his name to and was not thanked.

7. **Jo Lask's interval guarantee, on the page in three terms, and the price.** Granted on the third of December and **printed in a rule book that names the issuing officer and the conductor called out: J. LASK, conductor, Alder Quay depot.** The terms: a count on the reach is recorded as a count, a number given by a person to a person, and **is not recorded as a signal**; a count at the Ferry Road turn or the interchange **may not be replaced by an automatic function without the running time being restored in full on the day it is replaced**; **no box on any sheet is filled by a person on the reach except under a published standing instruction naming the officer who issued it.** The consideration: **the extra Bellwether stop is not printed on the public timetable, the diversion panel carries no name, and the district makes no claim against the amendment of the first of December in any court, forum or review.** The price: **his name in print for the rest of his life, and the end of his own inquiry**, because a man who has asked a department to publish his name as a guarantee is not aggrieved, and because he will not be conducting on that reach again although he has been on it every day since the fourth of October. **He is not suspended. His hearing still has no date, and the reason is a queue, not kindness.** He also took the count by hand on the sixth where a car comes out of a run and nobody is in the box, and **did not write it down.**

8. **The second stage of the orientation cost, and it is separate from the first, and it is not persistent inside the volume.** The written line home went on **Monday the twenty-fourth of November at about ten past eight in the morning** and is nine lines in his own hand with the loop he has never been able to stop making on the capital L, from a Monday in a year that does not matter, and the page is there, the hand is there, the ink on either side is the same ink, and it is clean to the bottom of the line. **This is not fading and he wrote that down and did not write down what he thought it was.** **It is recoverable when marked: he walked home on the twenty-eighth on the back of a chalk arrow he put on his own door, and a chalk arrow is a gate-man's practice and is not his.** It was marked for him by other people on the seventh of December (Ola Petrossian, a card) and the ninth (a woman of sixty from the second landing, at Rue's insistence) and the fourteenth (Pell Harrow, three arrows, one of them on a second-floor landing with no date on it). **He asked two people to write it for him in Chapter 60 and was refused twice, by people who had no reason to refuse him, and the blank is still blank.** He writes down in order that it might be a cost, a bad memory, or a page that needs another person's mark, and that **he does not know which, and has not decided.** **The two stages are not collapsed and Dr Sen's number is the argument: there are eight remembered routes and there is a page, and they are not one thing.**

9. **The orientation blank's list, now ten, and the rule about it.** **The original eight are intact and none has been removed**, and later batches may add and may not remove. Added in this batch: **the hundred and one steps of the stair tower at the Alder Quay interchange** (a card from Ola Petrossian, twenty-three, four months in the job, never been past the tram steps, given at the ground-floor door at ten to four on the seventh of December, *I am writing it on a card because you are going to lose that and not the pier*) and **the walk of the eighteenth of October with a lamp and a cat in a laundry bag — ground-floor door, coal hatch side, along the path, out the works gate, round by the gate-man's stool, to the tram steps, and back** (marked on the ninth, in Rue's hand, on a card, and **she would not do it herself and made a woman on the second landing do it, because that woman has not been on a list since the fourth of December by her own hand**). **All ten are recoverable when marked and all ten are marked by somebody who is not him.**

10. **The two crossings this batch adds, and all four of the signed conditions, and the tally.** **The seventh: Sunday the seventh of December, in at 16.06 and out at 16.35, twenty-nine minutes inside, from the low end of the reach; count held by Hettie Brann from the path head; walked in and out by Doreen Sankey; nine minutes stated; the four conditions signed in October all satisfied; the fourth condition, the clinic, satisfied.** **The eighth: Monday the eighth of December, in at 09.06 and out at 09.33, twenty-seven minutes inside; count held by Pell Harrow with a counter and no words for twenty-seven minutes; walked by Doreen Sankey; clinic at a quarter past eight by Ola Petrossian, who was not asked.** **Both were read out in a yard with Iona Sen's tally present — the seventh at about six o'clock on the seventh to about thirty-one people, the eighth read out on the fifteenth at about twenty past eight in the evening to about eleven, a week after the passage and on the day of the hall, and she makes him say the number out loud, and the tally now has eight lines on it and is the record.** **Milo is across eight times in Volume 1: the fourth, sixth, eighteenth, twenty-first and twenty-sixth of Sazur, the tenth of the month at 14.06, the seventh and eighth of December.** **Each crossing cost one route. The second-stage cost was not paid twice for the same passage, because it had already been paid, without a crossing, on the twenty-fourth of November.**

11. **The overnight mark, tested and stated, and nobody asked for it.** Idar's double arrow with a date went down on the reach at about eleven on Saturday the sixth of December and was gone by about two in the morning on the seventh, and Rue said it in a yard in four sentences without softening: **a mark holds for a working shift, and on the way out and on the way back in, and does not hold overnight, and nobody in this district is going to be asked to hold one overnight, and that includes the boy, and I want that said on the record because I know exactly what a yard does with a rule when the pressure is on and it is the first rule anybody drops.** **Nobody had asked Pell. He volunteered that it was him on Saturday at eleven and refused to do it again, unprompted, and it is on the page that he refused and not that he was asked.**

12. **The crew, the reach, and the sentence that explains the mechanism.** Tomas Rusk, on a telephone at seven in the morning on the seventh, with a clock on the table and his hand about two inches from it, put on the line by a woman of sixty from the second landing who was told it could not be done and let him try anyway: *On the second of that June the crew came off the frontage to the pump house. That is the whole of it… from the moment the crew came off, that length of ground was not a road. It was a gap. And a gap is not a road and it is not a hazard and it is not a street. It is a place where a road used to be, and the difference between those is a crew.* And then, on the sixth of December at ten to two, **there were about eleven of the first gang and nine of the second and forty of the tram and a foreman with a radio standing on the reach**, *and that is exactly the condition I have been describing to you since June six years ago, and paragraph six says the crew change is sounded from the gantry and the paragraph above the one that was taken out says the frontage is at all times a service access and not a public route.* **He said it once, would not say it again, and hung up.** The instrument, given again from a chair on the twenty-eighth of November: **do not count, wait until it stops, and then say it.** The interval is **twenty-nine seconds in the pump house and thirty-one in the street**, and a person counting to thirty-one at a door is counting a machine.

13. **The floodgate test, on the sixth of December, and what it drove, and what it is not.** **The western culvert came out to full flow at 06.20** and the form reads **eighteen inches before, eleven feet four inches clear after, returned to twenty-four inches on completion**, with the *alternative considered* box filled in the foreman's own hand: *Full flow required on the flood side to the head of the test. No alternative. Return aperture to be twenty-four inches on completion and the culvert to be left silted and re-aligned and gated, and the re-alignment to be carried out under a separate instruction.* **The gate went back to twenty-four inches at 14.06, fourteen minutes after the call that asked went out.** **This is the same structure Chapter 7 wound down to eighteen inches of sill, it is not a first opening, and it is not sealed at any point in Volume 1.** The test was answered at about half past nine: the clay held, the wall held, the low point came up on a schedule level, **the third pump on Bridge Street could be taken off**, and **about forty gallons a minute had been coming off the district's own drainage for eleven weeks by means of a fire engine with a man volunteering on it, paid out of a petty float the works had no authority to spend and never told anybody about.** At **11.52** the west side of the interchange went nine inches out of true over about thirty feet in one movement and nothing came down. **Eleven hurt, four through the treatment room.**

14. **The seamquake, and the four hundred answers, and the joint finding of the ninth of December.** At ten to two on the sixth the third call asked **and about four hundred people answered it** — *not a chorus; a check went out on a reach on which there were four hundred people, and a space with a person in it has been described as clear by the person in it, in about four hundred separate and entirely ordinary ways, in under two seconds.* **A woman of sixty-eight was not in the district on the sixth of December and did not say anything on the sixth of December, and nobody went up three flights of stairs about her.** Then **the reach did not go down: it went out of true, eleven inches over forty feet, in one movement, and it did not take the check and it did not refuse the check; it took it and used it.** A rail came up four feet and over, and a man of forty-nine got a hand up about a fifth of a second before it and says he did not decide to. **The service door — four inches of steel, four bosses, fitted at ten past six on the twelfth of November in nine minutes — came off its fixings at about half past two and went into the works zone eleven feet away, on its side, with the four bosses up.** **The joint finding of the ninth of December, in four paragraphs, made by the works and Survey Enforcement together: the third is that sentence, and the fourth is that a reach which has taken a check in this way did not return to its former state within the period of the finding and was not expected to.** The third call at four minutes past two **did not ask**, and a mechanic of sixty-one has a possible answer and has not been asked for it.

15. **Orrin Sable's order, and his refusal of it, and what the crossing was.** At about half past eight on the seventh of December he **ordered the crossing closed at nine and reversed himself in public inside four minutes.** The reason is on the page in his own words: **close the crossing and the reach becomes a road; a road is kept by somebody; and a road with forty people on it at ten to two is a crew, and I would be putting the district's own sentence into the reach against the order of the district, and I have spent six years watching a man do that to about four hundred people and I am not going to be the second one.** And it is not kindness: **paragraph six is still in the file, the department has never withdrawn it, and there is nothing in this city that has ever been recorded as a person in one**, and he would drive every one of the forty out in a wagon if he had the authority. He put **a woman of sixty-three on a wall with a whistle who is not on his payroll and does not want to be**, and said in a yard that on the sixth of December the district goes into a room and asks for the order to be suspended **and he is going to be the man in that room who does not argue, and on Saturday he had said the opposite about a fence, and he would like both recorded.** **About forty people were in the reach on the sixth of December and nobody caused it and nobody stopped it**: about twenty-six of them were people who had come for the door and went down the path at about eleven because it was open and a foreman had invited them, and about fourteen came over a fence about four feet high with a hole in it about eleven feet along that a man had been told to fill on the Friday and had asked for it in writing and filled on the Saturday morning.

16. **The triad accounting, used once, in Chapter 55, in Cass Ardery's mouth, and correctly.** It is an argument about a document and not about a category. *Your order took the name off the index in the first week and it is still off, and it closed the routes in the first week and they are still shut. Those are two things a document did, and they are both in the file, and nobody in this district has ever argued with either of them and neither of them is in dispute. And the third thing is that in six years nobody in that department has ever once put a person on that reach, and I am not saying a word about why, because you have got a form in your case and a form is the wrong place for it… a thing a document does twice is a fact and a thing a document never does is an oversight, and an oversight is the only one of the three that can be corrected by a different department in a different building in about a year, and the other two cannot.* It is said on the back of a works truck, on the condition that it stays there, and **Ardery writes it into a report and refuses the word *triad*, because he has read it twice in the file and cannot define it and *a term in a report that nobody in the report can define is a term that gets removed by the next man*.** **It is never written as three withdrawn and never as a thin remnant that grew. The thin remnant at the western culvert is not mentioned anywhere in Chapters 51–60.**

17. **The answer to the bells, used on six days and not re-decided.** The third call is at a door on the twenty-eighth of November; on the reach on the sixth of December, where about four hundred people answer it; on the seventh; on the eighth; on the twelfth, once, from Lena Marr; and once more on the nineteenth, from a woman of about seventy from Wragg Street who has never been asked to do anything in her life. **A check is a person saying a space is clear before the next turn**, and the instrument a mechanic of sixty-one gave the district in November is still the instrument that holds it: **do not count, wait until it stops, and then say it.** Nobody in this batch hedges it, re-argues it, or decides it again.

18. **The residents' choices, on the seventh and the eighth, and the arithmetic closes.** Of the **twenty-six** on Rue's sheet: **eleven said no and did not go at all** (two in a yard, eight in a doorway, one to Doreen Sankey at the mouth of the path); **three said no and asked to be counted anyway and were counted from outside**, allowed by Rue in four seconds; **five went out through the ground-floor door to Cedar Court**, three of them with their own boxes and one of them with nothing packed; **six went back into the reach to the first forty feet and said the word**; and **one walked the frontage and did not go in — ninety minutes, in daylight, front to back, at about two miles an hour, with about thirty people behind her at eleven feet and not one of them touching her arm.** **Eleven plus three plus twelve is twenty-six; five plus six plus one is twelve.** She is the woman on the fourth floor, she is not named on the page, and her three lines are on it, written by her and read aloud by Rue at her own request not being honoured: *I walked it. Once, in daylight, front to back, and I did not go in and I am not going in. / I am not coming down again for anybody. Not for the sheet, not for the door, not for a turn, not for a hearing, and not for a thing that is in a good coat. I have said that four times now and this is the fourth and I am not saying it a fifth. / The road is not mine and I have not claimed it and nobody is to put my name on it. If it is on anything that is my name on it, take it off in front of me.* **Nobody thanked her. Milo Rook was about four hundred yards away and has written down twice that he was about four hundred yards away.**

19. **Ada Fenn's absence, and the moment she was nearly reached for.** **She does not appear in Chapters 51–60 and her name is not written once in them. She is referred to in five places across four chapters, never by name and always as *a woman of sixty-eight* or *a name of sixty-eight*, and the seven age tags are these: Chapter 54's account of the four hundred answers (twice in one paragraph), Chapter 57 in an unknown man's mouth and then in Lena Marr's (twice in her second paragraph), and once each inside Milo's own Field Book in Chapters 59 and 60. There is one further sixty-eight tag in Chapter 57 and it is not her: the woman on the fourth floor gives her own age in her own mouth, she is a different resident, and no state file may fold the two together.** On Monday the eighth of December a man said *then go and ask the old woman on the third floor* in a yard of about twenty-nine, **and Rue said no first, before anybody else could**, and Lena Marr gave the reason in about sixty words: **She is not ours.** *If we go up and ask her, then the district's answer on the eighth of December is a woman of sixty-eight, and every one of us in this yard goes back to being a thing that happens next to a woman of sixty-eight… and the report will be true, and it will be the end of it, because a report that says a person remained is a report that says a place is kept by one person, and one person is not a district and one person is what this whole street was closed over. And I have not been up those stairs either, and I live in that building, and that is not a thing I am proud of and I am not going to pretend it is.* **Nobody went up. It is on the page that nobody went up, and that about six people in that yard noticed who had not said it and who had, and that not one of them said a word about it afterwards.** **Later batches may not solve her, thank her, name her, or let a neighbour become her door-check.**

20. **The map other people can read, and the map that is deliberately incomplete.** The crossing instrument is **a sheet of accountancy paper with a heading, two columns — TURN and DONE — and the rule at the foot in Rue's own hand**, and **Milo Rook drew nothing and read the rule out loud on the reach**, because the argument of six weeks is that a map with no rule at the foot is an invitation to whoever has got a pen. **The version left after the passage has four lines with something on them and two crossed out** — one because a woman of sixty-three on a wall is the correct use of a wall and if it is written it becomes a job, and one because it is hers, *and if I am on this then anybody can lean on me* — **and the deliberate space at the foot is still empty, went through both days empty, and about four people have checked it.** Rue has written down in her own book that she checked it four times and **has not written down who checked it and why, because she did not check it and she did not ask anybody to.** **Mara Quill's return document is four pages on Registry paper in her hand** — date, out, back, and a column headed *the choice of the person* with **no number in it and the condition that if a number is put in it the whole of the page is void** — with **ten entries in the people's own words, twenty-six names, no floor and no number**, and five conditions including *no person who went is to be asked to go again, and no person who did not go is to be asked why, in any proceeding, hearing, or room.* **This document is the fourth instrument in the case and it is the only one that is not going to Crown Heights.**

21. **The resolution, in the terms the department's answer used, on Monday the fifteenth of December in the Hall of Wards, which is not a hearing.** **A temporary route**, as a supervised passage on named dates, in daylight, with a named person outside holding a count and a named person walking in and out, and no schedule of persons. **A temporary stay** against demolition and against forced relocation, until the conclusion of the investigation, and not thereafter unless the investigation recommends it. **Access**, to every household, to its own housing file and its own compensation file, on application, in person, at a counter, and to no household's file by any other party. **A public hearing** in the Hall of Wards, on a date to be set, **to which the district is entitled as of right and not as of favour** — not yet set, and the reporting officer on the investigation is to be a person other than any person named in the Prefect's letter or in the order. **And the public name restored as a contested community and memorial name: entered on the community and memorial register, and not on the street name index, and not as a rateable place, marked *contested*, and capable of being produced against any person who uses it.** The register entry's last line is the woman of sixty's own request, noted: *Requested in part by a resident of nine years who asked for a street and has been given a thing that can be used against her, and who has asked that this be noted, and it is noted, and the name is entered.*

22. **The order suspended, opened for investigation, and the machinery, and the letter.** **The office of the Prefect, one page, Thursday the eleventh of December at about four in the afternoon, on the office's letterhead, signed in a hand that is not a hand, with a name at the bottom of it, and that name is Halden Vey — the only appearance of that name in the whole case.** A man in a car collected it at a quarter to five and would not come to the yard. Six paragraphs: (1) **the order of the sixth year back, ref. CO/6/BELL, is suspended with effect from ten o'clock on Friday the twelfth, and its schedule is suspended, and the order is not varied, withdrawn or discharged, and the public name is not restored by this suspension**; (2) **the order is opened for investigation** into the grounds of its making, into the classification of the site dated the eleventh of the current year, and into the removal of the sentence *no evidence was found of any hazard to persons at the service court*, **the withdrawal reference against which is not a district reference and is held in the office of the First Surveyor**; (3) **Mr O. Sable is suspended from duty in this district and is required to give account, and this suspension is not a finding and does not imply one — it is made because an officer may not investigate his own order**; (4) the investigation is by Survey Enforcement, reported to the office, **by a person other than any person named in the present letter or in the order**; (5) **the classification of the eleventh is withdrawn and is not to be relied on by any party, and the department notes that it was made correctly on the day it was made and that its withdrawal is not a criticism of the officer who made it**; and (6) **upon the suspension, all timetables, gates, notices, classifications, drains, and records of the district and of any department are to be re-cut to the extent that they point at the withdrawn alignment, and are to cease to point at it, and re-cutting is to be certified by a person familiar with the route withdrawn.** **Paragraph six is the mechanism, and it is in the order of a Prefect and not in the order of a district, and it is what unpinning means, and nobody in this volume says that out loud.** The appeal was lodged at 09.10 on the eighth of December on a statutory clock of six working days, and the answer came on the fifteenth.

23. **The re-cut, which is three documents and a shovel, and it is unpaid and it is the best work in the case.** **The drainage record**: the 1904 book's own columns, a hand four inches high, *Bridge Reach*, 140 ft, maintained frontage, the six-year-old pencil strike unstruck on the Thursday morning of the ninth with the date beside it and *reverting on the suspension of CO/6/BELL*, and four words to Rue at the coal hatch side — *Requested by a mechanic.* **The works maintenance schedule**: a frontage with a name, a reach, a gang and a fortnightly inspection, on the contractor's own schedule under the employer's head, costing about four hundred words mostly about insurance. **Barrow's amendment, one paragraph**, which **does not reinstate anything** and which **puts the name on the diversion panel as *Bridge Reach (works frontage)***, and which he was asked twice by men in his own division not to do: *a works frontage is a thing that appears on about four hundred sheets in this authority's files and has a maintenance schedule behind it and a man to inspect it.*

24. **It does not close. It is quiet, not healed.** **The third call on Friday the twelfth at about ten to two asked once — Lena Marr, at the ground-floor door, on her own, having said in a yard on the Thursday that she was not going to stand at a door again and was not going to be asked — and then it did not ask again for six days, from the thirteenth to the eighteenth, and on the Friday the nineteenth a woman of about seventy from Wragg Street said it once at the north door, and it did not ask on the twentieth.** Dr Sen: **it is not fixed, it is quiet; a fixed thing is one you stop watching; the day somebody in this district stops watching it is the day it starts again, and it will not start with a noise, it will start with a schedule.** **And the material state, which stays:** the culvert at full flow, twenty-four inches, **stop-screw through the stem and a padlock on the wheel**, silted to about a third of its diameter from the low end, re-aligned over two days by nine men under a separate instruction, **and it is not the culvert that was there before the order and it will not be**; the disused platform out of service with its nameboards in the water; the stair tower a hundred and one steps on a new pier with a note in the works file about differential founding; **the four-inch plate on a bench in the works compound with a hole in it where a fixing was**, the door behind it shut and staying shut, and the reason the plate is on a bench is that the works found it and there was nowhere else for it; the reach out of true by **eleven inches over forty feet**, fenced, with a settlement plate and a date on it, and the rail cut off and in a skip on the Terrace.

25. **The address/money trap, paid, on the fifteenth of December at about half past eleven, in a hall, by costing somebody money.** A tenancy needs a street and there is no street. A temporary registration needs a head of household, **and the register was read out by a clerk of the Recovery office in about four seconds, correctly, and the answer is a floor and a file number; nobody in the room said the name of the man on the third floor, nobody woke him, and no clerk noticed anything and said nothing: a clerk of the Recovery office read a file number out at four seconds' notice and went back to his counter.** The third way is a **temporary accommodation household with a named head who is a rateable person** — entered on a per-head additional schedule, not on the street index, not on a rate book, not as a rateable place, **so the compensation, which is assessed on displacement from a named place, is untouched** — and the seven children are enrolled on a **Continuity referral** naming a responsible person, because a referral asks for a place and a place needs a person. **The consequence, stated aloud by the department because he had to state it: two and four a head a week, charged to the named head and not to the district rate, for every person entered in the household, week by week, in advance, not a one-time sum, with no term, ended only by the household ceasing to exist or by the district producing a rateable basis for a block that has no street.** **Lena Marr stood up at about ten to twelve, after writing both her questions down and showing them to a woman of sixty-three on a step, and signed.** The same signature opens the intake for the seven. **The two lost in October are still two and she said so twice in the room, unasked. Nobody thanked her.** **The district's own index now carries a line against the reach reading *no rateable place — works frontage — see Bridge Reach*, in the only box there was, and nobody is going to have it tidied.**
