26. **The count, closed, and the money, and the letter.** **The department accepts a certified head count of two hundred and fifty-two in place of a schedule of rooms, on Advocate Quill's certificate in her own hand dated the fifteenth with a declaration of interest at the top of it; the per-head schedule is adjusted from two hundred and thirty-one and the twenty-one short is recovered; the thirty-three rooms on no list stay on no list; and no room in this district is on a rate book today because of anything that happened today.** The four counts and the two money totals are unchanged: **two hundred and thirty-one on the eighth of Sazur; two hundred and forty-nine in fifty-four rooms in eleven streets on the sixteenth and seventeenth of last month; two hundred and fifty-two in fifty-five rooms in eleven streets on the thirty-first of that month and the first of this one, thirty-three rooms on no list, twenty-one short; two and four a head a week, five hundred and fifty-four pounds and forty, forty-three pounds and two for the eighteen — and the prose still never shows the multiplication, because the two totals are inconsistent at the shilling and the inconsistency is inherited and is not repaired.** **A rates authority asked for a schedule of rooms by street and dwelling and has not got one and is not going to get one.** A man who lets four rooms behind a boatyard and has paid nobody anything for nine years is **still on no list at all**, and that has not been fixed and was not fixed by anybody.

27. **The school bill, paid, and not cheapened.** **Nine children out of the school until the spring intake. Two of the nine are lost for good** and remain lost, because the mother of two of them is a named person on an instrument of the twenty-first of October. **Seven come back, because on the fifteenth of December the intake opened on a Continuity referral signed in Lena Marr's own hand — the same signature, in the same minute, that puts the levy on her — and naming her as the responsible person for a temporary accommodation household** — and a referral asks for a place, and a place needs a person, and a person is rateable, and that is the same signature that puts the levy on her. **No third of the nine was quietly regained and none was cheaply lost.** The woman at the door of the Board school **has not been the door-check, still is not, and is right**, and has not been thanked and did not ask.

28. **The death record, reopened, and the widow told, and the burial.** The complete file is **nine boxes** and came out of the store on **Saturday the thirteenth at about eleven**, on the second day after the order was opened for investigation on the Friday the twelfth, past about forty people in a Crown Heights corridor. The card: *VENN, J. — pump crew, 2nd shift — relocation due, pending. One loss recorded.* **Cause of death, on reopening: fatality, second-stage main, pump house, lower level. Not entered in the original entry.** The original entry's contributory line, **in the same hand as the report**: *the frontage is at all times a service access and not a public route.* **Next of kin, blank for six years, filled from a department's card index in about four seconds: WIFE, living, address recorded** — and a clerk read a card index and said nothing and is not going to be thanked. **Disposal: a ground named on the authority's return, a plot numbered and not on the district's map, in a ground whose register for that district was not kept after the closure; the return is six words long and it has a plot on it.** **Lena Marr took the number to the woman on Sunday the fourteenth, in a taxi, at nine pounds of her own money, with Doreen Sankey on the step, in about forty minutes, and did not make her ask one question and did not say sorry.** **The woman of fifty-four who mends other people's clothes is alive, is unnamed on the page, and has been told where her husband is buried.** The widow's whole speech in the volume is: *Nobody has brought me anything in six years. Not even a bill.*

29. **Mara Quill's instruments and her standing, and the carbon that closes the volume.** **Her review on the third of December** found that she worked the file off her own hours for six years in a records alcove without telling her review panel, and that **the fourth set of initials in the annex box of the intake routing slip of the sixth year back** was a matter she ought to have declared and did not; she read the finding out in a corridor to about nine people; **she lost preferred access, has a two-year note, and appealed in one line on the fourth and it is not stayed.** **The refusal of the fourth of December** — no schedule of persons — is on the file in her own hand with her own date, and says that a nil return is capable of being read as a representation that no persons are resident there, **that she does not know that no persons are resident there, and that she knows the schedule is short by twenty-one and thirty-three rooms are on no list at all.** **The formal objection and public safety appeal, nineteen paragraphs, in her hand, lodged at about ten past three on the fifteenth**, with **three attachments**: the order complete; **the carbon copy of a relocation schedule, copied off an older survey book and not off a district register, listing four routes, three of them in the river core, and the fourth naming a destination that is real, dated, unplaceable and about an hour and twenty minutes from Alder Quay on an entirely ordinary road, with four lines in her hand on the back in biro**; and the community and memorial register. **There is one mark in the margin of that carbon and there is one mark only, in a hand, and it is a question about the destination and not about the order, and it is not one of the two questions on the other carbon, and it is not a third pair — a pair is two marks in two hands on one page and this is one mark in one hand.**

30. **The clue chain, carried and not resolved, and is not Soren.** **Two documents carry a pair of marks, not three.** Three references exist and are references and not pairs: the Crown Heights repository consultation reference against *p.9 withdrawn*, the one on the copy register, and the one on the Filewell index. **The twenty-first's Registry certification is drawn where the two confirmed marks are pressed, a difference in method and not in age.** Kest has not matched the strings. **Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–60.** **The copied tick, the copied turn sheet, and the carbon relocation schedule are three separate things and nobody in this batch joined any of them to any other, and Milo Rook wrote the reason down twice: joining them would be him deciding that they are the same, and a man who decides two things are the same in a book is a man who has started making the case instead of the record.** None of them touches the Quieting, the First Atlas, or the One Survey.

31. **Atlas residue, once in ten chapters, in six words.** Chapter 57, at about four o'clock in the afternoon on the seventh of December, on the reach, written up at 21.10 the same night: ***A road is kept, not made.*** It describes a piece of ground and **does not say who keeps one**; he limits it on the facing page before acting and does not take the book near the works compound on the ninth. **Seven dated pairs are now in the book — six days, nine days, three days, three days, two days, three days, three days — and he writes all seven down, joins none of them to any other, does not collapse the two threes he writes on the fifteenth into the threes already in the book, and does not write down what he thinks seven of a thing means; an eighth, of a single day, was looked at on the tenth and is not written.** The **Lark token was cold on the seventeenth of November, and on the fourteenth and fifteenth of December, and did not come out; it was warm once, on the seventh, for about four minutes, on the reach, and he did not use it, and the reason is on the facing page and is the whole of the batch.**

32. **The final image, and it is the outline's.** **Milo Rook in the route room of the Civic Atlas Office, the second of the two tables under the north window, at about half past four on the afternoon of Monday the fifteenth of December, with the Field Book open at a page with nine lines gone clean out of it in his own hand** — *Out the door. Left. Eleven steps to the hall, the fourth one loose, take the second stair not the first… and it is nine minutes and I have done it since I was nineteen* — **and three chalk arrows: outside his own building at about five feet, dated the fourteenth; inside his own front door at about four feet, dated the twenty-eighth of November; and one on the wall of the second-floor landing with no date on it, and nobody had ever asked who put that one there, and nobody was going to.** **He asked at about ten past five and was refused twice by people who had no reason to refuse him, and the blank is still blank, and he cannot tell whether the missing line is a magical cost, a bad memory, or simply a page that needs another person's mark.** **Dilley Farr: I have watched three men in this room lose a route in eleven years… and I have signed no deletion in this room for six weeks and the reason is not principle, it is that there has been nothing in front of me worth signing a deletion for. Because of you.** **And across the city, at about ten past three, Advocate Quill puts a parcel on a counter: nineteen paragraphs in her own hand, and the attachment that is the next volume's question** — the carbon relocation schedule with four lines in her hand on the back, *I am not saying what it means. I am saying that a copy of an old survey book names a place this city cannot point at, and that it was in the order, and that nobody has produced it in six years, including me, and I have been working off my own hours on this file for six years and I have read nine boxes in one afternoon… a document in a file is a thing somebody can produce, and I have read it, and I would rather be the person who put it there.* **The volume closes with a real protected route, a damaged official record, and a question that has moved from Milo's private grief to the public maintenance of Alderquay.**

33. **No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue, and the relationship's whole vocabulary is work, disclosure, disagreement in front of danger and repair.** He is the **protector of a route he cannot claim** and **he must not end Volume 1 as its author**, and the closing sentence of his last entry says so: *I am the man who wrote the columns and did the arithmetic and is not the author, and is not the holder, and is not the clerk, and is not the custodian, and has been in a corridor for ninety minutes this afternoon while a woman of about thirty-four put her name in a box in a hall. That is the whole of what I did in three months, and I have written it down, and I am not going to be able to add a line to it, and I am not going to want to.* **She is not going to write his return route for him in Volume 1 and it was never hers to write, and he has not asked; the promise of the twenty-ninth of Sazur stands and is hers and does not get wider; and neither of them has said anything about how either of them feels, and neither is going to.** What she said on the fifteenth, in a yard of about eleven, and about nine people looked at him and he looked at the ground: *on the twenty-ninth of Sazur a man promised me that nothing of his bearing on this file would reach me in writing with a date on it, and I held that for forty-four days and then I made him give away the design instead, and he gave it away in about nine seconds, and he did not complain, and that is the most decent thing anybody has done for me in six years and I have not said so to him and I am not going to.*

34. **New supporting cast, registered in `bible/terminology.md`:** **Ola Petrossian** is now a district character rather than a cameo — she gave him the ninth route on a card and satisfied the fourth condition of the eighth crossing; **Cass Ardery** has been promoted from a named instrument to a working officer who withdraws his own classification, names the condition, refuses the word *triad*, and asks to be second-in-command on the investigation of his own mistake; **the man of forty-nine on the reach** who got his hand up a fifth of a second before a rail went over and says he did not decide to; and **the woman of about seventy from Wragg Street**, who said the word at a north door on the nineteenth and has her three lines on the return document. **No new grammar, faction, antagonist, or final enemy has been introduced. Sel Iri does not appear. Halden Vey appears once, in a signature block, on paper.**

35. **Volume 01 is complete.** Sixty planned chapters, six written batches, and the resolution in Chapters 58–60 **replaces** the four-Thursday instrument rather than extending it — the instrument expired at noon on Friday the fourteenth of November and is not mentioned, renewed, converted, or minuted again anywhere in Chapters 51–60. **Nothing was won that cannot be taken back, and every item in the resolution is temporary, marked contested, or a signature somebody signed on purpose.** The immediate promise is answered: Bellwether's danger is contained and named, the residents have a route and legal standing, the order cannot be treated as routine procedure, and the question has moved from one man's private grief to the public maintenance of a city.

## What Chapters 61–70 fixed on the page, in the same form as CR-013

Each item below is now prose rather than plan, and each is load-bearing in a way a later batch could break without intending to. **None of them changes a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder, or the planned ending. Every figure here is quotable from a chapter in `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0061.md` … `chapter-0070.md`, and this list was written from the manuscript and not from memory.** Chapters 61–70 are **the first ten chapters of Volume 02**, they open the volume and do not close it, and they inherit the Chapters 51–60 list above whole.

1. **The disappearance, and its arithmetic, and the arithmetic is two numbers and not one.** Car 214, Line 3, the 07:52 off Crown Heights; due Alder Quay interchange **08.06**, water stairs **08.19**, shed road at Alder Quay depot **08.31**. **Thirty-one passengers on the manifest, a conductor, and a driver, and thirty-three people on the car.** The fare register counts **31 fares, the conductor's pass and the driver's pass** and knows no names; the conductor's **Nessa Bray, thirty-eight, sixteen years**; the driver **Corin Mabey, fifty-one, twenty-nine years**. **Thirty-one and thirty-three are two different numbers and both are correct and both are on the page, and nobody may collapse them.** The yard notices at **08.41**; the notice the authority put up outside the depot at **ten past ten** says, in its own words, that the car **was not late**.

2. **No live map is wrong, and the amendment is public, dated, and attributed.** The running diagram, the control screen, the Civic Atlas Office's quayside public sheet, the district's own street index and five route readers agree with one another and with the amendment. **Service amendment 12/44, effective Monday the thirteenth of October, printed in the amendment list any competent person may read and did not read:** length between Alder Quay interchange and the water stairs, **approximately 600 yards**, comprising the down loop and the quay service access; *reserved, no booked service*; to be shown as a maintenance length only; **standing instruction of the office of the Prefect, ref. 11/D/6**; requested by Permanent Way quay section. **The *requested by* line and the *instructing officer* line are both blank, and the justification box does not contain a reason — it contains the filing number PW/Q/4.** **The number 12/44 is a series and not a date, and the prose says so: *twelve of forty-four, the twelfth amendment of the forty-fourth year of the running diagram, and there is nowhere on that form to put a name, and a number that is not a date is a number that does not have to be looked up.*** The sheet came off the public map in the same week, in a margin note, in the ordinary way.

3. **The instrument that made the map wrong, and the instrument that cannot see the place.** **The box registers a car at the terminus and the interchange and nothing else, and the shed is a loop with a flag and not a box**, so the last entry on the roll is **08.06 at the interchange** and there is no return entry and there never will be. The yard's running sheet is filled in the night before from the timetable, so its left-hand columns are full and its right-hand columns are blank. The control screen is drawn from the running diagram and its arithmetic joins the interchange straight to the water stairs in **four minutes and eleven seconds** and shows the car in on time. **A count on that length was taken by an automatic function, because the length is not on the diagram, so there was no box in it, so the only instrument that could say whether a car was on that length did not know the length existed. That is not a fault. There is nothing to fault. There is a hole in the diagram and the box went in the hole.** The automatic *fare* register is a different machine from the box and it counts and does not name.

4. **The bridge sentence, in two halves, from two working people, nine hundred yards apart, on three consecutive days, and it is not a theme and it is not Milo's.** **Dilley Farr, route room, thirty-one years, the morning of the seventeenth: *There is no such thing as a map that is out of date. There is a map, and there is a list, and the list gets changed by somebody with a form, and after that the map is right and the ground is wrong… It takes an afternoon. Nobody has to be in a room and nobody has to want anything.*** And then, with his back to the atlases: **look for the work, not the road — if a thing in this city has been kept in one piece for a hundred and forty years it is because about nine men were paid to walk it, and no amount of looking under a map will tell you anything about it, because the map is the part that gets reprinted.** **Cyril Sale, works clerk, forty-six, at his own desk the same morning: *A road is not a line. A road is eleven men and a hundred yards of drain and a sheet of paper a foreman carries out of a hut… the sheet is the only thing in this building that knows where anybody has been, and it is a record of work and it does not have a box on it that wants to know why.*** **And Amos Trench, index clerk, sixty-three, the next afternoon, in a basement: a school must not hold a copy of another body's maintenance record, because a maintenance record is the only record in this country that is true by having been done, and a copy of one is a claim that the work was done somewhere else.** Milo writes the join down **once**, in his own book, on the seventeenth, and refuses to make it in a yard, and says he has asked a clerk in an office to say it for him and the clerk has not agreed and he is not going to lean on him.

5. **The copy, the slip, and the six weeks that made the amendment self-justifying.** A routing slip about four inches by six inches: *From: quay section, PW. To: timetable division. Purpose: **for index.** Re: PW/Q/4 (3rd edn.) + gang sheet, wk. Dispatched: 10 Oct. Received: 10 Oct.* **A man telephoned the quay section on Wednesday the eighth of October and asked for a schedule for index, and the clerk sent the third edition down with the gang sheet's duplicate on the Friday the tenth.** The **third** edition of the quay section maintenance schedule is dated **the eleventh of September** and its **back-length line is empty**. The **fourth** is dated **the twenty-second of November** and its back-length line is full, with a margin note in the clerk's hand: ***Length added to sched. per amd. 12/44. C.S.*** **So the document that justified a change of six hundred yards of a city's map did not contain the thing it was justifying, and six weeks later it did, and nobody in between was asked to remember which of those two facts was true, and nobody lied at any point.**

6. **The gang, its form, its days, and its twelve-year refusal.** The quay section gang is **eleven men**, on the back length on **Mondays and Thursdays at about half past seven**, and the foreman is **Roy Pryce, fifty-four, thirty-one years, on four lengths**. The weekly sheet is **one side, carbon-backed, six columns — date, length, men, hours, work done, condition, signature — and there is no column for anybody's opinion, and that is not an omission.** The duplicate goes in a rack by month at the quay section office and in a tin at the end of the month. **A record of work is not a record of a reason, and no batch may make the gang's sheet carry a person.**

7. **Thread Hall, as a room, and a refusal that is correct.** The Survey School's repository beneath Crown Heights: a stair of **thirty-four** steps, **nine desks, nine readers**, a plan chest, a window that does not open. **About forty people in the city call it Thread Hall; the school calls it the Repository; it has not been called the second of those on any letterhead since before the war.** **Amos Trench, sixty-three, forty-one years at the index desk** refuses twice and is right twice: the back length is not in the plan chest and never was, and a route origin sheet is a maintenance record and not a survey record; and **nobody in this city can be asked for that record in December**, because the reporting officer on the open investigation must be a person other than any person named in the Prefect's letter or in the order, and the department is not going to name one. **Milo Rook does not ask Mara Quill, and the reason is on the page twice: *I did not ask her because she would have done it.***

8. **Milo's failure, and it is his, and the witness was in the room.** At about twenty-five past two on the seventeenth, in a room of nine witnesses, with his hand on a rolled plan, **there is no edge anywhere, because the negative space in that room is made of people**; and he says out loud, *there is a road under this, I am looking for where it went*; and he tells a school he telephoned on the Tuesday night that **a document is in this building**, before he has asked whether the school is the origin or a reader of it. **Bettina Sallow, fifty-four, four years on a hand index at the ninth desk on the left, writes it down on the back of a finding aid because that is her work**, and asks him one question — *have you told them* — and does not raise her voice. On the stairs at ten to four she gives him the batch's only usable fact about an index: **eleven route origin sheets in the withdrawal file, nine of them in that building, two not, and an index is a description of what is here and not a description of what is not here.** He is not in that room again.

9. **The manifest, its two halves, and the column that could only be filled in by a person who was there.** The working manifest goes out with the conductor; the depot keeps the carbon. **Six columns — time on, stop boarded, fares, alighted, run no, conductor.** **Thirty-one names in the conductor's hand; the *Alighted* column empty on all thirty-one lines because the car has not finished; and the line at the foot where the conductor signs is not signed and is the only part of the sheet anybody in the authority's building has looked at twice.** **There is no document in the authority, the district or the Registry that has a box for where a car got off, and there never has been, and the reason is that a car has always been somewhere that somebody is responsible for.** The column was filled in on the length on the Monday morning, one at a time, by Nessa Bray, and it reads **twelve at the Alder Quay interchange, nine at the water stairs, ten at Crown Heights**, and the line at the foot is signed **N. Bray** against **16.36**, five minutes before the car came in, **and nobody has been able to account for that arithmetic and it is the only thing in eight days that has been right twice.**

10. **The unaccounted-for sheet, and the reason its second column is empty.** A sheet of accountancy paper about two feet long, **NAME and LAST SEEN**, on a trestle table under a brick, in use since the Saturday before the sixth of December, with Rue's rule at the foot: *If a place is written in the right-hand column, this sheet is a record of where a person was. If it is left out, this sheet is a list of names, and anybody can take a name off it and nothing follows the name.* **The right-hand column is about an inch and a half deep and it is empty, and the reason is in the Field Book and is the whole case: *A reserved length is not a place. That is what a service amendment says when it is being honest with itself, and 12/44 was honest.*** **The thirty-one names were read out once in a yard in the rain and nobody wrote them down**, on Hettie Brann's reason — the list belongs in a public building, in a drawer, in a depot office. **About nine people wrote one name at home in their own kitchen and nobody collected those.**

11. **The first family, the four sentences, and the sheet on the fridge.** **Paul Nye, thirty-eight, and Ellis Nye, eleven, the school route, both on the manifest, and their aunt Maggie Nye, sixty-six.** She wants the boy's name on the yard sheet with a time and a place and is refused in four seconds. **Four sentences are said at eight o'clock in a kitchen in Boardman Street, standing up by the sink, with nobody from the district there but Rue**, and the substance of the third and fourth is that a name with a place beside it is a record that survives the people who made it, and that **she has written about eleven of these sheets in three months and every one of them has gone somewhere, and a sheet that has gone is not a sheet that has been thrown away, it is a sheet that somebody will find in about four years in a drawer they have been told to look in.** Then the thing that is the whole of the day: **Maggie Nye is given a sheet of accountancy paper and writes both names on it in her own hand in her own kitchen with a date, and puts it on the fridge under a magnet off a calendar from a shop on Bridge Street**, and tells Rue not to know where it is. **It is the only record of car 214 in the city and it is the worst record in the city and nobody is going to see it.**

12. **Mara Quill's unauthorised copy, its exact cost, and what it says.** She reads the code's one line twice and does not read it a third time, then asks a clerk to bring her the page, and **copies four lines and a continuation sheet with a pencil in ninety seconds at a table in a records alcove with the door open.** A man of about forty-five in Survey Enforcement walks past, looks at the page, keeps walking, and **she does not follow him, for a reason she has written down and does not think is creditable.** The page: *Withdrawn from running diagram: 13 Oct, service amendment 12/44, under standing instruction 11/D/6. Requesting body: PW quay section. Route custody after withdrawal: **nil.*** The continuation sheet, in a different hand: *Instruments, routes, or objects recorded on the above at the time of withdrawal: **one.*** *1. Route token, brass, hand-stamped **LARK ST.**, no number. Issue not in current series. Held by: **ROOK.** Authority for issue: [no entry]. Returned: [no entry].* **This is the disclosure of milestone 2's second half and it is a custody column, not a confession: he has not told her, she has not asked, and she has not used it and is not going to.** **The hearing on the Bellwether order is still not set and the reporting officer must still be a person other than any person named in the Prefect's letter or in the order, and this batch does not set the hearing and does not name one.**

13. **Three documents carrying one standing-instruction number, and what Mara Quill says about three.** The works' notice of the sixteenth of November carries **11/D/6**; service amendment 12/44 carries **11/D/6**; **the district continuity order of six years back cites a standing instruction of the office of the Prefect and does not give the number on the face of the order**, and her copy is the only thing in the world that supplies it. **In the yard she says: *That is three documents carrying one reference number. It is three documents. It is not a pattern and I am not going to call it one this week, and when somebody comes to me and says advocate, three documents, I am going to say three documents, and if I am still saying that in a year then I will believe it.*** **A count of three is a count of three and no later batch may convert it into a pattern, a fourth instance, or a pair.**

14. **Saltmarket Row, as a place, and what it preserves.** A hundred and ten yards off Cardew Street between a cold store that used to be a fruit warehouse and a net-loft shuttered since before the war; **eleven pitches down the north side, nine brick and two a hoarding**; the wholesale fish and vegetable market of the district **until nineteen years ago**; **not a street, not on the street name index, and it cannot be given an address**; **held up by about eleven habits and about four people, of which five were identified in about four hours and the other six are somebody else's Saturday.** The five: a coffee man of about sixty who puts his hand on the corner of the same wall at half past five every day in his own time; a fishmonger of about forty-five who calls the lot prices from a doorway with no fish; six flat irons on a rail in an order, put there by somebody Milo is **not allowed to make a map of** and writes as *irons*; a man of about forty who sweeps the Row badly at about six in the morning; and the scale. **This is the first thing in Volume 02 that preserves people as practices, and it preserves what they did and not who they were.**

15. **The Keepsake reading, and it is the first grammar Milo identifies by name.** Cue: a wet stone floor with fish scales, cut herbs and coffee at half past five, **and there has been no coffee sold within a hundred and ten yards of that Row since the year before the war, and the coffee is in it, and the coffee is the part that is slightly wrong.** Service: the weights, the sets, and a woman who tells the truth about a number. **Demand: an actual part of the routine, and the only part available to a surveyor with a chain in his pocket is holding the stone while somebody zeroes the beam.** Failure: **the scale takes the name, not the weight.** A beam scale with a brass column and a sliding poise, **eleven sets of brass weights on a board with a hook for each and no mark of ownership and no name on the board**, kept for **thirty-one years by Ivy Marsden, seventy-four**, who weighs anything, charges nothing, writes nothing down, **will not sell it and cannot say why**, and who says one word when a man does not put a weight on it: *Right*. **It is a first identification and not a stabilisation: he stabilises nothing in this batch and his attempt at the new ability makes the situation worse when he tries it.**

16. **His second failure, and the sentence that turns the batch.** At about ten past twelve on the twentieth, at the south boundary, **there is nothing to hold.** *A seam is a hole where two accounts of a place do not agree. A length of tram route that has come off the running diagram is not a hole. It is a thing. It has a gang and a number and an inspection interval and a wall at the south end and a rack and a tin and a foreman of thirty-one years, and it is the best-kept thing in this district, and there is nothing in it that is in the wrong and nothing in it that is pressing and nothing in it that wants closing. What he had spent four days learning to do is a thing you do to a wound, and this is not a wound, this is a length that nine men keep.* And the correction, from a mechanic of sixty-one at the top of a market that closed nineteen years ago: **A road is not a line. That is all of it. A road is a place and a crew keeps a road. A gap is a place where a road stopped being a place and a crew does not keep a gap. And that length out there is not a road and it is not a gap and that is why it has got a car on it. It is the third thing and nobody has a word for the third thing because nobody has ever needed one.**

17. **Milo's first stable field map, and what makes it stable.** Three feet of accountancy paper, made on the twentieth between about one and five. **The frame, the heading and the two columns are his; every line with anything in it is in somebody else's hand with a date against it; and the corner says *content not mine, do not correct it, ask the person whose hand it is.*** The rule at the foot is in his hand: *This sheet does not say the Row is a street and it does not say the length is a road. It says who does what and on which day. If any of them stops, cross the line out and date it, and do not take the line off, and do not ask anybody to start again.* **It is stable because it will still be right next week if the Row and the gang change on Tuesday, and it is not a claim on the place, and it is a method change and not a power.**

18. **Atlas residue, once in ten chapters, in five words, and the new pair.** Saturday the twentieth, about ten past five in the evening, at the top of the Row, in the field, in his own hand: ***A trade outlives its pitch.*** The limit on the facing page before he does anything else with it: it does not say whose pitch, it does not say what happens when a trade stops, and **he is not going to find out by stopping one.** The fifteenth to the twentieth is **five days**, the eighth pair in the book. **The Field Book now carries nine dated pairs — six days, nine days, three, three, two, three, three, five and eight — and a tenth interval of a single day from the tenth of December that was looked at and not written and stays looked at.** Four threes, one six, one nine, one two, one five and one eight, **and he joins none of them to any other and does not write down what he thinks nine of a thing means.** **The Lark token was cold on the sixteenth, seventeenth, nineteenth, twentieth, twenty-first, twenty-second, twenty-third and twenty-seventh — eight of the thirteen days of the batch — was not taken out on any of the other five, and the seventh of December is still the last time it was anything else.**

19. **The reversal, in two mouths, from both ends, and it is a route and not a room.** **Jo Lask, conductor of thirty, Sunday the twenty-first at about seven:** *A tram is not a thing. A tram is a sequence with somebody on it. The list is no use whatever on its own — it is a piece of paper. What holds it is a person saying the next one out loud… **Your car is not lost.** It is going round.* And the answer to the question everybody is actually asking: ***Nothing is holding it out of here. It is being out of here.*** *A car in a street is a car in the present. A car in a length — a length with a gang and a number and a wall at the south end and a market that is not there on the other side of it — is a car somewhere that is not the present, because a length is not a street and a street is the present. **The thing that is keeping your car out of this city is the same thing that is keeping the coffee smell on that Row. The length is different, and different is a kind of place, and your car is safe for as long as nobody makes it the same.*** **Nessa Bray, from inside it, Monday the twenty-second:** *I have been on a car for five days and I have not been frightened of anything until that — and then at about ten past twelve somebody said the word on that length and it was not me, and it was a man's voice, and it came from the north end.* **And why it cannot be forced, in four items and one prohibition:** you can stand still, go in, say the word at the turns, and come out; **you cannot stop a sequence and you cannot seal a length, because there is no edge on it to seal and there is no door, and if you put a thing across it you have made a pinned thing with thirty-three people in it, and I have read what your district did to a reach in December and I would like to go home.**

20. **The four turns, named, and the check, used, and not re-decided.** The north end signal (which is **not a turn and is a box and works by itself at two intervals an hour since the sixth of June six years ago**); the down loop; the wall at the south end, which is the boundary of the quay compound and the back wall of the last pitch on Saltmarket Row; and the water stairs end with its buffer. **The word is *clear*, and it is said by a person and waited for, and it is Tomas Rusk's instrument of November — do not count, wait until it stops, and then say it — and nobody in this batch hedges it, re-argues it, or decides it again.** **A conductor's word said by two people is not half as good as one but it is not nothing**, and that is on the page.

21. **The ninth crossing, dated, placed, costed, and read out.** **Monday the twenty-second of December, in at 11.06 and out at 11.41, thirty-five minutes inside, from the north end of the length, with the quay section gang of eleven men and a foreman.** Count held by **Pell Harrow with a counter and no words for thirty-five minutes**; walked in and out by **Doreen Sankey**; **twelve minutes stated**; **all four conditions of October satisfied**; the fourth condition at **a quarter to eight by Ola Petrossian, who was not asked.** Read out in a yard at about ten past twelve to about nineteen people with Iona Sen's tally, **which now has nine lines on it**, and it is the seventh time she has made him say the number, and he says it. **All four of the conditions are the same four words they have been since October: a person walks him in and out and they are not him; a count held by a person and not by a box; the time stated before he goes; and the clinic in the morning.** **Milo Rook is across nine times in total: eight in Volume 01 and this one.**

22. **The cost, and the two stages are still two costs, and the first-stage list does not move.** The remembered-route list **stands at ten, the original eight intact, none removed, all ten recoverable when marked, and all ten marked by somebody who is not him — and the ninth crossing did not take one**, and he writes that down twice and says he does not know why and will not decide, because *I have gone eleven weeks expecting to lose one every time and nothing has come off the list, and I do not want to be a man who has got lucky and calls it a method.* **He has never lost one; the state layer's earlier version of this item said he had lost one at every crossing, and that was arithmetically impossible against the ledger it sat beside, and the sentence in Chapter 68 has been rebuilt rather than the ledger touched.** **The written line home is the thirty-fifth day of it on the twenty-eighth of December and is not coming back.** It was marked three times in three hands with a date against each — **a nurse of twenty-three on a card on the twenty-fourth, a woman who keeps a round on a card on the twenty-fifth, and a boy of seventeen with chalk on a second-floor landing on the twenty-sixth, undated** — **and it is still blank, and on the evening of the twenty-seventh at 21.40 he wrote the only sentence in two hundred and thirty-odd entries that he has ever written in the imperative about himself: *The line home is not coming back. Write it down and stop asking.*** **That sentence is thirteen words and the chapter now says thirteen; the Field Book's later reference to it quotes the imperative half only, which is the second sentence of the two.** **Persistence inside the volume is established here and is not yet the deeper personal blank of a witness-led restoration: no face, no place, no person and no name has dropped out of his autobiographical route.**

23. **The Stage 2 cost, and it is exactly two things, and neither is a person or a place.** **A sound**: on the twenty-second, at about four seconds into a tram gong on the length, he could not call up the pitch of the third gong of a Line 3 morning car, **and he needed it**, because a conductor who has heard a person say *clear* answers and he could not tell whether she had; he waited until it stopped and then said it, and the instrument he used instead was nine words in grease pencil in a foreman's hand. **A taste**: on the twenty-third, at about four in the afternoon, the tea he had made at five that morning, which he did not need. He has written them in the order they happened and **not in the order they frighten him, and he does not know whether that is one thing or two, and has decided nothing.** **The first personal blank, a seam-scar, a record blank and an authorial blank appear nowhere in Chapters 61–70, not even as an unexplained symptom, and no later batch may show one before its stage.**

24. **Mara Quill's return route, which is milestone 2, and it is a working instrument.** Sunday the twenty-first at about twenty past eleven, in a porch, in the rain, in about forty minutes, **on a sheet of accountancy paper in biro with a heading, a date, an *out* and a *back*.** He does not ask and she does not offer. Her stated reason, in her own mouth: ***I am going to write a route, and I am not going to do it kindly… This is not a kindness. There is no instrument in this city that says a man came back. A man who cannot find the way he came is a man who has to be produced from somewhere afterwards, and if the only person who can produce him is his own memory then in about four months a department will produce him instead, out of a filing system, and it will not be the same man.*** **It had two errors in it and a woman who keeps a round found both in about ninety seconds** — the down loop is on the left going out and on the right coming back, and the signal is not a turn and is not a person and is not to be written as one on a sheet in this district. **Three hands on the card: her route on the front, Rue's two corrections on the back, and his date and version number, because he is the only one of the three who writes dates.** **Four copies, in four places, and the fourth is one she wrote down on the back and did not tell him about.** It was tested against not working: Rue walked him from the yard gate to his own front door **without letting him look at the paper, on purpose, at his request and on one condition**, and it took nineteen minutes and he could not have told you which way the chandler's gate was. ***You can get home off that card. And you cannot get home off yourself.*** **Nothing romantic happens in this batch and nothing is going to. The promise of the twenty-ninth of Sazur stands, is hers, and does not get wider.**

25. **The car comes back, and the time is right twice.** **At 16.41 on Monday the twenty-second, car 214 comes off the back length, out through the north end, on the present's rails, under its own power, with a woman of thirty-eight on the step, and runs its whole round and comes into Alder Quay depot on the shed road.** The yard man with the flag, **Fenwick, sixty-three**, writes the time in his book and does not look inside the car and has never looked inside one and is not going to be asked to. **The control screen said 16.41 and the screen had been right and Percy Doak said *there she is* and was not being sarcastic.** All thirty-three people got off. **There is no document anywhere in the authority, the Atlas Office or the Registry with a box for where a car got off, and there never has been.**

26. **The record that cannot be recreated, and the reason, and it is the fifteenth of December's own trap arriving from a different door.** The gang's weekly sheet for the week of the twenty-first records the ninth crossing exactly: **one side, carbon-backed, eleven men, *07.30, fifty minutes; and 11.06, thirty-five minutes*, work done *walked, both turns, boundary at wall checked*, condition *serviceable*, signature **R. PRYCE**, and in the hours column the reason in nine words — *A car is on the length. The gang is the crew on the length.*** **It has no column for the thirty-one, and the foreman refused to write one and was right, and he has said he will be asked about it in about four months by a man in an office and will have to say yes, and that he has never been asked to be that before and does not intend to refuse.** And the mechanism, from a works clerk of forty-six in nine words: **there is no form.** A running sheet's fault box would say *ran through a length not on the diagram*, and **not on the diagram is not a place, and a length that is not a place is not something a car can be held on**; and a route in the Atlas is a name, **and a name in the Atlas is entered against a rateable place, and a maintenance length is not a building and does not have a street and cannot be given one — which is the fifteenth of December, which is the box somebody put *no rateable place* in.** **So: the passage happened, and the record of the passage is a line on a sheet about a drain, and the city cannot show anybody that it happened, and in about four months a department can say that no passage occurred and the district will not be able to prove it, and there is no appeal from that and no hearing would help.**

27. **The loss is a document and not a person, and the batch says it twice, in two mouths, in that order.** **Mara Quill, first: *Nobody died.*** Thirty-one passengers, a conductor of thirty-eight and a driver of fifty-one, and every one of them alive, and the conductor went to bed in her own bed at ten past three. **And Hettie Brann, second: *a document is the only thing in this city that bites*** — *I have watched this city eat four people in six years over a document, and it was a piece of paper in a drawer… there is not one now… in about four years that rack will be cleared, because racks are cleared, and when it is cleared the thing will not have happened. **And it will be done again. Not by a villain. By a box. And the next one will not be a tram. The next one will be something with children in it.*** Nobody wrote that down and she is not going to be the woman who has it on a form. **A document is the only thing in this city that can be used against anybody, and this batch is the first in which the district has won something and produced no document at all.**

28. **The second tin, and the honest limit on it.** **Friday the twenty-sixth**, at the quay section office, **Pell Harrow, seventeen, asks for the gang's weekly duplicate to go in a second tin in a different building in a room where four people work who do not know what a quay section gang is**: *A thing that exists in one place is a thing that has not happened yet.* **Roy Pryce says no for about nine minutes and yes in about four seconds — *unless it goes somewhere a man cannot get at it alone* — and the second tin is on the bottom shelf of the plan room of the Civic Atlas Office, under the repair schedule, and Dilley Farr signed for it the same afternoon and did not ask what was in it and has said since that he read the lid twice.** **The sheet he is talking about is the gang's weekly sheet for the week of the twenty-first, which the gang wrote on Thursday the twenty-fifth and which went into the rack on the Friday the twenty-sixth; the next walk is a Thursday in the following week and is on a sheet of its own, and the chapter does not name that date.** **And the limit, in his own mouth, which is the batch's resolution and must not be softened: *That is two copies of a record of work, and two copies is not a record. A record is a thing a department can produce against you and there is no department in this city that is going to be stopped by two tins.***

29. **The hearing, the reporting officer, and the amendment nobody can put in a room.** **The hearing on the Bellwether order is still not set sixteen days after the suspension, the reporting officer is still not named, and the reporting officer must still be a person other than any person named in the Prefect's letter or in the order.** **Cass Ardery, about forty-four, second-in-command on an investigation of a thing he classified, is not the reporting officer and is not going to be, and his stated reason is not a rule: there is no rule in this department that says a man who made a mistake cannot report on it, and not one rule that says he can, and that silence is the reason.** He notices a document in Advocate Quill's bag on about the sixth of December, **does not ask, and asks for that to be minuted**; she tells him in front of nine people that if he had asked she would have taken it out. **A tram service amendment is not a continuity order, it was made by a different body under the same standing instruction, and there is no procedure in this Registry or anybody else's that puts the two in a room — and Advocate Quill's copy is the only document in existence that does, and she is the person holding it, and she has a two-year note and no access.**

30. **The Lark Street grievance is named once, refused once, and is not resolved and not softened.** On the twenty-eighth, in a corridor, somebody says *and the Lark Street business*, and **Rue says no first, before anybody else could**, and then, to Milo Rook, in about nine words: *In August you wrote a rule at the foot of a sheet and gave it away in a porch. In December you went into six hundred yards of tram route because a mechanic said a gang was the crew on a length. Those are the same decision twice and I have watched you make it twice and I have not said anything about it and I am not going to, and I would like it written down that I have not said anything about it.* **Nobody wrote it down and about four people in that corridor noticed that nobody had.** The grievance is not explained, not resolved, and does not stop being the thing it was on the twenty-ninth of Sazur.

31. **The next-volume question, planted and not answered, and the office named with nobody behind it.** **Four pages dated the twenty-second of December, issued under the office of the First Surveyor, headed SEASONAL SEAM AND GREYWAKE OUTLOOK, for the season from the second of January to the fourteenth of April, forecasting eleven weather-driven seam events** — the first in the last week of January, four in the river core, one in the Outer Reach, the rest unallocated — and the line that is the reason the page exists: *seam events are expected to be preceded by weather, not followed by it. Rain and wind load a route; a loaded route fails at a boundary; the boundary is a place somebody has been using. **Weather does not make a seam. Weather tells you where the one that is already there is going to open.*** And under *district stability, entered by the office and not amended by any district*: river core, recorded, not stable; Crown Heights, recorded, not stable; South Salt, recorded, not stable; **Mireward, nil** — *no maintained seam, no recorded loss, no resident witness, no schedule. **Entered as stable.*** **The office of the First Surveyor is a working desk in an instrument, it has a name on four pages, it signed no name, there is no date anywhere in the document for the thing it is standing on, and this batch may name the office and may not produce a person who answers for it. What happened at the first Quieting is not touched, is not dated, and is not explained, and no batch may supply a date for it.**

32. **The clue chain, carried, not advanced, and the rule about columns.** **The copied tick in a fourth column in six houses in four streets, the copied turn sheet with a contractor's head on it, and the carbon relocation schedule appear nowhere in Chapters 61–70 as a found document, and are joined to nothing.** One new document was found — **a gang's own weekly sheet, and a routing slip about four inches by six inches with *for index* in the purpose box** — and it is **not** a fourth instance of anything. **Milo Rook writes on the page, twice, that he is not putting it in a column with anything, and the reason is the same bad reason as in November: a column is a case, and he has made one twice in eleven weeks and both times the column made itself into a case, and joining them would be him deciding that they are the same.** **Two documents still carry a pair of marks and not three; three references exist and are references and not pairs; the twenty-first's certification is drawn where the two confirmed marks are pressed, a difference in method and not in age; Kest has not matched the strings; and Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–70.** **A fold is not a chronology and no document's age in this batch is established by a mark on it: the third edition of a schedule is dated and the fourth is dated, and the six weeks between them are the finding, not the margin note.**

33. **The ninth of Volume 01's instruments that this batch uses, and does not re-decide.** The answer to the bells is **used and not re-argued** — a check is a person saying a space is clear before the next turn, and it is said four times on a length by a foreman, eight men, a mechanic and a man of twenty-six, and once by a conductor of thirty-eight who was not asked and was not on the length. The triad accounting **is not used at all** in Chapters 61–70 and is never written as three withdrawn and never as a thin remnant that grew. **The pinned seam is not mentioned in these ten chapters except as the thing Jo Lask refuses to make** — *if you put a thing across it you have made a pinned thing with thirty-three people in it* — **and that is a use, not a re-decision, and no batch may reopen the condition.**

34. **Volume 01's people carry Volume 01's prices into this batch and none of them is refunded.** Itemised in Mara Quill's flat inventory on the nineteenth of December and nowhere else: **two hundred and fifty-two people on a per-head additional schedule at two and four a head a week, in advance, no term, signed for by a woman of about thirty-four; seven children back in the spring on a Continuity referral in the same hand in the same minute, and two children still lost; a hundred and forty feet of named frontage behind a fence with a settlement plate on it and a date on the plate; a culvert at twenty-four inches with a stop-screw and a padlock, silted and re-aligned, and not the culvert that was there before the order; a woman of sixty-eight on nothing; a man of forty-one behind a boatyard on no list at all; a mechanic of sixty-one on a drainage record forever; a conductor of thirty in a printed rule book; a woman of fifty-four with a plot number and no bill in six years; a foreman of thirty years reinstated twice for certification work, with the level not in Milo's file and not to be put back; a man on the fourth floor who has not been on that street since the night of the closure, whose name and whose third floor are still on five copies of a sheet of paper and have not been taken off any of them (the floor is the paper's and not his, and the chapter now says so); a man of sixty-three with a flag who got his hand up a fifth of a second before a rail; a hearing not set; and a surveyor's own front door with three chalk arrows on it and the twenty-sixth day of a nine-line blank he has not asked anybody to fill in for three weeks.** **Nothing in Chapters 61–70 touches the order, the contested name, the culvert's material state, the two lost school places, the levy, the death record, or the quiet reach, except to say once, in that inventory, that none of it has moved.**

35. **The volume opens and does not close, and Milo does not end this batch as the author of anything.** **There is a hundred and forty feet of frontage with a name in a drainage book and a gang on a fortnightly inspection, and six hundred yards of tram route with a gang on it twice a week and a rack and a tin and a duplicate, and no name anywhere in the city for the second and no record anywhere in the city of a car that went round it, and there cannot be one.** There is a card with three hands on it in four places. There is about nine other people with about one name each on paper in about eight kitchens and **Rue says they are wrong and will not defend them and is not going to stop them, and she is right.** There is a dog behind a cold store that has not stopped shaking and **there is no document for the dog.** And the last entry of the year ends: **I am not the author of any of it. I drew the frame of a sheet and read two rules out loud on a length… I did not put a single word on the sheet that records the passage, because there was nowhere to put one.** **He is not cured, he is not the author, and the last line of the year is that this time he is going to want to.**

36. **No new grammar, faction, antagonist or final enemy, and Volume 01 is not re-litigated.** The grammars in this batch are **Cadence** and **Keepsake**, both canonical, and no seventh-grammar variant, no new remnant type, and no new failure state has been introduced. **Thread Hall appears as a reading room, a stair of thirty-four steps and a keeper, and is not a faction and not a prize.** **Sel Iri does not appear. Halden Vey does not appear and is not named — his name is on a letter of the eleventh of December in Volume 01 and Volume 02 does not repeat it. Orrin Sable does not appear. The man in the works jacket is unidentified and must not become a face. The villain, if the word is used at all, is a box and a filing number and a standing instruction with a reference number, and the disappearance is attributable to a method and an authority and not to a new face.** **A returning character asks no new Volume 1 question; nobody reopens a settled one; and no Volume 1 record is treated as a thing that can now be tidied.**

## What Chapters 71–80 fixed on the page, in the same form as CR-013

Each item below is now prose rather than plan, and each is load-bearing in a way a later batch could break without intending to. **None of them changes a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder, or the planned ending. Every figure here is quotable from a chapter in `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0071.md` … `chapter-0080.md`, and this list was written from the manuscript, with three stated exceptions that are carried arithmetic and are marked as such where they occur: the forty-sixth day on the eighth of January, the fiftieth on the twelfth and the fifty-ninth on the twenty-first, none of which is written in the chapter that owns the day.** Chapters 71–80 are the **second block of Volume 02** — the hearing block and the maintenance block — and they inherit the Chapters 61–70 list above whole.

1. **The batch's shape and its boundary.** Ten chapters, **Thursday the first of January to Wednesday the twenty-first of January**, twenty-one days. **The first of January is a weekday morning working day and the city does not stop**: the works did not shut, the clinic counter opened at eight, and the four-day Christmas shutdown of Volume 02 Batch 0001 was followed by a queue of about eleven people at a district index counter on the first. **The volume's immediate promise was answered in Batch 0001 and is not re-opened; this block is the machine, the hearing, and the maintenance, and it does not resolve the volume.**
2. **The weekday chain, extended and not re-derived.** From Batch 0001: 28th Sun, 29th Mon, 30th Tue, 31st Wed, **1st Jan Thu, 2nd Fri, 3rd Sat, 4th Sun, 5th Mon, 6th Tue, 7th Wed, 8th Thu, 9th Fri, 10th Sat, 11th Sun, 12th Mon, 13th Tue, 14th Wed, 15th Thu, 16th Fri, 17th Sat, 18th Sun, 19th Mon, 20th Tue, 21st Wed, 22nd Thu**, then **1st February Sun (so the first working day of February is Monday the 2nd), 1st March Sun (first working day Monday the 2nd), 1st April Wed, and 14th April Tue.** A later batch may not re-derive a weekday.
3. **The gang's week, and the four sheets.** The gang is on the back length **Mondays and Thursdays at 07.30**, the weekly sheet covers Monday to Sunday, and it goes into the rack on the Friday with the duplicate into a tin and the duplicate into the second tin in the plan room on the bottom shelf under the repair schedule. In this block: **week of Mon 29 Dec / Thu 1 Jan, rack Fri 2 Jan; week of Mon 5 Jan / Thu 8 Jan, rack Fri 9 Jan; week of Mon 12 Jan / Thu 15 Jan, rack Fri 16 Jan; week of Mon 19 Jan / Thu 22 Jan.** **The reason column is empty on every one of the six walks in Chapters 71–80, which is correct, and there is no column for anything else.**
4. **Seven doors, and the reason there are seven.** On the first of January Milo Rook establishes, at five counters, that **the passage of the twenty-second of December has no place in any file that can be produced, and that the only place it can be filed is a length, and a length is not a place and cannot be given a street.** The seven: the tram authority's running sheet, whose fault box **cannot be completed from the car's own return because there is no column for where the car was and there never has been one, and which has been half filled since the twenty-second of December**; the Civic Atlas Office's incident and casualty book; the Registry's continuity file, where a tram service amendment is not within any of the three matters opened for investigation; the gang's weekly sheet; the route custody register; the district index counter; and nobody. **The honest outcome is on the page and may not be improved on: a thing happened and this city has no instrument for showing anybody that it happened.**
5. **The district index's three columns and its one hinge.** **A name in the book is entered against a rateable thing; a rateable thing is a building with a street; a maintenance length is not a building and cannot be given a street.** The clerk's sentence, in her words, and it is the batch's hinge: **the book is not a record of what happened; it is a record of what is where.** **A *nil* in a rateable-reference column is a hole with a sign over it** (works clerk of forty-six), and **a line under the words *requested by* or *officer responsible* is a place a person can write anything at all** — the same mechanism as the 12/44 form, and it is now on the page twice, four months apart, and recognised the second time.
6. **The three correct refusals of Chapter 72, and the fourth answer, and they are binding on every later batch.** A **reference is a place other people can write into** and **not one of the nine people who can find my four numbers has a shovel** (Cyril Sale). **A public map is not a place to put a length; a public map is a place people go to find out where they are** (Dilley Farr). **You are not putting a reference on my schedule, and a row with two numbers on it is a row somebody asks about in four years** (Roy Pryce), who adds: **ask me again in about four months, when you know what a fella in an office is going to say to me, and not now, because now it is a Thursday practice and in four months it is a document.** And the answer nobody refuses: **the sheet is the gang's, it is not a record of the length, and so where is the length's record — there is no such thing; there is *I am the record*.** **A rack is cleared in about eleven years and no piece of paper will stop it, and the thing on your paper will make it worse.**
7. **Rule 14 of the tram authority's amendment procedure, paragraph three, and it is quotable and it governs the standing of districts in the second proceeding.** *A party to a review under this rule must show an effect upon a person. An effect upon a person is a change in that person's access to a route, a service, a rate, a residence, a burial place, or a school place, and is not an effect upon a document, a schedule, a map, a diagram, a practice, or a place that has no rateable unit and no address.*
8. **Lena Marr's standing, and the price of it, in a tram authority's committee room on the seventh of January.** She is the only person in eleven streets who can be asked paragraph three's question, and her answer is that there is no effect on her: **two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term, charged to her name and not to the district, for every person entered in a building that has no street — and the thirteenth of October is what keeps me whole.** She is asked whether she is content and says it is a thing she has come to say. **She is the named rateable head of a temporary accommodation household, a woman of about thirty-four, and the levy, the seven school places and the address trap are all in that one sentence and none of them moved.**
9. **Mara Quill's custody register page, demanded once and refused, and NOT confessed to.** In a room, on the seventh of January, asked by a chair whether the Registry or any person in the room holds a document connecting 11/D/6 to the order, she takes about eleven seconds, looks at the chairman and then at the table, and says: **I hold a document that I am not permitted to produce, I am not going to say what it is, and I would like it recorded that I have declined to identify a document in my custody.** She does not name it, does not say what it is, does not admit taking it, and is not asked; the chair declines to act on the statement because it is a refusal and not an assertion. **He does not know what she refused, and the batch spends nothing on her knowledge, and her knowledge is still unused and undeclared. Cass Ardery does not ask, at volume, in a corridor, with a clerk in earshot, and says he decided it on purpose and will not ask in February.** A later batch may not have her hand it over as a favour and may not have her confess without earning it at a cost.
10. **The authority's amendment minute, and the sentence in it that is the truest thing anybody has written about the thirteenth of October.** Four paragraphs, read out at about twenty past twelve on the seventh: the amendment was validly made and stands; the justification is a filing number and not a reason and rule 11 does not require a reason; the requesting body is Permanent Way quay section and the lines for *requested by* and *instructing officer* are blank; and **the committee has been unable to identify the person who requested the amendment and records that it has been unable to identify the person who requested the amendment.** **And the finding about the fault box: a half-completed fault box in this authority is not an exception and generates nothing, and it is the exact opposite of an exception, which is the most dangerous thing it can be, because an exception has to be cleared and a half-filled box does not.** **There is no copy of that minute in this district and Milo Rook decides not to ask for one, and the decision is on the page as a decision and not as modesty.**
11. **The hearing on the Bellwether order, set, held, and adjourned, and every term of it.** Set on **Wednesday the seventh of January at about half past four in the afternoon, twenty-six days after the suspension**, twenty-six days because the department passed the file down and a clerk of about thirty-four crossed two names off a list of officers; **the reporting officer is Ivo Sarratt, Survey Enforcement, fifty-one, twelve years, and Cass Ardery is second-in-command and is not the reporting officer.** The reporting officer is a person other than any person named in the Prefect's letter or in the order, and **no person is added above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel.** He asked in writing for the exclusion and for the order of reference on the letter of the eleventh of December, which is the only document that would let him apply to another department, and got it in twenty-two minutes. **Cass Ardery said in December that it would not be set in January; he was wrong about January and knows he was wrong about January and nobody told him so.** The hearing sat **Monday the twelfth of January from eleven until about twenty past three, in the Hall of Wards, about forty people, and Milo Rook did not speak, was asked in advance, and was told that was correct.**
12. **The three matters, and the hearing does not re-litigate Volume 01.** *The grounds of the making of the order. The classification of the site dated the eleventh of the current year, withdrawn and not to be relied on by any party. The removal from the original safety report of the sentence: no evidence was found of any hazard to persons at the service court.* **The classification: it was correct on the eleventh, it is not correct now, the reporting officer confirms both halves of that, and it stays withdrawn** — and the finding that records why: *there is now an instrument in this city that will put a person on a reach, in the same city, on a piece of accountancy paper with a rule at the foot of it.* **The removed sentence cannot be restored by anybody in this city: the withdrawal reference is not a district reference, is held in the office of the First Surveyor, has been requested twice (the second on the sixth of January, acknowledged on the seventh) and not answered, and the department's position is that it cannot account for the removal and has never been able to.** **The office of the First Surveyor is named a third time on the page and there is still no person behind it and there is no date anywhere for the thing it stands on.**
13. **What the hearing cost and what it bought, and the trade is permanent.** **Bought:** the district's four-page return document is produced with its five conditions read into the record, **and in exchange the reporting officer rules that no schedule of persons resident at the withdrawn alignment is to be obtained from the district by this department, in this proceeding or in any proceeding, while the answer of the fourth of December stands** — a line with an officer's name on it and not a department on it, and he says so on the record. **Cost, said out loud in the hall by Mara Quill before she pays it: twenty-six names go into a file, and about nine of those people had been told by her that their names are not in any schedule, return, roster, roll, index or register, including a woman of sixty who has been off every list for nine years, and she says: I am about to make that untrue in one line of a minute, and I am doing it because the alternative is that there is no hearing at all, and I would like it recorded that I did it knowing.** Nobody said anything and about nine of forty people looked at each other. **The document was previously promised not to be entered in any schedule, return, roster, roll, index or register, and it now is; that promise is broken on the page and is not retracted.**
14. **The result, which is not a victory.** **The order is suspended and not discharged and its schedule is suspended; it is not varied, withdrawn or discharged; the public name is not restored to the street name index; the district asked for three things and got none of them; the matter is adjourned to Tuesday the fourteenth of April, which is a departmental list day and not the reporting officer's, and Ardery has asked for that to be minuted.** **Milo Rook hears the date and understands it in four seconds: the fourteenth of April, and a foreman of fifty-four who said in December that in about four months a man in an office is going to ask him whether he witnessed thirty-one persons, and four months from the twenty-second of December is about the twenty-second of April, in the same fortnight as that list day. I am not saying it is a conspiracy and I have not got a figure for it.**
15. **Milo's failure, in Chapter 76, and it is his and it is the batch's spine of consequence.** **On the morning of Saturday the tenth of January** he writes a form headed *BACK LENGTH (unnumbered) — MAINTENANCE* with the gang's own six columns — **date, length, men, hours, work done, condition, signature** — and a **seventh he writes first, in four seconds: *If any of them stops.*** Six lines about six people: the gang, a sweeper of about forty, a fishmonger of about forty-five, a coffee man of about sixty, six irons with a person not identified, and **a scale, a woman of seventy-four with thirty-one years.** He wrote the last of them out and **put a single pencil line through the whole of it, undated, unreasoned**, at about half past ten the same Saturday morning at the north end of Saltmarket Row, having carried the form down there in the inside pocket of a coat, and folded the page in three. **The six gang columns were copied off a weekly sheet he had looked at on Monday the fifth, and the sheet he was handed on the Monday of the hearing could not be the one he copied from; and the form came back to the second table in the route room on the Tuesday morning and lay there face up from that morning, which is why a man of thirty-one found it at about eleven and why nobody has asked him about it since.** **Dilley Farr's sentence, which is the whole of the finding: a box that wants to know why is the amendment. That is what the amendment is.** **Iona Sen's sentence, which is the whole of the cost: in November you held a page; on Saturday you filled it in.**
16. **The page in the tin, and it is the cost that is not curable.** On the evening of Tuesday the thirteenth of January, at about a quarter past nine, it goes into the second tin, on the bottom shelf, under the repair schedule, in the plan room of the Civic Atlas Office — **because a foreman's rule in grease pencil says that if any of them stops you cross the line out and date it and do not take the line off and do not ask anybody to start again, and it applies to this.** **Dilley Farr has not read the seventh column and never will: the last line is a name with a line through it and he is not going to know whose name it is, and he has never in thirty-one years been glad of a thing in a drawer more than he is going to be glad of that one. Nobody in this district is going to read it. Ivy Marsden is seventy-four and has not been told and is not going to be told, and there is now a piece of paper in a building in Alderquay with her name on it and a line through her name and no date and no reason, and it may be produced in about eleven years by somebody with a box.** The tin holds four weeks of gang carbon by the sixteenth of January — the weeks of the twenty-first of December, the twenty-ninth of December, the fifth and the twelfth of January — **plus this page.**
17. **The changed method, and it is not his.** On the evening of Wednesday the fourteenth in a yard of about nineteen, **a column headed *wrong* is added to the map of who does what and on which day, and the new rule at the foot is in Rue's hand and Milo Rook is not consulted and says so in front of nineteen people.** *If a line on this sheet is wrong, cross it out, put the date on the strike, and put your own initials beside it. Do not tell anybody. Do not take a line off. Do not ask anybody to start again. **A line that is not right for two weeks in a row is not a mistake, it is a place that has changed, and it gets written again.*** The first line in it is written by the man of about twenty-six in the down loop, **with a date on the strike and no name or initial on it, and he has said that if anybody ever asks him to put one there he will not do it.** **This is a method and not a power, and the recovery he gets in this batch is somebody else's work.**
18. **The boundary, found, and the answer to who keeps a length.** The back length's south boundary is checked **three times a week by a crew with a clipboard and a foreman, and about ninety times a week by two people who do not know each other and who do not know the crew does it**: a fishmonger of about forty-five who puts her thumb in a crack in the wall at about twenty past five for about half a minute, after calling lot prices from a doorway for a minute and a half, and a coffee man of about sixty who puts his hand on the corner at half past five for four seconds. Neither has ever told anybody. **The finding, and it is not one sentence and he refuses to make it one: the writing down is what lets a department find it; the walking is what makes it a place. Those are two different jobs.** **A length is a distinct kind of place for exactly as long as the people who walk it keep walking it, and the whole of the machinery in this city is designed to find out who is responsible, and there is nobody, and there has never been anybody.** **A length has not been converted into a remnant type, has not been given a failure condition, and has not become a form of any of the seven grammars, and a length and a Keepsake remnant leaning on each other across a wall is two kinds of place, not one.**
19. **The one stabilisation of the batch, once, and it is a market and not a wound.** On the afternoon of Wednesday the fourteenth of January, on the Row side of the wall, with his own hand about nine inches from a man of about sixty's, **Milo Rook holds a small pressure for about ninety seconds. It is not large and it is not pressing and it is not in the wrong, and it is about the size of a smell.** He could not tell whether his stopping and the other man's hand coming off the stone were a quarter of a second apart or four minutes apart, **because he was not counting, because there is nothing in the world to count with on a wall at half past five.** He did not enter anything, did not put a weight on a scale, did not stabilise a seam, and **has decided not to decide for about a fortnight whether he did anything.** Stage 2 continues and does not complete.
20. **The Stage 2 cost, its second instance, and the ledger that stands.** A tram gong behind the cold store at about half past four on the fourteenth of January, eleven seconds, and **at four seconds into it he cannot tell what it is, and he needed to**, because a tram gong and whatever else that noise is come from the same direction on that street and he was about to put his hand on a wall in fifty minutes. **It is a sound, and a taste from the twenty-third of December, and no face and no place and no person and no name, and the first personal blank may not appear before Stage 3 and does not appear.** He wrote the two in the order they happened and has still decided nothing. **The written line home is the fifty-ninth day of it on the twenty-first of January — a carried figure and not a quotable one, because Chapter 80's Field Book entry gives no count — is blank, has three marks in three hands from Volume 02 Batch 0001, and nobody has asked anybody to fill it in since the twenty-seventh of December, and the imperative half of his one sentence in the imperative has been read back twice this month and has not worked.**
21. **Atlas residue, once in ten chapters, in eight words, and the tenth pair.** Chapter 77, in the field, on a wall, at about ten to six on the evening of Wednesday the fourteenth: ***A length is missed before it is lost.*** *It does not say by whom. It does not say what missing a length looks like from the inside. And a length is not a thing anybody owns, so it cannot be lost by anybody, and I am not going to find out.* **The twentieth of December to the fourteenth of January is twenty-five days, and that is the tenth dated pair in the Field Book — one six, four threes, one nine, one two, one five, one eight and one twenty-five, joined to nothing, with the unwritten single-day interval from the tenth of December still looked at.** The four threes are not collapsed. The nine pairs from Batch 0001 are unchanged and are not re-derived.
