and **I have a week in my head that nobody in this district has ever written down, and it is not a schedule and it is not on a rota and it is not a column, and I have never had to say it to anybody, and I have been doing it since the second year.** She will not take a machine, twice offered, and the reason is not dignity: **the day I am on a schedule one of these six stops becomes an appointment, and an appointment can be moved, and nobody in the world needs an appointment to be moved.** **He measures the water wrong by about a third because he measured a roof and not a road, and she tells him so, and he has written that she is better at it than he is.** **A board outside a portacabin with four questions in a clerk's hand, the fourth of them *subsidence observed*, a tick and a time and no column for anything else, is on the page and is what her mother's boy died of. He does not write it down, he asks her nothing about it, and he has written down that he is not going to ask and that this is the first time in six months he has put a decision in that book instead of a restraint. A later batch may not ask her about the board and may not have her name it in a document.**

8. **His failure, and it is the fourth time, and it is the same one, and the district knows the shape of it before he finishes.** A form headed **SECOND SIDE — DAYS WITH NO LINE**, the columns of the sheet it is about, and a seventh headed **WHY THERE IS NO LINE ON THIS DAY.** *A question about a practice, and there is no such question in this district that has ever been answered by anybody.* **He filled in Saturday the twenty-first and Sunday the twenty-second of February and filled them in well, and both reasons are true and neither is about a day** — *the round does not go out at a weekend and the sheet goes with the round, and the sheet has a week on it that runs Monday to Sunday, and the sheet is not carried on the Saturday.* **Doreen Sankey said four words before he had got to the bottom of the page: *There is a why in it.*** **The fishmonger's correction, which is the method of the whole volume: a rule at the foot of a sheet is for the person whose hand it is going to be asked about, and his seventh column is a rule for nobody, because there is not one person in eleven streets whose hand is going to be in the answer — *and the answer is for a man in an office in about four years, and I have known that since about the tenth of January and I have had a fish stall to run.*** **Doreen Sankey's arithmetic, which is not February's: you have not asked anybody, and it does not matter, because you have written the sentence down; the asking has already happened, in advance, in a kitchen, in your own hand, under a heading.**** **The cost, and it is not a form: he broke a written undertaking in a yard, having sworn in the same yard on the sixth of February that he was not going to be the man who said that in a room again for about a year, and said out loud that he had promised and done it, and asked for it to be written down, and nobody wrote it down — the eighth time in nine weeks.** **And the resolution, which is new and is a method: he put it face up on a trestle table in the rain, squared, took his hand off it, and did not take it, did not burn it and did not give it to anybody** — *giving it to somebody is a favour and I have had two of those this winter and one of them was a page in a tin* — **and it was still there on the Sunday evening and was gone off the table in a wind by about Wednesday, and it is the first of his that has left a district without him.** **A later batch may not find that form, may not have it copied, and may not have him apologise for it in a room.**

9. **The report that could not be made, and the form that wants a place it can send a man to, and a nil that is a lie.** A gang of eleven and a foreman tried to report a cast-iron cover that has been rocking since the second of February. **The authority's permanent way defect report has five boxes — *location, chainage, description, risk to passengers, action taken* — and four of the five refusals on Monday the sixteenth of March are correct, and the reason is nine words from a works clerk of forty-six at his own desk: *there is no box for a length, and the box wants a place it can send a man to, and that is not a complaint about the form, because that box is a man with a barrow on a Tuesday and he cannot find six hundred yards of track because it has not got a name, and he will not find it on a diagram either, because the diagram took it off in October and the diagram was right to take it off.*** **A section's reckoning is not a chainage, and PW/Q/4 in the chainage box would be accepted on the Monday and rejected on the Thursday with the nine words *chainage not in section series*.** **The risk box cannot be filled nil**, because a nil there means that no passengers have ever been on any of the quay lengths and there were thirty-one of them on one of them in December with a conductor of thirty-eight and a driver of fifty-one. He wrote on the back of the form, in his own hand, that the box is left empty and why, and put it in the section's correspondence tray and not in a drawer, and said he had done that before in January and had noticed himself doing it again. **The cover is still rocking and nobody can report it and the form is not going to be changed.**

10. **The correction, and it is somebody else's, and it is the batch's changed method, and it is two sentences from a man with a broom.** **The coffee man of about sixty is not on the Row on a Wednesday and has not been since before the war, because there is a stall at Cardew Docks that opens at six and that is the day it is busy.** Said by **the man of about forty who has swept that row since he was twenty-one**, at about ten past six on the morning of Wednesday the nineteenth of March, in nine words, unasked. **The consequence, in eleven seconds: the second side has been blank on every Wednesday since the second of February, and a blank on a day-side is not a fault, and a thing that is not wrong cannot be struck out, and there is no column anywhere on that piece of paper for a man who is somewhere else** — *a day with no line on it is a day when nobody stood in the weather, and a man of about sixty has been standing in the weather eleven minutes away every Wednesday since before the war, and the sheet has been right, in its own terms, seven weeks running.* **The correction is twenty-eight words written on the second side under the rule, in the sweeper's own hand, undated and uninitialled: *Wed — he is at the shed at half past five and has been since before the war. He has not stopped. The side cannot see a Wednesday and a side is not a person.*** **And the reason for not initialing it, which is the reason the man of about twenty-six gave for initialing his line on the same side and did not say so to him: *the first side has got a column for whether I am wrong and I would have to initial it, and the second side is a day and I was not on the day.*** **The fishmonger refused to add a paragraph to her own rule, in these words: *the moment there is a second paragraph there is an instruction in it, and a person can say no to an instruction, and nobody can say no to a line.*** **The coffee man has not been told that a piece of paper exists, is not going to be told, and the sweeper has not told him, and the reason is: *he has said twice it is not for anybody and he means it every time, and I have swept this row since I was twenty-one and I have never once told him what time I am out here and I am not going to start by telling him about a piece of paper.*** **Milo Rook did not go near the north end of the Row in this block, did not look at the scale a second time, and has written down that he did not and has not written down why.**

11. **The recovery, and it is a nurse's, and the first list in this district that cannot be turned into anybody.** **Ola Petrossian, twenty-three, four months in the job, never been past the tram steps, walked four hours in eleven streets on Sunday the twenty-second of March in her own time, unpaid, and came back with nine doors and no names.** The reason, in eleven words in a yard: *then what is a water clinic for on a Tuesday morning, and who is standing in the kitchen?* The rule she set on the doorstep is four words — **doors and not people** — and the reason is that she has been taking the sheets since the middle of January and *a sentence about a person is a record of a person, and a door is not a person.* **Nine doors, eight of them houses, and the ninth a side door on a store that is shut, because a store is not a dwelling and nobody in this district has ever counted a store.** **Milo Rook objected in eleven seconds — *a list of doors is the first step onto a rate book* — and was refused by the person the objection was about, and was right, and has written down that being right is not the same as being the person who decides, which is the fourth time he has written that sentence and the first time he has been on the wrong side of it in public.** **At the counter, Hettie Brann made the second piece of paper of her life on purpose, said so out loud in front of about eleven people, promised not to make a third without saying so, and put both under the counter face down and neither in the machine.** Her sentence, which is the volume's: ***I want it to be wrong-shaped. The moment a thing can be used it is in use.*** And hers and January's are the same act: *in nineteen years this counter has never had a piece of paper on it that is about a place, and if that list goes anywhere it goes to a surveyor or a clerk or a rate, and a rate needs a property, and this is not a property, so the list will sit there until somebody in about four years asks what a door is a rateable unit for, and by then whoever wrote it has been doing it for four years and has about nine of them.* **The list is on the back of a clinic form in a bag and is not in the machine, and it is going to happen again on the seventh of April, and nobody is to stop her and nobody has said so out loud.**

12. **The cost that is not curable, paid in a room over a fitter's shop, and it is a box with a heading nobody has built before.** **FORM 4 — QUERY ON ANNUAL RETURN**, six boxes, the fourth of them **DOES THE SECTION STAND BEHIND THE ENTRY?** — the only column on it that is about a person — and the query is on **line six**, the fifty-four words a works clerk of forty-six wrote on the fourteenth of January and a foreman of fifty-four signed without reading out. **And the query does not ask what he meant.** The cost in about eleven minutes: the answer has to be signed by the officer of the section, the officer is the foreman, the only honest answer is yes, the only answer that keeps eleven men and a foreman out of a room in April is no, **and there is no column on that form for what a sentence meant**, so the box was left empty with a line through it and a date against it. **And he did not tell the foreman that the line was his, because the foreman would have said yes in about four seconds, and when the foreman said *read it to me* he said no, in one word, and that one word is the whole of what happened in that office on the twenty-sixth of March.** **And the other half, which is worse and is the finding: nobody has ever been asked about line five.** A nil on *persons observed on the length during the year* has now survived a fortnight of a man with a pencil and nine returns reading it and has not been queried, **and the reason is that a man with a pencil and nine returns is not looking for a box about persons, he is looking for a line that is not work — and that is the second time in six months that this district has been protected by a clerk being fast, and the clerk has said that he could have been that pencil.** **Milo Rook's own instance, given in a yard and not to another character: a page in a tin on a bottom shelf in a plan room in his own office, kept there by a rule in grease pencil about not taking a line off, and the person who does not know that rule exists is the shop, and Rue says *that is the same shape* and he stops after about four seconds and asks for it to be read back to him in April.** **A later batch may not answer the query, may not put the foreman's name to the sentence, may not have the foreman read line six, and may not have anybody query line five.**

13. **The answer, and it is said by working people, and it is about the fifteenth of April.** **There is nothing printed in this city for the day after the fourteenth of April.** A man of about fifty in a room with a machine in it, nineteen years in that room, put his pen down for about four minutes and said: *I have been in this room nineteen years and I have seen the one that came in December and I have never seen another and I have never asked for one and I have never known anybody who has, and I am telling you that as a fact about a room and not as a finding, because I have never once in nineteen years been in a position to know whether a thing does not exist or whether it exists and has not been sent.* **A seasonal outlook is four pages, it comes by hand, from an office that signed no name, and there is no second one.** The question was found because a woman of sixty-one asked a **returns** question — *what do you print in the second week of May?* — and the extraordinary question came out of the ordinary one. **Her answer to *what do you want to happen on the fifteenth of April*, which took nine days to find and is one word: nothing** — *a district that has spent the winter being careful not to write anything down is going to be standing in the weather on the fifteenth of April with no reason to think anything is different, and a thing with no reason to think anything is different is a thing that changes its practice on the fifteenth of April, and about nine people in eleven streets will be the only people in this city who do that, and they will do it out of a guess and for nothing.* **And what her job has been for nineteen years and has never been asked about, in about thirty seconds: *I read across. Two departments, two documents, and I am the one who notices when they disagree… and I have never once been told that any of it was a season, and I found out this year because there is a piece of paper with a date on it, and I have had forty-one of them and I did not know there was a number.*** **Rue's refusal of it, unasked for, and it is the standing correction: *you have just said a good thing and it is going to be used… you have been saying nothing in a yard for four months and it has been the best thing anybody has done for this district, and you have just stopped.*** **A later batch may not print a second outlook, may not have anybody ask for one in writing, and may not resolve what happened on the fifteenth.**

14. **The list day, the six items, and the word *presented*, and the chapter's finding.** A single printed side came to the district by hand in an envelope on Tuesday the thirty-first of March, **because the district is a party and parties are given things.** It is headed **HALL OF WARDS — DEPARTMENTAL LIST DAY, BUSINESS OF TUESDAY THE FOURTEENTH OF APRIL**, and the fourth line of it says *it is not a notice of hearing and it confers no right and no obligation and nothing in it may be produced in any proceeding except the proceeding on the item to which it relates.* **Nobody in this district had ever seen one.** **Item one is the hearing and the reporting officer's name is on it, and this is the first time his name has been on a document in front of this district since the twelfth of January: *Reporting officer: I. Sarratt, Survey Enforcement.*** **Item two is the three matters. Item four is a line about ground and seam returns outstanding from sections, and which sections are on it is not knowable from the district, and Mara Quill says unasked that she cannot tell and is not going to find out, and nobody touched it. Item six is *Maintenance returns, current year, presented. Permanent Way (quay section) — presented*,** and **the whole of what that list says about six hundred yards of reserved tram route is the word *presented*, in a line about forms, on an item about forms, four lines away from a hearing about a sentence that no one in this city can produce, and neither of them is going to find out about the other on the fourteenth.** **The finding, in about eleven seconds, from an advocate of thirty-one: *this sheet is the only piece of paper in five months that has this district's name on it, and it has our name on it in the first line, and it has asked us for nothing, and it has given us a chair. A hearing is a thing somebody asks questions in. A list is a thing nobody asks anything in, and a list goes on a wall and gets read by about nine people and then goes in a file for eleven years.*** **And two voices in a yard, and both are standing rules: *a document in the hand of a party is a thing that can be produced against the party*, and every line on the sheet is correct and the one that is wrong is item four; and *a list is a thing you read and a minute is a thing you are in*, and the only thing this district has won in five months is on that sheet as a number, and if a man in an office reads that sheet in April and says *and what is item one about*, then somebody has to be in a room on the fourteenth and say it out loud, and that is not a document and it cannot be made into one.** **A later batch may not move or re-author the adjournment, may not make the list a notice of hearing, may not have item six expanded, and may not let either office learn of the other.**

15. **The run number, advanced one step, and the blank, and the plant is advanced and not turned into the volume's final image.** On Wednesday the first of April at about eleven in the morning **a depot clerk of fifty-one, asked by nobody, looked it up**, on a Wednesday rather than the Monday because *a person who is going to do a thing nobody asked her to do should do it on a day when she has a half hour, and the fifteenth is off and the third is a Saturday.* **The same crew's number, in the same book, in the working timetable for the week commencing Sunday the twenty-first of December, is entered against nothing at all: the crew is on that timetable for a week and is not worked on any day of it.** *There is no column on that page for a week in which a crew does not work, because a crew that does not work does not happen, and the row of a thing that did not happen is the same blank as the row of a thing that did, and a man reading that page in April cannot tell which of a hundred rows he is looking at.* **Milo Rook did not write it down, left a card under a brick, and was told not to come back — and his reason for not asking her twice in five months and not once in between is on the page: *once is a favour and twice is a habit and a habit is a thing you can be asked about.*** **No destination is named, no passenger's name appears, and the volume's final image — a recovered timetable containing a destination that does not exist with the passengers' names real in it — is not written in this batch and may not be written by a later one without the outline.** The card under the brick has two lines on the back of it and the line on the front has not been taken off, and the three things under that brick — the run-number card, the works-jacket card and Pell Harrow's blank card — are **unchanged at three.**

16. **The fourth mark in a fourth hand, and it is not a cure and it is not an absence of one.** On Wednesday the first of April at about half past one in the afternoon, at the water clinic counterr, **a woman of sixty-one who is not M.Q. and is not M.R. and will not be put nine lines of the written route home down in front of about nine people**, having been asked by nobody, and **refused to put it in the envelope: *the envelope is four copies in four buildings with three hands on it and a disagreement that started in January over a gate, and this is a different thing and it does not go in there.*** **The four marks are the nurse of twenty-three on a card on the twenty-fourth of December, the woman who keeps a round on a card on the twenty-fifth, the boy of seventeen with chalk on a second-floor landing on the twenty-sixth, and this one, on the first of April, in a hand of sixty-one at a counter, in about four seconds.** The line is his own hand from the twenty-fourth of November and the loop on the capital L is still in it. **It is there on the eighth, and on the twelfth, and on the fourteenth, and in April, and in May, and it does not come back.** His sentence, and it is the standing vocabulary: *it is not a cure and it is not an absence of one. It is a line somebody else can walk me home by, and on the twenty-seventh of December that is all it was, and on the first of April that is all it is, and the only thing that has changed is that a person I have never asked has now done it twice.* **A later batch may have somebody else mark it again and it may still not come back, and it may not be cured, argued away, or got better by anybody writing the right thing on the right page, and the district's refusal to keep asking remains a refusal and not a kindness.** He has written down in his own book that the not-asking has stopped being procedure and become something else and that he has not decided what.

17. **The relationship, milestone 2, and it is a working instrument and it is not warm, and the thing that did not happen in it is the finding.** **There is no kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene and no rescue in these ten chapters, and the Lark Street business is not raised in any of them.** **Mara Quill appears once, in a corridor in Crown Heights on Tuesday the thirty-first of March, in about eleven seconds, and the exchange is about a list.** **The custody register page is not produced, not demanded, not subpoenaed, not refused again, not confessed to and not mentioned in dialogue; it is in a drawer with a coat in it and he did not ask about it and she did not offer it, and the reader is given nothing new and spends nothing.** **And the one thing that is new and is on the page is what he did not tell her: he did not tell her about the season, and he has written down in his own book that not telling her was not the correct procedure and was not a courtesy, and that a four-page document from that office is not evidence in a hearing about a sentence in a report, and that if the word weather goes into a file by Friday then on the fourteenth of April a man will ask what season, and that if he goes on not telling her after the fourteenth then it is not a rule and he will write that down in April in the same hand as everything else in the book.** **She told him, unasked, that she cannot tell which sections are on item four and is not going to find out, and he wrote that down as the fourth time in a fortnight he has not asked her something and the first time he has recorded that the not-asking has changed.** **She knows about the token and the custody column and her knowledge is still unused and undeclared and he still does not know she refused anything.** **Her second letter, written on the eighteenth of February and not sent, is not mentioned in these ten chapters and remains unsent.** **The promise of the twenty-ninth of Sazur stands, is hers, and does not get wider.**

18. **Milo's orientation ledger, and the Stage 2 cost, and the Stage 2 gain, and none of them moved.** **The remembered-route list stands at TEN, the original eight intact, none removed, all ten marked by somebody who is not him, and nothing was added and nothing was lost in Chapters 91–100. The ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained and is not explained by anything in this block.** **The written line home went on the twenty-fourth of November, which is day one, and the ten figures printed in this block are 92, 103, 108, 112, 115, 119, 123, 127, 128 and 129, on the twenty-third of February, the sixth, eleventh, fifteenth, eighteenth, twenty-second, twenty-sixth, thirtieth and thirty-first of March and the first of April** — blank throughout, with three marks in three hands until the first of April, when it was marked in a fourth hand by somebody who was not asked (item 16), **and it is not cured, and the district's refusal to keep asking is still a refusal and not a kindness.** **The Lark token was cold throughout, was not taken out, and is the seventy-eighth, eighty-ninth, ninety-fourth, ninety-eighth, hundred and first, hundred and fifth, hundred and ninth, hundred and thirteenth, hundred and fourteenth and hundred and fifteenth day since the seventh of December.** **The Stage 2 cost recurred once, as a sound, on Monday the sixteenth of March at about ten to six in the evening at the end of Cardew Street: a tram gong, eleven seconds, and at four seconds into it he could not tell what it was, and he needed it, because a Line 3 car comes down that street at about that time and the coffee man walks against the traffic at half past five, and he wanted to know whether a car was coming so that he could say so.** He used a watch. **It is a sound and a taste and no face, no place, no person and no name, and he has still decided nothing about whether that is one thing or two.** **There is no stabilisation in Chapters 91–100: he went into nowhere, held nothing, and crossed nothing, and the reason is on the page twice — that he is not going to make a form about a practice, and that he did not go near the north end of the Row and has not written down why. The first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in these ten chapters.** Iona Sen's tally is unchanged at nine lines and the crossing count is nine, and he has not looked at it.

19. **Atlas residue, once in ten chapters, in six words, and the seventh three.** Monday the sixteenth of March, in the field, on a wall at the top of a hundred and ten yards of frontage, at about ten to six in the evening: ***A habit is not a calendar.*** *It does not say which habit. It does not say whose calendar. It does not say what a calendar is for. And I have a piece of accountancy paper in a yard in the rain that is about six people's Saturdays and a column in it with a why at the end, and I am not going to find out what a habit is by putting it in a column, and I have had six ideas in six months and every one of them has been about a page, and I am not going to let this one be about a market.* **The Field Book's dated pairs are now TWENTY: one six, SEVEN threes, one nine, one two, TWO fives, ONE eight, one twenty-five, two sevens, and FOUR fours, all of them in March. There is no thirteen-day pair; the figure is twenty and it is a claim to be checked against the chapter that owns each interval and not an authority.** **The four threes that were here in December are the four that were here in December and are not collapsed; the fifth was added in the first week of February, the sixth on the twenty-third of February and the seventh on the eighteenth of March, and none of the original four is one of the other three.** The four fours are the eleventh to the fifteenth, the eighteenth to the twenty-second, the twenty-second to the twenty-sixth and the twenty-sixth to the thirtieth of March. **The single-day interval from the tenth of December is still looked at and is still not written, and it was looked at three times in the last fortnight of this block and written down none. There are no one-day gaps in this block: the runs of nothing are two days (the twenty-first and twenty-second of February), eleven (the twenty-fourth of February to the fifth of March), three (the twelfth, thirteenth and fourteenth of March), two (the sixteenth and seventeenth), three (the nineteenth, twentieth and twenty-first), three (the twenty-third, twenty-fourth and twenty-fifth) and three (the twenty-seventh, twenty-eighth and twenty-ninth), and the two single-day intervals at the end of the block — the thirtieth to the thirty-first of March, and the thirty-first of March to the first of April — are silences and are not pairs.** **There is no thirty-six, no eleventh pair, no thirteenth pair, no twenty-first pair and no twenty-second pair.** He joins none of them to any other and has written down that he does not understand what twenty of a thing means, and that four fours in a month is a sentence he has already written in nine better words in a different book in February.

20. **Volume 01's people carry Volume 01's prices into this block and not one of them is refunded, and this is stated once, in Chapter 100's schedule, and the block adds exactly one declared reference.** **A woman of sixty-eight on nothing** — **this block's addition, declared: the sixth reference across six chapters of Volume 02 and the first in these ten chapters, always as *a woman of sixty-eight on nothing*, never by name; and Chapter 100 states in its own hand that it is adding one and that it is keeping the count. Nobody went up three flights of stairs about her and she is not thanked, argued back, put on anything or solved.** A hundred and forty feet of named frontage behind a fence with a settlement plate and a date on it. A man asleep on a third floor who is not written down and whom nobody has woken. A man of forty-one behind a boatyard on no list at all. A mechanic of sixty-one on a drainage record forever who will not swear. A conductor of thirty in a printed rule book whose own hearing has no date. A woman of fifty-four with a plot number and no bill in six years. Two children lost in October and still lost. Two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term, for a building with no number, and a woman of thirty-one with a two-year note and a page in a drawer with a coat in it. **The two money totals, five hundred and fifty-four pounds and forty and forty-three pounds and two, are carried forward unchanged, are not printed in these ten chapters, and the multiplication is still never shown.** **Roy Pryce's nine words in the hours column are still nine words and are not re-counted anywhere in these ten chapters.**

21. **The standing answer, and the standing question, and neither is closed.** The answer, this block's, in two mouths and not a surveyor's: **there is nothing printed for the fifteenth of April, and what should happen on the fifteenth of April is nothing, and about nine people in eleven streets will be standing in the weather on it for no reason at all, out of a guess, and they will be the only people in this city who do that** — and the method that produces it, in two more mouths: **a rule at the foot of a sheet is for the person whose hand is going to be asked about, and the moment there is a second paragraph in a rule there is an instruction in it, and a person can say no to an instruction and nobody can say no to a line; and a side with nothing on it is a day when nobody stood in the weather, and about nine of the coming blanks will be days a man of about sixty was at a shed, and the sheet will not know and it is not going to be able to.** The question, one step closer: **why a region the Registry enters as stable appears on a forecast of weather-driven seam events at all, when the only fact about it in this city is that nobody has ever been there to write down what happens in it** — and beside it now, **why there is no second outlook, and who would print one, and whether the absence of one is a decision or an oversight, and no person in this city is in a position to know.**

22. **No new grammar, faction, antagonist, final enemy, or fourth kind of place.** **The grammars in use in these ten chapters are Keepsake (the Row and its Wednesday) and the block names no new dominant grammar. A length is still a third kind of place and is not a remnant, not a failure state, and not a form of any of the seven grammars. The Row is still a Keepsake remnant, has not been converted into a fourth category, and the hole in the paperwork has not been turned into one. A defect form, an allocation sheet, a district returns card, a form of query, a departmental list and a work timetable are not kinds of place and none of them has been described as one.** **Sel Iri does not appear. Halden Vey does not appear and is not named. Orrin Sable does not appear. Tarin Kest does not appear. Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–100. The man in the works jacket is not seen once in these ten chapters, nobody was asked to look, and no fifth sighting and no fifth refusal was made by anybody — and the three things under the brick are still three.** **The copied tick, the copied turn sheet and the carbon relocation schedule appear in no chapter of Chapters 91–100 as a document and are joined to nothing, and no fifth refusal to join them was made by anybody. Two documents still carry a pair of marks and not three; three references exist and are references and not pairs; the twenty-first's Registry certification is drawn where the two confirmed marks are pressed, a difference in method and not in age; Kest has not matched the strings; and a fold is still not a chronology and no document's age in this block is established by a mark on it.** **A crease was not used as a date in this block; Hettie Brann's count on the outside of the envelope is a count of versions and not a date of reading, and the instrument that reads four copies by softness was not used again.**

23. **Pell Harrow, and Tern, and the standing refusal, unchanged and unasked.** He is on the round, out at eight and in at four. **Tern is not found, the list in the tin is not a finding, Ostrow confirms nothing, and no chapter in this block rescued anybody or asked anything about the twenty-two days.** **His card under the brick is blank and has been since the nineteenth of January and he has not taken it and nobody has.** **He appears once, in a yard on the thirty-first of March, with a man of forty-one, and what he says in this block is not about Tern and is about a document: *a list is a thing you read and a minute is a thing you are in*, and then he names the standing rule about the card under the brick, which is a man of forty-one's and which he has not contradicted. He is not Milo's assistant, he has no power, and his mark still holds for a working shift and not overnight and was not used and is not going to be.**

24. **The pressures, and the shape of the batch, stated for a later writer.** **The batch leans on the second month of a season that cannot be dated, on ordinary work, and on the narrowing distance to the fourteenth of April.** It contains **one working day with a person in it** (Ch 93), **one field or boundary pair** (Ch 95, the Row at ten past six on a Wednesday morning and a wall at ten to six on a Monday evening), **one failure** (Ch 94), **one recovery by somebody else's work** (Ch 96), **one changed method** (Ch 95), **one refusal that is correct** (Ch 92's twenty words, and the four correct refusals in Ch 95), **one cost that is not curable paid in a room** (Ch 97), **the answer in working people's mouths** (Ch 98), **an escalation nobody expected** (Ch 99), **and the standing question** (Ch 100). **Five moments in which a thing somebody built for one purpose turned out to be the wrong shape for another:** a second side kept by a satchel that only runs on a working day (Ch 91); an *allocated to* box that records a promise being kept on a day that does not exist (Ch 92); a defect form with no box for a length and a risk box that cannot be filled nil (Ch 95); a day-side with no column for a man who is somewhere else (Ch 95); and a query box that wants to know whether a section agrees with a clerk instead of what a clerk meant (Ch 97). **The block's boundary is honest: the volume's final image is not written, the order is not discharged, the name is not on the street index, the adjournment is not moved, the fifth sighting and the fifth refusal are both not made, and a crossing is not required and not made, and Milo Rook does not end it as the author of the back length's maintenance — *and he has not been for four months, and the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and that is a list of other people.**"

---

## What Chapters 101–110 fixed on the page, in the same form as CR-013

Each item below is now prose rather than plan, and each is load-bearing in a way a later batch could break without intending to. **None of them changes a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder, or the planned ending. Every figure here is quotable from a chapter in `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0101.md` … `chapter-0110.md`, and this list was written from the manuscript after a weekday, day-count, pair-count, word-count and meta-language pass on the batch.** Chapters 101–110 are the **fifth block of Volume 02** — the resumed hearing, the eleven days, and the fortnight after — and they inherit the Chapters 91–100, 81–90, 71–80, 61–70 and Volume 01 lists above whole.

1. **The batch's shape and its boundary.** Ten chapters, **Saturday the fourth of April to Saturday the twenty-fifth of April, twenty-two days**, and it includes **Tuesday the fourteenth of April** and the days immediately around it. **The volume's immediate promise was answered in Batch 0001 and is not re-opened. The hearing was held in January and adjourned and the one of the fourteenth is a continuation of an adjourned matter and it is not re-litigated. This batch does not resolve the volume and it does not write the volume's final image.** The run number on the card under a brick is **advanced one step and stopped**, and what it advanced to is registered at item 20.

2. **The weekday chain, extended and not re-derived.** From Batch 0004, which left the chain at 1 April Wed, 2nd Thu, 3rd Fri: **4th Sat, 5th Sun, 6th Mon, 7th Tue, 8th Wed, 9th Thu, 10th Fri, 11th Sat, 12th Sun, 13th Mon, 14th April Tue, 15th Wed, 16th Thu, 17th Fri, 18th Sat, 19th Sun, 20th Mon, 21st Tue, 22nd Wed, 23rd Thu, 24th Fri, 25th Sat**, and the fixed point **3rd May Mon** (the next first working day of the month) and **5th May Tue** (the business printed on the second list). **A later batch may not re-derive a weekday and may not move the third of May or the fifth of May.** **The chapter dates, all on the page: 4–10 April (Ch 101, Sat–Fri), 14 April (Ch 102, Tue), 14 April (Ch 103, Tue), 15–17 April (Ch 104, Wed–Fri), 20 April (Ch 105, Mon), 21 April (Ch 106, Tue), 22 April (Ch 107, Wed), 23 April (Ch 108, Thu), 24 April (Ch 109, Fri), 25 April (Ch 110, Sat).**

3. **The gang's week, the walks, the racks, and the second tin's arithmetic.** The gang is on the back length **Mondays and Thursdays at 07.30**, the weekly sheet covers Monday to Sunday, it goes into the rack on the Friday, the duplicate into a tin, and the duplicate into the second tin on the bottom shelf of the plan room of the Civic Atlas Office under a repair schedule. **The walks in April are the 6th, 9th, 13th, 16th, 20th and 23rd, and the rack days are the 10th, 17th and 24th — three Fridays, and the twenty-first of April is a Tuesday and is not one of them.** **The second tin held SIXTEEN weeks of carbon plus the one page that is not a gang sheet on Friday the third of April (inherited, printed in Chapter 100), SEVENTEEN on Friday the tenth of April, EIGHTEEN on Friday the seventeenth, and NINETEEN on Friday the twenty-fourth, which is printed in Chapter 109 and repeated in Chapter 110.** **The condition column has been empty since the twelfth of February and is empty on all six April walks, and the reason column is empty on all of them, and that is correct, and no column on that sheet wants to know why a column is empty.** **A later batch may not reduce the figure, may not put a word in the condition column about why, and may not turn the six empty walks into a record of anything.**

4. **The third weather-driven thing, and it is a plate, and it is the smallest of the three, and nobody has claimed it.** **A settlement plate with a name and a date on it came off two nails on a fence at the low end of Bridge Street on Monday the sixth of April, in wind, and was face down in the grass on the far side.** Found by **Nell Crale, sixty-three, at about ten past ten in the morning**, in passing, while a surveyor was putting a peg back in about two hundred yards of a boundary because the March returns had not been signed. Nobody was hurt, nothing fell down, it is not a seam, and **it is not a length and it is not a remnant and it is not a place of any kind that anybody in this city has a word for.** **The four doors are all correct and all four are closed:** the district's drainage book is a book of records and has no line headed *condition of notices on the boundary*; the works own the plate and the fence and the gang and their answer is November; a district with no rateable unit and no address cannot write to a company; and a surveyor would make it into a document with a reference on it, and in about four years there would be two. **A gang came on the fortnightly inspection on the eighteenth of April and eleven of them walked a hundred and forty feet of frontage with a plate missing off a fence, and there is no column for a fence.** **And nobody has said out loud that it was one of the eleven, and there is no form on which to say it, and Milo Rook has not said it and has written down that he is not going to.**

5. **The cover stopped rocking, and nothing records that it stopped, and a foreman noticed a fortnight late.** **The condition column on the gang's weekly sheet has been empty since the twelfth of February because a cast-iron cover that had been two inches proud since the second of February was found flush on the sixth of April, and nobody reset it, nobody packed it, and nobody knows when it stopped.** The foreman wrote the three words and then took them out with the flat of his thumb, and his reason is four sentences long and is in a yard at about half past three on the sixth: **I did not do it… and a foreman of thirty-one years walked past a thing that had stopped happening for four weeks and did not notice, and that is the difference between a hazard and a place.** The reason column is empty and that is the correct entry, and **there is no column on that sheet that wants to know why a column is empty.** Cyril Sale put a sentence on the back of his own form that evening, with his name and the date, and put it in the correspondence tray and not a drawer, **and said out loud that he has now done that three times since the middle of January and that he has a rule about it which is a number and not a sentence and which he will not write down, and which nobody has asked him for and which Milo Rook did not push for.**

6. **The hearing, and it is a continuation of an adjourned matter, and it was named as one.** **Tuesday the fourteenth of April, the Hall of Wards, from eleven, about thirty people, a register on a table by the door with a name in it in ink.** **Ivo Sarratt, Survey Enforcement, fifty-one, twelve years, is the reporting officer and he read the list of the business of the day aloud because, in his words, *that is the sheet of the business of the day, and it is not a notice of hearing and it does not tell you what is being heard, and I am going to say what is being heard, because there is no document in this city that is going to tell you.* The three matters are not re-argued. *One,* the grounds: not in dispute as to validity, not determined, not determined on the papers, and the department contends neither that the order was properly nor improperly made. *Two,* the classification of the eleventh: **withdrawn, not to be relied on by any party, the withdrawal stands and is not restored**, and the department's own four-month-old finding is read out in full. *Three,* the removal of the sentence *no evidence was found of any hazard to persons at the service court*: **not determined; the department cannot account for the removal and has never been able to; the reference is not a district reference and is not held by the department; the sentence cannot be restored by this department.** Nothing in this batch discharges the order, varies it, withdraws it, restores the public name to the street name index, or re-argues any Volume 01 question.

7. **Why Milo Rook spoke on the fourteenth, and the reason is statable out loud, and it was stated by Mara Quill in a corridor at ten past ten.** *In January the district had nothing to say that a minute could hold; you told me that before I spoke about it and you were correct and I did not speak. I have had three months to find a better reason and I have not found one, and what I have found instead is that there is one thing in that room this morning that is not a document, and I am the only person in it who can say the shape of the thing that is not there.* **He spoke for about two minutes. He did not speak about the department's position, and offered nothing as evidence or as a submission, and handed nothing in, and the three conditions his advocate gave him in advance were: do not put a number on it, do not put a reference on it, and do not improve it afterwards in a yard, because I will be in that yard — and you would not be able to; I would be able to.** **What he said, in the hearing, is the volume's structural statement in the mouth of the protagonist for the first time: this district cannot show the department an effect upon a person, not because nothing has happened to it, but because a name in this city's records is entered against a rateable thing and a rateable thing is a building with a street, and there are three places inside the city boundary that are not rateable things and cannot be given a street — about six hundred yards of reserved tram route, a hundred and ten yards of frontage that is not a street, and a hundred and forty feet of named frontage behind a fence — and all three are held by people who are not in any of the department's records, and the department has correctly declined to enter them, and is therefore relying on people it cannot name, cannot pay, cannot write to, and cannot ask a question of without producing a document that in four years can be produced against a person.** The chair said the matter did not stand as stated; **he said, *Then I have said it badly, and the advocate will do it again properly, and I have said it out loud in a room, which is what a thing that cannot be made into a document is for.*** **Cass Ardery said one nine-word sentence to him in a corridor: *You asked for a date. There is not one. I am sorry.*** Ardery is not the reporting officer, and nobody is added above him, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel.

8. **The permanent line was read out again and its condition was named by the department, and nobody could fix it.** **The line of the twelfth of January — no schedule of persons resident at the withdrawn alignment is to be obtained from the district by this department, in this proceeding or in any proceeding, while the answer of the fourth of December stands — was read out by Ivo Sarratt on the fourteenth and he said: *It stands while the answer of the fourth of December stands. I have to say that it is a conditional line and that the condition is not mine, and that is a thing a department should not have to say in a room, and I am saying it in a room.*** **A later batch may not remove the line, may not vary its condition, may not have the condition lapse, and may not have anybody discharge it by another route.**

9. **The cost that is not curable, paid in a room, in four seconds, by the person who drafted it.** **At about half past one on the fourteenth, the reporting officer said he was going to ask the party one question and would rather ask it now, because there is no column in this proceeding in which to ask it nicely. He asked whether the district stands the answer of the fourth of December. Lena Marr said *yes*, and he asked her to say it again into the record for his own sake rather than hers, and she said it again.** Then, at length, in the record: **if we let that line go this morning then two hundred and fifty-two people go into one schedule that a man with a pencil and nine returns fills in about four minutes, and if we keep it then there is one woman of sixty on a second landing in a building with no number who is the ninth of twenty-six names in a file in Crown Heights, and she cannot see it and she cannot come off it and she cannot ask anybody whether her name is spelled right** — and **I drafted the prohibition that is stopping her. It is forbidden by us. We asked for it. I wrote the paragraph that keeps her from her own name, and I would like that minuted, and I know exactly what the minute is going to do with it: which is that it is going to print that I asked. And that is all it is going to do, and I am asking anyway, because there is no other instrument in this city and I have had a plastic wallet for two years and I have never once been in a room that wrote a thing down for me.** **The minute prints that she asked. Nothing else moved: the levy is still two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term, charged to her name and not to the district, for two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number.** The woman of sixty on the second landing is not named and is not in the room and was not asked, and **on Wednesday the eighth of April Lena Marr went up one flight and could not repeat the twenty-three words word for word, and the condition she had set was word for word or not at all, and she did not do it and came down and stood in a doorway about four minutes.**

10. **The minute, in six paragraphs, and it is about four hundred words, and the whole of what six months bought is in paragraph four.** *1.* Grounds: not determined. *2.* Classification of the eleventh: **withdrawn; not to be relied on by any party; the withdrawal stands.** *3.* Removal of the sentence: **not determined; the department cannot account for the removal and has not been able to; the sentence cannot be restored by this department.** *4.* The line of the twelfth of January stands, and **the reporting officer has recorded that the line is conditional and that the condition is not the department's**, and the party was asked whether the district stands the answer of the fourth of December and answered that it does, and asked the department to minute that the party drafted the ask, and the department has minuted it. *5.* **The matter stands over. It is not adjourned. The department has no date to give and has not the standing to give one, and records that it is not adjourning the matter and that the matter remains before it undetermined and without a date.** *6.* **The order is suspended and is not discharged, is not varied, is not withdrawn and is not restored to the public name index. The public name is not restored by this minute and was not argued.** **A matter that ends without being adjourned is the volume's structural finding and it is a fact about a department's standing, not a decision, and the reporting officer said so in the room and asked for the distinction to be minuted and the minute does not distinguish them.** **A later batch may not discharge the order, may not restore the name, may not re-author or move anything, and may not turn *stood over* into *adjourned*.**

11. **The escalation nobody expected: a second departmental list, and the matter is not on it, and nobody can make a form about it.** **On Thursday the twenty-third of April a printed side came to the district by hand, in an envelope, because the district is a party — headed HALL OF WARDS — DEPARTMENTAL LIST DAY, BUSINESS OF TUESDAY THE FIFTH OF MAY, six items, with the same fourth line.** **The matter is not on it. Item one is *Continuations*. The order number is not on it, the string CO/6 is not on it, the date of the order is not on it, the reporting officer's name is not on it, and there is no hearing and no sentence on it. The district's name is in the first line, as it was on the thirty-first of March. It is the same district, the same envelope, the same hand, the same door.** Its items: *1. Continuations. 2. Applications, orders, variations, consents. 3. Ground and seam returns, current quarter: outstanding from sections. 4. Reports of departments and officers. 5. Maintenance returns, current year, presented. 6. Such other business as the chair may take.* **The ground and seam returns line is the same line as on the first list and this district still cannot tell which sections are on it, and that has not changed, and nobody has asked.** **The finding, in eleven seconds, from the advocate: a matter is adjourned by a chair in a room on a minute, and a thing does not lose its date by being left off a list — it loses its date by being discharged, withdrawn or varied, and none of those three words is in this district's name anywhere — and the only instrument in this city that would have put the district in front of anybody again is a list, and it goes on a board in a corridor, and the board changed on about the twentieth of April and the envelope came on the twenty-third, three days later, by hand, to a door, and nobody rang the district and nobody wrote.** **A later batch may not put the matter back on a list, may not restore the chair, may not make a form about a matter that is absent from a list, may not have anybody discharge the order to get it off, and may not expand the first list's item six.** The quay section's maintenance return was on the first list as *presented* and is not named on the second, and **no section is named anywhere on the second sheet.**

12. **The batch's changed method, and it is somebody else's, and it is the second half of a conjunction, and it is about custody and not about four people.** **The rule at the foot of the return-route envelope has read, in Hettie Brann's hand since the nineteenth of January: *If one of them is not the same, **ask the person whose hand it is and nobody else.*** Milo Rook read that rule in a corridor in the third week of March, took the first half of it and left the second, and has written down that he left it, and that he left it because the second half is about four people he does not know the names of. On Tuesday the twenty-first of April a woman of sixty-one at a counter said *it is not about four people, it is about a hand*, and the changed method is: *ask the person whose hand it is and nobody else* — and custody is a thing a person holds. **Not a tin. Not a coat. Not a table in the rain. Not an office. Not a drawer in his own office that a man of thirty-one signed for in December and has never opened. Not under a brick. A drawer under a counter is wrong-shaped and cannot be chased, cannot be produced and cannot be given a reference, because a counter is not a repository.** **This is the method the volume needed and it was written in December by a woman of sixty-one about four pieces of paper in four buildings.**

13. **Hettie Brann's third piece of paper, and she counted it out loud, and the rule at the foot has three parts.** **Under the water clinic counter there were two pieces of paper after the twenty-second of March — one from the thirty-first of January about a man with no rateable unit, one of Ola Petrossian's nine doors — and on Monday the nineteenth of April a works clerk of forty-six put a third piece of paper face down on top of them, and it is a form about a hearing that was not determined, with a seventh column with a heading in it and two lines in it, one of which has a heading in the seventh column and one of which does not.** **On Wednesday morning the twenty-second of April at about half past six, on a Wednesday, in the open, with about nine people present, because there is no coffee and about nine of them are there anyway, she said the count out loud: *It is three.* And she said that the one from the nineteenth was not a list, not a schedule, not an instrument and not about nine people's week, and that the day she chose was a day when people would already be standing about. **She is not going to be thanked and has not been. She is neither of the two people who made any of the three. A later batch may not make a fourth without her saying so out loud, may not put any of the three in a machine, may not read any of them, and may not have the holder of the envelope and the holder of the three papers be conflated — the envelope holder is the same woman and the rule at the foot of the envelope names her exclusions, and that is one woman in two arrangements and not two women.** The third part of her condition stands and has not moved since January: **if the holder is ill the round does it, and the round is a woman with a satchel who has said in a yard that she is not going to stand in a canteen and ask a man to put her round in a book.**

14. **The answer to the volume, said by a working person who has been doing it and not writing it down, and it is nine words and it is not a surveyor's.** **On Wednesday the twenty-second of April at about ten past six in the morning, on a hundred and ten yards of frontage that is not a street, with about nine people standing on it and no coffee on it, a woman of about forty-five who calls the lot prices from a doorway with no fish behind her, and who puts her thumb in a crack in the wall at the south end for about half a minute, said it: *Nobody keeps this row.*** *That is what it is. If somebody kept it then there would be a book, and in the book there would be a column, and in the column there would be my name, and the other four of us would be a line under mine, and then in about four years somebody could ask me what I did, and I would have to say it, and I would have to say why, and there is no why in it. There has never been a why in it. I do it because I do it and I have never once in nineteen years thought about doing it.* **And: *It is kept by nine people doing it and not writing it down… you cannot have a book of that, because the moment you have a book of it somebody is keeping it, and then it is not nine people, it is one person with a book and nine people who have been asked.*** And the last of it: *I have said it is not for anybody five times. It is not for a man in an office. It is not for a form. It is not for anybody and that is not modesty, it is the arrangement.* **She has not given her name to anybody, is not on any list, has not been thanked, and Milo Rook has written down four times that he has not asked for it and that he is not going to, and in Chapter 110 he states that he is not going to ask in April or in May or in the fourth year. The coffee man has not been told any of this and is not going to be, and the man of about forty who sweeps that row has not told him and has said why, in a yard, in March, unasked: *I have swept this row since I was twenty-one and I have never once told him what time I am out here and I am not going to start by telling him about a piece of paper.*** The registered distinctions are unchanged and were not re-litigated: **a length is a third kind of place, a row of frontages is a Keepsake remnant, a practice is not a place and is not a day, a habit is not a calendar, and none of the four may be converted into another.**

15. **The failure, and it is his, and it is the fifth time, and it is the same one, and the district knew the shape of it before he had finished.** A form headed **CO/6/BELL — MATTERS NOT DETERMINED**, six columns, and a seventh headed **WHY THIS MATTER WAS NOT DETERMINED**, written on Saturday the eighteenth of April between two and four in the afternoon at a kitchen table. He filled in two lines and filled them in well and both reasons are true and neither is a reason anybody can do anything with. **Cyril Sale named it before he had got to the bottom of the page — *you have got a column and it is the same column… you have got a seventh one and I know what it says because I have had one a fortnight and a fortnight and a fortnight and I have watched four men in this city fill one in, and every single one of them did it in a kitchen, and you are the fifth* — and then the half of it that is the batch's finding: *A column that wants to know why wants a person to put their name against the why. That is what it is for. There is no name on your page, and you are about to write one, and in about four years the name on that page is the name of a woman of about thirty-one who drafted the paragraph that caused the third matter, and you will have made her the officer responsible for a sentence nobody can produce.*** **The resolution is new and it is not a resolution: he did not finish it.** A heading in the seventh column and a line under words, two of six columns filled, the seventh column headed, the first of the two headings written and the second not. **A half-filled box in this authority is not an exception and generates nothing — it is the exact opposite of an exception, because an exception has to be cleared and a half-filled box does not.** He said the reason out loud and it is not modesty: **there is no row on that piece of paper for me, and I have been putting myself in a row five times.** His five failures are now, in his own hand and on the page across five chapters: a form headed *BACK LENGTH (unnumbered) — MAINTENANCE* with a woman's name struck through it, undated and unreasoned, in a tin on a bottom shelf; a form with a seventh column headed *scheduled* with about nine days of another woman's week in it, in his own coat; a yard in which he said the argument for entering a zero against his own district; a form headed *SECOND SIDE — DAYS WITH NO LINE* with a seventh column headed *why there is no line on this day*, left face up on a trestle table in the rain and gone in a wind; and this one. **A later batch may not remove the page in the tin, may not have it read, may not have Ivy Marsden told, and may not use any of the five as a device to stop him writing.**

16. **The working day, and the person is a reader of fifty-four who has never had a chapter, and the sentence is the volume's structural thesis in her mouth.** **Bettina Sallow, fifty-four, four years on a hand index at the ninth desk on the left in the Survey School's repository beneath Crown Heights: a stair of thirty-four steps, nine desks on the left, a plan chest, and a window that does not open and a chair under it.** The ordinary thing she does four times a day and has never been thanked for: **she will not tell you a document does not exist unless she has read the whole card, and she will tell you what she looked at, how far she went, and what she did not open.** **And what she said, in about eleven seconds: *Every card in this room is a place. A hand index is built out of one card for one named thing, and the named things in it are rateable things with a street on them, because that is what the printing order of this building has said since about 1830… I have been at the ninth desk on the left for four years and there is no card in it for a drain, a cover, a length, a row of shops, a fence, a woman, a market, or a hole. There is a card for a rateable thing with a street. Everything in this room is built out of that one sentence, and the building is two hundred years old and the street list is a hundred and forty.*** **And the index is not a record of what happened, it is a record of what is where — which is the same sentence the index clerk of that same room said to him in December in other words, standing up, to nine people, in that room, with the word *claim* in both of them, and she was one of the nine, and the two of them have never said one word to the other one about it, and their one exchange was nine words she wrote on a finding aid on the eighteenth of December and turned round to him and he read it and said nothing. **He has heard both halves and has not joined them, and he did not tell her that the man had said it in front of her, and she did not ask, and that was the only correct thing either of them did that morning.** The scene is on **Thursday the sixteenth of April** and the Field Book entry recording it is the same night at 22.40, which is why the two days off the fourteenth and the hundred and forty-fourth day both stand.** **And the broom, which is the method in a third pair of hands: there is a broom leaning in that office since the twelfth of March and she has not used it, because the day she sweeps under the plan chest she is the person who sweeps that room, and then somebody in about four years can ask her what she did in there and she will have to say why, and there is no why in it.** **She is registered from Volume 02 Batch 0001 and is not a new character, and she has no power, and she is not a device, and she has never been thanked.**

17. **The FORM 4 query came back a second time and stopped asking about the sentence and started asking about the box.** **Thursday the ninth of April, a fortnight after the twenty-sixth of March, and a fortnight is what a works clerk of forty-six predicted out loud: *it will come back with a sentence on it instead of a dash.* The sentence was: *Return received. Item 4 not completed. Section to complete.*** **Item four, not line six, and not *what did you mean*. The box is left empty with a line through it and a date, and now a second line through it with a second date, and there is no way to answer it that is not a lie, and the box went up empty again. And nobody has ever been asked about line five, and the nil on *persons observed on the length during the year* has now survived being read, a query on the line above it, a second query, and a defect report that could not be made — seven weeks, and the fifth box gets filled nil in January and in every January after this one, and the only true answer to it is nil, and nobody has been thanked for it.** **A later batch may not answer the query, may not have the foreman read the sixth line, may not have anybody query line five, and may not have the sixth line come to light.**

18. **A working day in a district that has decided what should happen on the day after the season, and what it decided was nothing, and then it did the thing.** **About nine people in eleven streets stood in the weather between about five and seven on Wednesday the fifteenth of April, for no reason at all, out of a guess, and they were the only people in this city who did that, exactly as a woman of sixty-one said in a yard on the thirtieth of March she expected.** The round went out at about ten past eight and came in at about seven, four hours longer than it needed, unpaid, and the water clinic counter was open from eight until six as it is every day. There was no coffee on the Row and there has never been coffee on that row on a Wednesday, and a man of about sixty was at a shed at Cardew Docks. **Milo Rook was not there and was not asked and did not go, and the reason is nine words: *I was the only one who would have had a reason*, and Rue's answer was that if he had stood in it then on the Wednesday after that he would have had a reason, and on the Wednesday after that a habit, and by the middle of May a column, and *you have been careful not to write anything down for six months and you nearly ruined it in one evening by being right about something.* He has written down that she did not say it to him twice.** **Nobody wrote it down, and the second side of the sheet of who does what and on which day has a blank on the fifteenth, and a blank on that side is a day when nobody stood in the weather, and the sheet is right, in its own terms, and it is not going to be able to be otherwise.** **This is the fourth thing in five months that has happened in this district and cannot be shown to anybody, and Milo Rook named all four out loud in a yard on the twenty-fourth of April in about forty seconds: a rectangle of standing water on the twenty-ninth of January; a hundred and ten yards of frontage in the rain on the nineteenth of February; a plate in a field on the sixth of April; and about nine people standing about in eleven streets on the fifteenth of April for nothing at all.** The two money totals are carried forward unchanged and are not printed in this batch and the multiplication is still never shown.

19. **The honest arithmetic of a season nobody could name the day of, and it is four lines long.** **Eleven weather-driven seam events were forecast for a season that ended on Tuesday the fourteenth of April, and the season ended, and therefore eleven happened or eleven did not, and this city cannot tell you which, and neither can the four pages.** **There is nothing printed in Alderquay for the fifteenth. There has never been a second outlook, and a man of fifty-one in a room with a machine in it has been in that room nineteen years and has seen one and has never known anybody who has seen a second, and he cannot tell whether the second one does not exist or whether it exists and has not been sent, and no person in this city is in a position to know.** **Two of the four in the river core have happened, in the only sense this city can put a number to: one is a line in a man's own hand in a printed sheet with a district in the row; the other happened on a hundred and ten yards of frontage in the rain in front of about nine people and there is no row anywhere in this city for it.** A third thing happened on the sixth of April at a fence and nobody has said it was one of the eleven and there is no form on which to say it. **The second column of the printed sheet is a forecast, and a forecast does not come down because a thing happened, and that is the whole of what a surveyor learned in six months about a number in a column, and it took a plate in a field and about two minutes in a room with a machine in it.** **Nine of the eleven are unallocated; the one in the Outer Reach has not been allocated; Mireward carries a typed zero, which is the third nil about Mireward in twenty weeks, and it is not going to get a fourth; the eleventh fell before or on a Tuesday; and there is no calendar anywhere in Alderquay with a day on it and the four pages have no column in which to put a day.** **Nobody has gone into Mireward this winter and nobody is going to. A later batch may not print a second outlook, may not have anybody ask for one in writing, may not explain the absence, may not resolve what happened on the fifteenth, may not add a fourth nil about Mireward, and may not have anybody go there.**

20. **The run number, advanced one step, and the plant is advanced and is not turned into the volume's final image.** **On Friday the twenty-fourth of April, at about four in the afternoon, in a room above a canteen on Parcel Street, a man of forty-one with a wet cap who is on no list at all asked a question nobody sent him, because about four times a day somebody in this district looks at a job and decides it is not theirs.** The man behind the counter is a clerk in the timetable division, **nineteen years in the job, a list of nine items in front of him, no first name and not going to get one, registered from Volume 01 as Barrow.** What he said, and it is the whole of the step: **there is a column for run number and there is a column for depot move and they have never been the same column; a number in the run column is a crew booked to a run and a number in the move column is a crew going to a gate; a booked run takes people and a depot move takes nobody and is on the sheet because the cars have to be accounted for; and the occurrence in the run column is the 07:52 booked working off Crown Heights on the morning of Tuesday the sixteenth of December, which is the car that went round, and the other occurrence is a light engine and a two-car set going to a gate, and it is not it, and I am telling you which one so that you can stop.** And the standing limit he named and is a rule for a later batch: **in nineteen years he has never once seen a crew entered in the run column for a morning when the car did not go out, and if a car does not go out the column is empty and the row is not there, because a crew that does not work does not happen.** And: **if you want to know anything else, ask nobody, and do not come here, and do not send the man from the round, and if you do any of those three things then I will say that the timetable division does not discuss working timetables outside the department, and it will be true, and it will be in a book, and it will keep.** **The card went back under the brick with a third line on the back of it in a hand that is not Milo's and is not Hester Coombe's, and the line on the front of it was not taken off, and there are three things under that brick, and there have been three since the twenty-first of January: the run-number card, the works-jacket card, and Pell Harrow's own blank card.** **Milo Rook has not written down what the clerk told him, and has recorded that as a decision and not as a shape. No destination is named, no passenger's name appears, there is no thirty-one in it, and the volume's final image — a recovered timetable containing a destination that does not exist with the passengers' names real in it — is not written in this batch and may not be written by a later one without the outline.**

21. **The orientation ledger, the Stage 2 cost, and the Stage 2 gain, and none of them moved.** **The remembered-route list stands at TEN**, the original eight intact, none removed, all ten marked by somebody who is not him, **and nothing was added and nothing was lost in Chapters 101–110. The ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained and is not explained by anything in this block.** **Milo Rook crossed nothing.** Iona Sen's tally is unchanged at nine lines, the paper is Hettie Brann's and the pocket is Dr Sen's, and he has not looked at it since the thirteenth of January. **There is no stabilisation in Chapters 101–110: he went into nowhere, held nothing, and crossed nothing, and on the fourteenth of April he stood up in a room for about two minutes and it was in the coat on his own front door and it was cold and it did nothing, and he has written that down six times and it is still the truest thing he has got about it.** **The written line home went on the twenty-fourth of November, which is day one, and the eight figures in this block's Field Book entries are 138, 142, 144, 148, 149, 150, 151 and 153**, on the tenth, fourteenth, sixteenth, twentieth, twenty-first, twenty-second, twenty-third and twenty-fifth of April, **all quotable from the chapter that owns the day** — and one further figure, **136 on the eighth of April**, is written in the prose of Chapter 101 and is the day the entry and the token were both there. **The full inherited register carried forward unchanged and not re-derived: 92, 103, 108, 112, 115, 119, 123, 127, 128 and 129 on the twenty-third of February and the six, eleventh, fifteenth, eighteenth, twenty-second, twenty-sixth, thirtieth and thirty-first of March and the first of April, and the earlier figures 35, 39, 43, 45, 46, 50, 51, 52, 54, 57, 59 and 67, 68, 69, 70, 71, 71, 74, 81, 88, 89.** The line is blank throughout, with three marks in three hands until the first of April and a fourth in a fourth hand on that day, written at a counter in four seconds by a woman of sixty-one who was not asked and refused to put it in the envelope, **and it is there on the eighth, and on the twelfth, and on the fourteenth, and on the fifteenth, and it is not a cure and it is not an absence of one, and the district's refusal to keep asking remains a refusal and not a kindness.** **The Lark token was cold throughout, was not taken out, and the eight figures in this block's Field Book entries are 124, 128, 130, 134, 135, 136, 137 and 139**, on the tenth, fourteenth, sixteenth, twentieth, twenty-first, twenty-second, twenty-third and twenty-fifth of April, **and the inherited register is 78, 89, 94, 98, 101, 105, 109, 113, 114 and 115, and the earlier figures 53, 54, 55, 56, 57, 57, 60, 67, 74 and 75. Twenty-five days is the first of January and a hundred and fifteen is the first of April and the two must not be confused.** **The Stage 2 sensory cost did not recur in these ten chapters, and it is a sound and a taste and no face, no place, no person and no name, and he has still decided nothing about whether that is one thing or two. The first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in Chapters 101–110.** **Atlas residue was used once in ten chapters, in Chapter 108, in seven words: *It is dated before it is read.*** He did not use it in the room in Chapter 104 and has written that down, and he did not use it in the yard, and he did not use it on the Row, and he has written that a wall is not an occasion for it either.

22. **The Field Book, and this is the entry a later batch is most likely to get wrong, because this is the eleventh pass and the fifth block in which a pair count has been asserted.** **The dated pairs are TWENTY-SEVEN: two sixes, seven threes, one nine, four twos, three fives, one eight, one twenty-five, two sevens, and six fours — four of them in March and two of them in April, and five of the six since the eleventh of March.** The seven new intervals written in this block are **a six (30 March to 5 April), a five (5 to 10 April), a four (10 to 14 April), a two (14 to 16 April), a four (16 to 20 April), a two (21 to 23 April) and a two (23 to 25 April)**, and the single-day interval of 20 to 21 April and 21 to 22 April and 23 to 24 April **is one day, and one day is looked at and is not written, and is not a pair.** The inherited twenty at the end of Batch 0004 was one six, seven threes, one nine, one two, two fives, one eight, one twenty-five, two sevens and four fours. **The figure must not be read as twenty-one, twenty-two, twenty-six or twenty-eight. The four threes that were here in December are the four that were here in December and are not collapsed; the fifth was added in the first week of February, the sixth on the twenty-third of February and the seventh on the eighteenth of March, and none of the original four is one of the other three. There is one eight. There is no thirty-six, no eleventh pair, no thirteenth pair, no twenty-first pair and no twenty-second pair. The threes are seven and not eight, and the sixes are two and not one, and the twos are four and not one, two or three.** He joins none of them to any other and has written down twice this month that he does not understand what twenty-seven of a thing means, and the same entry says that a man of about sixty has been proving the same sentence on a corner of a wall for nineteen years and does not know he has been written down four times. **Chapter 101's list, dated Friday the tenth of April, reads TWENTY-TWO and that figure is correct on that date and must not be read against the closing list.** The single-day interval from the tenth of December is still looked at and is still not written.

23. **The relationship, milestone 2, and the two things in it that are new are both wrong and he has written down that he knows they are wrong.** **Nothing romantic has happened and nothing is going to. No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue.
