A later volume may not re-date the decision and may not make anybody's asking into the thing that made it.** The decision, in his own book, on the first of June, at night, and carried and not improved to the twelfth: **that the plate is never reported, the works is never told, and nobody in this district ever says out loud that it was one of the eleven, and that is final, and it is not because he has found out it was not one of them, and it is not because nobody has asked, and it is because there is nowhere to put the answer and the only place it could go is a form and a form is a document — and a refusal cannot be produced and a decision can, and a thing a person intends can be described by whoever asks him what he intends, and this is the first of the whole of this volume's resolutions that is a fact about a man rather than a place.** Nobody has been told that he has decided it. A later volume may not report the plate, may not put it back, may not have anybody tell the works, may not have anybody say out loud that it was one of the eleven, and may not soften the sentence about a decision.

7. **The failure, the seventh, the same one, and the resolution, which is the first one anybody else can walk into.** **Wednesday the third of June, between about seven and half past nine, a table in a front room: a form headed *ROUND — MONTHLY RETURN (COURIER)*, six columns copied off the boy's own return, and a seventh written first in about four seconds, headed *why this round is carried by one man*, and ONE line of about eighty words in it, every word true, with no boy's name in it and no woman's and no department's, and it is the best thing he has written in two months and he read it back and did not feel the thing he felt in March and April and he has felt nothing four times now and has not decided what that is.** Named before the pencil was out, at a gate at about ten past seven the next morning, in nine seconds, by a man of forty-one on no list at all: *you have written a seventh column, and it is about a boy.* And then: ***seven.*** — **that is the seventh column and not the seventh time, and the two are not connected and the chapter keeps them apart.** **The count of the man of forty-one's nine words is a separate ledger and it now ascends without a duplicate: the FIFTH in Ch 117, the SIXTH on Monday the first of June (Ch 121), the SEVENTH on Thursday the fourth (Ch 123, both in the Field Book and implied by the man of fifty-one's earlier claim) and the EIGHTH on Friday the twelfth (Ch 128). Chapter 123 has also been given the sentence that separates the two figures, because a man who is in the gate four times a day and who has said nine words eight times since the twenty-eighth of December is a contradiction a reader trips over: *four times a day is not the same as four times a day with a job in front of me, and there have not been many of those since the twenty-eighth of December, and I have not written the number down anywhere because nobody has ever asked me for it and there is no column on a gate.*** And: *that is seven of them and I have watched six and I have never once seen you learn it, and nobody in this district has.* **The finding, from a works clerk of forty-six in about eleven seconds and it is not about the seventh column: a round carried by one man and a round carried by two return the same six lines; the return is not asking whether the round was enough, it is asking whether the work was done, and a shortage is not work undone; in about four years somebody in a depot reads twelve perfect months, sees no shortage anywhere in them, and is right, and there is no column in that authority that will let him say the other thing.** **The resolution, and it is the eighth of eight: the form is on the top of his own table in his own office, face up, with nothing on the outside of it and nothing on the back of it, and it is not in a drawer, not in a rack, not under a brick and not in a book in a coat, and he has not told anybody and the man of forty-one did not ask and the not-asking took four seconds — and a table is not custody and a table is not hidden, and this is the first of the seven that can be produced against him, and he chose it and nobody asked him to.** **And the clause written before the rest of it, in the imperative: he is going to draw another one in about four months and he will not be able to help it, and the reason this one is on a table is not that he has stopped, and nobody in this district, including him, is to turn a custody decision into a reason for not writing.** The eight resolutions, in order: **a tin, a coat, a yard, a table in the rain, a counter, a book, a sentence with a why in it, and a table in his own office with nothing on the outside of it — six of the eight are places and two are sentences.** A later volume may not remove the form, may not move it, may not file it, may not improve any of the eight, and may not use any of the seven earlier ones as a device to stop him writing.

8. **Every figure in this district is a week old, and nobody has ever asked, and there is no column anywhere for the day a figure is of.** **Friday the fifth of June, at about ten past ten, a woman of sixty-one with nineteen years on a counter reads four returns out loud, standing up, in order, to a man of thirty-one who had not asked.** Her reason, in nineteen words: **because in about four years these four sheets are going to be produced against somebody who lives in one of the buildings on them, and that person is not going to have been in this room, and being asked about a figure is a different thing from having heard a figure read out in a room by a person who was there when it was wrong. One of those two you can answer. The other one you can only be asked.** She does them in the order the post came in and not in the order of the days, **because the days are somebody else's order and the post is hers.** Then the question he asked, three words — *what day is the figure of* — and the answer, one word: ***Saturday.*** **The counts are taken on a Saturday, written up on the Friday, and travel on the Monday, and the date on the sheet is the Monday, and the figures are a week old every single time anybody in this district reads one of them, and there is no column on any of the four returns, or on any other paper in this city, for the day a figure is of, and there has not been one since about 1971, and nobody has ever asked.** And the consequence for him, in a front step at about half past eleven: **not one entry in about two hundred and forty is wrong and every one of them is late, and the last walk that went into the condition column was the twelfth of February and he entered it on the sixteenth and had believed for four months that he had been exact about it, and he is the only person in eleven streets who has the day of a figure wrong, and there is nowhere to put being right about it.** He corrected nothing: **a corrected book is a second book, and a second book is two dates against one fact.** The thing he got is **a knowledge and not a record.** A later volume may not correct the Field Book, may not print a day-of-figure column, and may not make the knowledge into a document.

9. **A return is a form with no memory, and a quarterly thing arrives twice, and both copies are in one file.** **Monday the eighth of June at about half past eleven, the envelope comes back with a date stamp across the front and nothing else on the outside of it** — not *returned*, not *not stated*, no note, no initials, no letterhead, the flap sealed again with a wafer, **and the district was not told that the waiting had stopped.** Inside it, on the authority's own paper, **CAR 214 — LINE THREE. RETURN OF SERVICE PARTICULARS, quarter ending the thirtieth of June**, the same form and the same four paragraphs and the same six boxes: item 6 is **the number of persons carried on the working of 07:52 off Crown Heights on Tuesday the ninth of June**, and a reply is requested by **the twenty-fourth of June, which is a WEDNESDAY in the chain and is thirty-four days after the date the first one asked for, which was the twenty-first of May, and the printed day on the sheet is right and the day this file used to give it was not**, and if no reply is received the item will be returned as not stated and the quarter will close with the item standing open. **Item 6 on the December return is the working of the sixteenth of December and item 6 on this one is the ninth of June, neither mentions the other, there is no column for *the same section was not completed last quarter*, no reference and no box, and they are in the same file in the same drawer in the same room eleven weeks apart, and a person who opens that file in about four years gets both of them and both of them are correct.** **A quarterly thing arrives twice. A return is not a record of a quarter; it is a form issued at the front of one and closed at the back of it, and the closing of the first one produced a stamp and no correspondence, and the issuing of the second one did not require anybody to have answered the first one, because the form has no memory. A form that had memory would be a file, and a file is a place a person can be asked about.** A works clerk of forty-six read both sheets, said *it is a return*, and then said the half of it that is true, and added that he has had four chances to give a number and none was taken. **The March quarter's item is now on a closed return as not stated and nothing is waiting for anybody, and nobody in this district was told that the waiting stopped, and no batch has closed it and none may.** A later volume may not answer either return, may not supply a figure or a refusal, may not close either item, and may not add a column to that form.

10. **The number is available, and the district is not going to put it on a line, and the reason is not the January line and is not the shield and is not the gag.** **A punch roll is about nine inches of paper in a wooden case with a hole through it for every person that gets on, it comes off a machine at the end of a run and goes in a tray, it is the only thing in this city that knows how many people were on that car, and it does not know who one of them is, and that is not a defect, and a conductor of thirty-eight said so before anybody could and she was right.** **The count for the working of 07:52 on the ninth of June is in that tray, and the district could answer the second return in about four minutes, and it is the easiest thing this district has ever been asked for, and it is not going to.** The reason, in his own book, and it is the batch's answer to the third part of the standing question and it is **not** an answer: **a number that has been put on a return once is a number somebody will ask for twice, and the second time it is not for the return; a roll is not a return and there is no column on nine inches of paper and there never was and it has never wanted one, and a machine counts it and a person does not; and if this district puts one figure on one line, then in about four years there is a woman of forty-five being asked to produce a roll for a working in a quarter, in a room, in front of somebody, by a man with a box, and that is the day the nine hundred becomes one.** **The third part of the standing question is now: a punch roll, a yard man's flag and book, a month spent in a field book — three things that are not one — and half an answer is not an answer, and there is a fourth part under the third that he has never been down to and is not going to in June and is not going to describe.** A later volume may not answer a return, may not ask the conductor, and may not make the roll a return or the return a roll.

11. **The round, and the cost that is not curable, and the two reasons that are not joined, and the refusal that is correct.** **THE COURIER ROUND GOES ON MONDAYS AND THURSDAYS AND HAS SINCE THE SECOND OF JANUARY, and it was thirty-nine minutes late on Thursday the eleventh of June because a lorry was chained across the quay section gate at about one o'clock and a man in a yellow jacket on the other end of the chain did not know the district.** **The round is not late on the sheet. The sheet is who does what and on which day; it is not a clock; there is no column on that paper or on any paper in this city for a round that was late; and a thing that is not on the sheet is not a fault of the sheet.** **THE COST, SAID OUT LOUD IN FULL FOR THE FIRST TIME, BY A MAN OF FORTY-ONE ON NO LIST AT ALL IN A YARD AT ABOUT HALF PAST FOUR: a round is carried by a postman and a postman is on a form and the form wants a depot and a depot is an address, and this district has no rateable unit and no address and has not had one since the eleventh of October six years ago when a street name came off an index, so the district cannot have a second man on its round, and that is not an injustice, and nobody has done anything wrong, and it is a design, and it is being paid at about ten minutes to five on a Thursday by a boy of seventeen with a handcart, and there is no column for it and the sheet is right.** **There are two reasons and they are NOT joined and may not be joined: the twenty-two days up the south, unasked and going to stay unasked, which is a refusal of a man of forty-one made in about four seconds in January; and the depot-is-an-address design.** The refusal that is correct, and it is a woman's: **on Tuesday the ninth of June she would not have the second return behind her counter, because a document behind that counter makes the counter a repository, and a repository is a place, and there are three pieces of paper face down under it that were counted out loud in a yard in April, and if a tram authority's return lives under that counter then the three are in a place with a return in it, and a thing in a place can be chased** — and then the finding, in front of four returns: **you left yours where it can be taken, I am not going to bring mine where it cannot, and those are not the same decision, and one of us is going to be wrong about it in about four years and I do not know which and I am not going to find out in a minute and a half.** **Neither Lena Marr nor the woman of sixty paid any part of this cost and neither of them was in the room.**

12. **A foreman is seven weeks past four months and nobody is going to ask him, and he has not given anybody that silence, and Milo Rook did not count his words.** **Thursday the ELEVENTH of June at about ten past four, in a yard, Roy Pryce, fifty-four, thirty-one years:** a man of thirty-four said in December that in about four months a fella in an office was going to ask him whether he witnessed thirty-one persons, and **four months off the twenty-second of December is about the twenty-second of April, and it is the eleventh of June, which is seven weeks past that date and is not two months past it, and nobody has asked him and nobody in this district is going to ask him, and that has been done on purpose and it is the yard's doing and not his and not Milo's.** *A thing that has never been asked about is not protected. It is just not yet asked about. And in about four years a man with a box is going to ask me, and I am going to have to say yes, and the yes is going to be about a length and not about a morning, and I have had seven weeks to think about which of those two I am going to answer and I have not decided and I am not going to be asked about it either.* **Milo Rook did not count the words. He has counted that man's nine words twice, on the second and the seventh of May, and did not count this, and noticed at about ten to five that he was not counting, and wrote that down the following night and not the afternoon, and it is the smallest thing that has happened to him in seven months and nobody in this district knows it happened and the not-telling is the eighth of his own habits and none of the eight is a resolution.** A later volume may not have the foreman read the sixth line, may not query line five, may not answer either query, and may not have anybody ask him what he saw.

13. **A boundary grew by eleven inches because a gang had a kettle, and a pin is in a hole in a road that the council owns and does not know about, and nobody has gone over the fence.** **Saturday the sixth of June, about ten in the morning, the hundred and forty feet at the low end, walked from the corner end, alone, for the third time since the twenty-eighth of April.** **The far end is eleven inches further away than it was five weeks ago, and the hundred and forty feet is a hundred and forty feet and eleven inches, and there is no form in this city that has a box for that.** The cause: a gang of four patching the carriageway, the road peg he drove himself on the second of May taken out because it was in the way of a shovel and put back in the new tar straighter than he put it in, **and the gutter peg has not moved a tenth of an inch in ten weeks and could not have, because a man of fifty-four said in May that the water takes it out twice in four years and puts it somewhere else. The water has not been here since February. A kettle has moved the boundary, in the direction of the fence, and it is correct, and there is nobody to enter it, and the standing sentence that a form cannot be wrong in a direction has been made wrong by a kettle and the foreman who said it is not going to be told.** A man of about twenty-six, unasked: **it is not ours, it is not anybody's, the road is the council's, and the council does not know it is there and is not going to know, and there are about nine of them on this side of the river and every one of them is somebody's idea of where a thing was, and in about nine years there will be a hole in this road with nothing in it and a man will put one in and not know what for.** **Milo Rook did not ask him anything else, the second time in two months.** The two ends: **one is a corner in a street because somebody built a street there and the other is a fence because somebody put one up, and neither of them is an agreement and both of them are a boundary, and a boundary does not have to have anybody in it to keep working.** The standing condition on the fence is unchanged and not improved: **do not go over the fence, and not because I have told you not to — there is nobody on the far side to stop you and there has not been for six years.** A later volume may not go over it, may not improve the nine words, may not add a chainage, and may not turn a boundary into a fourth kind of place.

14. **Two printed lists that are both correct and do not agree, and a day book with no mark between them, and a doctor who did not ask him to find a sound, and a woman at a counter who has been on that counter nineteen years and not at four rooms above a fitter's shop.** **Chapter 124 no longer describes Milo Rook as a man of thirty-one *in four rooms above a fitter's shop* — that is the works clerk's address and the only character in the block who has it — and Chapter 128 no longer puts the woman of sixty-one at four in the afternoon in those rooms either; she finishes four returns at the dispensary counter at the water clinic, which is where she has been since Chapter 100. A reader in about four years is going to put those two people in the same room, and the room is not hers.** **Tuesday the second of June, the water clinic at the water steps, the third consulting room. The water authority's *Premises at which treatment may be obtained*, printed in February, lists the clinic on seven days and not on a Sunday; the hospital's *Sunday and holiday medical cover*, printed in January and revised in April, lists the clinic on a Sunday morning from ten to one. Both are correct and four months apart and printed by two offices about a mile apart, and the authority prints from a list of premises compiled about 1839 and added to ever since, and A LIST OF PREMISES IS NOT A LIST OF WHAT IS OPEN, IT IS A LIST OF WHAT EXISTS, and there is no form in this city on which a doctor puts *these two printed lists say different things and I have checked both*, and the two printing offices have never heard of each other.** Her whole of it: **a list is only wrong when somebody has to act on it and the other one is in a different building.** What she did: **one line in her own day book with no mark between the two halves, and she is not going to either office, because if she goes the clinic is a place somebody has asked about, and being asked about is the whole of what this district has been doing for six months.** In about four years the file in the other building will say there was no Sunday service and there was. **And the two sensory losses: on the second of June, at about a quarter to ten, at that clinic, he did not hear the counter, which has made that noise every day for nineteen years, and it was not there for about four seconds and it was there again, and it did not come back, and it is a sound and it is not a taste, and no face and no place and no person and no name has come with it, and he has still not decided whether the two things he has lost are one thing or two.** **She did not ask him to find it and did not ask him to describe it and did not ask whether a face or a place was attached to it, on the stated ground that a person who is asked to find a sound starts looking for it, and looking for it is a thing you do with your hands, and then you have got a routine, and a routine about a sound is the fourth thing.** A later volume may not cure either loss, may not give either a face, a place, a person or a name, may not have a doctor ask him to listen for it, and may not add a second sounding in this volume.

15. **The timetable division's standing refusal held and was not tested, and the run number moved one step because of a person who was not sent.** **On Monday the fifteenth of June at about half past eight he picked the card up from under a brick, read four characters off the front and put it back; THE LINE ON THE FRONT OF IT WAS NOT TAKEN OFF, and there are three things under that brick and there have been three since the twenty-first of January.** **He did not go to the timetable division, he did not ask anybody anything, and he sent nobody anywhere, and the division has not said its sentence once since the twenty-fourth of April and the sentence is still true and it is in a book and it will keep.** **A person who was not sent: Hester Coombe, depot clerk, fifty-one, twenty-two years in the room at Alder Quay, who counts fares, goes down a carbon with a clerk's pencil, keeps the carbon in the second drawer, has not got a second copy and says so — and who wrote the card that is under the brick, in her own hand, on the twenty-first of January: *The run number on the carbon of the 07:52, 16 Dec. To be looked up in the working timetable. Not before Friday. — H.C.*** **She opened the book on her own shelf in her own office because a crew is a body of people and a body of people is booked to a working and not to a car, and the working has a destination, and she was booking a crew for a Monday in June. That was her job. He did not ask her about it and nobody did, and she said on purpose that nobody had asked her about a morning in December and nobody was going to.** The step, and the only one: **the four characters are in the type, in the run column and the crew column, in a bound book, on a shelf, four feet from her desk, in a room — and it is not lost and it has never been lost and nobody in the world has to find anything.** And at the door, in nine words, unasked, and the reason the chapter ends where it does: **there is a thing in the margin of that page, and it is not mine, and I have not read it and I am not going to, and I am not going to tell you when it was done and I am not going to work it out from what the page has not got on it, and I have not told anybody and I am not going to tell anybody, and if it is a woman's handwriting then I am not going to be the one who says so, and neither is the timetable division, and neither is the man from the round.** **The margin is NOT dated on the page and may not be dated by what the page is silent about; in Chapter 130 the names are dated only by the working whose page they are in, and Chapter 130 says so in eight words: a fold is not a chronology and a document's date is not established by what it is silent about. A later volume may not date the margin by silence, may not date it by the weekday it happened to share with a chapter, and may not have anybody say whose handwriting it is.** **He did not ask, he did not ask her to look, and he has not asked what is in it.** **THE PRINTED ROW AND THE DIVISION'S RULE ARE NOT IN CONFLICT AND THE DISTINCTION IS NOW ON THE PAGE, in Chapter 128's Field Book, and it is CR-043's second decision. The standing rule is *he has never once seen a crew entered in the run column for a morning when the car did not go out; if a car does not go out the column is empty and the row is not there, because a crew that does not work does not happen* — and that rule is about an ENTRY, made by a hand, at the time the working is booked. A bound working timetable is printed from a list, and a printed row is a PLAN and a plan is not a record, and four characters stand against the working because four characters are what the list says. A car did go out on the sixteenth of December and it came back; what did not happen is that it got to the end of the working, and the end of the working is a place that is not on the diagram. Chapter 128 says this in one paragraph and says that both the rule and the row are still true, which is the batch's own theme: a column takes what it is given, and a list of a hundred and forty years ago has never been queried by anybody in this city. THREE OBJECTS ARE NOT TO BE MERGED: the printed type in the bound timetable, the district's own weekly booking book, in which the same crew's number stands against the week commencing Sunday the twenty-first of December with no days against it (Ch 100, and that is the row the rule was written about), and a card under a brick with three lines on the back of it.** A later volume may not have Milo Rook, Pell Harrow or any working person ask a timetable clerk anything, may not send anybody, may not go to the division, may not have the division say its sentence twice in the same building, and may not name the Line 7 clerk.

16. **The standing conditions of the district on the morning of the sixteenth of June, gathered on one page of a book in a coat so that a person can find them together, and none of them moved.** **The order is suspended and is not discharged and is not varied and is not withdrawn, and the public name is not on the street name index, and the classification of the eleventh is withdrawn and is not to be relied on by any party and stays withdrawn, and the removed sentence cannot be restored and the department cannot account for its removal and has been asked twice and has not answered twice, and the matter stands over and is not adjourned and has no date and was not on the business of the fifth of May and is not on anything and is not going to be on the business of the twenty-fourth of June.** **The line of the twelfth of January stands, its condition is the answer of the fourth of December and the condition is not the department's, it was used once in May as a shield, and a woman of thirty-one said in a corridor that she does not know whether it is the shield or the gag and has not softened it.** **The levy is two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term, for two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number, and it is not curable; the woman of sixty on a second landing is the ninth of twenty-six names, is unnamed, was not in the room on the fourteenth of April, and is not going to be told anything by anybody in this district; the woman of about thirty-four knows all twenty-six names and has deliberately not written them down for about four years.** A round is one man short and will be one man short in July and in the fourth year and it cannot be cured from inside this district. **A boy of seventeen's own blank card is under a brick with two other cards, blank since the nineteenth of January, nobody has taken it and nobody is going to, and Tern is still not found and the twenty-two days are unasked and unsaid and no batch is going to ask.** A cast-iron cover has been flush since the sixth of April and there is no form in this city that will take a sentence about a fault that stopped. An annual maintenance return goes in every January, its fifth box is filled nil, and **nobody has ever been asked about the nil on the line above it, and a box on that return has been asked about twice and went up empty twice, and in about four years two ruled lines and a signature will not be able to tell anybody whether a clerk refused twice or whether a section ran out of answers, and Milo Rook does not know what is in the sixth line and nobody in this district has told him and nobody is going to.** **Four things have happened in five months in this district and cannot be shown to anybody, and there are ten blank Wednesdays on a sheet that is right about all ten, and the fifteenth of April is the fourth of the four and there is no fifth.** **Nothing is printed in this city for the fifteenth of April, there has never been a second outlook, nobody has asked for one in writing in six months, and a man of fifty-one in a room with a machine in it has been in that room nineteen years and cannot tell whether the next one does not exist or whether it exists and has not been sent.** A district stability column in this city carries a place as nil on the stated ground that there is no maintained seam, no recorded loss, no resident witness and no schedule, and **that is the third nil about it and it is not going to be a fourth, and nobody has gone there.** The allocation sheet is weekly, five rows, four columns, the figures hand-transferred with a pen by a man of about fifty who is fifty-one next month and has been in that room nineteen years, **the fourth column has been filled in three times in nineteen years and the line under the words *officer responsible* has never been filled in at all, and a forecast does not come down because a thing happened.** The two money totals do not agree with each other at the shilling, have never been made to agree, and are not going to be. **A later volume may not discharge, vary, withdraw or re-author the order, may not restore the name or the classification or the sentence, may not move the adjournment, may not put the matter back on a list, may not answer or close a return, may not print a second outlook, may not make a fourth nil, may not query line five, may not have the foreman read the sixth line, may not report the plate, may not ask Pell about the twenty-two days, and may not improve any of the sentences in this item.**

17. **The relationship, milestone 2, and the two things in it are both wrong and he has written down that he knows they are wrong.** **Nothing romantic has happened and nothing is going to. No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue.** **Mara Quill appears once, on Monday the fifteenth of June, in a corridor in Crown Heights, and there is a second corridor exchange in the same morning about the two returns, and both are work.** **The Lark Street grievance is not named, not resolved, not softened and not referred to in any of the ten chapters.** **Her custody register page is untouched for the sixth consecutive block: it is in a drawer with a coat in it, it has not been produced, it was not named, it was not confessed to, it was not mentioned in dialogue, and he did not ask, and he was not allowed to. IT IS HERS AND ONLY HERS, AND CHAPTER 122 NO LONGER GIVES IT TO THE WOMAN OF SIXTY-ONE: Chapter 122's Field Book first printed the page as *her* custody register page in an entry otherwise about the four copies and the woman of sixty-one at the counter, which handed one drawer with a coat in it to two women in the same volume, and it now says *a custody register page is in a drawer with a coat in it and it is an advocate of thirty-one's*, and adds *I want that written down, because a reader of this book in about four years is going to take the drawer for hers.* The woman of sixty-one's own papers are the three pieces of paper face down under the counter and the envelope in a drawer with *1 of 4. — 1.iv.* in pencil on the outside, and no batch may merge the two.** What she said about it, once, in that corridor, in June: **she is not going to say anything else about the drawer, not this week and not in June, and that is not her being cold, that is her having said it in a corridor in May and it being the whole of what she had, and she has not got a second thing and she is not going to go and find one, and she has stopped requiring anybody in this district to know things.** **And the not-telling about the season: it is the ninth week, and she said it in a corridor in June in exactly the words she was using, and she is not going to ask, and *the warmth is not coming and the not-coming is not a punishment, it is a Tuesday*.** And: **he has not asked her about the seventh of January since the fifth of May and has stopped counting and started dating, which is worse, because a date is a column — and she said *you are going to keep doing that, I am not going to ask you to stop and you are not going to stop and there is no column for either of us, and that is the whole of what this is and it is not going to improve in a year and I have stopped waiting to see whether it does.*** **The two returns: he told her he had had both and had given her neither, and the reason is that she is a party to a proceeding in a building about four hundred yards from that office and there is no column on a return for a party, and she said that was correct and it was a very cold way to be correct and it was the first thing in six months he had done that she would not have done, and she was not going to be warm about it.** A later volume may not have her hand the page over as a favour, may not have her confess unless the scene earns it at a cost, may not change the number on the outside of the envelope, may not stage a fifth comparison, may not have a second comparison before the first working day of any month, and he must not ask.

18. **A head is the only repository in this city that cannot be produced against anybody, and a number is the one thing about four people that is allowed, and a fact has no reference, and he has not joined any of them and has not written a fifth refusal of anything.** Inherited unchanged from Batch 0006: **a head is the only repository in this city that cannot be produced against anybody** (Lena Marr, a doorway, the thirtieth of April, not improved and not attributed to anybody else); **a number is the one thing about four people that is allowed, and the fact that changed in May is that she did not rewrite the date** (a woman of sixty-one, at a counter, the fourth of May, and again on the second of June when she said for the first time that she has wanted to write something next to it four times and is not going to, and that the fourth time she has stopped saying it out loud is the one she means); and **the new third thing from a works clerk of forty-six on Wednesday the third of June: a fact is the one thing in this city with no reference, and a man of thirty-one has built six places where nothing can be produced against anybody, and every one of them is a place, and a place has a reference in it the moment anybody looks for it.** **He put all three in a yard on Friday the fifth of June, in forty-one words, and said he was not going to say the sentence in a yard, and a woman of forty-five asked him for the other half of it and got it, and nobody wrote any of it down.** **He has joined none of them and has written down that he did not, and he has not added a fifth refusal of the three Volume 01 clues to anything, and this block adds none on their behalf, and the routing slip with *for index* on it is still a fourth kind of document and not a fourth instance of anything; two documents still carry a pair of marks and not three; the new carbon carries one; Kest has not matched the strings; and Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–130.**

19. **The orientation ledger, the Stage 2 costs, the Stage 2 gain, and the Field Book's twenty-seven pairs, and none of them moved.** **The remembered-route list stands at TEN**, the original eight intact, none removed, all ten marked by somebody who is not him, **and nothing was added and nothing was lost, because he did not cross.** **MILO ROOK CROSSED NOTHING IN CHAPTERS 121–130, and this is stated on the page and there is no crossing in the block and no crossing is going to be added to it.** Iona Sen's tally is unchanged at **nine lines**, the paper is Hettie Brann's and the pocket is Dr Sen's, **and he has not looked at it since the thirteenth of January and has written that he is not going to look at it in June, which is now in the book six times and has been improved none of the six.** **There is no stabilisation in Chapters 121–130: he held nothing, he went nowhere, and the token was in the inside pocket of a coat in a clinic and in a depot office at Alder Quay and did nothing.** **The written line home went on the twenty-fourth of November, which is day one, and the figures printed in this block's Field Book entries are the page home at 190 (1 June), 191 (2 June), 193 (4 June), 194 (5 June), 195 (6 June), 197 (8 June), 199 (11 June), 201 (12 June), 204 (15 June) and 205 (16 June), and the token at 176 (1 June), 177 (2 June), 179 (4 June), 180 (5 June), 181 (6 June), 183 (8 June), 187 (12 June) and 190 (15 June), with 166 (22 May), 178 (3 June), 182 (7 June), 184 (9 June), 185 (10 June), 186 (11 June) and 191 (16 June) carried arithmetic and not printed. Page-home 192 (3 June) and 198 (9 June) are likewise carried and not printed, and the page home on 22 May was not given a count in the chapter that owns the day.** **TWO FIGURES COLLIDE ACROSS THE TWO REGISTERS AND MUST NOT BE MERGED: 190 is the page home on Monday the first of June (Ch 121) and the token on Monday the fifteenth of June (Ch 129); and 179 is the token on Thursday the fourth of June (Ch 123) and the page home carried on Thursday the twenty-first of May in Batch 0006, and Chapter 123 puts the two of them side by side on purpose so that there is one place in the book where they are together and nobody is to take them together.** The inherited registers carry forward unchanged and are not re-derived. **The Field Book's pairs are TWENTY-SEVEN and they have not moved, because there is an entry in the book on every night from Sunday the twenty-sixth of April to Tuesday the sixteenth of June, which is FIFTY-TWO CONSECUTIVE NIGHTS, and every interval since the twenty-fifth of April is a single day, and an interval of one day is not a pair whether or not either of the two days has anything on it.** The composition is inherited and unchanged: two sixes, SEVEN threes, one nine, FOUR twos, three fives, one eight, one twenty-five, two sevens, SIX fours. **NO INTERVAL OF TWO DAYS OR MORE WAS WRITTEN IN THIS BLOCK AND THE FIGURE MUST NOT BE READ AS TWENTY-EIGHT, TWENTY-NINE OR THIRTY, and there is no thirty-six, no eleventh pair, no thirteenth pair, no twenty-first pair and no twenty-second pair, and the four threes that were here in December are not collapsed and there is one eight and not two.** And the sentence that came with it in May, printed once more at the top of Chapter 130's entry: **the list has not moved since the twenty-fifth of April, and that is the first thing in seven months I wanted to make go up and I could not, and I am not going to put a why next to it.**

20. **No new grammar, faction, antagonist, final enemy, or fourth kind of place, and the standing position that closes the volume, printed ten times on purpose and recorded here so that the decision is explicit and not an accident.** **THE CLOSING SENTENCE IS THE BRIEF'S STANDING LAST LINE AND IT IS THE LAST LINE OF EVERY ONE OF THE TEN FIELD BOOK ENTRIES AND IT IS NOT VARIED: *the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and that is a list of about fourteen other people and not one of them has been asked to be on it.* It is a device inherited from Batch 0006 and from the batches before it, it is a Field Book entry and never narration, it is never improved, and ten identical iterations in a volume-close block is the intended effect and not an oversight: it is the same book, the same man, the same clause, written on fifty-two consecutive nights, and a reader who notices it has been told the truth about the register. A later volume may not vary it, may not move it out of the Field Book, and may not improve it.** **A length is still a third kind of place and is not a remnant, not a failure state, and not a form of any of the seven grammars; a boundary is a place where two people agreed to have stopped and is a statement about a boundary and not a fourth kind of place; a row of frontages that is not a street is not a fourth kind of place; a practice is not a place and is not a day; a working timetable is a working timetable; a punch roll is a piece of paper about nine inches long; a returns sheet is a returns sheet; a pin is a pin; a plate is a plate; a carbon is a carbon; and a column is a column.** The grammars named in these ten chapters are Keepsake (the Row, by inheritance) and **none of the ten names a new dominant grammar and none identifies a new one.** **Sel Iri does not appear. Halden Vey does not appear and is not named. Orrin Sable does not appear. Tarin Kest does not appear. Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–130. The office of the First Surveyor is named nowhere in these ten chapters and no person stands behind it.** **The man in the works jacket is not seen once in Chapters 121–130, nobody was asked to look, no fifth sighting and no fifth refusal was made by anybody, and the four sightings are the four there were on the nineteenth of February.** **The three things under the brick are still three and there are still three: the run-number card, the works-jacket card, and Pell Harrow's own blank card, and the third of them has been blank since the nineteenth of January.** Ivo Sarratt is not in these ten chapters and neither is Cass Ardery, and nobody was added above Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel. Barrow is not in these ten chapters, has not been asked anything, has not given his name, and the timetable division's standing refusal was not tested and not broken. **A woman of sixty-eight on nothing is referred to ZERO times in Chapters 121–130 and the phrase is written in six of the ten chapters only as a count, declared, and the count stands at EIGHT across eight chapters of this volume and is not nine; and her name is not written once in any of the ten.** **The woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court is not mentioned in any of the ten, so that the two sixty-eights cannot be folded, and the woman of sixty on a second landing is a different and unnamed resident of the same building and is the ninth of the twenty-six names and is not named either.** Old Mabb is asleep and is not named and is not woken. Ivy Marsden is not told anything and the page with her name struck through on it is on a bottom shelf, unread, and a beam scale found on the nineteenth of February with its poise off nothing was not written down, and **Milo Rook did not go near the north end of that row in this block and has not gone since the nineteenth of February and has written down that he did not and has not written down why.** The two school places lost in October are still lost. A woman of fifty-four has a plot number and no bill in six years. A conductor of thirty is in a printed rule book and his own hearing still has no date. About nine people hold one name each on paper in about eight kitchens and Rue says they are wrong and will not defend them and she is right. **Owen Fitch's name is still on five copies of a sheet of paper with a floor that is not his and it has not been taken off any of them.** A drainage record keeps a mechanic's name forever and he will not swear. **And the standing position, which is the last line of Volume 02 and which this block does not improve: the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and that is a list of about fourteen other people and not one of them has been asked to be on it, and the reason he has it is that he could not think of a way to do anything else.**

21. **Atlas residue, once in ten chapters, nine words, in Chapter 130, in his own book, given to nobody and not to be given to anybody.** ***The line was made up. The names were not.*** He has not handed it to a woman of sixty-one, who has said twice in six months that the moment a thing can be used it is in use, **and he has written down that this one is worse than that, because this one would answer a question that nobody has asked.** **The six residues inherited are unchanged and were not restated in these ten chapters: *A trade outlives its pitch* (Ch 66, five), *A length is missed before it is lost* (Ch 77, eight), *Weather uses whatever door is open* (Ch 89, six), *A habit is not a calendar* (Ch 95, six), *It is dated before it is read* (Ch 108, seven), *Whoever holds the pen is not the writer* (Ch 120, eight, in chalk on the inside of a gate, which nobody photographed and which two people have not been told about and still have not been).** A later volume may not use residue more than once in ten chapters and may not repeat any of the seven sentences.

22. **Volume 01's people carry Volume 01's prices into the close of Volume 02 and not one of them is refunded, and the honest outcome of the volume is that a thing happened and cannot be shown to anybody.** **The passage happened. The only record of it is a line on a sheet about a drain in a rack in an office above a fitter's shop, with a foreman's nine words in the hours column and no column for the thirty-one, and a second copy in a second tin in a plan room is two copies and not a record, and a man of thirty-one years in that building has said so out loud and has signed for the tin anyway.** The thirty-one went home on the afternoon of the twenty-second of December and nobody came back and nobody was told, and **the fourteenth of April was a day on which a district could not show that an event occurred in it, and that is the spine of the volume and it is not a mystery to be solved and no block has found a form for it.** **Roy Pryce's reason in the hours column is still called nine words, in Chapter 69, in `state/continuity.md` item 26, in CR-026 and in the reviews, and nothing in this block re-counts it.** **The two money totals, five hundred and fifty-four pounds and forty, and forty-three pounds and two, are carried forward unchanged and the multiplication is still never shown, and neither total is printed in Chapters 121–130, and the two are inconsistent at the shilling and that is inherited and is not repaired.** **And the volume's whole gain, on the page on the sixteenth of June and not improved: paragraph four of a six-paragraph minute of about four hundred words, which is one sentence long, and it is a question and an answer, and these ten chapters did not improve it.**

---

## What Chapters 131–140 fixed on the page, in the same form as CR-013

Each item below is now prose rather than plan. **None of them changes a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder, or the planned ending. Every figure here was made from the manuscript and is quotable from a chapter in `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0131.md` … `chapter-0140.md`, after the writer's own travel-logic, weekday, day-count, pair-count, walk-and-rack, plate-count, self-audit and meta-language pass, which is item 3 in CR-044. `outline/volume-03.md` is the volume outline and was written before the chapters; `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-01.md` were not edited.** Chapters 131–140 are the **first block of Volume 03** and they inherit the Chapters 121–130, 111–120, 101–110, 91–100, 81–90, 71–80, 61–70 and Volume 01 lists above whole. **Volume 02 remains closed at Chapter 130 and nothing in these ten chapters discharges, restores, varies, withdraws, re-authors or re-lists anything in it.**

1. **THE SIX WORDS, AND THEY ARE THE WHOLE OF THE OPENING.** A district returns card for **MIREWARD** came off the same machine as about nine hundred others a month, on Wednesday the seventeenth of June, and the reason column — where the machine prints a dash on a card where nothing is wrong — carried **six words in a hand: *Asked. Nothing came.*** **It is the first sentence anybody has ever written in that column, and it is not a reason for a nil: every other reason in that column in this city is a reason that nothing is wrong, and this one is the reason a person is still asking.** A man of about fifty put the card down face up, which he has not done in nineteen years, and said *if you want to argue with me about it do it at half past six and not in this room* and *take it out of this room and do not tell me whose it is, because I have got about nine hundred of these a month and I have never once been told whose hand is on one and I am not starting in June.* **The six words are said out loud twice, in a yard on the seventeenth of June and in a yard on the twenty-seventh, and he says he is not going to say them a third time, and they are not written in his Field Book, and the reason is that a Field Book is paper and lives in a coat and this coat goes into rooms, and he calls that the third time he has done a thing of that shape in seven months.**

2. **The batch's shape, its calendar and its movement.** Ten chapters, **Wednesday the seventeenth of June to Thursday the second of July, sixteen days.** It contains **one working day with a person in it** (Ch 132, Percy), **one field or boundary scene** (Ch 133, the bridge and the grass line and the *side* column), **one failure** (Ch 134, the eighth seventh column), **one changed method and one recovery, both somebody else's work and neither of them a word** (Ch 137, a folding rule and a woman who will not let it be said in a yard), **two correct refusals that are not Milo's** (Ch 134, Garn Ollerton's and Percy's), **a cost that is not curable and paid by neither Lena Marr nor the woman of sixty** (Ch 132, forty-one years in a drawer; and Ch 139, a bar held on a Tuesday), **an escalation that is a document** (Ch 138, a reference number on a sentence), **a crossing** (Ch 136), and **one Atlas residue in ten chapters, eight words, in Chapter 136, on a bank, given to nobody: *A dry week is a promise about water.*** **The count of wrong-shaped things is not numbered in this block, and the numbering is a Volume 02 tic and Volume 03 does not carry it.** The chapter dates, all on the page: 17 June (Ch 131, Wed), 18 June (Ch 132, Thu), 19–20 June (Ch 133, Fri–Sat), 20–22 June (Ch 134, Sat–Mon), 23–24 June (Ch 135, Tue–Wed), 25 June (Ch 136, Thu), 26 June (Ch 137, Fri), 27–28 June (Ch 138, Sat–Sun), 29–30 June (Ch 139, Mon–Tue), 1–2 July (Ch 140, Wed–Thu).

3. **THE WEEKDAY CHAIN, extended and not re-derived, and the round's two days.** From Volume 02 Batch 0007, which left the chain at **16 June Tuesday**: **17th Wed, 18th Thu, 19th Fri, 20th Sat, 21st Sun, 22nd Mon, 23rd Tue, 24th Wed, 25th Thu, 26th Fri, 27th Sat, 28th Sun, 29th Mon, 30th Tue — and then 1st July Wed, 2nd July Thu, 3rd July Fri, 6th July Mon, 7th July Tue, 9th July Thu, 10th July Fri, 13th July Mon, 14th July Tue, 16th July Thu, 17th July Fri, 20th July Mon, 21st July Tue, 23rd July Thu, 24th July Fri, 27th July Mon, 28th July Tue, 30th July Thu, 31st July Fri — and then 1st August Sat, 2nd Sun, 3rd Mon, 4th Tue, 5th Wed, 6th Thu, 7th Fri, 8th Sat, 9th Sun, 9th Mon, 10th Tue, 11th Wed, 12th Thu, 13th Fri, 14th Sat, 15th Sun, 16th Mon.** **The third of May was a Sunday, the sixteenth of June was a Tuesday, and nothing in this block has re-derived either.** **THE COURIER ROUND GOES ON MONDAYS AND THURSDAYS ONLY AND HAS SINCE THE SECOND OF JANUARY, and it goes out at about ten past eight and comes in at about four, and nothing in Chapters 131–140 puts it on a Tuesday, a Wednesday, a Friday, a Saturday or a Sunday: it goes on the twenty-second, twenty-fifth, twenty-ninth of June and the second of July, it is absent on the twenty-fourth of June and the first of July, and the Saturday the twenty-seventh says so on the page.**

4. **The gang's week, the walks, the racks and the second tin's arithmetic.** The gang is on the back length **Mondays and Thursdays at 07.30**, the weekly sheet covers Monday to Sunday, it goes into the rack on the **Friday**, the duplicate into a tin, and the duplicate into the second tin on the bottom shelf of the plan room of the Civic Atlas Office under a repair schedule. **THE WALKS IN THIS BLOCK ARE THE EIGHTEENTH, TWENTY-THIRD, TWENTY-FOURTH, TWENTY-FIFTH AND TWENTY-SIXTH SINCE THE FIRST OF APRIL — the 18th of June, the 22nd, the 25th, the 29th and the 2nd of July — FIVE, all inside the block's own sixteen days, and the twenty-second and the twenty-third and the twenty-fourth are inherited as the twenty-first, and he was NOT on the walks of the twenty-second, the twenty-third and the twenty-fourth of June and nobody telephoned either time and the sheet is right about that as well.** **THE RACKS ARE THE NINETEENTH AND THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF JUNE — TWO FRIDAYS, and two, which is the number of Fridays in the sixteen days of the block. On Thursday the second of July a Friday had not been and a rack day is a Friday, so the sheet for that week was still in a drawer.** The second tin held **twenty-six** on Friday the twelfth of June (inherited) and now holds **TWENTY-SEVEN on Friday the nineteenth (Ch 133) and TWENTY-EIGHT on Friday the twenty-sixth (Ch 137)**, each figure printed in the chapter that owns the day. **The condition column is empty on every walk in the block and the reason column is empty on all five, and both of those are correct, and there is no column on that sheet that wants to know why a column is empty.** **NO TOTAL RUNNING COUNT OF WALKS SINCE THE FIRST OF APRIL IS PRINTED IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS AND NONE IS PRINTED HERE, because CR-023 requires every figure in this file to be quotable from a chapter that can be named.**

5. **The second side has THIRTEEN blank Wednesdays in it and the sheet is right about every one of them, and for the first time the reason the sheet is right is not only a shed.** The inherited ten are the eighth, fifteenth, twenty-second and twenty-ninth of April and the sixth, thirteenth and twentieth of May. **The eleventh is Wednesday the seventeenth of June (Ch 131), the twelfth is Wednesday the twenty-fourth of June (Ch 135), and the thirteenth is Wednesday the first of July (Ch 140).** On every one of the thirteen the only person who stood in the weather in **this district** was a man of about sixty at a shed at Cardew Docks, and he does not know a piece of paper exists and is not going to be told. **AND FROM THE SEVENTEENTH OF JUNE THERE IS A SECOND REASON, AND IT IS A DIFFERENT KIND OF REASON: a district entered as nil is a place this city's paper cannot see, and a sheet that is right about a day for two reasons is not more right than a sheet that is right about a day for one.** The four unshowable things in five months are **four**, and it is two and a half months since the fourth, and **a fifth thing needs somebody to be surprised and about nine people in this district were not surprised, and that is the correct condition of a district that has no chair.** No batch may make a fifth.

6. **The plate, and it did not move, and the decision did not move.** Face down in the grass since **Monday the sixth of April**. **The figures printed in the chapters are SEVENTY-TWO on Wednesday the seventeenth of June (Ch 131), SEVENTY-THREE on Thursday the eighteenth (Ch 132), EIGHTY on Thursday the twenty-fifth (Ch 136), EIGHTY-ONE on Friday the twenty-sixth (Ch 137), EIGHTY-TWO on Saturday the twenty-seventh (Ch 138) and EIGHTY-SEVEN on Thursday the second of July (Ch 140).** The decision is **the first of June**, in four chapters, and is a decision and not a refusal: **the plate is never reported, the works is never told, and nobody in this district ever says out loud that it was one of the eleven.** In Chapter 140 it is restated with the reason it does not change: **a refusal cannot be produced and a decision can, and what he intends is final, and it is not because he has found out it was not one of them, and it is not because nobody has asked, and it is because there is nowhere to put the answer and the only place it could go is a form and a form is a document.** Nobody in Chapters 131–140 has reported it, nobody has been asked about it, and the man of fifty-eight and the woman of sixty-three and the woman of sixty-one and the man of fifty with the machine are not told about it.

7. **The failure, the eighth, and it is the first one whose answer is not a person, and the ninth resolution is the first one that is not a place.** **Sunday the twenty-first of June, in a room in Cobb Lock, a form headed *DISTRICT RETURNS — MIREWARD (no rateable unit)***, a seventh column written first in about four seconds and headed ***why this district is entered as nil***, **one line of about a hundred and ten words in it, every word true, and no name in it, because there is no name to put there.** Named on the Monday morning at about seven at the top of the raised path, in about eleven seconds, by a man of fifty-eight, **and it is the same mistake and it is the worst of the eight for one reason: for seven months the problem has been that there is a person on the other end of the paper who does not want their name on it, and this time there is nobody on the other end of the paper, and the paper does not know that, and he knew it on the Sunday morning before he finished the hundred and ten words.** **A column that wants a name and has nobody to be given one is the same thing as a column that wants a name and is refused one, and both of them are a form that wants a person.** The **ninth resolution is the first one that is not a place: he hands the paper to the person the column is about.** Garn Ollerton refused in about nine seconds and told him who to ask and made sure he could not come back and say she had not; Percy refused in four seconds in the same voice in the same order as the last one. **It is in the bottom of a bag with the Field Book, face down. It is the first of the eight kept by a person rather than by a place, and a person is not a custody, and the keeping is a third thing and the third thing is the one he actually does.** The eight of his own resolutions, in order, are unchanged: **a tin, a coat, a yard, a table in the rain, a counter, a book, a sentence with a why in it, and a table in his own office with nothing on the outside of it.**

8. **A pair is a fact about where he was and not about what happened, and the list moved, and this is the only thing on the ledger that moved for the better or the worse in this block.** **THE FIELD BOOK'S PAIRS ARE TWENTY-EIGHT AND THEY MOVED ON THE NIGHT OF THURSDAY THE TWENTY-FIFTH OF JUNE, and it is the first movement since the twenty-fifth of April — seventy-seven days.** **THE INTERVAL IS SEVEN DAYS, from the eighteenth of June to the twenty-fifth, and it moved because he was asleep for six nights in a place with no address with the book in the bottom of a bag and did not open it once.** The list now reads **two sixes, SEVEN threes, one nine, FOUR twos, three fives, one eight, one twenty-five, THREE sevens, six fours**, and **the retired figures must not come back: there is no thirty-six, no eleventh pair, no thirteenth pair, no twenty-first pair, no twenty-second pair, no twenty-eighth pair, and the list must not be read as twenty-one, twenty-six, twenty-nine or thirty.** **The four threes that were here in December are the four threes that were here in December and are not collapsed; there is one eight and not two.** **AN INTERVAL OF ONE DAY IS NOT A PAIR WHETHER OR NOT EITHER OF THE TWO DAYS HAS ANYTHING ON IT, and the run of fifty-two consecutive nights from the twenty-sixth of April to the sixteenth of June is over, and Chapter 140 says what replaced it, in his own words: there is a habit, and a habit is not a discipline and a habit is not a method.** **THE FIRST MISSING ENTRY IS NOT LIKE THE OTHERS AND THE CHAPTER SAYS SO: the Friday the nineteenth of June was a DECISION — there is a piece of paper in his coat and he was not going to sit down and write a book entry about a piece of paper — and the five nights from the twentieth to the twenty-fourth were not a decision at all.** And in the same entry: **I did not work the interval out on the Friday and I did not work it out on the Saturday and I did not work it out on the Sunday night or the Monday or the Tuesday, and the Friday was a decision and the other five were not, and the decision was for the wrong reason.**

9. **A resident witness is a person on a list, and that is the standing question's first part turning over, and the eleven streets and Mireward are the same kind of place.** A season of weather-driven seam events was issued for the period in which a district entered as **nil** and marked stable, **on the stated ground that there is no maintained seam, no recorded loss, no resident witness and no schedule.** **He has had that sentence in his own book since February and has never once turned it over, and it turns over like this: a resident witness is a person on a list, and there has not been a person on a list in Mireward since about 1911, and a hundred and forty people have been living in it the whole time, and the entry has never once been wrong about a fact and has been completely wrong about a place for sixty years. A district that is not a place cannot be frightened, and that is not a design and there is nobody in it to have designed anything.** **The eleven streets have a gang, a foreman, a weekly sheet, a rack, two tins, a condition column and a reason column; Mireward has a man of fifty-eight and forty-one years of a pad in a drawer in Crown Heights. Both of those places are the same kind of place and one of them has stationery.** And the transport fact, which is the cost of the whole block: **there is no bus and there has not been one since about 1961, because a bus wants a place to stop and a place to stop is a rateable thing; there are two motor vehicles a week in a district of a hundred and forty people and neither of them waits, and there is one tractor, and the tractor belongs to a man who will not be named on anything.** A person with an address can be in a place on a Tuesday because being in a place is not a day off; a person without an address cannot, **because the being there is a day off and the day off is somebody else's morning.**

10. **The answer the volume is built on, in a working man's mouth, and it is not Milo's and nobody has been told it is his.** **A place is not stable because nothing has happened in it. A place is stable because there is nobody writing down what happens in it.** Said on a raised path at about eleven on Thursday the eighteenth of June by Percy, fifty-eight, with a rod over his shoulder, and said to about four people in forty-one years, **two of them on his way and one in a pub**. **Milo Rook had written the same sentence in his own book in February out of something a man of about fifty said on a blackboard in a drainage section, and neither man knows the other said it, and he is not going to tell him and has written that down.** The chapter says it is the truest sentence anybody has said to him in seven months and **it is not put in a yard and it is not improved and it is not attributed to anybody else.** A later volume may not improve it, may not move it, and may not make it his.

11. **Half a bridge is a structure and the other half is a route, and the maintenance is on the wrong half.** A brick deck, three spans, a council parapet on the near side, a kerb, **eleven yards of grass** and a raised path. The boundary file is a quarter of an inch and complete: nine inspections in a hundred and thirty-one years, a load calculation revised twice, **a schedule of works with a column for *side* — every entry says CITY except two, and the two say EXT, and the key on the inside cover says *external — see the drainage board*, and the drainage board was abolished in about 1968 and its functions went to a body called the fen commission, and there is no fen commission on any letterhead in this city and has not been one since 1968.** **Half of that bridge has been maintained by this city for a hundred and thirty-one years. The other half has been maintained by nobody since 1968. And the half that is maintained is the half a lorry can stand on, and the half that is not maintained is the half that people walk on.** A man of about fifty with a mower has cut the city side for nine years, **has never been over, and says there is a line in the middle of it that is not painted, it is just where the mowing stops, and there is a different grass because there was a man used to cut that side and he stopped.** **A boundary is a place where two people agreed to have stopped, and nobody agreed to have stopped at this bridge: two things stopped, for about the same reasons, and neither of them was a person, and that is a different sentence and both are written down and they have not been joined.** And Garn Ollerton, at about three on the landing: **mine is a place where everybody agrees where the grass stops and nobody agrees about anything else.** **HE CROSSED IT AT ABOUT TEN PAST SEVEN ON THE FRIDAY MORNING AND IT TOOK ABOUT NINE SECONDS AND NOBODY STOPPED HIM AND THERE WAS NOBODY ON EITHER END TO SEE IT HAPPEN, and this is the first boundary in seven months that he did not not-cross, and the first fence in seven months that he went over, and the hundred and forty feet and eleven inches and the kettle are inherited and are not undone.**

12. **Nine tides is not nine years, and it is the first finding in seven months that runs the other way.** A woman of thirty-seven with a folding rule put his own figure right in about four seconds on Friday the twenty-sixth: **that is not nine years, that is nine tides; a man does not cut a mark because a year has gone by, he stands there when the water is at a place and cuts a mark, and the water comes about four times a year.** **He wrote a number of years onto a mark that has never had a year on it because he has got four marks in four hands in a line home and he is so used to a number with a date behind it that a number without one looks wrong to him.** And the recovery, and it is hers and not his: **he was wrong about a number and a person standing next to him told him so and it cost nothing and it is the best hour he has had since the first of April. He did not write it in a form and he has not written it in a form since, and the reason is that there is nowhere to put it: there is no returns card for a mark on a wall, no column on a gang sheet, and no box on a bridge file, and there is not going to be one, because the marks are on the half of the bridge that has been nobody's since 1968 and the file has a column for side and the side is not CITY.** And the finding: **two sets of marks about ninety feet apart, cut by two different hands, recording the same water on the same days, in a place with no address, and neither set of them is in a file, and the pair of them is a return route, and a route that cannot be produced cannot be asked after, and that is the only protection any route in this city has ever had.** **Every conclusion this district has produced in seven months has been that something was going to be found out about a person. This one is that a thing is safe because there is nothing to find, and he has no instrument for that and is not going to build one.** A later batch may not turn this into an instrument and may not improve it.

13. **A ferryman does not navigate to a place, and this is the first route instrument in this series written by somebody who was not going to be on it.** Four landings, twenty-six years, eleven minutes to the second, nineteen to the third, the fourth is the end of the water. **There are about nine routes on that water and every one of them was written by a person who was not going to be on it, and there is not one piece of paper about any of them, and if you put one on paper you will have killed about four of them inside a year, because a route on paper is a route somebody maintains and a route somebody maintains is a route somebody can be asked about.** The method, in full: **if you want to know whether somebody can get from the fourth landing to your office you do not ask the fourth landing; you stand on the fourth landing and you watch them come in, and you go backwards from the boat, and the last thing you do is the first thing they will do, which is get off it.** She also said, of the mark and the bell: **I do not want it fixed. I want it written down that it is low, because the day a man comes out here with a level and puts it right, about nine families lose the only thing they have got, and there is no form in this city for a thing that nine families use and that is not a building.** And she refused, in nine words, to take him again this week, and said it was not a rule and she had no reason he would print.

14. **THE TENTH CROSSING, dated, placed, costed and read out, and the four conditions are the same four words they have been since October.** **Thursday the twenty-fifth of June, in at 14.20 and out at 15.02, forty-two minutes inside, from the old ford in Mireward, on a Thursday afternoon in the fourth week of June, in a dry week, in a district entered as nil.** The four conditions said out loud at about ten past two **to a drain**, in front of about six people, in order: **a person walks him in and out and they are not him — Garn Ollerton, ferry pilot, thirty-seven, twenty-six years, doing her run, not asked and not offered; a count held by a person and not by a box — Sabra Trewin, thirty-four, nine years teaching, holding it in her head, nineteen children on no roll anywhere in this city, not writing this one down either; the time stated before he goes — in at twenty past two, out by five past three, forty-two minutes, said in advance so that if he is not out there are six people who know where he went and what time he said; and the clinic in the morning — Ola Petrossian, twenty-three, a nurse, on the bank since about half past ten, unasked, on her day off, walked four miles from a yard at the north end, and paid by a clinic with no record of where she went.** Read out in a yard at about half past five on Friday the twenty-sixth to about nine people with the tally, **the eighth time, and the fourth time in a place that is not this district's yard, and the tally has TEN LINES on it, and the paper is Hettie Brann's and the pocket is Dr Sen's and he has not looked at it since the thirteenth of January and is not going to in July.** **Milo Rook is across TEN TIMES IN TOTAL: eight in Volume 01, the ninth on the twenty-second of December, and this one. It cost one route, and the remembered-route list is ELEVEN, and the ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained and he wrote in his own book on the twenty-fifth that he is not going to explain it before the end of July.**

15. **What was inside, and what was not, and the seam is not a street.** **It had no edge and the far side of it was more of the same.** The ford has been dry since 1919 and is bone dry and hard, and **the water was up to a mark on the left-hand parapet about two feet below the top, and the mark is a line of tidemark, and there were three of them, and the top one was newer than the bottom one by about nine years — which is wrong, and is Chapter 137.** The ground went on being the same ground and the water went on being where the water was and **the two of them were together.** The smell of a fen in a dry week was about four times what it was outside and **it came from underneath the whole length of it rather than from anywhere, and he has lost a sound and a taste and this was neither of them, and it is the third time in a fortnight it has arrived through a sense he has not lost.** And at about four hundred yards along it, **a post with a school bell on it, five inches across the mouth, on a post of about seven feet, with no school attached to it and no school anywhere near it, and nineteen marks cut into the post low down in a row, two of them still pale on the Wednesday.** **It had been rung nine times. Not while he was there. He stood under it for about eleven minutes with his watch out and it did not ring, and he wrote all nine times down afterwards and he could not put a ring on any of them. He did not touch the post. He did not go and look at the marks from underneath. There was nothing else in the forty-two minutes and he came out, and this is the first of the ten he has been in and out of that he has come out of with less than he went in with, and he has got no demand and no service and no failure out of it yet.** **And there is no demand, no service and no failure out of it yet, and a later batch may not resolve it in Batch 0002, and the Rainway is the volume's midpoint and is not named in these ten chapters.**

16. **A hand on a card, and then a number on a hand, and the machine took the hand over.** The mechanism, in full: **the man of about fifty put the card down face up, and face up is not a state a card is in, and once a week at about four in the afternoon the day's cards are re-run so that a face-up one goes back through the machine in case an operator has left it the wrong way up, and there is a line at the foot of that run sheet that says *re-run — face up only*, and the line is on the form, and the form is about a printing error and not about a district.** So there are two cards, and one has no number, and **the other has a number in the top left corner, and the number is a reference in a series that runs from something like 41,000 upward, and about four hundred of those go out of that room a week.** And the whole of it, in a corridor, in about eleven seconds: **a sentence is a thing a person can be sent to look at, and a sentence with a reference on it is a thing a person can be asked for, and the asking is done in a form, and the form goes in a file, and about four years from now somebody can ask a room for 41,000-and-something and a room can produce it, and the hand is not on the form. The hand was on the paper and the machine has taken the hand over and there is nothing in this city that can get a machine to give a hand back.** The man of about fifty: **I have put a card face up twice in nineteen years and one of them was a card for the river core that had a coffee ring on it, and I did that in about 1981 and I have not done it since and I did it on Wednesday and I would like to go back to about 1981.**

17. **The cost that is not curable, and it is a wage, and it is paid by a man of forty-four and not by Lena Marr and not by the woman of sixty.** A ratepayer is a person at an address, **and a man with no address is not a person to a form, so the water authority cannot bill Coll Sarrowe for the water he is holding and cannot employ him to hold it and cannot write a letter to him that would arrive, and he is doing it out of a farm wage by the hour for ditching, and the farm pays him for the Tuesday and the Tuesday is not a ditch and the farm does not know that either.** His own account of what the cost is: **the cost is not mine, the cost is that it is a Tuesday; if I am somewhere else on a Tuesday in a wet week then about eleven houses have a bad morning, and nobody will ever write that down, and in about four years somebody will say the dyke failed, and the dyke did not fail, and there is a bar in it.** One rescue in nine years: a woman of about thirty and a boy of about nine off a top bank in July, **two hours with a chain and the tractor, and the tractor is not insured to be in that water and I knew that, and the chain came off the second time and I went round it three times, and the boy came out on the third and the woman came out on the fourth attempt, and the tractor is a tractor.** And there is no form for it: **a form for an accident at work and I am not at work, a form for a drowning and nobody drowned, and a form for a rescue, and a rescue asks who they were and where they lived and what their number was, and I have got none of those, and the tractor has a serial number and the tractor is not a person.** And the refusal, and it is the batch's second correct refusal about a name: **a named ditch keeper is a ditch keeper who can be got rid of; there have been four of us named in this district in thirty-one years and three of them left inside two years, and the one who is still here is the one who is not, and that is not a mystery and it is not a grievance, it is just what happens.**

18. **A village that has no address is not a place that has been forgotten.** Cobb Lock: about a hundred and forty people in high water, **no address, and the address is not missing, it was never there** — a school is a rateable thing, a rateable thing needs a street, the village had a street number in about 1911 and it was renumbered for a drain and the new numbers went on a sheet in a drawer and nobody has ever asked for the sheet. **Nineteen children on a Tuesday and twenty-one on a Monday, and the roll exists only in Sabra Trewin's head, and that is not a system of hers, it is the absence of a system.** They are not on the education roll, the relief list, or any clinic's records, **and if there was a flood tomorrow they would be counted on the back of a bus by a man with a pen, and that would be the first piece of paper ever made about these nineteen children and it would be made on the day by somebody who does not know any of their names — and nineteen is exactly a number a bus can be counted out of, and if she is on that bus then it is gone.** The refused address, in full, and it is the sentence this district has been standing in for seven months with a different name on it: **a village that has no address is not a place that has been forgotten; a place that has been forgotten was on something and came off it; this was never on anything; nobody did this to us; there is no order, there is no sheet, and there is no meeting at which anybody decided that Cobb Lock should not have a street number, and there is a drawer in Crown Heights with the numbers in it from 1911 and it has been in that drawer the whole time, and if somebody went and got it out and put it back we would be a place with a number inside a fortnight, and nobody has ever gone; and it would be a thing that could be taken away again, and we have been un-takeable for about sixty years by accident, and I am not going to be a woman who asks a city to do us a favour.**
