19. **The date came and went, and a date that passes is not a thing that can be late.** **Wednesday the twenty-fourth of June, the only date this district has had since the fourteenth of April, and it was a date about a car.** It went at midnight on Tuesday in an office in another borough, and **the round did not go, and he was about eleven miles away, and nobody in eleven streets stood in the weather, and the twelfth blank Wednesday is right.** **The district was not going to answer it and did not answer it, and there is no form in this city for a date that arrives and no form for a date that passes, and the two are the same form and it is a piece of paper about a quarter that is already over.** The return will be closed with the item standing open, and **in about four years somebody will pull that file and find two item sixes, one with a June figure and a date and one with a December figure and a date, and no column anywhere in that authority for the difference between the two, and both of them are correct and neither of them is late, because late is a thing that happens to a person.** He found out about it in a yard on Saturday the twenty-seventh from a woman of sixty-three with a hand barrow, in about eleven words.

20. **The handrail post is four inches shorter than the one it took out, and the file says *replaced like for like*, and about nine families navigate by it.** Put in about 1979 by two men from a yard who were not liars and the timber was not bad, **and it is the only thing anybody has ever used to measure that path, and the note in the file is in about 1974 and it says *replaced like for like*, and it was not replaced like for like, and there are about nine people on this path who can tell you which post that was and there is nobody in Crown Heights who can.** And the same file holds the key that leads to a body abolished in 1968. **Two documents about the same bridge, one of them wrong in a direction, both of them correct on their face, and a load calculation that is about a structure and a post that is about a route, and the council maintains the structure.**

21. **The relationship, milestone 2, moved once, and it was about a form, and she told him something first.** No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue. **The Lark Street grievance is not named, not resolved, not softened and not referred to in any of the ten chapters.** **The custody register page is untouched for the seventh consecutive block: it is in a drawer with a coat in it, it was not produced, not named, not confessed to, not mentioned in dialogue, and he did not ask, and he was not allowed to.** He has now not told her about the season for **eleven weeks** and she said in June that she is not going to ask. **What is new is work and it is a document, and it is the first time in six months she has told him anything at all**: **there is a line in a return that I reconcile on the last Thursday of every month which has nothing to do with you and nothing to do with a tram, and I have looked at it, and I am not going to tell you what it says, and I am telling you that I have looked at it.** He asked what kind of line it was. **That is the second time, and I said once. He did not ask again, and he wrote that he had not, and that if she tells him he is going to be a person who has been told and that is a different column — and that his not-asking about the drawer and his not-asking about the line are the same act, and that he has known that since the fifth of May and has done nothing about it.** **That line in that return is the only thing in Chapters 131–140 that is about land, and it is not explained here, and she will not explain it, and it is the seed of Batch 0002.**

22. **A head is a person on a list, and three things that are not one, and he has not joined any of them and has not written a fifth refusal of anything.** Inherited unchanged and not restated as a pattern: **a head is the only repository in this city that cannot be produced against anybody**; **a number is the one thing about four people that is allowed**; **a fact is the one thing in this city with no reference, and a man of thirty-one has built six places where nothing can be produced against anybody, and every one of them is a place, and a place has a reference in it the moment anybody looks for it**; and **the three clues from Volume 01 remain three and are unjoined for a hundred and forty chapters** — **nobody in Chapters 131–140 joins any of them to anything, including the working timetable, and he has written down that he did not.** **The two marks on a bridge and nineteen marks on a post are NOT a fourth clue and NOT a second instance of anything, and the routing slip with *for index* on it is still a fourth kind of document and not a fourth instance of anything; Kest has not matched the strings; and Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–140.**

23. **The orientation ledger, the Stage 2 costs, the Stage 2 gain, and the day-counts, and none of it moved except two figures that were supposed to.** **The remembered-route list is ELEVEN and the crossings are TEN and the tally is TEN LINES, and every crossing cost one route, and the ninth's failure to cost one is still unexplained.** **There is no stabilisation in Chapters 131–140: he held nothing, he pinned nothing, and Chapter 136 says he could have and wrote down that he could have.** The written line home went on the twenty-fourth of November, which is day one, and **the page-home figures printed in this block are 206 (17 June), 207 (18 June), 214 (25 June), 215 (26 June), 216 (27 June), 218 (29 June), 219 (30 June) and 221 (2 July)**, with **208, 209, 210, 211, 212, 213, 217 and 220 carried arithmetic and not printed. The token, day zero = the seventh of December, is printed at 192 (17 June), 193 (18 June), 200 (25 June), 201 (26 June), 202 (27 June), 204 (29 June), 205 (30 June) and 207 (2 July)**, with 194, 195, 196, 197, 198, 199, 203 and 206 carried and not printed. **THE TWO REGISTERS MUST NOT BE MERGED AND 207 IS IN BOTH AND MEANS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS: the page home on Thursday the second of July, and the token on the same day, both at 207, and they are different registers and a reader of this file in about four years is going to try to make them one.** Twenty-five is the first of January, a hundred and fifteen the first of April, a hundred and thirty-nine the twenty-fifth of April, a hundred and seventy-nine the twenty-first of May, a hundred and five the sixteenth of June, and a hundred and five the page home on the sixteenth of June, and the last of those is the page home. **Failing sensory detail: the sound recurred once, on Wednesday the twenty-fourth of June at about ten past eleven at a clinic counter, for about four seconds, and it was there again and it did not stay, and a seam physician refused for the second time to ask him to describe it or to find it, and said the reason again, that a person who is asked to find a sound starts looking for it and a routine about a sound is the fourth thing. It is a sound and a taste and it has no face, no place, no person and no name, and he has decided nothing about whether that is one thing or two, and it may not become Neris, it may not become Lark, and it may not become a key.** **The first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in Chapters 131–140.**

24. **He gave up the seventh column for two months, in his own hand, on the facing page, and it is the first thing he has ever put there that is not a measurement, and it is not a resolution and the cost is on the page.** Wednesday the first of July, at about half past four: **No seventh column before the first of September**, and then four lines under it, and then the cost nine minutes later. **WHAT STOPS ON THE FIRST OF SEPTEMBER IS HIM AND NOTHING ELSE STOPS ON THE FIRST OF SEPTEMBER.** Roy Pryce's condition column will be empty for another eight weeks; **on the eleventh of July a man of fifty-four will be eleven weeks past the four months and he is not going to be asked, and that silence was the yard's and it has now got him in it in his own handwriting, and he cannot get it out of his handwriting**; and **on the third of July a man of about fifty is going to transfer a zero off a sheet with a pen and the zero is going to be a zero, and there is no column in this city, in that room, in that building or in this book for the man who could have gone.** And the sentence that makes it a character beat and not a resolution: **that is the most self-serving sentence I have written in seven months and it is dressed up as a discipline and it is not one, and I know exactly what it is, and the knowing has not stopped me writing the line.** **He has four of his own now — the not-telling, the plate, the table, and this — and none of them is a habit, and a habit is not a discipline, and the run of fifty-two consecutive nights is over and what replaced it is a habit and it is not a method.** **A later volume may not break the line and may not report that he broke it, and a later batch may not treat it as a resolution, and the block may not be improved by anybody else standing in a yard and saying it is the answer.**

25. **The standing conditions of the district on the morning of the second of July, gathered on one page of a book in a coat, and none of them moved and one of them is new.** The order is suspended and is not discharged and is not varied and is not withdrawn; the public name is not on the street name index; the classification of the eleventh is withdrawn and is not to be relied on by any party and stays withdrawn; the removed sentence cannot be restored and the department cannot account for its removal and has been asked twice and has not answered twice, **and a query came back down a second time in April and stopped asking about a sentence and started asking about a box, and the box went up empty a second time with a second line through it and a second date**; the matter stands over and is not adjourned and has no date and is on no list, **and the twenty-fourth of June came and went on a Wednesday**; the line of the twelfth of January stands and its condition is the answer of the fourth of December and is not the department's; the levy is two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term, for two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number, and it is not curable; a woman of sixty on a second landing is the ninth of twenty-six names, is unnamed, and is not going to be told anything; a round is one man short and cannot be cured from inside here; a boy's blank card is under a brick with two other cards and has been blank since the nineteenth of January; a plate is eighty-seven days in the grass and is never reported; a cover has been flush since the sixth of April and there is no form for a fault that stopped; an annual return goes in every January with its fifth box filled nil and a box asked about twice and **a sixth line of fifty-four words that nobody in this district has read and that Milo Rook does not know the contents of**; four things in five months cannot be shown to anybody and there are four; thirteen blank Wednesdays are on a sheet that is right about all thirteen; there has been no second outlook and nobody has asked for one in writing and the absence is not explained; **a foreman is ten weeks past four months and on the eleventh of July it will be eleven weeks, and he is not going to be asked, and that silence is now partly Milo's**; a timetable clerk was not asked anything and no working person was sent anywhere and the division's sentence has not been said since the twenty-fourth of April and will keep; Tern is not found and the twenty-two days are unasked; and **a woman of sixty-eight on nothing is not named in these ten chapters and is not referred to in them, so the count stands at eight, and the woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court is not mentioned, so that the two sixty-eights cannot be folded.**

26. **No new grammar, no new faction, no new antagonist, no new final enemy, and no fourth kind of place.** **A length is still a third kind of place and is not a remnant, not a failure state, and not a form of any of the seven grammars. A boundary is a place where two people agreed to have stopped and is a statement about a boundary and not a fourth kind of place. A practice is not a place and is not a day. A ferry route is a ferry route. A tidemark is a tidemark. A pad is a pad. A bell is a bell. A sluice is a sluice. A bridge is a structure on one half of it and a route on the other and it is neither of those things by itself.** **The grammars named in these ten chapters are Keepsake (the Row, by inheritance) and NONE of the ten names a new dominant grammar and none identifies a new one, and the Rainway is not named, and a Weather remnant in Mireward is not identified, and the volume's midpoint is not written.** **Sel Iri does not appear and is a Volume 4–10 character. Halden Vey does not appear and is not named. Orrin Sable does not appear. Tarin Kest does not appear. The office of the First Surveyor is not named in these ten chapters and did not request, receive or act on anything. Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–140. Nobody was added above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel.** And the closing sentence stands, unimproved, in ten Field Book entries and the volume's first block: ***the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and that is a list of about fourteen other people and not one of them has been asked to be on it.***

27. **Atlas residue, once in ten chapters, eight words, in Chapter 136, on a bank at a ford, in his own book, given to nobody and to be given to nobody: *A dry week is a promise about water.*** **The whole of what the chapter says about it is this: he wrote it down and did not act on it and did not know what it was about, it is the eighth time anything like it has appeared in a book in seven months, he is not going to hand it to a woman of sixty-one at a counter, he is not going to say it in a yard, and there are six people on a bank and none of them has it.** **No year, no name and no speaker is attached to it anywhere and none may be, and the line must not be dated by what it looks like a reference to.** The seven residues inherited are unchanged and were not restated in these ten chapters: *A trade outlives its pitch* (Ch 66, five), *A length is missed before it is lost* (Ch 77, eight), *Weather uses whatever door is open* (Ch 89, six), *A habit is not a calendar* (Ch 95, six), *It is dated before it is read* (Ch 108, seven), *Whoever holds the pen is not the writer* (Ch 120, eight, in chalk on the inside of a gate, which nobody photographed and which two people have not been told about), and *The line was made up. The names were not.* (Ch 130, nine, given to nobody). **A later volume may not use residue more than once in ten chapters and may not repeat any of the eight sentences.**

---

## What Chapters 141–150 fixed on the page, in the same form as CR-013

Each item below is now prose rather than plan. **None of them changes a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder, or the planned ending. Every figure here was made from the manuscript and is quotable from a chapter in `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0141.md` … `chapter-0150.md`, after the writer's own pass, which is item 3 in CR-046. `outline/volume-03.md` was NOT edited, `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-01.md` were not edited, and the volume's final image — a village school painting its route on a wall in several colours, none of them official — is NOT written and belongs to Chapter 200.** Chapters 141–150 are the **second block of Volume 03** and they inherit the Chapters 131–140, 121–130, 111–120, 101–110, 91–100, 81–90, 71–80, 61–70 and Volume 01 lists above whole. **Volume 02 remains closed at Chapter 130 and nothing in these ten chapters discharges, restores, varies, withdraws, re-authors or re-lists anything in it.**

1. **THE INSTRUMENT, IN FULL, IN THE WORDS OF CHAPTER 141, WHICH OWNS IT.** *CONTINUANCE OF SURFACE AND DRAINAGE WORKS — DISTRICT SCHEDULE, SEASON 3.* Printed both sides, four sides in all, issued under a section number and not a name, with **the season's reference printed at the top in the size of the title**. Under the title, in smaller type: ***To be completed by the person responsible for maintenance in the district and returned within seven days of issue.*** Six columns: **district; stability of district; works of record; units benefited; ground of discontinuance; signature of the person responsible.** **A KEY OF FOUR GROUNDS, PRINTED ON THE SECOND SIDE UNDER A RULE, AND EVERY WORD OF THE KEY IS A WORD THIS CITY CAN STAND BEHIND: (a) no maintained seam of record; (b) no resident witness; (c) nil returns; (d) works not reimbursable at the present rating.** At the foot of the last side: ***A district that has not returned within the period stated will be completed by this office.*** And at the foot of the last side, under a short rule, **a printed box for a signature with nothing on the rule: OFFICE OF THE FIRST SURVEYOR.** **IT IS A LAND INSTRUMENT WEARING A WEATHER COAT, and the whole of what the weather does on it is to be the occasion.** It arrived in the quay section office **in a bundle with the week's papers on Friday the third of July at about half past eleven**, and **nobody had to ask anybody for it**, and it was issued to districts on the third, **the office kept it, and side four came back out on Friday the seventeenth of July with a row completed on it, and nobody in this district was told that it had been kept.** THE OFFICE OF THE FIRST SURVEYOR **did not act, did not ask for anything, did not receive anything and cannot be asked for anything, and it is a signature block and not an issuing line and not a person, and no person may be produced behind it in any later block, and asking who is behind it is the one move that would turn a desk into a man and he has refused it on the page.**

2. **The batch's shape, its calendar and its movement.** Ten chapters, **Friday the third of July to Monday the twentieth of July, eighteen days.** It contains **one working day with a person in it** (Ch 142, a foreman of fifty-four and a sheet out of a rack), **one field or boundary scene** (Ch 143, as far as the third post), **one failure** (Ch 144, the ninth seventh column and a ninety-second true sentence that did not go anywhere), **one changed method and one recovery, both somebody else's work and neither a word** (Ch 145, five men and a bar), **two correct refusals that are not Milo's** (Ch 144, a woman of thirty-four who will not hold a form for a schoolroom; and the man of about forty-one's eleventh nine words, *standing in a room is standing in a file*), **a cost that is not curable paid by neither Lena Marr nor the woman of sixty** (Ch 146, a gang of eleven men and a contract to the thirty-first of August), **an escalation that is a document and its consequence in a room and then a yard** (Ch 141 and Ch 146), **one Atlas residue in ten chapters, six words, in Chapter 149, given to nobody: *A printed ground holds nobody up.*** The chapter dates, all on the page: 3 July (Ch 141, Fri), 6–7 July (Ch 142, Mon–Tue), 10 July (Ch 143, Fri), 11 July (Ch 144, Sat), 14 July (Ch 145, Tue), 17 July (Ch 146, Fri), 18 July (Ch 147, Sat), 19 July (Ch 148, Sun), 20 July (Ch 149 and Ch 150, Mon).

3. **THE WEEKDAY CHAIN, extended and not re-derived, and the round's two days.** From Batch 0001, which left the chain at **2 July Thursday**: **3rd Fri, 4th Sat, 5th Sun, 6th Mon, 7th Tue, 8th Wed, 9th Thu, 10th Fri, 11th Sat, 12th Sun, 13th Mon, 14th Tue, 15th Wed, 16th Thu, 17th Fri, 18th Sat, 19th Sun, 20th Mon** — and then **21st Tue, 23rd Thu, 24th Fri, 27th Mon, 28th Tue, 30th Thu, 31st Fri — and then 1st August Sat, 2nd Sun, 3rd Mon, 4th Tue, 5th Aug Wed, 6th Thu, 7th Fri, 8th Sat, 9th Sun, 10th Aug Mon, 11th Tue, 12th Wed, 13th Aug Thu, 14th Fri, 15th Sat, 16th Sun, 17th Mon, 18th Tue, 19th Wed, 20th Aug Thu, 21st Fri, 22nd Sat, 23rd Sun, 24th Mon, 25th Tue, 26th Wed, 27th Aug Thu, 28th Fri, 29th Sat, 30th Sun, 31st MON.** **The third of May was a Sunday, the sixteenth of June was a Tuesday, and the eleventh of July is a SATURDAY, and nothing in this block has re-derived any of them.** **THE COURIER ROUND GOES ON MONDAYS AND THURSDAYS ONLY AND HAS SINCE THE SECOND OF JANUARY**, it goes out at about ten past eight and comes in at about four, **and nothing in Chapters 141–150 puts it on a Tuesday, a Wednesday, a Friday, a Saturday or a Sunday: it runs on the sixth, ninth, thirteenth, sixteenth and twentieth of July, and it is one man short, and it ran on the thirteenth and the sixteenth without him because he was in Mireward.**

4. **THE TRAVEL LOGIC, WHICH IS THE BLOCK'S COST AND WHICH A REVIEWER MUST CHECK FIRST. THE DIRECTION OF THE FLATBED IS PINNED IN CHAPTER 143 AND IT RUNS MIREWARD-WARD IN THE MORNING AND CITY-WARD IN THE AFTERNOON:** it comes in from the city on a Friday morning at about a quarter to seven and is in Mireward at about half past ten with the week's post and the sacks on the bed, and it goes back to the city on the Friday afternoon and is in the yard there at about half past four, and it does not wait either way; a lorry to a yard at the north end comes in on a Thursday morning; there are two motor vehicles a week and one tractor and there has not been a bus since about 1961. **The morning run is the long one, because the post and the sacks go round Mireward; the afternoon return is direct.** **He is in eleven streets on Friday the third, Monday the sixth, Tuesday the seventh, Wednesday the eighth, Thursday the ninth; he stands at the gate at ten to seven on Friday the tenth with the form in his bag, gets off in Mireward at about half past ten, and is in MIREWARD from Friday the tenth until about half past four on Friday the seventeenth, when the flatbed brings him back; and he is in eleven streets on Friday the seventeenth evening, Saturday the eighteenth, Sunday the nineteenth and Monday the twentieth.** Chapters 141, 142, 146, 147, 148, 149 and 150 are in eleven streets; Chapters 143, 144 and 145 are in Mireward. **THE TENTH IS THE ONLY DAY ON WHICH THE VEHICLE HE NEEDS GOES THE RIGHT WAY WHILE HE IS ON THE WRONG SIDE OF IT: the only thing that would have carried an envelope back to a building about eleven miles away went back to the city at about four that afternoon, and he was two hundred yards along a raised path in a fenland, and the form was in his bag, and that was the last day for the district's answer. There is no second vehicle until Thursday, and the Thursday one is a lorry to a yard at the north end and it does not go to Crown Heights.** **A Friday had not been and a rack day is a Friday, and the last rack day in the block is Friday the seventeenth, so the sheet for the week of Monday the twentieth of July is in a drawer at half past eleven that night and nothing goes into the rack until Friday the twenty-fourth, which belongs to the next block. THE RACK PARAGRAPH IN CHAPTER 143 IS A FRAMING ASIDE ABOUT TWO THINGS THAT DID NOT NEED HIM AND IT CARRIES NO TIME AND NO PLACE, and a reader must not read it as a location.**

5. **The gang's week, the walks, the racks and the second tin's arithmetic.** The gang is on the back length **Mondays and Thursdays at 07.30**, the weekly sheet covers Monday to Sunday, it goes into the rack on the **Friday**, the duplicate into a tin, and the duplicate into the second tin on the bottom shelf of the plan room of the Civic Atlas Office under a repair schedule. **THE WALKS IN THIS BLOCK ARE THE TWENTY-SEVENTH, TWENTY-EIGHTH, TWENTY-NINTH, THIRTIETH AND THIRTY-FIRST SINCE THE FIRST OF APRIL — the 6th, 9th, 13th, 16th and 20th of July — FIVE, and the printed figures are the TWENTY-SEVENTH in Chapter 142 and the THIRTY-FIRST in Chapter 149, and no total running count is printed in any chapter. HE WAS NOT ON THE WALKS OF THE THIRTEENTH AND THE SIXTEENTH OF JULY AND NOBODY TELEPHONED EITHER TIME AND THE SHEET IS RIGHT ABOUT THAT AS WELL. THE RACKS ARE THE THIRD, THE TENTH AND THE SEVENTEENTH OF JULY — THREE FRIDAYS, and three, which is the number of Fridays in the period.** The second tin held **twenty-eight** on Friday the twenty-sixth of June (inherited) and now holds **TWENTY-NINE on Friday the third (Ch 141), THIRTY on Friday the tenth (Ch 143) and THIRTY-ONE on Friday the seventeenth (Ch 146)**, each printed in the chapter that owns the day, and **thirty-two falls on Friday the twenty-fourth, which is in the next block.** **The condition column is empty on the first four walks of this block and is NOT empty on the fifth, and the reason column is empty on all five, and there is no column on that sheet or on any paper in this city that wants to know why a column is empty.**

6. **THE CONDITION COLUMN, AND IT IS THE ONLY COLUMN IN THIS BLOCK THAT ANYBODY WROTE IN, AND IT IS WRONG-SHAPED, AND IT IS SEVENTEEN WEEKS TOO LATE.** The condition column has been empty since **the twelfth of February** and a works clerk of forty-six said in May, once, that it is the only column in that building allowed to be empty, **because the other five columns are about what we did and that one is about what it was like, and nobody in this city has ever asked a gang what a length was like.** **The first entry in a hundred and thirty-seven days was written on Monday the thirteenth of July and the second on Monday the twentieth of July, both by Roy Pryce, in pencil, in the same four words and a figure, and neither of them is a fault: *Mon 20/7. 60 yds. No defect observed.*** **A column that wants to know what a length was like has been used for a hundred and fifty-eight days to record a hole; a hole is an event, and an event is something that happened to a gang and can be asked about; a state is not an event; and the whole of the difference between the two is four words.** The foreman's reason for not being asked to fill it, said twice, on the seventh: **if you put a question in that column then the sheet stops being a record of a length and starts being a record of a man, and a man can be asked what he wrote, and I have got eleven men and I have got a contract to the thirty-first and I am not spending any of it on that. The column is for a hole.** **A later block may not put a word in either column about why it was empty, may not fault the entries, may not have anybody ask a gang what a length was like, and may not improve the four words.**

7. **THE ANSWER, AND IT IS IN A FOREMAN'S MOUTH AND IT IS NOT ENOUGH.** **The best answer anybody hears in this fortnight, given in about four seconds, unasked, in a corridor four rooms above a fitter's shop on Tuesday the seventh of July: there is nothing wrong with it. And there is no box on that form, or on the sheet, or in that building, or in this city, for *there is nothing wrong with it*.** He takes three of the four grounds apart himself, standing up, in about forty seconds, and he is right about all three: **(a) is not true, because there is a level of record in a file with a date on it under his signature which has not moved since the fourth of December; (b) is not a lie, because there are two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number and not one of them is on a list, and (b) does not mean nobody is here; (c) is not true, because the sheet comes Monday to Sunday, goes into the rack on the Friday, the duplicate into a tin, and there has not been one Monday in nine months without a sheet in it; and (d) is a question about money and is not his.** The wrong shape, in about eleven minutes at a counter: **the form's *works of record* column cannot take a sheet in a rack, a duplicate in a tin, and a carbon in a plan room in another building, and there is no column anywhere that can, because the piece of paper was never supposed to be the works of record; it was supposed to be a note that a foreman had walked.** And the form's own answer to the only way to keep them: **I have. Look at the foot of it. There is one way to keep them and it is on the Friday, and it is not printed as a way to keep them, it is printed as a way to complete them, and both of those are the same sentence with a different Friday in it.**

8. **THE NAMING, AND THE MECHANISM, AND THERE IS NO ONE TO BE ANGRY AT.** **Side four, one line, typed: THE ELEVENTH STREET DISTRICT. Stability of district: — Works of record: — Units benefited: — Ground of discontinuance: (a) no maintained seam of record. NOT TO BE MAINTAINED AFTER 31 AUGUST.** A reference in the same series as the number on the face-up card, dated **the sixteenth, a Thursday**, completed **in about a minute and a half by an office about a mile away**, and **nobody lied.** The mechanism, in full: **the check is done by reading the district's own file; the file has a level, a date and the joint finding of the fourth of December in it; the entry that would put the sixteenth of December in that file rests on a classification; the classification of the eleventh is withdrawn and is not to be relied on by any party; a record that rests on a withdrawn classification is not to be relied on by any party; so the sixteenth of December is not in the file, so the check finds nil, so the key gets written down.** **A bridge is a structure on one half of it and a route on the other. A seam event is a thing a district is entered as having or not having, and the eleven streets has not got one of record and it has got one of fact, and the two of those have never been the same thing in this city and today they cost a gang of eleven men.** The refusal that is correct and changed nothing, in about four seconds, by a works clerk of forty-six: **I am not signing column six… because if I sign that, I have put a sentence about the sixteenth of December into a file, and there is not one sentence about the sixteenth of December that is both true and mine. There is a true one and it is not mine. There is one that is mine and it is a form about a length of road and it is not about the sixteenth of December.** A later volume may not name a person behind the office that completed the row, may not make the row a fraud, may not have it withdrawn or varied, and may not make the eleven streets a district that is not on a list of anything again.

9. **THE COST, AND IT IS A DATE, AND IT IS THE FIRST DATE THIS DISTRICT HAS HAD SINCE THE FOURTEENTH OF APRIL THAT IS NOT ABOUT A CAR.** **The form says *not to be maintained after 31 August*, **the thirty-first of August is a MONDAY**, and the gang is on a Monday and a Thursday, so **the last walk of the season is on Monday the thirty-first of August, which is the last day of the contract and is itself a gang day**, and the contract is not the city's and the eleven men are not the city's and the foreman is not the city's.** After that date: **no gang on the back length, no sheet covering Monday to Sunday, no rack on a Friday with anything in it, no duplicate, no carbon in a tin, and no carbon in the second tin on the bottom shelf of the plan room under a repair schedule — and a repair schedule is a schedule, and a schedule is on a printed side somewhere, and nobody has ever asked when it ends.** **The condition column is a hundred and fifty-five days empty on Friday the seventeenth of July and on the thirty-first of August it will not be empty, it will not be there, and those are two different sentences and this city has no word for the second one.** **AND THE ARITHMETIC OF THE DATE, which is a scene and not a note: A PRINTED DATE DOES NOT KNOW WHAT DAY OF THE WEEK IT IS, and nobody in the office that printed the form looked at a calendar. The thirty-first of August is a Monday and that is the last day a printed date could have fallen on for a gang that works on Mondays and Thursdays. THE LAST MORNING OF THE GANG IS THE LAST DAY OF THE CONTRACT — Monday the thirty-first of August at half past seven, and it is a working morning and it is the last one. The last Thursday is the twenty-seventh. THE LAST RACK DAY IS FRIDAY THE TWENTY-EIGHTH, and the sheet that goes into the rack on the twenty-eighth is the sheet for the week of Monday the twenty-fourth, it covers Monday to Sunday, and it is the last sheet that will ever be printed for this gang. THERE IS NO SHEET AT ALL FOR THE WEEK OF MONDAY THE THIRTY-FIRST, because the schedule of works ends and there is nothing after it to print one, and so the last walk of the season is not on a sheet and not in the rack and not in either tin and there is no form in this city for a walk that is not on a sheet. THE SECOND TIN GOES TO THIRTY-SEVEN ON FRIDAY THE TWENTY-EIGHTH AND THEN IT DOES NOT GO ANYWHERE, and it does not know that and neither does the repair schedule. THE RACK IS A PIECE OF FURNITURE IN A CORRIDOR AND NOBODY EMPTIES A RACK, and on Friday the fourth of September there is a rack with nothing in it in it, and on Friday the eleventh, and there is nothing in this city that says a rack is finished. THIS WAS WRONG IN THE FIRST DRAFT, WAS THEN CORRECTED ONTO A STATE-LAYER CHAIN THAT WAS ITSELF WRONG, AND HAS NOW BEEN CORRECTED FROM THE COUNTED CHAIN; see CR-046 item 10(l) and CR-047.** And: **he cannot do one single thing about it, and there is no form for that, and the reason there is no form for that is that nothing in this district is a party to anything — the order is suspended and not discharged and not varied and not withdrawn, and the matter stands over and is not adjourned and has no date and is on no list, and this instrument is a different instrument about different work and does not mention the order, the district, the classification, the level or him.** A later block may not discharge the order on the strength of this instrument, may not use the order against anybody, and may not give the district a chair.

10. **THE NINTH SEVENTH COLUMN, AND WHAT HE COULD HAVE DONE, AND THE STOP, AND WHO PAID.** **Saturday the eleventh of July, about four in the afternoon, in a room above a kitchen in Cobb Lock, eleven miles from the district it was about, on the day after the day it was due.** Two face-down seventh columns in one bag, **and a bag is not a custody.** **THE STOP WAS HIS OWN HANDWRITING ON THE FACING PAGE OF THE FIRST OF JULY, AND A STOP THAT A MAN WROTE ABOUT HIMSELF IS NOT A STOP, and he said that out loud, in a village, on a Saturday, in July, to nobody, and it is the truest thing he has said in eight months and it is not a resolution.** And in ninety seconds, in pencil, on the back of their own schedule, **a true sentence for column two of the eleven streets' row, naming the seam, the level, the joint finding of the fourth of December, the withdrawn classification, the twice-weekly works and the empty condition column** — **and if that had gone in on the tenth of July, the check would not have found nil, the office would not have had a key to write down, and there would not have been a sentence in a file about a morning in December that a person in a room a mile away could ask for.** He did not send it, and the reason is on the facing page and is about him, **and a fifth column filled in by a man who is not responsible for maintenance in any district means the only row out of nine hundred with a man from another district's building in it has one, and the man is him, and about four years from now a room can produce it, and so there is no version of this that is not him deciding.** **THE COST FELL ON A FOREMAN OF FIFTY-FOUR AND NOT ON HIM.** **A district with no rateable unit has no stationery, and a district that was never on anything has nothing to put anything in, and the post goes to a rateable unit, and the satchel is the only thing that comes to eleven streets, and the thing went out in the satchel on the Friday morning and came back in it on the Friday afternoon and has been in it every day since.** It was a woman of thirty-four, refusing to hold a form, who told him one sentence on her way to the door. **AND IN A YARD AT HALF PAST SIX ON THE SEVENTEENTH HE SAID: I would like it noted that I did not stand there on the Friday morning with it in my hand and ask anybody, because I did not think there was anywhere to ask. And then, after eleven seconds: that condition column has been empty for a hundred and fifty-five days, and a man in a building a mile away has written a sentence about it, and it is the first sentence anybody has ever written about that column, and it is not mine, and I am not going to pretend it is. And Milo Rook stood in that yard and could not say one word, and could not have, and did not have one: HE THINKS I DID NOT FILL IN THE COLUMN BECAUSE OF HIM.** **Nobody has asked that foreman about December and nobody is going to, and a later block may not ask him, may not tell him that a surveyor wrote a sentence on the back of a schedule, and may not put the two facts together on his behalf.**

11. **THE FIFTH OF HIS OWN FIVE, AND IT IS THE FIRST ONE THAT IS ABOUT A PERSON WHO IS HIM, AND IT IS THE FIRST ONE HE HAS PAID FOR.** **Sunday the nineteenth of July, about twenty-five past four, in a front room in eleven streets, in the two inches of ruled space at the bottom of a Mireward district returns card: *Received 17 July. The district has no rateable unit and no correspondence address and no person responsible for maintenance. The works of record in this district are a number cast into the collar of a culvert and about nine feet of drain that a man of fifty-eight writes four lines about once a year into a pad in a drawer in Crown Heights. — M. Rook, surveyor.*** **The four before it cost somebody else something: the plate, about a person; the seventh column on a table in his own office, about a person; the not-telling, about a person; and the rule on the facing page, which is about a habit and cost a foreman a silence that is now partly in his handwriting.** **HE WROTE IT FACE DOWN AND DID NOT PUT IT IN THE MACHINE, and the reason is the whole of why it works and the reason is worth more than what he wrote: a card put down face up is re-run once a week on a line at the foot of a run sheet headed *re-run — face up only* and comes back as a second piece of paper with a reference number in the top left corner in a series running from something like 41,000 upward, and about four hundred of those go out of that room a week, and a sentence with a reference on it is a thing a person can be asked for. SO THE HAND STAYS ON THE PAPER AND THE MACHINE DOES NOT TAKE THE HAND OVER, and that is the first time in eight months he has wanted a machine NOT to take a hand over, and he has written down that he does not know what that is and is not going to find out.** **A CARD WITH A THING STRUCK THROUGH IT IS A CARD WITH A MARK ON IT, and the re-run is on a Friday at about four, and he cannot get the name off it.** And the limits, printed: **the nil is still a nil; the stability column is still hand-transferred; the column is still not typed and has never been typed; there is still a row for it and the row is as wide as the others; the three nils about Mireward are three and this is the same zero off the same sheet; nobody has been frightened; nobody has been asked for anything; and a district that is not a place cannot be frightened, and he has given a place a name, and it is the only one of the five he cannot get back.** **A later block may not name him on any other document, may not make the name useful, may not have the district become frightened, and may not let him take the name off.**

12. **THE SECOND USE, AND IT IS ABOUT LAND, AND IT IS EIGHT WEEKS OLDER THAN THE COPY IN HIS BAG.** **Column two, stability of district, is the column a land schedule wants, found in about nine seconds on a Saturday morning in a records room with two people waiting, and she did not go into a room.** **A land schedule does not want a condition; it wants to know who is on the ground. Column two of that form is a hand-transferred column of about nine hundred rows, about eight hundred with a word or a zero in it, and a hand-transferred nil for a fen district on the ground that there is no maintained seam of record, no recorded loss, no resident witness and no schedule — and a thing on the ground in a district that column two says has nobody in it is a thing on the ground that nobody is on.** **THE SCHEDULE WAS PRINTED IN THE MIDDLE OF MAY AND ISSUED IN THE FIRST WEEK OF JULY, and the same press run went to a land desk about a mile from where the form was drafted, and that desk has had a schedule saying a place is unoccupied for about eight weeks while eleven streets received its copy nine days ago, and nobody in eleven streets and nobody in Mireward has been told that there are two of these.** The row, read out loud, standing up, in the order the print run went: **MIREWARD — 41-2, 4 to 12 inclusive — works of record — unoccupied, no rateable unit. Nine plots, held on shares, and there is a book of shares, and the book of shares is in a different building and it is the only book about this district that anybody in this city has ever opened.** **The line in the return that Mara Quill reconciles on the last Thursday of every month has still NOT been described. She gave four facts and no sentence — the last Thursday was the sixteenth, she reconciles it on the last Thursday of every month, she has done so for about two years before that, and it has never come up in a conversation with anybody — and he did not ask a third time, and she noticed that he did not, and he noticed her noticing, and neither of those is one of the three Volume 01 clues and he has put it in the book anyway because it is on the page.** A later block may not have her describe it, may not have him ask a third time, and may not turn the nine plots into a land transaction, a claim, a person or a fight in this volume.

13. **THE RELATIONSHIP, MILESTONE 2, AND IT MOVED ONCE, AND IT WAS A DISAGREEMENT, AND IT IS MILESTONE 3 SET UP AND NOT REPAIRED.** No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue, and nothing is going to. **The Lark Street grievance is not named, not resolved, not softened and not referred to in any of the ten chapters.** **The custody register page is untouched for the EIGHTH consecutive block: it is in a drawer with a coat in it, it was not produced, not named, not confessed to, not mentioned in dialogue, he did not ask, and he was not allowed to. She said, once, that she is not going to say anything about it, this week or in July, and that that is not her being cold, and that he has known since May.** He has not told her about the season, **and the number of weeks is not printed in these ten chapters: he said *twelve* out loud in Chapter 147, and he has stopped counting and started dating, and dating it is a column, and a column is worse than a count.** The disagreement, in full and correct on both sides: **she: you should put the district's answer on the record, because it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing that exists. He: it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing a man with a box can ask for in about four years. She: yes, that is the same sentence and you have heard it and I have heard it and neither of us has a better one.** And his reason for not putting it, which names the people it would be produced against: **a woman of thirty-four who knows twenty-six names and has not written them down for four years on purpose, and a foreman of fifty-four who is not going to be asked about December and will be asked about a file.** And what he says about it, printed: **we are two people who have both said that and neither of us has moved and neither of us is going to, and I am not going to improve it in a records room on a Saturday with two people waiting.** **A later block may not repair this in this volume, may not make either of them warm, and may not let either of them win it.**

14. **A RUN OF THINGS THAT WERE BUILT FOR ONE PURPOSE AND ARE THE WRONG SHAPE, AND THE COUNT IS NOT NUMBERED.** A works schedule whose fifth column is a column about whether a place deserves to be kept; a key of four grounds with **no ground for *there is nothing wrong with it***; a *works of record* column that cannot take a sheet in a rack and two tins in three buildings; a **district-stability column that is a hand-transferred column about people and is the exact column a land schedule wants**; a **condition column built for a hole and used for a state**; **two inches of ruled space for a district's own use, used to put a man's name in**; a **reference number in a series, which is a machine taking a district's existence over**; and **a carbon, which is a copy of a sentence and is not a second record until a man goes over it in hard pencil for four minutes on his own account.**

15. **A CARBON IS NOT A SECOND COPY OF A SENTENCE, AND ON MONDAY THE TWENTIETH OF JULY IT BECAME ONE.** **A foreman's pencil on the top sheet comes through onto the duplicate underneath as a grey smear, and it has been a grey smear on every sheet in this district since the second of January, and nobody has ever minded, because there has never been anything in the condition column to mind about. A man of about twenty-six in a high-visibility jacket sat down on the low wall at the north end of the length at about half past eight with a hard pencil and copied the four words and the figure onto the carbon, letter for letter, in about four minutes, so that the sentence would be legible on the copy. Nobody asked him to. Nobody thanked him. Milo Rook did not thank him either, because a person who has thanked somebody is a person who has asked, which a conductor of thirty-eight said in April about a card and which is not improved in this block.** **He has walked this length for about four years and has never once had anything to write in it and did not know that was unusual, and he has been put on the back length twice in Volume 02 and asked nothing both times, and he is not a fifth instance of anything and he is not a clue.** A later block may not thank him, may not ask him what he copied or why, and may not make him a narrator.

16. **THE PAIRS ARE TWENTY-NINE AND THEY MOVED ONCE, AND THE REASON IS ON THE PAGE.** **The list on the facing page reads twenty-nine, it has moved once since Thursday the twenty-fifth of June, and the interval is seven days, from the ninth of July to the sixteenth. There is no entry for the tenth, the eleventh, the twelfth, the thirteenth, the fourteenth or the fifteenth, and there are six nights in that. THE TENTH OF JULY WAS A DECISION — he had the form in the bag, he took it out, he read it, he put it back, and he did not open the book — and the other five were not a decision at all.** The list is **two sixes, seven threes, one nine, four twos, three fives, one eight, one twenty-five, FOUR sevens, six fours.** **The list must not be read as twenty-one, twenty-six, twenty-eight, thirty or thirty-one. There is no thirtieth pair, no eleventh pair, no thirteenth pair, no twenty-first pair, no twenty-second pair and no twenty-eight pair. The four threes that were here in December are the four threes that were here in December and are not collapsed. There is one eight and not two.** **A PAIR IS A FACT ABOUT WHERE HE WAS AND NOT ABOUT WHAT HAPPENED, and an interval of one day is not a pair whether or not either of the two days has anything on it, and the run of fifty-two consecutive nights is over and there is a habit, and a habit is not a discipline and a habit is not a method, AND TWO SEVENS IN ABOUT A FORTNIGHT HAS NOT TURNED THE HABIT INTO A RUN AND HE HAS CHECKED TWICE.** The last time the list moved, the interval was also seven days, and **the chapter says that is a coincidence and has written nothing about it.**

17. **THE ORIENTATION LEDGER, THE STAGE 2 COSTS, THE STAGE 2 GAIN, AND THE DAY-COUNTS, AND NONE OF IT MOVED BUT THE FIGURES.** **The remembered-route list is ELEVEN, the crossings are TEN, and the tally is TEN LINES, and every crossing cost one route, and the ninth's failure to cost one is still unexplained. THERE IS NO CROSSING IN CHAPTERS 141–150, he held nothing, stabilised nothing, pinned nothing, and the page home, the token and the tally did not move.** The written line home went on the twenty-fourth of November, which is day one, **and the page-home figures printed in this block are 222 (3 Jul), 226 (7 Jul), 229 (10 Jul), 230 (11 Jul), 233 (14 Jul), 236 (17 Jul), 237 (18 Jul), 238 (19 Jul) and 239 (20 Jul)**, with **225, 231, 232, 234 and 235 carried arithmetic and not printed. The Lark token, day zero = the seventh of December, is printed at 208 (3 Jul), 212 (7 Jul), 215 (10 Jul), 216 (11 Jul), 219 (14 Jul), 222 (17 Jul), 223 (18 Jul), 224 (19 Jul) and 225 (20 Jul)**, with 209, 210, 211, 213, 214, 217, 218, 220 and 221 carried and not printed. **THE TWO REGISTERS MUST NOT BE MERGED, AND THREE FIGURES COLLIDE ACROSS THEM AND MUST NOT BE MERGED: 221 is the page home on Thursday the second of July (inherited) and the token on Thursday the sixteenth of July; 225 is the page home on Monday the twentieth of July and the token carried on Monday the thirteenth; and 230 is the page home on Saturday the eleventh of July and nothing else, which is the only one of the three that is not a collision. A HUNDRED AND FIVE IS THE PLATE ON THE TWENTIETH OF JULY AND ALSO THE PAGE HOME ON THE SIXTEENTH OF JUNE, and Chapter 149 says so on the page and the two must not be confused.** The plate, from the sixth of April, is printed at **88, 92, 95, 96, 99, 102, 103, 104 and 105 days**. **The condition column, empty since the twelfth of February, is printed at 141, 145, 148, 152, 155, 157 and 158 days**, and **no new cumulative figure is printed for the reason column in this block**, which has been empty since about the fourth of April, and there is no column anywhere that wants to know why a column is empty. **A foreman was eleven weeks and three days past four months on Saturday the eleventh of July — a Saturday, because a Saturday is the day a foreman of thirty-one years is on a gate and not in a building — and is twelve weeks and five days past it on Monday the twentieth, and nobody has asked him and nobody is going to.** **Failing sensory detail did not recur in nineteen days; it is a sound and a taste with no face, no place, no person and no name, and he has written down that he does not know whether a thing that has not come for nineteen days is a thing that has stopped or a thing that has not come, and he has written that before and improved it none of the times. The first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in Chapters 141–150.**

18. **The blank Wednesdays, and the four unshowable things, and the three sets of marks, and the four that are still unjoined, and every one of them is declared in the manuscript.** **The second side of the sheet is right about Wednesday the eighth of July and Wednesday the fifteenth of July and about every one of them since the eighth of April, for the same reason and the second kind of reason, and HE IS NOT COUNTING THEM AND HAS NOT COUNTED THEM SINCE THE FIRST OF JULY AND IS NOT GOING TO START AGAIN IN THE LAST WEEK OF A MONTH.** **NOBODY IN CHAPTERS 141–150 STANDS IN THE WEATHER ON A WEDNESDAY, and the four things in five months are four, and there has been no fifth, and a fifth needs somebody to be surprised.** **The three sets of marks are three sets:** **two sets about ninety feet apart near the second post on the far side of the bridge, two hands, the same water on the same days, neither in a file, the file being on the other side of the water; and nineteen cuts in a post at a ford four miles away; and a cast number in the collar of a culvert head beside the third post, which is in column three of the schedule and which he held a form against for about four minutes and did not write down and is not going to.** **None of the three is a second instance of anything, none is a fourth clue, and the three Volume 01 clues — the copied tick, the copied turn sheet with a contractor's head on it and *Owen Fitch's name and third floor* in a space left empty on purpose, and the carbon relocation schedule with ONE margin mark filed as the second attachment to a nineteen-paragraph objection — are unjoined for a hundred and fifty chapters, and a fourth is not permitted to become a fourth pattern, and the routing slip with *for index* on it is a fourth kind of document and not a fourth instance of anything.** **And the one join this block invites and refuses: a card came off a machine on the seventeenth of June with a hand on it and six words in the reason column and a reference number on it, and side four has a reference number out of the same series, and both of the numbers are about a district with no rateable unit — A DISTRICT IS PRINTED ON BOTH OF THEM AND THE DISTRICT IS THE WHOLE OF THE CONNECTION AND THE TWO SENTENCES HAVE NOTHING TO DO WITH EACH OTHER, and he does not know whose hand the first one is in and is not going to find out, and he has not said the six words out loud since the twenty-seventh of June and they are not in the Field Book.** **The timetable division's standing refusal holds and was not tested: ask nobody, and do not come here, and do not send the man from the round. Nobody has asked a timetable clerk anything and no working person has been sent anywhere. The working timetable at Alder Quay, the terminus column that says HOLLOWGATE, the ruled margin headed *actual* with thirty-one real names in it in the order the people got on, and the eight sentences that bind them, are inherited and were not queried, dated, explained or asked about in any of these ten chapters. Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–150. Sel Iri does not appear. Halden Vey is not named and did not appear. Orrin Sable did not appear. Tarin Kest did not appear and there is no fifth sighting paired with a fifth refusal. Nobody was added above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel, and nothing that went wrong this month is attributable to a face.**

19. **NO NEW GRAMMAR, NO NEW FACTION, NO NEW ANTAGONIST, NO NEW FINAL ENEMY, NO NEW KIND OF PLACE, AND THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT IS NOT WRITTEN.** **A length is still a third kind of place and is not a remnant. A boundary is a place where two people agreed to have stopped, and there are now three on the page and they are three and they are not joined: a corner and a fence at the low end of Bridge Street, a mowing line where two things stopped for the same reasons and neither was a person, and a column in a quarter-inch file that says CITY or EXT whose two ends are a kerb and a bank. A practice is not a place and not a day. A pad is a pad. A roll in a teacher's head is a roll in a teacher's head. A ferry route is a ferry route. A tidemark is a tidemark. A bell is a bell. A sluice is a sluice. A bar is a bar. A cast number in a collar is a cast number in a collar. A carbon is a copy. A reference number is a reference number. A condition column is a column.** **The grammars named in these ten chapters are Keepsake (the Row, by inheritance) and NONE of the ten names a new dominant grammar and none identifies a new one, and the Rainway is not named, and a Weather remnant in Mireward is not identified, and the volume's midpoint — that the Rainway is carrying a public mourning displaced from the Quieting — is not written and is Batch 0004's work. THE FORD IS NOT RESOLVED: there is no demand, no service and no failure out of it, the bell has not been dated, nobody has been named who rang it, nobody has been put inside it, and it has not been made a haunting, a claim, a ghost, a voice, a knock, a person, a curse, a debt or an inherited role. THE FIRST QUIETING HAS NO DATE IN THIS VOLUME AND NONE MAY BE SUPPLIED. The office of the First Surveyor is a printed box with a rule and nothing on the rule and it did not ask for, receive or answer anything.** And the standing last line stands, unimproved, in ten Field Book entries: ***the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and that is a list of about fourteen other people and not one of them has been asked to be on it.***

20. **Atlas residue, once in ten chapters, SIX words, in Chapter 149, in his own book at about eleven at night on Monday the twentieth of July, given to nobody and to be given to nobody: *A printed ground holds nobody up.*** **The whole of what the chapter says about it is that he wrote it down and did not act on it and does not know what it is about, that he is not going to hand it to a woman of sixty-one at a counter, that he is not going to say it in a yard, that there are about nine people in this district and none of them has it, and that there is a woman of thirty-four in a village who would understand it and he is not going to tell her either, and a man of fifty-four on a gate who would argue with it. The eight already used are unchanged and were not restated in these ten chapters: *A trade outlives its pitch* (Ch 66, five), *A length is missed before it is lost* (Ch 77, eight), *Weather uses whatever door is open* (Ch 89, six), *A habit is not a calendar* (Ch 95, six), *It is dated before it is read* (Ch 108, seven), *Whoever holds the pen is not the writer* (Ch 120, eight, in chalk on the inside of a gate), *The line was made up. The names were not.* (Ch 130, nine), and *A dry week is a promise about water.* (Ch 136, eight, on a bank, given to nobody).** **THE TENTH, and the only one in Chapters 151–160, and it is in the list below because a list of nine that the manuscript has already gone past is a trap: ***Nothing written down can be got wrong.*** (Ch 155, six, in his own book at about eleven at night on Wednesday the twelfth of August, given to nobody). **A later volume may not use residue more than once in ten chapters and may not repeat any of the ten sentences, and no year, no name and no speaker may ever be attached to the Chapter 136 line, and BATCH 0004 HAS ALREADY USED THE ONE ITS TEN CHAPTERS ALLOW.**

## What Chapters 151–160 fixed on the page, in the same form as CR-013

Each item below is now prose rather than plan. **None of them changes a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder, or the planned ending. Every figure here was made from the manuscript and is quotable from a chapter in `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0151.md` … `chapter-0160.md`, after the writer's own pass, which is recorded in `CR-050`. `outline/volume-03.md` was NOT edited, `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-01.md` were not edited, and the volume's final image — a village school painting its route on a wall in several colours, none of them official — is NOT written and belongs to Chapter 200.** Chapters 151–160 are the **third block of Volume 03** and they inherit the Chapters 141–150, 131–140, 121–130, 111–120, 101–110, 91–100, 81–90, 71–80, 61–70 and Volume 01 lists above whole. **Volume 02 remains closed at Chapter 130 and nothing in these ten chapters discharges, restores, varies, withdraws, re-authors or re-lists anything in it.**

1. **THE PRACTICE, IN FULL, IN THE WORDS OF CHAPTERS 152, 153, 156 AND 160, WHICH OWN THE FOUR PARTS BETWEEN THEM. FOUR PEOPLE DOING A THING IS FOUR PEOPLE DOING A THING. IT IS NOT A FORM. IT IS NOT A PLACE. IT IS NOT A DAY. NO BATCH MAY MAKE IT ONE, AND THE LAST THING THAT HAS TO HAPPEN IN THIS DISTRICT IS SOMEBODY WRITING DOWN THE FOUR.** **AND THE PHRASE IS HIS, WHICH THE BATCH TITLE, THE CHAPTER TITLE AND THE EARLIER VERSION OF THIS ITEM ALL GOT WRONG IN DIFFERENT WAYS: *Four copies of a bell* was coined by Milo Rook in his own book on Friday the twenty-fourth of July, in about four seconds, and the entry that owns it says on the page that *copies* is the wrong word, because there is not one thing here four times over — a work rhythm, a death, a daily signal off a boat, and a bell on a post at a ford that nobody has attributed. FOUR BELLS DOING FOUR JOBS IS A BETTER THING THAN ONE PRACTICE REPLICATED FOUR TIMES, AND A LATER BLOCK MAY NOT REPLICATE IT, MAY NOT GIVE THE PHRASE TO ANYBODY ELSE, MAY NOT PUT IT IN A DOCUMENT, AND MAY NOT LET THE FOUR BECOME A ROW. HE HAS NAMED IT AND NOBODY HAS IT BUT HIM — see `CR-051` item 9.** (a) **A handbell on a nail inside the door of a house that is not made of brick and has no number, rung TWICE before the man starts work and ONCE when he stops, with his left hand while he digs with his right, not stopping to ring it — Barnaby Pike, sixty-one, about thirty-one years, on no rate roll, no employer, paid in coin out of a jar on a table, no wage, no record, no holiday, no name on anything in this city.** The bell goes **the day somebody dies, and again the following year on the same date, and again, and it has gone every year since, and nobody arranged it and there is no meeting and there was no first one anybody can point at**; he opens the hole up, puts it right, does the grass, takes the weed off it, and goes; nobody is watching; nobody thanks him; **he has never asked to be thanked, because if you thank a man for this then you have to give him something, and there is nothing to give him.** (b) **A bell on a bracket over a schoolroom door, hand bell, screwed to a frame from about 1911, rung ONCE, by Sabra Trewin, thirty-four, for about nine years, and only when somebody in that room has had somebody die, with four children taking turns on the rope, giving it nine and seven and eleven in one term, and she has never once corrected one of them because she does not know that she is entitled to.** (c) **A bell about four inches across the mouth, off a boat, on a wall at the top of a landing, rung at ten past seven and at four EVERY DAY since about 1979 by Garn Ollerton, thirty-seven, including days when she is not on the water at either of those, and on Sundays, and she has not missed one.** (d) **A bell on a post at a ford dry since about 1919, heard NINE times in June from about four hundred yards, and no ring could be put on any of them.** **THE FINDING THAT GOES WITH IT, UNIMPROVED: a practice is the only thing in this district that has a shape no form was ever built to fit, and the reason there is no form for it is not that a form is missing — it is that the woman on the schoolroom step would have to decide how many pulls of the rope is the right number before she could put a figure in a box, and four old women who are dead taught four children that number, and she does not know that she has the right to flatten it out, and he does not know that she has, and neither of them has asked her, and there is no form for *I do not know that I have the right*, and that is not a gap in a form, it is the whole subject.** **THE SENTENCE THE WHOLE BLOCK TURNS ON, said unasked by a man with a spade on Friday the twenty-fourth of July, in about four seconds: *whatever you write down will be the first time anybody has written down any of it, and that is not a service, that is a door.*** **MILO ROOK IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THIS CITY WHO KNOWS THERE ARE FOUR, HE HAS NOT WRITTEN THEM DOWN, HE HAS NOT TOLD ANYBODY, AND THERE ARE FOUR PEOPLE WHO HAVE NOT BEEN ASKED WHETHER IT IS FOUR.** A practice has not been made a mood, a haunting, a claim, a ghost, a voice, a knock, a person, a curse, a debt or an inherited role, and the bell at the ford has not been dated, nobody has been named who rings it, and nobody has been put inside it.

2. **The batch's shape, its calendar and its movement.** Ten chapters, **Tuesday the twenty-first of July to Friday the fourth of September, forty-six days.** It contains **one working day with a person in it, done well by somebody who is not the protagonist** (Ch 157, eleven men and a foreman and a typed name in a box), **one field or boundary scene** (Ch 154, the far half of a bridge), **one failure** (Ch 155, a fifth column that was not asked for, and what it cost a man of sixty-one), **one changed method and one recovery, both somebody else's work and neither a word** (Ch 156, *then that is four*), **three correct refusals that are not Milo's and change nothing** (Ch 153, *I am not going to let you have the date*; Ch 157, *a disagreement wants two, and there is one*; Ch 159, *that is a machine, and the machine is right*), **a cost that is not curable paid by a foreman of fifty-four who did not ask and is not going to** (Ch 159), **an escalation that is a document and its consequence in a yard** (Ch 157), **a POV break that is the relationship milestone carried by a different person** (Ch 158, Mara Quill), and **one Atlas residue in ten chapters, WHICH IS ONE AND NOT NONE, in Chapter 155, six words, in his own book at about eleven at night on Wednesday the twelfth of August, given to nobody and to be given to nobody: *Nothing written down can be got wrong.* — the tenth of the ten sentences of that kind, and the brief permits at most one in ten chapters and says one is not required, and this block used the one it had, so BATCH 0004 HAS NONE AVAILABLE AND MAY NOT REACH FOR ONE. The earlier version of this item said *which is none*, and that was false: the line is in `chapter-0155.md` and the Batch 0003 review is the reason the claim is restated rather than certified; see `CR-051` item 11.** The chapter dates, all on the page: 21–23 July (Ch 151, Tue–Thu), 24 July (Ch 152, Fri), 31 July (Ch 153, Fri), 7 Aug (Ch 154, Fri), 12 Aug (Ch 155, Wed), 14 Aug (Ch 156, Fri), 20 and 24 Aug (Ch 157, Thu and Mon), 27 Aug (Ch 158, Thu), 31 Aug (Ch 159, Mon), 1 and 4 Sept (Ch 160, Tue and Fri).

3. **THE WEEKDAY CHAIN, extended and not re-derived, and the round's two days.** From Batch 0002, which left the chain at **20 July Monday**: **21st Tue, 22nd Wed, 23rd Thu, 24th Fri, 25th Sat, 26th Sun, 27th Mon, 28th Tue, 29th Wed, 30th Thu, 31st Fri — and then 1st August Sat, 2nd Sun, 3rd Mon, 4th Tue, 5th Wed, 6th Thu, 7th Fri, 8th Sat, 9th Sun, 10th Mon, 11th Tue, 12th Wed, 13th Thu, 14th Fri, 15th Sat, 16th Sun, 17th Mon, 18th Tue, 19th Wed, 20th Thu, 21st Fri, 22nd Sat, 23rd Sun, 24th Mon, 25th Tue, 26th Wed, 27th Thu, 28th Fri, 29th Sat, 30th Sun, 31st MONDAY — and then 1st September Tue, 2nd Wed, 3rd Thu, 4th Fri.** `1st July Wed` is a chapter heading and so are `3rd July Fri`, `6th July Mon`, `10th July Fri`, `11th July Sat`, `13th July Mon`, `17th July Fri`, `19th July Sun` and `20th July Mon`: **count forward from a heading and never copy a date out of a state file, because the state file is where this error lived from Batch 0001 onward.** **THE COURIER ROUND GOES ON MONDAYS AND THURSDAYS ONLY AND HAS SINCE THE SECOND OF JANUARY, it goes out at about ten past eight and comes in at about four, it is one man short, and nothing in Chapters 151–160 puts it on a Tuesday, a Wednesday, a Friday, a Saturday or a Sunday: on Friday the fourth of September it did not go, and he walked up on his own.** **NO CALENDAR YEAR APPEARS ANYWHERE IN VOLUME 03, AND NONE MAY BE ADDED TO THE PRESENT-DAY CHRONOLOGY, TO A DAY ON THE COUNTED CHAIN, TO A DAY-COUNT OR TO ANY EVENT. THE YEARS THAT ARE ON THE PAGE ARE THE AGES OF OBJECTS, BUILDINGS, INSTITUTIONS AND WAYS OF DOING THINGS, AND THEY ARE 1911, 1919, 1961, 1968, 1969, 1974, 1975, 1979, 1986 and 1998, AND A NEW ONE MAY NOT BE ADDED TO THAT LIST. The earlier wording of this item, *no year appears anywhere in Volume 03*, was never true — Chapters 131–150 print the same years — and the Batch 0003 review is the reason it is restated here rather than certified; see `CR-051` item 11.**

4. **THE CONTRACT RAN TO ITS END AND NOTHING WENT WRONG.** **The form says *not to be maintained after 31 August*. 31 August is a MONDAY, and the gang is on a Monday and a Thursday, and nobody in the office that printed the form looked at a calendar, because A PRINTED DATE DOES NOT KNOW WHAT DAY OF THE WEEK IT IS — and that sentence is repeated, in a working man's mouth, on the last morning, in Chapter 159.** So, all four of these, and none of them is new and none of them may be moved: **the last walk of the season is MONDAY THE THIRTY-FIRST OF AUGUST, which is the last day of the contract and is itself a gang day, and it is the FORTY-THIRD walk since the first of April; the last Thursday is the twenty-seventh; the last rack day is FRIDAY THE TWENTY-EIGHTH; the sheet that goes into the rack on the twenty-eighth covers Monday the twenty-fourth to Sunday the thirtieth and is the last sheet that will ever be printed for this gang; and THERE IS NO SHEET AT ALL FOR THE WEEK OF MONDAY THE THIRTY-FIRST, so the last walk of the season is on no paper anywhere, and a machine cannot print a form for a period that is not in a contract, and Roy Pryce asked on the Friday and was told there is no week after the contract and said that is a machine and the machine is right.**

5. **THE FIFTH THING, THE SIXTH, THE SEVENTH, THE EIGHTH AND THE NINTH, ON THE PAGE, IN SEQUENCE.** (i) **Thursday the twenty-third of July, the back length, half past eight, a bell went once from past the end of the length and did not go again: he wrote the time and the word *bell* and did not write down what it was, and he told nobody, and that is the ninth thing of that shape in eight months and the first one that has cost nothing.** (ii) **The plate, a hundred and fifty-one days in the grass on Friday the fourth of September, never reported and never asked about, the decision made on the first of June, **TOLD TO NOBODY, and four people in this city know it is in that grass and have said nothing about it, and one of them is him — the Chapter 159 line that said told to four people was the wrong polarity of the same fact and is corrected in `CR-051` item 4, and a later block may not report it, may not ask anybody about it, may not put it on a list, and may not convert the four who know into four who have been told.** (iii) **The seventh column, given up on Thursday the twenty-third of July with the heading in his hand in about eleven seconds, and again on Wednesday the twelfth of August, when a machine in a room about a mile away would have taken a fifth column and he did not ask, and again on the first of September, when the rule ran out and he did not draw one — and the reason he was not going to wait for the first of September was that he was hoping a month would make it correct.** (iv) **The not-telling, about Mara Quill, since the middle of April, and she has not asked since July.** (v) **The paragraph and signature in two inches of ruled space on a Mireward district returns card, face down, Sunday the nineteenth of July, thirty-nine days old on the twenty-seventh of August, and it cannot be taken off.** (vi) ****The seventy-eight words** not said to Roy Pryce, on the last morning, with four hours available — *the earlier version of this item called them eleven words and it is seventy-eight, and a later block may not improve them, shorten them or call them any other number; see `CR-051` item 8*: *I did not put your name on it and I have known since Friday morning that it was going to be your name, and I have had four days to say something and I have not said anything, and there is no form in this city for saying something about a box on a printed side in an office about a mile away, and there is no form because nothing in this district is a party to anything.*** The Field Book is explicit that the five and the six are **two different lists** and that a reader in about four years is going to merge them. **A STOP THAT A MAN WROTE ABOUT HIMSELF IS NOT A STOP, and he said that out loud in a village in July to nobody, and no later block may report that he broke the rule, may have anybody forgive him, and may not be improved by anybody standing in a yard saying it is the answer.**

6. **THE THINGS THAT STOPPED WHEN THE CONTRACT STOPPED, AND THE COST IS NOT CURABLE.** (a) **THE CONDITION COLUMN IS NOT EMPTY, IT IS NOT THERE — and that is now available on the page, and the difference has been available to him since the thirty-first of August.** A column that is empty is a column somebody could have written in; a column that is not there is a column that does not exist on any form in any building. **THE REASON COLUMN IS NOT THERE EITHER. A LATER BLOCK MAY NOT PUT A WORD IN EITHER COLUMN ABOUT WHY THE DAYS IN BETWEEN WERE BLANK, MAY NOT FAULT THE TWO ENTRIES, AND MAY NOT IMPROVE THE FOUR WORDS.** (b) **THE SATCHEL, and this is the sixth thing and it is the one nobody has been told about: the post that comes to eleven streets has come on a Friday morning in a satchel that went out with the works' papers and came back on the Friday afternoon, because a district with no correspondence address is on no other post, and the works' papers were the only thing in this district that ever weighed anything. THERE HAS NOT BEEN A SATCHEL OUT SINCE FRIDAY THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF AUGUST AND THERE IS NOT GOING TO BE ANOTHER ONE, AND THE DISTRICT RETURNS CARD THAT HAS COME IN THAT FRIDAY POST SINCE THE MIDDLE OF JANUARY HAS NOT COME, AND IT IS A PRINTED SIDE, AND IT IS ONE, AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE THAT ARRIVES, AND IT ARRIVES WEEKLY, AND IT HAS NOT ARRIVED.** (c) **THE SECOND TIN IS AT THIRTY-SEVEN AND THERE IS NO THIRTY-EIGHT, on a bottom shelf in a plan room in another building under a repair schedule, and a repair schedule is a schedule, and nobody has ever asked when it ends, and he is not going to ask.** (d) **THE RACK IS A PIECE OF FURNITURE, ABOUT FOUR FEET HIGH, OPEN AT THE FRONT, AGAINST A WALL, FOUR ROOMS ABOVE A FITTER'S SHOP, AND ON FRIDAY THE FOURTH OF SEPTEMBER THERE IS A RACK WITH NOTHING IN IT IN IT, and there is a mark on the shelf about nine inches from the left edge and about two inches wide, and it is the only trace of nine months of weekly sheets in this city and nobody put it there on purpose. NOBODY EMPTIES A RACK, THAT IS NOT A DECISION ANYBODY MADE, IT IS A THING THAT HAS NEVER HAD TO BE DONE, AND A THING THAT HAS NEVER HAD TO BE DONE DOES NOT HAVE A FORM, AND THE ABSENCE OF A FORM IS NOT A FAULT, AND THERE IS NOTHING IN THIS CITY THAT SAYS A RACK IS FINISHED, AND THERE IS GOING TO BE ONE ON FRIDAY THE ELEVENTH AND ON FRIDAY THE EIGHTEENTH AND ON THE FRIDAY AFTER THAT.** (e) **THE ELEVEN MEN AND THE FOREMAN ARE SOMEWHERE AND THE DISTRICT DOES NOT KNOW WHERE, because the contract is between the city and a firm, and a firm is rateable, and a firm has an address and a file and a name and can be written to, and the district cannot, and there are no eleven men and no foreman in the file at all.** The eleventh streets is **not** a district that is not on a list of anything: it is on **exactly one printed side in the whole of this city**, and the side names it as a thing that is not going to be maintained, and that is the one line it has ever had and it is a line about stopping.

7. **THE TYPED NAME, THE MECHANISM, AND WHO IS NOT TO BE ANGRY AT — AND IT IS THE SAME MECHANISM AS THE SEVENTH COLUMN AND THE SAME MECHANISM AS THE REGISTER.** **Side four, sixth column, typed, completed in about a minute and a half on a THURSDAY — and they complete them on the Thursday, and it has been a Thursday thing for about eleven years and it is not a punishment and it is not a meeting: *Ground of discontinuance: (a) no maintained seam of record* / *NOT TO BE MAINTAINED AFTER 31 AUGUST* / *Signature of the person responsible: R. PRYCE*.** **NOBODY LIED.** A district with no rateable unit has no correspondence address, so it cannot be sent anything and cannot be asked anything, **and it has been exactly that since 1911**, so the office did the only thing available to it and went to the document it could reach; **a contract for surface and drainage works names the supervisor, because a contract has to be able to say who is in charge of a gang on a Tuesday.** **THE THING HE WROTE AND DID NOT SEND, in ninety seconds on the back of their own schedule, and the thing she would not put in a cell, and the entry he would not make in a register, are THE SAME FINDING ARRIVED AT THREE TIMES BY THREE PEOPLE: A FILE IS A PARTY, A PARTY IS A PERSON, A PERSON IS A NAME IN A BOX, AND THE ONLY WAY A DEPARTMENT HAS OF WRITING TO A PLACE WITH NO ADDRESS IS TO GO TO A CONTRACT.** A later volume may not name a person behind the office that completed the row, may not make the row a fraud, may not have it withdrawn or varied, and may not make the eleven streets the thing a document was refused for. **THE SAME MECHANISM, AGAIN, IN CH 158: a district with a correspondence address is a district that can be written to; a district that can be written to is a district whose returns are held somewhere; a district whose returns are held somewhere is a district somebody can ask a card from; and there is ONE card in this city with a hand on it for the fen country and it has a line through it and a man's name under the line and the re-run is on a Friday at about four. SHE OPENED THE REGISTER OF CORRESPONDENTS AND ENTERED NOTHING.**

8. **THE RELATIONSHIP, MILESTONE 2, DEEPENED, AND IT IS MILESTONE 3 SET UP AND NOT REPAIRED, AND THE BLOCK'S POV BREAK IS THE MECHANISM OF IT.** **Chapter 158 is in Mara Quill's close third person and Milo Rook is not in the room and has not been in that room on a last Thursday in about two years; it is the second Mara-POV chapter in the manuscript after Chapter 30 and it is the only available way to give her the half that is hers while keeping him out of the room.** No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue, and nothing is going to. **The custody register page is untouched for the NINTH consecutive block: it is in a drawer with a coat in it, it was not produced, not named, not confessed to, and NOT MENTIONED IN DIALOGUE, and he did not ask and he was not allowed to.** He has not told her about the season and **no week-count is printed in these ten chapters**; she found out he had stopped counting by noticing that the number had stopped going up, **and has not told him she noticed.** She did not describe the line in the return, **nobody asked her to, and Chapter 158 says so in its own text and declares that a cell and a line are two different things in a return and that paper is not a reason.** The disagreement, third time, unrepaired, colder, and **she is right and he is right and neither of them won it**: *it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing that exists* / *it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing a man with a box can ask for in about four years* / **Yes. And I have proved both of those in one morning, at the same desk, with the same pen, and you were not in the room, and I am not going to pretend that either of us is going to win it, and I am not going to be warm with you about it in a corridor.** **Nor am I.** **A later block may not repair this in this volume, may not make either of them warm, and may not have her describe the line on a reconciliation day or have him in the room.**
