9. **THE FOUR UNSHOWABLE THINGS ARE FOUR AND THIS BLOCK DID NOT MAKE A FIFTH, AND HE IS NOT COUNTING.** **A rectangle of standing water on the twenty-ninth of January; a hundred and ten yards of frontage in the rain on the nineteenth of February; a plate in a field on the sixth of April; and about nine people in eleven streets standing in the weather for nothing at all on the fifteenth of April.** **NOBODY IN CHAPTERS 151–160 STANDS IN THE WEATHER ON A WEDNESDAY**, and the second side of the sheet is right about Wednesday the twenty-second of July and Wednesday the twelfth of August and about every one since the eighth of April, **and those two are the only Wednesdays the block prints — the earlier version of this item said the fifth of August, which no chapter is dated to and which is now struck; see `CR-051` item 3(a) —** **for the same reason and a second reason: the man of about sixty at a shed at Cardew Docks is not on the Row on a Wednesday and has not been since before the war and does not know that a piece of paper exists and is not going to be told, and a district entered as nil has not got a second side of anything and could not have one. HE IS NOT COUNTING THEM AND HAS NOT COUNTED THEM SINCE THE FIRST OF JULY AND THE CHAPTER SAYS TWICE THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO START AGAIN IN THE LAST WEEK OF A MONTH. A FIFTH NEEDS SOMEBODY TO BE SURPRISED.** The plate in the grass at the low end of Bridge Street is a **hundred and fifty-one days old** on Friday the fourth of September and he did not go and look at it on the last morning there would be eleven men on that side of the bridge, **and that is the fifth time he has not gone and looked at a thing he could have looked at, and he has written that the not-going is now about a place rather than about a person and that he has decided to stop finding out what the difference is worth.**

10. **THE POWER LEDGER, THE COSTS, THE GAIN, AND THE DAY-COUNTS, AND NONE OF IT MOVED BUT THE FIGURES.** The remembered-route list is **ELEVEN**, the crossings are **TEN**, the tally is **TEN LINES**, and **THERE IS NO CROSSING IN CHAPTERS 151–160** — he held nothing, stabilised nothing, pinned nothing, and **if you do not stage a crossing both figures do not move.** The ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained and the promise is now broken in writing, in the form given in item 5(vi)'s companion: **on Monday the thirty-first of August he wrote that he is not going to explain it, that the end of July was the thirty-first of July, that he did not do it then, that this is the second time he has said so and the second time has cost a day, and that a promise kept in a book and broken in a book is not a promise and is not a break and is a third thing, and nothing on that page is a device.** The written line home went on the twenty-fourth of November, which is day one, **and the page-home figures printed in this block are 242 (23 Jul), 243 (24 Jul), 250 (31 Jul), 257 (7 Aug), 262 (12 Aug), 264 (14 Aug), 274 (24 Aug), 281 (31 Aug), 282 (1 Sep) and 285 (4 Sep)**, with 240, 241, 244 to 249, 251 to 256, 258 to 261, 263, 265 to 273, 275 to 280, 283 and 284 carried and not printed. **The Lark token, day zero = the seventh of December, is printed at 228 (23 Jul), 229 (24 Jul), 236 (31 Jul), 243 (7 Aug), 248 (12 Aug), 250 (14 Aug), 260 (24 Aug), 267 (31 Aug), 268 (1 Sep) and 271 (4 Sep)**. **THE TWO REGISTERS MUST NOT BE MERGED.** The plate, from the sixth of April, is printed at **107 (22 Jul), 108 (23 Jul), 109 (24 Jul), 116 (31 Jul), 123 (7 Aug), 128 (12 Aug), 130 (14 Aug), 140 (24 Aug), 147 (31 Aug), 148 (1 Sep) and 151 (4 Sep)**. The condition column, empty since the twelfth of February, is printed at **161 (23 Jul), 162 (24 Jul), 169 (31 Jul), 176 (7 Aug), 181 (12 Aug), 183 (14 Aug), 193 (24 Aug), 200 (31 Aug), 201 (1 Sep) and 204 (4 Sep)**, and **on the thirty-first of August it is two hundred days and it is not there.** **NO NEW CUMULATIVE FIGURE IS PRINTED FOR THE REASON COLUMN AND THERE IS NEVER GOING TO BE ONE, and a nil on a printed side is the same zero and not a fourth nil.** The foreman's clock, **which nobody is going to ask him about, and the silence is on the facing page of the Field Book in Milo's own handwriting and it now has a date on it, and the whole chain is now counted from a chapter heading and not from this item: 13 weeks 1 day (23 Jul, Ch 151), 13 weeks 2 days (24 Jul, Ch 152), 14 weeks 2 days (31 Jul, Ch 153), 15 weeks 2 days (7 Aug, Ch 154), 16 weeks exactly (12 Aug, NOT PRINTED), 17 weeks 5 days (24 Aug, Ch 157), 18 weeks 5 days (31 Aug, Ch 159) and 19 weeks 2 days (4 Sep, Ch 160 — the chapter printed 19 weeks 3 days and that was one day out, because 31 August is 18 weeks 5 days and four days later is 19 weeks 2 days; see `CR-051` item 2). THERE IS NO FOREMAN FIGURE FOR TUESDAY THE FIRST OF SEPTEMBER AND NONE MAY BE PRINTED, because no chapter gives one; the earlier version of this item carried 19 weeks exactly (1 Sep) and had nothing behind it — see `CR-051` items 2 and 3(b).**

11. **THE FIELD BOOK'S PAIRS ARE TWENTY-NINE AND THEY DID NOT MOVE ONCE IN FORTY-SIX DAYS, AND THAT IS CORRECT, AND THE REASON IS ON THE PAGE SIX TIMES.** **The list has not moved since the night of the sixteenth of July and he has written an entry on most nights since then, and an interval of one day is not a pair whether or not either of the two days has anything on it, and a pair is a fact about where he was and not about what happened. THE ORDINALS ON THOSE SIX ENTRIES ARE NOW 5, 6 AND 7 AND NOTHING ELSE, AT Ch 151, Ch 153 AND Ch 159, and the other three — 152, 156, 160 — carry no ordinal, which is correct; the first version had *fifth* twice and *sixth* once, and the Batch 0003 review is why this is stated; see `CR-051` item 5. A LATER BLOCK MAY NOT REUSE ANY OF THE THREE, AND MAY NOT PRINT A TWELFTH THING OF THAT SHAPE.** IF THE COUNT OF PAIRS IN THIS BLOCK IS A SMALL NUMBER AND NOT A LARGE ONE, THAT IS CORRECT.** The list is still **two sixes, seven threes, one nine, four twos, three fives, one eight, one twenty-five, four sevens, six fours**, and it must not be read as twenty-one, twenty-six, twenty-eight, thirty or thirty-one, and **the four threes that were here in December are not collapsed and there is one eight and not two.** **TWO SEVENS IN ABOUT A FORTNIGHT WAS A COINCIDENCE IN JULY AND IS STILL A COINCIDENCE ON THE FOURTH OF SEPTEMBER, AND HE HAS WRITTEN NOTHING ABOUT IT, AND A LATER BLOCK MAY NOT MAKE IT A PATTERN.** There is no thirtieth pair, no eleventh pair, no thirteenth pair, no twenty-first pair, no twenty-second pair and no twenty-eight pair.

12. **THE SENSORY FAILURE RECURRED ONCE AND ACQUIRED NOTHING, AND IT DID NOT BECOME THE BELL.** **At about half past one on Friday the twenty-fourth of July, on a top track, for about four seconds, the sound went — no sound at all, with the bell, the spade, the man, the wind in a willow and the water in a ditch eleven feet away all exactly where they had been — and under it, in the same four seconds, a taste he has never put a name to and has never put a place to, and it came and it went in the same four seconds, and it was not the same as the silence and it was not in it.** It had no face, no place, no person and no name on it, it acquired none, **it did not become Neris and it did not become Lark and it did not become a key, AND IT DID NOT BECOME THE BELL, and the reason is on the page in his own hand: *a lost sound and a bell that rings are two different things and I am not going to write them in the same column*, and that is the only rule on that page which is not about a month.** He has written down for the third time that he does not know whether a thing that has not come is a thing that has stopped or a thing that has not come, and has improved it none of the three. **The first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in Chapters 151–160.**

13. **THE BOUNDARIES ARE THREE, THE SETS OF MARKS ARE THREE, AND NEITHER IS JOINED, AND THE THIRD POST IS NOT PASSED.** **AND CHAPTER 154 IS NOW ONE WALK AND NOT THREE POSITIONS, WHICH IS THE REPAIR THE BATCH 0003 REVIEW ASKED FOR AND WHICH CHANGED NOBODY'S PLACE: the first version had him on the bridge deck, at the second post and at the third post at once. He crosses the deck at about a quarter past eleven, goes out along the path, is at the second post at about half past eleven, does not go and look at the two sets of marks, goes on to the third, reads the cast number out loud standing up about three hundred yards from a bridge deck, stands at the third post for about four minutes and does not go on, and is back over the bridge at about twenty past twelve. The mower man is still on the deck, the two sets of marks are still at the second post, the cast number is still at the third, the bridge is in MIREWARD, and he walks back to the north end and rides the Friday flatbed to the city — see `CR-051` items 6 and 7.** **Three boundaries: a corner and a fence at the low end of Bridge Street; a mowing line on a bridge deck where two things stopped and neither was a person — and a MAN has now stopped there as well, which is a man and not a boundary, and Chapter 154 says so and refuses to say what it is; and a column in a quarter-inch file that says CITY or EXT whose two ends are a kerb and a bank.** **Three sets of marks: two sets about ninety feet apart near the second post on the far side of the bridge, two hands, the same water on the same days, neither in a file; nineteen cuts in a post at a ford; and a cast number in the collar of a culvert head beside the third post, which is in column three of the schedule.** In Chapter 154 **he was about eleven feet from the two sets of marks and could have walked ten yards and did not**, and **he read the cast number out loud, standing up, in about four seconds, to nobody, on a Friday morning in August, and did not write it down — the first of the ten things of that shape in eight months that was neither producing it nor writing it down, and a thing said out loud to nobody in a fenland is not a record, and the only reason that is true is that fenlands are large and there is nobody in them.** **NOBODY HAS EVER GONE PAST THE THIRD POST AND NOBODY IS GOING TO, and that is not a rule and it is not a resolution and it is not on the facing page. NOBODY HAS LEVELED THE HANDRAIL POST AND NOBODY IS GOING TO, and about nine families navigate that path by a post four inches short of the one it took out, and he did not ask the man who cuts both halves of the bridge about it while standing eleven feet from him, and he has written that down.** A later block may not add a fourth kind of boundary, may not join the three, may not merge the sets of marks, and may not treat the posts as instances of the marks.

14. **THE THREE VOLUME 01 CLUES ARE THREE AND ARE UNJOINED, AND THE THREE VOLUME 02 LINES ARE STILL LINES.** The copied tick in a fourth column in six houses in four streets. The copied turn sheet with a contractor's head on it in five houses, one of them with **Owen Fitch's name and *third floor* in a space left empty on purpose**. The carbon relocation schedule with **one** margin mark, filed as the second attachment to a nineteen-paragraph objection. **NOBODY IN CHAPTERS 151–160 JOINS ANY OF THEM TO ANY OTHER, TO THE ROUTING SLIP, TO THE WORKING TIMETABLE, TO THE THREE SETS OF MARKS, TO THE SIX WORDS ON THE CARD, TO THE LINE IN MARA QUILL'S RETURN, TO THE DISTRICT SCHEDULE, TO THE CAST NUMBER, OR TO THE FOUR COPIES OF THE BELL. A fourth is not permitted to become a fourth pattern and this block adds none on their behalf.** Two documents carry a pair of marks and not three, Kest has not matched the strings, and **SOREN ROOK IS NOT NAMED ANYWHERE IN CHAPTERS 1–160.** The order is suspended and not discharged, not varied and not withdrawn; the public name is not on the street name index; the classification of the eleventh is withdrawn and stays withdrawn; **the removed sentence cannot be restored and the department cannot account for its removal and has been asked twice and has not answered twice, and a query came back down a second time in April and stopped asking about a sentence and started asking about a box, and the box went up empty a second time**; the matter stands over and is not adjourned and has no date and is on no list, and **NOTHING IN THIS BLOCK HAS GIVEN IT A CHAIR**; the line of the twelfth of January stands and its condition is the answer of the fourth of December and is not the department's; the levy is two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term, on two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number, and it is not curable, and **LENA MARR IS NOT IN THIS BLOCK AND IS NOT PAID ANYTHING IN IT**; a woman of sixty on a second landing is unnamed, is the ninth of twenty-six names and is not going to be told anything; **a round is one man short; a plate is in the grass and is never reported; a cover has been flush since the sixth of April and there is no form for a fault that stopped; an annual return goes in every January with its fifth box filled nil, a box asked about twice, and a sixth line of fifty-four words that nobody in this district has read and that Milo Rook does not know the contents of — AND NOBODY IN THIS BLOCK READ IT, QUERIES LINE FIVE, OR ANSWERS EITHER QUERY.** A card under a brick is a thing and not a job; **Pell Harrow is seventeen, has no power of any kind, and is not in this batch, TERN IS STILL NOT FOUND, his own blank card is one of the three things under a brick and has been blank since the nineteenth of January, and the run-number card's line on the front has not been taken off**; **the man in the works jacket is unidentified, has four sightings, the last on the nineteenth of February, and there is no fifth sighting and no fifth refusal of it**; the working timetable, the terminus column, the ruled margin headed *actual* with thirty-one names in it, and the eight sentences that bind them are inherited and **were not queried, dated, explained or asked about by anybody including him**, and the division's standing refusal held — **nobody asked a timetable clerk anything, nobody was sent, the man from the round was not sent, the clerk has no first name, and the sentence was not said twice in the same building.**

15. **NO NEW GRAMMAR, NO NEW GRAMMAR BUT THE ONE, NO NEW FACTION, NO NEW ANTAGONIST, NO NEW FINAL ENEMY, NO NEW KIND OF PLACE, AND THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT IS NOT WRITTEN.** **Weather with Cadence leaking is named on the page in Chapter 153, on Milo Rook's own initiative, and it may not be solved by knowing the category: it has got him a question and not an answer, and there is a street in this city that would settle it in about a day, and he is not going to look at it, and he has written that down twice.** **THE RAINWAY IS NOT NAMED AND THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT IS NOT WRITTEN** — that the Rainway is carrying a public mourning displaced from the Quieting is Batch 0004's work and nothing in these ten chapters anticipates it, is a weapon designed by anybody, or belongs to a department that cannot record a loss it has no form for; **no Weather remnant is identified and no Weather remnant's demand is satisfied**; **THE FIRST QUIETING IS NOT EXPLAINED AND HAS NO DATE IN THIS VOLUME AND NONE MAY BE SUPPLIED**; the First Atlas, the Singular Mode and the Quieting are not mentioned anywhere in these ten chapters. **THE OFFICE OF THE FIRST SURVEYOR DID NOTHING AT ALL IN THIS BLOCK** — it is a printed box with a rule and nothing on the rule, it may not request, receive or answer anything, and nobody may ask who is behind it. **THE SEASONAL OUTLOOK IS STILL THE LAST ONE AND THERE IS STILL NO SECOND OUTLOOK, nobody has asked for one in writing since May, and the absence is not explained.** **A length is still a third kind of place and is not a remnant; a boundary is a place where two people agreed to have stopped; a practice is not a place and is not a day; a pad is a pad; a roll in a teacher's head is a roll in a teacher's head; a ferry route is a ferry route; a tidemark is a tidemark; a bell is a bell; a sluice is a sluice; a bar is a bar; a cast number in a collar is a cast number in a collar; a carbon is a copy; a reference number is a reference number; a condition column is a column; a rack is a rack; a dash is a dash.** **THE FINDING THAT GOES WITH THE PROTECTIVE THINGS, UNIMPROVED: a route that cannot be produced cannot be asked after, and that is the only protection any route in this city has ever had, and every conclusion this district has produced in eight months has been that something was going to be found out about a person, and this one is that a thing is safe because there is nothing to find — and there is no form for the smell of a fen in a dry week and he is not going to build one.** **NOBODY WAS ADDED ABOVE CASS ARDERY, THE OFFICE OF THE PREFECT, OR A HEARING PANEL, and nothing that went wrong in this block is attributable to a new face: the escalation is a typed name in a box on a printed side, a contract, a dash in a cell, a register not entered, a sheet that was never printed, a week that was never printed, and a Thursday.** **SOREN ROOK IS NOT NAMED. Sel Iri does not appear and is a Volume 4–10 character. Halden Vey, Orrin Sable and Tarin Kest do not appear.** The grammars named in these ten chapters are Keepsake (the Row, by inheritance) and **Weather with Cadence leaking**, and nothing else. **DOREEN SANKEY DID NOT USE THE INSTRUMENT, and did not in Batches 4, 5, 6, 7 or 8.** And the standing last line stands, unimproved, in the four Field Book entries of this block and in the closing entry of the tenth chapter: ***the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and that is a list of about fourteen other people and not one of them has been asked to be on it.***

16. **A RUN OF THINGS THAT WERE BUILT FOR ONE PURPOSE AND ARE THE WRONG SHAPE, AND THE COUNT IS NOT NUMBERED.** A **weekly sheet, which is a week, being the only place a morning can go**; a **machine that prints a form for a period that is not in a contract**, told to a man by a man of about fifty and not asked for; a **column set once for nine hundred districts and not again, because the day is a column and you cannot take a column out without taking the day out**; **two inches of ruled space at the bottom of a card, used to put a man's name in**; a **register of correspondents, a list of where a letter goes, being the only way a district becomes a thing that generates requests**; a **cell in a return, which is a week and a figure, having to carry *nothing was sent***; **a contract's supervisor, used as a signature, by an office that could not write to the district**; a **condition column built for a hole and asked to record a state, and then not there at all**; and a **bell on a wall rung every day at the same hour, which is a timetable, and a timetable is a document, and a document is a form with a signature, and the moment anybody in this district is responsible for it there is a form and the four people stop** — which is the one wrong-shaped thing in eight months that was found in somebody else's work and not put there, and he wrote it down and did not tell her and is not going to.

17. **THE THREE COUNTS A REVIEWER MUST CHECK, PRINTED ON THE PAGE, AND EACH ONE AGAINST ITS OWN RULE.** **THE WALKS: gang days are Mondays and Thursdays at 07.30 and there are TWELVE of them between the twenty-first of July and the thirty-first of August, and every one of them is inside this block — Thu 23 July, Mon 27 July, Thu 30 July, Mon 3 Aug, Thu 6 Aug, Mon 10 Aug, Thu 13 Aug, Mon 17 Aug, Thu 20 Aug, Mon 24 Aug, Thu 27 Aug, Mon 31 Aug — twelve; of those, this block prints the THIRTY-SECOND (23 Jul, Ch 151), the FORTY-FIRST (24 Aug, Ch 157) and the FORTY-THIRD (31 Aug, Ch 159), and the forty-third is the last walk of the season, and the arithmetic 32 → 41 → 43 is fixed by the gang days between them (seven days, 23 Jul to 24 Aug, is nine gang days: 27 and 30 July, 3, 6, 10, 13, 17, 20 and 24 August) and by the last Thursday (27 August, the forty-second, carried and not printed). NO TOTAL RUNNING COUNT IS PRINTED.** **THE RACKS: rack days are Fridays and there are SIX in this block — 24 July, 31 July, 7 August, 14 August, 21 August and 28 August — and the twenty-eighth is the last Friday inside the contract, so the count of rack days must come out at the number of Fridays in the period and the last of them must be the twenty-eighth, and there is no Friday the thirtieth because the thirtieth of August is a SUNDAY.** **THE SECOND TIN, which counts rack days, not weeks, and not the last walk: thirty-one (23 Jul, last rack day 17 Jul), thirty-two (24 Jul), thirty-three (31 Jul), thirty-four (7 Aug), thirty-five (14 Aug), **thirty-six (24 Aug, last rack day Friday the twenty-first)**, THIRTY-SEVEN (the Friday twenty-eighth of August — the LAST FIGURE THE SECOND TIN EVER HOLDS), and it does not go anywhere after that, and the tin does not know that and neither does the repair schedule.** **A READER WHO FINDS A RACK COUNT THAT IS NOT THE NUMBER OF FRIDAYS HAS PUT A RACK DAY ON A TUESDAY; A READER WHO FINDS A WALK THAT IS NOT A MONDAY OR A THURSDAY HAS PUT A WALK ON A WEDNESDAY; AND A READER WHO FINDS THE TIN AT THIRTY-EIGHT HAS INVENTED A SHEET.**

---

## What Chapters 161–170 fixed on the page, in the same form as CR-013

Each item below is now prose rather than plan. **None of them changes a premise, a world rule, a power rule, the antagonist ladder, or the planned ending. Every figure here was made from the manuscript and is quotable from a chapter in `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0161.md` … `chapter-0170.md`, after the writer's own verification pass, which is recorded in `CR-053`. `outline/volume-03.md` was NOT edited and needs no change; `outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-01.md` were not edited, and the volume's final image — a village school painting its route on a wall in several colours, none of them official — is NOT written and belongs to Chapter 200.** Chapters 161–170 are the **fourth block of Volume 03 and they are the volume's MIDPOINT**, and they inherit the Chapters 151–160, 141–150, 131–140, 121–130, 111–120, 101–110, 91–100, 81–90, 71–80, 61–70 and Volume 01 lists above whole. **Volume 02 remains closed at Chapter 130 and nothing in these ten chapters discharges, restores, varies, withdraws, re-authors or re-lists anything in it.**

1. **THE RAINWAY IS NAMED, THE DISPLACEMENT IS NAMED, AND NEITHER ONE EXPLAINS ANYTHING THAT CAME EARLIER. THIS IS ITEM 1 OF THE SIXTEENTH LIST AND A LATER BLOCK MUST NOT UNDO IT.**

   **THE NAME, AND IT IS A NAME OF A WATER AND IT IS MINE AND IT IS IN MY OWN BOOK, AND NOBODY ELSE HAS IT.** **Rainway**, on the outside of a wrapper, in a hand that is not a hand in that room, on a consignment of survey and sheet material from a drainage board that no longer exists. **It has been a water in this city's land papers since about 1911. It is the name on nine plots of about four acres held on shares. It runs about two miles above a ford that has been dry since about 1919. Nobody in this district has ever used the word in a conversation and a woman who has been in a repository for four years has never heard it said.** Chapter 169, in her words: *That is a water. It has been a water in this city's land papers since about 1911 and it is the name on nine plots of about four acres each that are held on shares, and it runs about two miles above a ford that has been dry since about 1919, and I have never once in four years heard a person say that word in a conversation and I did not know it was a word anybody used until I read it. It is the name of the water. That is all it is on the outside of that bundle.*

   **THE DISPLACEMENT, IN CHAPTER 169'S OWN SENTENCES, AND IT IS A FACT ABOUT A MOURNING AND NOT ABOUT A PLACE.** *Somebody kept a day. **The day was a public mourning. Not a district's and not a family's. It was kept, and people came to it, and it was in the city, and it was announced on a printed side, and it is the only instance in this whole novel of a public grief that anybody ever entered anywhere.** And the sheet at the end of the printed side was how they entered it. A day was kept and a column was provided and the column wanted names, and forty people wrote their names in one hand, and the hand was one hand because a column wants one hand and a column is a column.* And then: **the sheet became the account. Not of who had been. Of who the day was for. Forty names in one hand went into a record and the record wanted one account, and the account wanted one account, and there was a setting for it, and the setting had a name, and the name was on the flap, and he had read it eleven seconds earlier. And the part of a mourning that could not be made to be one account did not stop. It went out of the record. And out of the record is the weather.**

   And the sentence that carries the whole reversal, said out loud, standing up, in a room where two people were, to nobody: ***A mourning that is displaced from the only form that could have held it is a mourning that is still happening, somewhere, in the weather. And it is not a weapon, and nobody designed it, and nobody put it there.***

   **AND THE REGISTRY'S REFUSAL IS CORRECT AND IS THE THING THAT MADE IT WORSE, AND THE REFUSAL IS NOT VILLAINY.** ***The Registry cannot acknowledge it. And the Registry is not refusing to. There is no form in this city in which a district can say that something happened to it*** — **a man of sixty-one said a version of it in about four hundred words on the twenty-fourth of July, a man of forty-four said a version of it in four seconds on the fifteenth of September, a woman of thirty-four has refused a date since July, and the second-in-command of Survey Enforcement said it in nine seconds in a room in Crown Heights on the twenty-third of September, and all four are right, and Milo Rook has no answer to any of them.** And the chain, used and not re-derived: **a district that cannot report a loss is a district with nothing to report; a district with nothing to report is entered as stable; a district entered as stable is a district that cannot be frightened; a district that cannot be frightened is a district where there is nothing to insure against; and a district where there is nothing to insure against is a district whose works are not reimbursable at the present rating, which is one of the four grounds on a key printed on the second side of a district schedule, and the key is correct, and he has got nothing against the key, and he has wanted something against the key since the third of July and he is not going to look.** **There is nobody to be angry at, and that has been the answer since the third of July, and it has got worse every week for twelve weeks, because every week the machinery does something correct, and not one of those things went wrong and not one of them has a person behind it who meant it.**

   **AND THE NAMING DOES NOT EXPLAIN THE NAMING, ON THE PAGE, IN HIS OWN HAND: a word out of a book is not a description of a thing; the name on a wrapper does not say what the water does, and the water does not say what the word means, and the two of them are in the same room because a bundle of paper that came from a drainage board has the board's water on the outside of it.** **He did not join it to the four bells, or to the bell on the post at the ford, or to the plate, or to the cast number in the collar, or to the handrail post, or to the card with his name in it, or to the two typed names, or to the yard; a name and a watercourse are two things and the reason they are in one bundle is a bundle, and a bundle is not a reason.** **AND A LATER BLOCK MAY NOT: give the name to anybody else; put it in a document, a card, a schedule, a return, a register or a book that goes into a building; turn it into a haunting, a claim, a ghost, a voice, a knock, a person, a curse, a debt or an inherited role; date any bell as a supernatural event; put anybody inside the bell on the post at the ford; satisfy any Weather remnant's demand; make the Rainway designed, or a weapon, or anybody's fault; say who was in the room when the Singular Mode was engaged; put a year on the sheet; attach a date to the Quieting; explain the removed sentence, the withdrawn classification of the eleventh, the suspended order or the matter that stands over; or turn a word in a coat into an instrument, of which there is no form and there is not going to be one.**

2. **THE THREE WORDS, NAMED ONCE, IN SMALL TYPE, ON A FLAP, AND NOT UNDERSTOOD.** On the outside flap of the bundle, printed under a rule, a *schedule of instruments* with three entries: **First Atlas** — citywide; custody distributed; gaps widened, not closed. **Singular Mode** — emergency setting; one account to become the Index's version; others suspended. **Quieting** — setting not shut down; forced output thereafter treated as the record. **He wrote them down once, in his own hand, in the printed order, and did not underline them and did not write what he thought they were and did not ask.** **A flap is not a catalogue item and there is no number on it and there is no shelf it can be asked for by, and that is the whole of the difference between the flap and the shelf, and it is why she read it out and did not give him the shelf number.** **THERE IS NO DATE ON THE WRAPPER, NONE ON THE FLAP, NO SECTION NUMBER, AND NO YEAR ON THE SHEET INSIDE, AND HE DID NOT ASK FOR ONE: a fold is not a chronology, a mark transferred this week is not older than the sheet it landed on, and a day with no year is not a date.** Nobody is named on the wrapper or the flap and no body is named except a drainage board that no longer exists. **The First Atlas, the Singular Mode and the Quieting are named in Chapters 161–170 for the first time in the novel, ONCE, and they explain NOTHING that came earlier — not the bell on a post at a ford dry since about 1919, not the plate in the grass, not the removed sentence, not the withdrawn classification, not the order, not the matter that stands over, not the three clues from January, not the four bells, and not a boy of seventeen. No person, faction, office, department or antagonist is named in connection with any of them, and the volume's antagonist remains rung four of the ladder in municipal form. A later block may not make them into a complete explanation in Volume 03.**

3. **THE BUNDLE, THE REPOSITORY, AND THE THREE CORRECT REFUSALS, ALL OF THEM HERS, ALL OF THEM CHANGING NOTHING.** **Bettina Sallow, fifty-four, four years on the hand index at the ninth desk on the left in the Survey School's repository beneath Crown Heights, thirty-four steps up, a plan chest, a window that does not open, a chair under it.** The bundle: paper tied with tape, about three inches thick, about forty leaves, **not on the index, not opened, came in with a consignment of drainage material about 1911 from a body that no longer exists and was not asked for by anybody, wrapper has a watercourse on the outside of it in a hand that is not a hand in that room, and on her own finding aid: not a survey, not a schedule, not an index card, nothing in it is a rateable thing. Outside, marked in the same hand: *received, unasked for. not evidence. not for index.*** **She got it down off the shelf herself on the twenty-ninth of September and neither of them said the number out loud and neither asked the other.** The three refusals, verbatim in substance: **(i) THE SHELF NUMBER — a number on a shelf in a room I am responsible for is a reference, a reference is a thing a person can be asked for in about four years, and if I say it then in about four years a man can say it, and then he can ask me whose hand wrote it down, and then there is a person. He did not ask a second time and did not write it down. (ii) THE SECOND FLAP — I have not looked and I am not going to look and that is not a rule I made for you, that is what I do, and if there is a second sheet then there are two sheets, and a thing that is in two places is a thing that somebody can be asked about in one of them. (iii) WHAT SHE THINKS THE THREE WORDS ARE — I have not got those three words in this room in four years of cards and I am not going to guess at them, and I am not going to tell you what I think they are, because a person in a good coat writes down what a person in a good coat thinks they are, and then in about four years there is a sentence in a book in a coat with three words in it that I had a feeling about, and that is the worst kind of document there is.** And the sentence she gave the volume, unasked: **a repository is a room where things go that a department has decided are not evidence. A bundle is not evidence of a thing. A bundle is evidence that a department sent a thing away, and I have never in four years seen a department send a thing away that it did not have a reason for, and the reason is always the same, and the reason is that the thing could not be put in the form.** **She is not the woman of fifty-four with a plot number and no bill in six years, who is not mentioned in any of these ten chapters. She has not been thanked, he did not thank her, she has said in about nine words that she does not want to be, and a person who has thanked somebody is a person who has asked.**

4. **THE CHANGED METHOD AND THE RECOVERY, BOTH SOMEBODY ELSE'S WORK AND NEITHER A WORD.** **Finding aids.** About nine, since about the middle of August, on a fifth pad whose headings are **what I looked at. how far I went. what I did not open** — no request column. **She had gone and looked before she wrote it down, and the reason is on the page: because a request on a pad is a person who wanted something, and a request is a party, and a party is a person, and in about four years somebody can come and ask me who wanted what, and there is no column anywhere in this room for who wanted what, and I am not going to be the woman who puts a column in.** And: **if a person wants the same thing twice then I have written the same description twice and there are two pieces of paper about a place and not about a person, and two pieces of paper about a place can be asked after, and one piece of paper about a person can be asked for. And that is not yours. That is a method, and it is four years old, and the change is five weeks old, and you are the first person who has been in this room since December.** A finding aid is **a description of what is in a room, as against an index, which is a description of what is where and is a claim; a finding aid is not a claim, it is not a catalogue item, nobody asks the office for one because there is no shelf number on it, and therefore nobody will ever find it, and that is the point of it and I did not invent the point.** **AND THE SEED, GIVEN ON THE TWENTY-SECOND AND NOT ASKED FOR, IN ABOUT ELEVEN WORDS: there is no card in a hand index in this city for a funeral either, and she has checked twice in about four years on her own time, and the second time was about two years ago, and she did it because she could not think of an example, and it is not a card that is missing — a funeral is not a rateable thing, it is not in a street, it does not have a plan, and there is nobody to hold a card about it, and a person dies and there is a paper for the person in a town eleven miles away and in her room there is nothing at all. That is not an oversight. No. That is the design. That is what an index is.** A later block may not make the finding aids an index, a catalogue, a list, a form or a document, and may not join them to the four bells: **a pad in a drawer in a repository is a pad, and four people doing a thing is four people doing a thing, and Milo Rook refused the join in writing and the refusals are the work.**

5. **THE FAILURE, AND IT IS HIS, AND IT COSTS A BOY OF SEVENTEEN, AND THE STANDING TEMPTATION IS NOT A COLUMN ANY MORE.** Monday the twenty-first of September, a room with four machines, a quarter to ten until about twenty past eleven. **The standing temptation is now the use: it is the thing a man in a room with four machines would be glad to be given.** The one good question in six weeks — *is there anything that goes out on a Friday* — answered unasked: **the Thursday bundle. It goes to the works office at about eleven and it has gone every Thursday since before you started coming in here. It is a firm. A firm is rateable. A firm has an address.** And then he asked for it in four seconds without building it, and said what he was doing out loud: **I am going to give the eleventh street district a correspondence address, and it is going to be a firm's, and you are going to do it because nobody has ever asked you for anything in this room and I told you in August there was nothing in it anybody could want, and I was half right, and this is the other half.** The card came off the machine on Wednesday the twenty-third at about nine, went into the bundle at about eleven, **was in a contractor's yard on Thursday the twenty-fourth and came back on Friday the twenty-fifth in a bin and in somebody else's envelope, read standing up by a foreman who then looked at the yard, and Roy Pryce does not know anybody sent it and is not going to be told, and the man in the room with four machines does not know either, and the two of them are each going to go on not knowing about the other for as long as either of them is in this city.**

   **THE THREE COSTS, ALL ON THE PAGE. (i) THE MAN IN THE ROOM HAS BEEN GIVEN A USE, and a district that a machine holds is a district that a machine can be asked for, and in about four years a room can ask that room for every card in that bundle, and the man who put them in is the man who pressed the button, and his name is not on any of them and he is the one who can find them. He did not know that when he said yes and Milo Rook did not tell him. (ii) THE ELEVENTH STREET DISTRICT HAS A CORRESPONDENCE ADDRESS FOR THE FIRST TIME SINCE ABOUT 1911, and it is a contractor's yard, and he has done in nine seconds on a Monday morning the thing a woman of thirty-one refused to do in about two minutes on the twenty-seventh of August, by a different road, and he has not told her, and telling her is not the repair. (iii) A BOY OF SEVENTEEN WAS ASKED, in a yard at about half past four, in front of about nine people, after eleven seconds of preparation, to carry the card, because the round is one man short, and he said NO IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS AND HE WAS RIGHT, and the cost is not the card: THE COST IS THAT HE WAS ASKED. Nobody in this district has ever asked that boy for anything and everybody knows it, and it is one of about fourteen people on a list in a book in a coat, and Milo Rook has put himself on the list of people who have asked him, and it took eleven seconds and he cannot get it off, and a no that a man in a good coat has heard in a yard in front of nine people is not the same as a no that nobody heard, and there is no form anywhere for the difference and there is not going to be one.** A later block may not discharge, vary, withdraw or counter this; may not put Pell Harrow on a sheet, a delivery, a round or a list; may not make him the carrier; and may not have anybody thank him.

6. **THE SECOND PRINTED SIDE IN SEPTEMBER, AND THE ESCALATION'S CONSEQUENCE, IN A ROOM, AND A PERSON NAMED ON SOMETHING AGAIN WHO IS NOT FRIGHTENED.** **Completed by the office on Thursday the seventeenth of September, on a Thursday, as the other one was, in about the same minute and a half, in a room in Crown Heights on Wednesday the twenty-third with about nine people in it and Cass Ardery at the table.** > **MAINTENANCE RESPONSIBILITY — SEASON 3 RETURN** / *Districts in which surface or drainage works have been discontinued during the season. To be completed by the office. State the name of the person responsible for maintenance up to the date of discontinuance, and the office at which returns for the district are held.* / *A return not received within the period stated will be completed by this office.* — **R. PRYCE** in the name box and, beneath it, the office at which returns for the district are held. **NOBODY LIED, and the mechanism is the one from August and it is the same one: a district with no correspondence address cannot be sent anything and cannot be asked anything and has been exactly that since 1911, so the office went to the document it could reach, and a contract names the supervisor, and there is no second document in this city that says who was responsible for that road on a Tuesday in June. And it will happen again next season for a different district.** **He did not sign the first one and he will not sign this one and nobody is going to ask him to, and nobody in the room thought he should be told, because a man of fifty-four with thirty-one years in charge of a gang is not frightened of a form and has been given two of them in five weeks by an office that cannot write to a place, and he does not know it, and nobody is going to tell him. THE BLOCK DID NOT TURN IT INTO A HAUNTING.**

   **THE SENTENCE OF THE BLOCK, IN A DEPARTMENT'S MOUTH, IN NINE SECONDS, AND IT IS CORRECT AND MILO ROOK COULD NOT ANSWER IT: THERE IS NO FORM IN THIS CITY IN WHICH A DISTRICT CAN SAY THAT SOMETHING HAPPENED TO IT. Not this one. Not the schedule. Not a return, not a card, not an inspection, not a condition column. There is a form for a death and a form for a rate and a form for a length of road and there is no form for a loss, and where a district has a loss then the office asks it for a figure, and the district has no figure, and the office completes the row, and the row says the district had nothing to report. That is the design. It is not a complaint and it is not a fault and nobody in this room did it and nobody in this room can undo it. And a district with nothing to report is entered as stable. And a district entered as stable is a district that cannot be frightened. And a district that cannot be frightened is a district where there is nothing to insure against.** **IT IS THE SECOND TIME IN EIGHT MONTHS THAT THE BEST SENTENCE SAID IN A ROOM HAS BEEN SAID BY SOMEBODY ELSE AND HE HAS HAD NO ANSWER TO IT**, and the other three versions of it are a man of sixty-one on a top track, a man of forty-four on a bank, and a woman of thirty-four on a schoolroom step, all inherited and all not re-explained. **AND THE WRONG-SHAPED THING NOBODY BUT HIM NOTICED: a description of a place is how a person gets found** — the second field of the side is the office at which returns are held, which is correct, and in about four years a man can ask the side for the office and then the office for the desk and then for the machine and then for the man, and the man does not know and is not going to be told. **Nobody was added above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel, and nothing that went wrong in this block is attributable to a new face: the escalation is a return, a contract, a box, a Thursday and a description of an office.**

7. **THE THING HE GAVE UP ON PURPOSE, AND IT IS THE SIXTH OF HIS OWN AND NOT THE SIXTH OF THE OTHER SHAPE.** Monday the twenty-eighth of September, about twenty past one, in a yard, with about nine people in it, **in about four seconds, standing up, with nobody watching him decide.** **A yard is the only instrument in this district that has never once turned into a document**, he knew that in June, and in August he wrote that he was not going to be able to use it again this year, **and on the twenty-eighth he decided the sentence was right and the year was wrong, and that a year was the wrong length of time to put on an instrument, and gave up a hundred and ten words he had had ready since the seventeenth of July.** **IT IS ABOUT A PERSON AND NOT ABOUT A HABIT — the rule on the facing page is the habit — and it cost Roy Pryce, who does not know it was a decision and it will not be told to him, because he said the whole of his argument once, correctly, in a yard, on the twenty-fourth of August and has said he is not going to say it again and was right to say that, and the only place that sentence will ever exist is about nine people and a man of forty with a brush. And Milo Rook stood in that yard for two hours and twenty minutes and did not say the seventy-eight words — which are seventy-eight words, and are not eleven words, and are not any other number, and nobody is going to improve them — and did not say anything else either, and nobody asked him to, and he is not going to be thanked for it by anybody.** **THE TWO LISTS NOW HOLD THE SAME NUMBER: five of his own became six, and the six of the other shape are six, and the two are two lists, and the sum of them is not a number, and a reader in about four years is going to add six and six together and get something that does not exist, and it is on the page in the same hand as the four words and the signature and he is not going to improve it. A LATER BLOCK MAY NOT MERGE THEM AND MAY NOT ADD A SEVENTH TO EITHER.**

8. **THE RELATIONSHIP, MILESTONE 3, DEEPER, IN FRONT OF THE MOST DANGEROUS THING YET, AND NOT REPAIRED, AND NEITHER OF THEM WON IT.** **Chapter 167 is his half of Thursday the twenty-fourth of September, the last Thursday of a month, and HE WAS NOT IN THE ROOM**, and he has not been in that room on a last Thursday in about two years. **The return goes to a board on the first Thursday of a month and the first Thursday of next month is the first of October.** She reconciled it: **the district is on the fourth page from the bottom and has not moved, and every cell on that row is a dash again this month, four of them, put in with a pen, the pen being the wrong instrument, and she has used a pen on that return once in about two years.** **SHE DID NOT DESCRIBE THE LINE. Nobody asked her to, he did not ask, he was not in the room, and a cell and a line are two different things in a return and paper is not a reason.** In the corridor at ten past four she opened it: **you have got a district with a correspondence address** — **I found it on Friday because a card that has been going round in a run since the middle of January has not come through my counter, and the only two places it comes from are a room with four machines and a satchel that went out with the works' papers, and the works' papers stopped on the thirty-first of August. I have been sitting with it for three days and I have not put a pen to anything, and I have worked out that I am not angry, and I would like to know what to do with the not being angry, because there is no box for it either.** And the fourth version of the register, and it is the worst: **it is not in a book, and it is a Thursday bundle going to a firm's yard, and nobody audits a contractor's yard.** And the whole of what it does: **a records counter is somewhere a department can be asked a question and a contractor's yard is not, and if the card comes back then a man in that yard will hand it to the only name in the file, and that name is R. PRYCE, and he will carry it out to the district on his own time on a Monday and put it in a bin, and then nothing arrives, and you will put another dash in a cell, and a dash is a statement about the post, and the post has stopped. And that is the first Monday since the twenty-eighth of August on which the correct thing and the useless thing have been the same thing, and there are now four of those places, and you made the fourth one in nine seconds on a Monday morning, and you did not ask me, and I am not going to pretend that you asking me would have stopped it.**

   The disagreement, fourth time, unrepaired, colder, and **she said his half for him**: *it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing that exists* / *it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing a man with a box can ask for in about four years* / **And I have said the second half for you, because you have said it four times and I have said the first half four times, and neither of us has a better one, and I am not going to have it a fifth time in a corridor with a bag over your shoulder.** And the true reason, said once, by him: **you did it because you are a man who writes things down and there was a week with nothing in it and you had nowhere to put it. And I have written that down at about eleven o'clock tonight and it is the truest thing in this entry and it is not a resolution and it is not going to be improved.** **The custody register page is untouched for the TENTH consecutive block: in a drawer with a coat in it, not produced, not named, not confessed to, NOT MENTIONED IN DIALOGUE, and neither of them mentioned it in one word in the corridor. He has not told her about the season and NO WEEK-COUNT IS PRINTED IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS; she has not asked since July; she found out he had stopped counting by noticing that the number stopped going up and has not told him she noticed, and there is no column for that and there is not going to be one. Neither of them was warm, neither won it, and nobody was thanked. A later block may not repair this in this volume, may not make either of them warm, may not let either of them win it, may not have her describe the line on a reconciliation day, and may not have him in the room.**

9. **THE WEEK OF ABSENCES, AND THE FIGURES NOBODY CAN PUT ANYWHERE, AND A FIFTH COLUMN HE DREW FOUR HEADINGS OF AND DID NOT DRAW.** Chapter 161, Monday the seventh to Saturday the twelfth of September. The count, in about four minutes standing up with a pencil and the back of a sheet of his own paper: **seven days since the last walk of the season; two rack days that were not rack days, the fourth and the eleventh, because a rack day is a Friday and a sheet covers Monday to Sunday; six Mondays and Thursdays with nobody on them.** And: **there is no column in this district that wants any of those three numbers; a district is entered as stable or not stable, a week is walked or not walked, a day is gone or not gone; nothing in this city can be told that a week went by and there was nothing in it; a period with nothing in it is a dash, and a dash is a statement about the post and not about the district; and AN ABSENCE IS NOT A FORM.** **THE FIFTH COLUMN: four headings in pencil on the back of a sheet, in about eleven seconds, in order — *Week ending. What was done. By whom. What was found. Why nothing was found.* It is the best one he has ever drawn and it is the same mistake for the eleventh time in eight months, and the mistake is a column that wants a person, and there is no person in it: there is a week and a nothing. A seventh column may be wanted, may be the shape of a whole scene, and he may write in his own book that he wants one and not draw it, and he did: on the seventh of September, in the same book, in the same pencil, under the same rule, he wrote that he wants one and that the rule on the facing page ran out on the thirty-first of August and nobody released him from it, and that a rule that runs out is not a release and it never was a stop, and that he does not know what the difference is between a fifth column and a seventh column and is not going to find out on the back of a sheet of his own paper in a front room on a Monday morning. NOBODY FORGAVE HIM AND NOBODY IS GOING TO.** And the drawer and the bus, written down for the second time and not acted on: **a sheet of 1911 street numbers in a drawer in Crown Heights, about nine hundred of those drawers, a bus every twenty minutes on a weekday, and he has not used one since the second of January, and he did not go on the Tuesday, and this is the second time he has written that sentence down, and a sentence written twice is not a rule and is not a resolution and is a third thing.**

10. **THE POWER LEDGER, THE COSTS, THE GAIN, AND THE DAY-COUNTS, AND NONE OF IT MOVED BUT THE FIGURES.** The remembered-route list is **ELEVEN**, the crossings are **TEN**, the tally is **TEN LINES**, and **THERE IS NO CROSSING IN CHAPTERS 161–170** — he held nothing, stabilised nothing, pinned nothing, and **if you do not stage a crossing both figures do not move.** The ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained and the promise about the end of July remains broken in writing, and **nothing on any page of this block is a device.** The written line home went on the twenty-fourth of November, which is day one, **and the page-home figures printed in this block are 288 (7 Sep), 293 (12 Sep), 296 (15 Sep), 299 (18 Sep), 302 (21 Sep), 303 (22 Sep), 304 (23 Sep), 305 (24 Sep), 309 (28 Sep), 310 (29 Sep) and 311 (30 Sep)**, with 289, 290, 291, 292, 295, 297, 298, 300, 301, 306, 307 and 308 carried and not printed. **The Lark token, day zero = the seventh of December, is printed at 274 (7 Sep), 279 (12 Sep), 282 (15 Sep), 285 (18 Sep), 288 (21 Sep), 289 (22 Sep), 290 (23 Sep), 291 (24 Sep), 295 (28 Sep), 296 (29 Sep) and 297 (30 Sep)**. **THE TWO REGISTERS MUST NOT BE MERGED.** The plate, from the sixth of April, is printed at **154 (7 Sep), 162 (15 Sep), 165 (18 Sep), 168 (21 Sep), 169 (22 Sep), 170 (23 Sep), 171 (24 Sep), 175 (28 Sep), 176 (29 Sep) and 177 (30 Sep)**. **The condition column, empty since the twelfth of February and NOT THERE as of the thirty-first of August, is printed at 207 (7 Sep), 215 (15 Sep), 218 (18 Sep), 221 (21 Sep), 222 (22 Sep), 223 (23 Sep), 224 (24 Sep), 228 (28 Sep), 229 (29 Sep) and 230 (30 Sep)**, and **NO NEW CUMULATIVE FIGURE IS PRINTED FOR THE REASON COLUMN AND THERE IS NEVER GOING TO BE ONE, and the second tin is at thirty-seven and there is no thirty-eight and the tin is not going anywhere and the tin does not know that and neither does the repair schedule it is filed under.** The foreman's clock, **which nobody is going to ask him about, and the silence is on the facing page of the Field Book in Milo's own handwriting and it has a date on it**: **19 weeks 3 days (12 Sep), 20 weeks 6 days (15 Sep), 21 weeks 2 days (18 Sep), 21 weeks 5 days (21 Sep), 21 weeks 6 days (22 Sep), 22 weeks exactly (23 Sep), 22 weeks 1 day (24 Sep), 22 weeks 5 days (28 Sep), 22 weeks 6 days (29 Sep) and 23 weeks exactly (30 Sep)**. The chain is counted forward from a chapter heading and never copied out of a state file, and **NO CALENDAR YEAR MAY BE ATTACHED TO THE PRESENT-DAY CHRONOLOGY, TO A DAY ON THE COUNTED CHAIN, TO A DAY-COUNT OR TO ANY EVENT; the years on the page are the ages of objects, buildings, institutions and ways of doing things, and a new one may not be added to that list.** **The first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in Chapters 161–170.**

11. **THE FIELD BOOK'S PAIRS ARE TWENTY-NINE AND THEY DID NOT MOVE ONCE IN TWENTY-FOUR DAYS, AND THAT IS CORRECT, AND THE REASON IS ON THE PAGE NINE TIMES.** **The list has not moved since the night of the sixteenth of July; he has written an entry on most nights since, and an interval of one day is not a pair whether or not either of the two days has anything on it, and a pair is a fact about where he was and not about what happened. NO ORDINAL IS PRINTED ANYWHERE IN THIS BLOCK, WHICH IS CORRECT — the chain of 5, 6, 7 belongs to Chapters 151, 153 and 159 and a later block may not reuse any of them. IF THE COUNT OF PAIRS IN THIS BLOCK IS A SMALL NUMBER AND NOT A LARGE ONE, THAT IS CORRECT.** The intervals printed are **fifty-eight days (12 Sep), sixty-one (15 Sep), sixty-four (18 Sep), sixty-seven (21 Sep), sixty-eight (22 Sep), sixty-nine (23 Sep), seventy (24 Sep), seventy-four (28 Sep), seventy-five (29 Sep) and seventy-six (30 Sep)**, all counted forward from the sixteenth of July and not from any state file. The list is still **two sixes, seven threes, one nine, four twos, three fives, one eight, one twenty-five, four sevens, six fours**, and it must not be read as twenty-one, twenty-six, twenty-eight, thirty or thirty-one, and **the four threes that were here in December are not collapsed and there is one eight and not two.** **TWO SEVENS IN ABOUT A FORTNIGHT WAS A COINCIDENCE IN JULY AND IS STILL A COINCIDENCE ON THE THIRTIETH OF SEPTEMBER, AND HE HAS WRITTEN NOTHING ABOUT IT, AND A LATER BLOCK MAY NOT MAKE IT A PATTERN.** There is no thirtieth pair, no eleventh pair, no thirteenth pair, no twenty-first pair, no twenty-second pair and no twenty-eight pair. **A later block may not print a twelfth thing of that shape**, and the count of the things he produced or wrote down and did not act on is not printed anywhere in these ten chapters.

12. **THE SENSORY FAILURE RECURRED ONCE, IN CHAPTER 163, AND IT ACQUIRED NOTHING, AND IT DID NOT BECOME ANYTHING, AND HE DID NOT WRITE THE TIME DOWN.** At about the second of four minutes at the third post on Friday the eighteenth of September, **for about four seconds, the sound went — no sound at all — and under it, in the same four seconds, the taste he has never put a name to and has never put a place to, and it came and went in the same four seconds and was not the same as the silence and was not in it.** It had no face, no place, no person and no name, it acquired none, **it did not become Neris and it did not become a key, IT DID NOT BECOME THE RAINWAY, it did not become the mowing line, the post, the marks or the ford, and the reason is on the page in his own hand: a lost sound and a bell that rings are two different things and I am not going to write them in the same column**, which is the only rule on that page which is not about a month, and then: **if it ever becomes a thing I will write the name of that thing in this book at the time, and until then it is four seconds and a taste and nothing, and I am not going to be the man who gives a four-second thing a name, because a name is the only thing in this story that anybody can be asked for.** **HE DID NOT WRITE THE TIME DOWN, WHICH IS THE FIRST TIME IN EIGHT MONTHS HE HAS NOT DONE THAT.** He did not read the cast number in the collar out loud this time either, having read it in August.

13. **THE BOUNDARIES ARE THREE, THE SETS OF MARKS ARE THREE, NEITHER IS JOINED, AND THE THIRD POST IS NOT PASSED.** **The mowing line now has three things stopped on it and is still one boundary**, and this is on the page in Chapter 163 in the mower man's mouth: **there are three things at that line and it is one boundary, and the boundary is the two, and I am not one of the two, because a party is somebody who agreed and I did not agree with anybody. I am a man who has stopped. There is a difference and it took me six weeks to find it.** **Three boundaries: a corner and a fence at the low end of Bridge Street; the mowing line on a bridge deck, where two things stopped and neither was a person and a man has now stopped as well; and a column in a quarter-inch file that says CITY or EXT whose two ends are a kerb and a bank. THE THREE ARE THREE AND ARE NOT JOINED AND NO FOURTH KIND OF BOUNDARY WAS ADDED. Three sets of marks: two sets about ninety feet apart near the second post on the far side of the bridge, two hands, the same water on the same days, neither in a file; nineteen cuts in a post at a ford; and a cast number in the collar of a culvert head beside the third post, which is in column three of the schedule in two buildings. HE WAS ABOUT ELEVEN FEET FROM THE TWO SETS OF MARKS ON THE EIGHTEENTH OF SEPTEMBER AND DID NOT GO, AND HE HAS STOPPED WRITING DOWN WHAT THAT WAS WORTH.** The handrail post is still four inches short, the file still says *replaced like for like*, about nine families navigate by it, **nobody has levelled it and nobody is going to, and he was four feet from the only man on that bridge and did not ask him, the second time, and wrote that down twice.** **NOBODY HAS EVER GONE PAST THE THIRD POST AND NOBODY IS GOING TO**, and he stood at it for about four minutes on the eighteenth of September and two of them were not his and he did not go on. A later block may not add a fourth kind of boundary, may not join the three, may not merge the sets of marks, and may not treat the posts as instances of the marks.

14. **THE THREE VOLUME 01 CLUES ARE THREE AND ARE UNJOINED, AND THE THREE VOLUME 02 LINES ARE STILL LINES, AND NOTHING IN THIS BLOCK RESOLVED ANY OF IT.** The copied tick in a fourth column in six houses in four streets. The copied turn sheet with a contractor's head on it in five houses, one of them with **Owen Fitch's name and *third floor*** in a space left empty on purpose. The carbon relocation schedule with **one** margin mark, filed as the second attachment to a nineteen-paragraph objection. **NOBODY IN CHAPTERS 161–170 JOINS ANY OF THEM TO ANY OTHER, TO THE ROUTING SLIP, TO THE WORKING TIMETABLE, TO THE THREE SETS OF MARKS, TO THE SIX WORDS ON THE CARD, TO THE LINE IN MARA QUILL'S RETURN, TO THE DISTRICT SCHEDULE, TO THE MAINTENANCE RESPONSIBILITY RETURN, TO THE CAST NUMBER, TO THE BUNDLE IN THE REPOSITORY, TO THE RAINWAY, OR TO THE FOUR BELLS.** Two documents carry a pair of marks and not three, Kest has not matched the strings, and **SOREN ROOK IS NOT NAMED ANYWHERE IN CHAPTERS 1–170.** The order is suspended and not discharged, not varied and not withdrawn; the public name is not on the street name index; the classification of the eleventh is withdrawn and stays withdrawn; **the removed sentence cannot be restored and the department cannot account for its removal and has been asked twice and has not answered twice, and a query came back down a second time in April and stopped asking about a sentence and started asking about a box, and the box went up empty a second time**; the matter stands over and is not adjourned and has no date and is on no list, and **NOTHING IN THIS BLOCK HAS GIVEN IT A CHAIR**; the line of the twelfth of January stands and its condition is the answer of the fourth of December and is not the department's; the levy is two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term, on two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number, and it is not curable, and **LENA MARR IS NOT IN THIS BLOCK AND IS NOT PAID ANYTHING IN IT**; a woman of sixty on a second landing is unnamed, is the ninth of twenty-six names and is not going to be told anything; **a round is one man short; a plate is in the grass and is never reported and the decision made on the first of June has been told TO NOBODY, and four people in this city know it is in that grass and have said nothing about it, and one of them is him; a cover has been flush since the sixth of April and there is no form for a fault that stopped; an annual return goes in every January with its fifth box filled nil, a box asked about twice, and a sixth line of fifty-four words that nobody in this district has read and that Milo Rook does not know the contents of — AND NOBODY IN THIS BLOCK READ IT, QUERIED LINE FIVE, OR ANSWERED EITHER QUERY.** A card under a brick is a thing and not a job; **PELL HARROW IS SEVENTEEN, HAS NO POWER OF ANY KIND, WAS ASKED FOR ONE THING IN THIS BLOCK AND REFUSED IT, TERN IS STILL NOT FOUND, his own blank card is one of the three things under a brick and has been blank since the nineteenth of January, and the run-number card's line on the front has not been taken off**; **the man in the works jacket is unidentified, has four sightings, the last on the nineteenth of February, and there is no fifth sighting and no fifth refusal of it**; the working timetable, the terminus column, the ruled margin headed *actual* with thirty-one names in it, and the eight sentences that bind them are inherited and **were not queried, dated, explained or asked about by anybody including him**, and the division's standing refusal held — **nobody asked a timetable clerk anything, nobody was sent, the man from the round was not sent, the clerk has no first name, and the sentence was not said twice in the same building.**

15. **THE FOUR UNSHOWABLE THINGS ARE FOUR AND THIS BLOCK DID NOT MAKE A FIFTH, AND NINE DAYS OF IT ARE WEDNESDAYS, AND HE IS NOT COUNTING.** **A rectangle of standing water on the twenty-ninth of January; a hundred and ten yards of frontage in the rain on the nineteenth of February; a plate in a field on the sixth of April; and about nine people in eleven streets standing in the weather for nothing at all on the fifteenth of April.** **NOBODY IN CHAPTERS 161–170 STANDS IN THE WEATHER ON A WEDNESDAY, and the thirtieth of September is a Wednesday and the second side of the sheet is right about it, for the same reason and a second reason: the man of about sixty at a shed at Cardew Docks is not on the Row on a Wednesday and has not been since before the war and does not know that a piece of paper exists and is not going to be told, and a district entered as nil has not got a second side of anything and could not have one. HE IS NOT COUNTING THEM, HAS NOT COUNTED THEM SINCE THE FIRST OF JULY, IS NOT GOING TO START ON THE LAST DAY OF A MONTH, AND NO TOTAL RUNNING COUNT IS PRINTED ANYWHERE IN THIS BLOCK. A fifth needs somebody to be surprised and nobody in this district has been surprised about anything since the third of July.** The plate is **a hundred and seventy-seven days old on the thirtieth of September**, has never been reported, has never been asked about, and the decision was made on the first of June and is told to nobody, and **a later block may not report it, may not ask anybody about it, may not put it on a list, and may not convert the four who know into four who have been told.**

16. **NO NEW GRAMMAR, NO NEW FACTION, NO NEW ANTAGONIST, NO NEW FINAL ENEMY, NO NEW KIND OF PLACE, AND THE BLOCK'S BEAT MAP IS SATISFIED.** **A length is still a third kind of place and is not a remnant; a boundary is a place where two people agreed to have stopped, and there are three and they are not joined; a practice is not a place and is not a day; a pad is a pad; a roll in a teacher's head is a roll in a teacher's head; a ferry route is a ferry route; a tidemark is a tidemark; a bell is a bell; a sluice is a sluice; a bar is a bar; a cast number in a collar is a cast number in a collar; a carbon is a copy; a reference number is a reference number; a condition column is a column; a rack is a rack; a dash is a dash; a van that belongs to a firm is a van that belongs to a firm; a barrow is a barrow; an envelope that belongs to somebody else is an envelope that belongs to somebody else; a broom is a broom; a finding aid is a finding aid and not a catalogue item.** **THE VOLUME'S GRAMMARS NAMED IN THIS BLOCK ARE Keepsake (the Row, by inheritance) and Weather with Cadence leaking (inherited from Chapter 153), and nothing else, and the Rainway is not a new grammar and is not a new remnant and is a name of a water on a wrapper.** **THE OFFICE OF THE FIRST SURVEYOR DID NOTHING AT ALL IN THIS BLOCK** — a printed box with a rule and nothing on the rule, no person behind it, nobody asked who is behind it, and it requested, received and answered nothing. **THE SEASONAL OUTLOOK IS STILL THE LAST ONE AND THERE IS STILL NO SECOND OUTLOOK, nobody has asked for one in writing since May, and the absence is not explained.** The district stability column still enters Mireward as nil and is still hand-transferred and has never been typed, and this block may not and did not reduce the zero, add a row, or type the column. **A man of fifty-eight with a rod and a chain did not go out in September, and about nine feet of drain in a pad in a drawer in Crown Heights is still not asked for by anybody, and he is not an office and did not become one.** **DOREEN SANKEY DID NOT USE THE INSTRUMENT, and did not in Batches 4, 5, 6, 7 or 8.** The three Volume 01 clues are three and unjoined. **A later block may not thank anybody in this district, may not ask the man of about twenty-six anything, may not give the fishmonger a name, may not ask the man of about forty for his twenty-eight undated words, and may not tell the man of about sixty at a shed that a piece of paper exists.** The standing last line stands, unimproved, in the nine Field Book entries of this block — **Chapters 161, 162, 163, 164, 166, 167, 168, 169 and 170** — and Chapter 165 has no Field Book entry and closes on a coda that is not one, and may not be promoted into one by a later block — *the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and that is a list of about fourteen other people and not one of them has been asked to be on it.*

17. **A RUN OF THINGS THAT WERE BUILT FOR ONE PURPOSE AND ARE THE WRONG SHAPE, AND THE COUNT IS NOT NUMBERED.** A **returns card, which is four columns about a district and two inches about a place, put into a Thursday bundle going to a contractor**; **a maintenance responsibility return, which is a form about who is responsible for keeping apparatus in repair, asked about a district, and answered out of a contract**; **the same return's second field, a description of a place, which is correct and is how a person gets found**; **a drainage return about a depth of water, sent to a farm because the man who holds the water has no address, asking for a figure that is only true on a day**; **a handrail post replaced not like for like, which is the wrong shape for a boundary and the right shape for nothing else**; **a hand index, which is a description of what is where and is a claim, being the nearest thing this city has to a place a loss could be entered in**; **a schedule of instruments printed on the outside flap of a bundle, in a room where a shelf number is a reference and a flap is nothing**; **a rack, about four feet high, with a mark on the shelf about nine inches from the left edge and about two inches wide, and there is no instrument in this city for a mark that has not changed**; and **a word, which is a name for a water and does not make it rain and does not stop it raining.** **THE FINDING THAT GOES WITH THE PROTECTIVE THINGS, UNIMPROVED, AND IT IS NOW ALSO THE FINDING ABOUT THE RAINWAY: a route that cannot be produced cannot be asked after, and that is the only protection any route in this city has ever had, and every conclusion this district has produced in eight months has been that something was going to be found out about a person, and this one is that a thing is safe because there is nothing to find — and a name in a coat is the safest kind of name there is, and there is no form for the smell of a fen in a dry week and he is not going to build one.**
